# Native features

This fork adds a set of pages, tools and game-sim options to Basketball GM. The first group (Newspaper, Wrapped, Storylines, Games to Watch, achievements, Trade Grader, Timeline) used to live in the separate League Explorer app: they are built from the league's own records and run locally in the game's worker. The rest are game features (play-by-play options, a realistic possession model, player traits, injury depth, contracts, trades, drafting, coaching staff, ...).

Most of it is **basketball only**: in Football, Hockey and Baseball GM the menu items and links are hidden and the routes show a short "only available for basketball" message. The exceptions, which work in every sport, are the contract features and the trade features (the code is shared), the Draft Board and the Scouting System.

`:lid` below is the league id, as in `/l/1/...`.

## Where the pages live

Pages that belong with an original BBGM page are tabs of that page: a tab bar at the top of the page (it scrolls sideways on a phone) switches between them. Each tab keeps its own URL, which is the page's old URL, so old links, bookmarks and the command palette (Ctrl+K) land on the right tab. Tabs of a League Extras feature that is off are hidden, and the side menu highlights the host page on any of its tabs. The tab list is `src/ui/util/pageTabs.ts`; `src/ui/util/consolidatedPages.ts` lists the pages kept out of the side menu.

| Page (first tab) | Tabs                                                                                                               |
| ---------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| League Settings  | League Settings, League Extras                                                                                     |
| Trading Block    | Trading Block, Trade Market                                                                                        |
| Trade            | 2 teams, 3 teams                                                                                                   |
| Transactions     | Transactions, Trade Grader                                                                                         |
| League Finances  | Finances, Contract Value, Economics, Home Court                                                                    |
| Draft History    | Draft History, Redraft, Pick Value                                                                                 |
| Draft Scouting   | Draft Scouting, Draft Board, Mock Draft, Combine, Big Boards, Scouts, Pro Scouting, Staff Report, College/Overseas |
| Relatives        | Relatives, Family Trees                                                                                            |
| Jersey Numbers   | League, By Team                                                                                                    |
| Player Ratings   | Player Ratings, Archetypes, Skill Badges                                                                           |
| League History   | League History, Timeline, Coaches, Front Offices, Records Book, Eras                                               |
| Player Stats     | Player Stats, Milestones                                                                                           |
| Daily Schedule   | Daily Schedule, Games to Watch, Best Games                                                                         |
| Head-to-Head     | Head-to-Head, Rivalries                                                                                            |
| Playoffs         | Playoffs, Series Recaps                                                                                            |
| Award Races      | Award Races, Award History, Award Vote                                                                             |

Other pages are still reached from _More_ links: League Achievements (League Stats / History group) and Coaching Staff (team pages and the Team menu).

**Side menu extras** (all sports, remembered in this browser per league with `safeLocalStorage`, `ui/util/sidebarExtras.ts`): _News Feed_, _Trade Offers_ and _Newspaper_ show a small count badge of what's new since you last opened them (News Feed: items at its default "big" level; Trade Offers: offers to you not seen yet; Newspaper: new issues, one per rebuild of the regular season paper and one per new edition). The counts come from the worker (`getSidebarCounts`, `worker/util/sidebarCounts.ts`) when the page, the phase or a sim changes; the first time a league is opened only sets the baseline. A _Recently visited_ group above _League_ lists the last 5 side menu pages you opened in this league (the exact address, so a season or team you picked is kept), newest first, without the page you're on, collapsible like the other groups.

**Side menu** (all sports; `ui/util/menuItems.tsx`, `ui/components/Controller/SideBar.tsx`). Groups: _League_ (Standings, Playoffs, college Conf. Tournaments and NIT, Schedule, Power Rankings, Transactions, Finances), _Newsroom_ (News Feed, Rumors, Newspaper, Wrapped, Storylines), _History_ (League History, League Achievements, Hall of Fame; Timeline, Eras and Records Book are tabs of League History), _Team_, _Players_ (free agency and trades, Compare Players, Watch List), _Draft / College_ (Draft, Draft Scouting with Scouts, Pro Scouting and Staff Report as tabs; college Recruiting, Transfer Portal, Departures & Arrivals, Coaching Carousel, Top 25), _Stats_, _Tools_ (Achievements, Auto Play, Screenshot, Frivolities, Face Style Gallery, Notes, exports), _Settings_ (Global, League, League Extras, Award Settings), _Commissioner_ (God Mode and the league editors, collapsed until expanded), _Danger_ (Delete Old Data, Danger Zone, Delete All Leagues) and _Help_. The top navbar keeps its six dropdowns (Newsroom and History are in League's, Draft / College in Players', Settings, Commissioner and Danger in Tools'). Collapsed groups are remembered in this browser (`sidebarGroups`; the old `sidebarCollapsedGroups` list is carried over once, a collapsed old group collapsing the groups it was split into). At the top: a _Filter menu_ box that lists the matching links (side menu items and command palette entries such as tab pages, with the command palette's rules for God Mode, League Extras and college leagues; ↓ or Enter go to the first result, arrows move between results, Esc clears); a _Now_ group with the pages of the current phase (playoffs; draft lottery and draft; re-signing; free agency; and in a college league conference tournaments, departures, the transfer portal and recruiting); and _Favorites_: the star next to any item (shown on hover or keyboard focus) pins it there, for every league in this browser (`sidebarFavorites`).

"Existing leagues" means leagues created before the setting existed (or league files without it): the switches that change how games or AI teams play are off there, so those leagues play exactly as before, and on for new leagues. The display-only features are on everywhere.

## League extras (feature toggles)

- **Where:** the _League Extras_ tab of League Settings (`/l/:lid/league_extras`; Tools → League Settings, then the tab). There is no separate menu or command palette item. The _League Settings_ tab leaves these settings out, and each tab saves only its own settings. The New League form still has them in its _League extras_ categories at the bottom.
- **What:** an on/off switch for every feature above, plus the options of some. A feature that is off disappears from the menus, the command palette, _More_ links, history/team/player page links and the dashboard, its pages show "turned off" if opened directly, and its background work stops (no hype computed, no achievement checks, no season odds simulations, no AI offers, no archetype updates, ...).
- **Options** (shown with their feature): hype _on Dashboard_ / _on Schedules and Box Scores_; achievement _Notifications_ / _on Dashboard_; box score play-by-play _Always show_ or _Show on button press_; _Games to Save Play-by-Play For_ (all games, my team's games, none); _Holdout Frequency_; _AI Trade Offers Frequency_ and _Expiration_; _Generate Pre-Draft Stats Automatically_; _Re-randomize New Draft Classes_; _Auto Draft From My Board_; Scouting Reports' _Scouting Summaries on Lists_ (off by default); unretirement chance, minimum age and ovr boost.
- **Sports:** basketball-only switches are hidden in the other sports (Extra Frivolities, the contract features and the trade features show there).

### Core features (the main model)

- **What:** most League Extras that change how the game plays are no longer settings: the whole game simulation model (realistic possessions, sim fixes, position-aware ratings, rotations, coaching systems, spacing and shot creation, matchup defense, end-of-game intelligence, load and fatigue, realistic turnovers and play types, offensive emphasis, star and clutch calibration, technical fouls, perimeter defense, tracking stats), the player model (expanded ratings, sub-ratings and their sim step and wider shapes, sim-calibrated ovr, realistic and career aging, improved positions, player traits, injury depth), the realistic schedule and marquee games, and the rest of the league (coaching staff and its depth, AI GM personalities and finance styles, front office, realistic economy, need-based AI trades, cap exceptions, contract options and extensions, competitive balance, the scouting system, reports and director, scout consensus and flags, AI draft needs, mock draft, draft-night trades, lottery night, pre-draft injuries and combine medicals, draft-and-stash, draft class shapes, pick protections, college season, frozen pre-draft ranks, new face styles and face aging, hyphenated names, rivalry stories, the complete profile export, rumors). They are marked `core` in `common/nativeFeatures.ts` and "always on (core)" in the tables here.
- **Every league has them:** new leagues by their defaults; an existing league or a league file with one off gets it on when it is loaded (`worker/core/league/forceCoreFeatures.ts`, called from `beforeLeague` right after the game attributes load), once, with the conversions turning it on used to run from the settings page: expanded ratings and sim-calibrated ovr derived and ovr, pot and skills recomputed, sub-ratings filled in, coaching staffs (and the depth jobs) and AI GMs made, retired players' tracking folded. From then on the league plays the fork's model: its games, the ratings shown, positions and archetype labels change from what it had, and it can't go back. The realistic schedule and marquee games start with the next regular season's schedule; realistic and career aging with the next development. A league that stored no play-by-play (`storePlayByPlay` off) keeps storing none, now as the scope "none".
- **Exceptions kept:** a college league keeps off what college mode turns off (`college: false` features); a feature for another sport is not touched; the conditions inside the features are unchanged (the realistic schedule is not used with _Group schedule into series_, the front office needs the budget setting).
- **The off code is kept:** each game attribute still exists, with its old default for a missing value, so the code paths with a feature off (stock BBGM) are still there for the tests that compare against them (`outcomeInvariance.test.ts`, the calibration scripts); no league reaches them.
- **Removed outright:** _Spread Out Matchups_ (it only ran with the realistic schedule off, which no league has now) and player nicknames (see Hall of Fame words).
- **Still settings:** the History & Stories pages and their display options, play-by-play display and storage scope, holdouts, trade requests, AI trade offers, unretirements, the shot clock and one-and-one rules, college mode's own options, archetype label source, draft board auto-draft, college stats generation and draft class re-randomizing.
- **Tests:** `worker/core/league/forceCoreFeatures.test.ts` (an older league with every core feature off: all on after the load, ratings derived, staffs made, play-by-play storage kept, a second load changes nothing, college exceptions).

### Settings table

Every setting the features add. "New / existing" is the value for a new league and for a league made before the setting existed. All of these are in League Settings → League Extras unless a row says otherwise.

| Key                                                                                                       | New / existing               | Notes                                                                                                                                  |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `feature*` (see the index)                                                                                | on / on, except below        | one switch per feature                                                                                                                 |
| `featureUnretire`                                                                                         | off / off                    | options `unretireChance` 10%, `unretireMinAge` 35, `unretireBoostMin` 3, `unretireBoostMax` 6                                          |
| `featureAchievementToasts`, `featureAchievementsDashboard`, `featureHypeDashboard`, `featureHypeSchedule` | on / on                      | options of Achievements and Games to Watch                                                                                             |
| `pbpLiveGame`                                                                                             | on / on                      | live feed in the live game view                                                                                                        |
| `featurePbpBoxScore` + `pbpBoxScore`                                                                      | on / on; `always` / `always` | box score play-by-play tab or button                                                                                                   |
| `storePlayByPlay` + `pbpStore`                                                                            | always on (core)             | `storePlayByPlay` off means no game keeps its play-by-play (old leagues); `pbpStore` is the scope                                      |
| `technicalFouls`                                                                                          | always on (core)             |                                                                                                                                        |
| `realisticPossessions`                                                                                    | always on (core)             |                                                                                                                                        |
| `playerTraits`, `playerTraitsSeed`                                                                        | always on (core)             | the seed is set once at league creation                                                                                                |
| `injuryDepth`                                                                                             | always on (core)             |                                                                                                                                        |
| `realisticSchedule`                                                                                       | always on (core)             | takes effect with the next regular season's schedule                                                                                   |

| `ovrV2`                                                                                                   | on / off                     | turning it on or off recomputes every player's ovr (and shifts pot by the same amount)                                                 |

| `advCareerTracking`                                                                                       | always on (core)             | off: a retiring player keeps his last 3 seasons of tracking (the old trim)                                                             |
| `contractOptions`, `contractExtensions`, `tradeRequests`                                                  | always on (core)             |                                                                                                                                        |
| `featureHoldouts` + `holdoutFrequency`                                                                    | on / off; 1 / 1              | frequency scales how often holdouts happen                                                                                             |

| `leagueType`, `collegeLevel`, `collegeAutoRecruit`                                                        | `pro` / `pro`                | set at creation (New League → College), not a League Extras switch; see [College mode](#college-mode)                                  |

### Adding a toggle (for developers)

The registry is `common/nativeFeatures.ts` (root `common/`, so the build tools can use it for the league file schema); game code imports it through `src/common/nativeFeatures.ts`. Add **one entry** to `NATIVE_FEATURES`:

```ts
{
	id: "myFeature", // used in code
	key: "featureMyFeature", // game attribute holding the switch
	name: "My Feature",
	description: "One line for the settings form.",
	default: true, // existing leagues (missing attribute)
	defaultNewLeague: true, // optional, if new leagues should differ from existing ones
	sports: BBGM, // omit for every sport
	existing: true, // optional: the key is a game attribute declared elsewhere (type, default, schema), so only its form entry comes from here
	options: [ // optional
		{ key: "myFeatureSomething", name: "…", description: "…", type: "bool" | "int" | "float" | "string", default: …, min?, max?, values?: [{ key, value }] /* for string */, decoration?: "percent", existing?: true },
	],
},
```

That entry alone gives: the `GameAttributesLeague` types, `defaultGameAttributes`, the new league defaults, the league file JSON schema, the settings form entries (League extras category and page), the settings view values, the UI sync (local state) and the sport filtering. For an `existing` key you still declare it in `types.ts`/`defaultGameAttributes.ts`, and add its explicit new league default in `worker/views/newLeague.ts` (`getDefaultSettings`). Then gate the feature's code with:

- Worker: `featureOn("myFeature")`, `featureOption("myFeatureSomething")`, and for a page's view `const off = featureOffError("myFeature"); if (off) return off;` (`src/worker/util/nativeFeatures.ts`).
- UI: `useFeatureOn("myFeature")` / `useFeatureOption(...)` in components, `isFeatureOn(...)` in plain functions (`src/ui/util/nativeFeatures.ts`); menu items take `feature: "myFeature"`; for links to the feature's pages add the URL to `featureForUrl` so `MoreLinks` and `FeatureLinks` drop them automatically.

Tests in `src/common/nativeFeatures.test.ts` check that every key has a default and is synced to the UI.

## Pages built from the league's records

### Newspaper

- **Where:** League menu → _Newspaper_, `/l/:lid/newspaper`, `/l/:lid/newspaper/:team/:season` (`all` or a team like `ATL_0`). Also linked from the dashboard ("From the records"), the team pages' _More_ links (team edition), League → History and each season row of Team History.
- **What:** a front page plus sections (Playoffs, Awards, Teams, Players, Features, Transactions, By the numbers, Opinion, Colour & letters) written from the season's results, awards, transactions and box scores. During a season it reports "so far"; after the playoffs it becomes the final edition. A team edition focuses on one franchise. Back issues link to earlier seasons. _Print edition_ gives a print-friendly layout.
- **Ratings:** player ratings in the paper (draft stories, breakouts, previews, rosters) are the ones the user's scouts see, with scouting fuzz, like the rest of the UI (draftees included: their draft-night ratings are fuzzed). Under the _No Visible Player Ratings_ challenge no rating is quoted: stories say it in words ("a breakout season", "among the league's best players") or leave it out, pages that are only about ratings (ratings previews, roster-rating ranks, title odds, rating gains) don't run, and rating columns are dropped from tables. The challenge is part of the paper's cache key, so toggling it rebuilds the paper. Tested in `common/newspaper/newspaper.noRatings.test.ts`.
- **Known limits:** pull quotes and letters marked _Colour_ are invented voices (the numbers are real). Player photos load lazily as you scroll (not while printing, so a photo that was never scrolled into view still prints). Very old seasons with deleted box scores get fewer game stories.

### Wrapped

- **Where:** League menu → _Wrapped_, `/l/:lid/wrapped` (chooser), `/l/:lid/wrapped/season/:season`, `/l/:lid/wrapped/team/:season/:team`, `/l/:lid/wrapped/player/:season/:pid`, `/l/:lid/wrapped/all`, and `/l/:lid/wrapped/compare` (two players, teams or seasons side by side). Linked from team, player and history pages.
- **What:** a tap-through story deck (9:16 cards) of a season, a team's year, a player's year or the whole league's history. Arrow keys, the on-card buttons, tapping the left/right of the card or swiping move between slides. Each slide can be saved or copied as a 1080×1920 PNG.
- **Deck settings** (gear icon on the card, remembered per browser): number of slides, autoplay, **quiz** (a few "who led the league in…" questions mixed into the deck, with a score slide; on by default) and **no spoilers** (hides results that would give away the season's outcome).
- **Compare:** a scoreboard of the main numbers (the better value is bold with ▸), the topics both have with a winner per topic, and the slides only one side has.
- **Ratings:** the snapshot keeps true ratings (League Achievements read them) plus each season's scouting fuzz; the slides read them through `LeagueIndex.withRatings`: fuzzed like the rest of the UI (true in God Mode or with several user teams), and none at all under the _No Visible Player Ratings_ challenge, when the slides that need a rating don't run. The ratings mode is part of the memoized slides key, so toggling the challenge never shows a stale deck. Tested in `common/wrapped/wrapped.noRatings.test.ts`.
- **Known limits:** the first visit to a big league builds a snapshot of the whole history, which can take a while (a progress placeholder shows; later visits use a cache, see _Performance_). Autoplay waits on an unanswered quiz question. A season that isn't over yet gets a "so far" deck without awards or playoffs.

### Storylines

- **Where:** League menu → _Storylines_, `/l/:lid/storylines`, `/l/:lid/storylines/team/:team[/:season]`, `/l/:lid/storylines/player/:pid`, `/l/:lid/storylines/all/all/:season`. _Wrapped_ and _Storylines_ buttons are on each player page; team pages and history pages link here too.
- **What:** story cards found in the league's history (Cinderella runs, upsets, droughts, dynasties, big triple-doubles, ageless wonders, ring chasers, homecomings, stacked draft classes and more), each with a _Why this story?_ button that shows the numbers behind it. The _Threads_ tab groups stories that belong together. Filter by season, kind and order, or search.
- **Known limits:** shares the Wrapped snapshot, so the first visit to a big league can take a while. Stories need history: a new league has only a handful.

### Games to Watch (hype)

- **Where:** League menu → _Games to Watch_, `/l/:lid/hype`; a _Games to Watch_ card on the league dashboard; a hype badge and reasons under games on Schedule and Daily Schedule; a hype card above box scores.
- **What:** a 0–100 must-watch score for upcoming games from what's known before tip-off, and for finished games a 0–100 game rating ("How it played out") plus a verdict comparing the two ("Did it live up to it?"). _Record chases_ lists players close to league or franchise records.
- **Pre-game signals** (each a listed reason with its points, up or down): playoff round and series state (elimination, winner takes all), play-in; two winning teams, marquee matchups (two of the league's top four), top-seed battles, playoff-line races late in the season, teams that can close in on a spot or are on the brink of elimination, tank races; rivalries (past playoff series, last year's knockout), division rivals; star power (stars, a superstar, stars on both sides); career milestones, record chases, heaters, revenge games, rookie duels, streaks, opening night. Lowering it: two losing teams, stars out injured, back-to-backs, late-season games where both teams' playoff fates are settled.
- **Game rating signals:** final margin (close up, blowouts and games over after three down; half in overtime games, which the overtime already counts), overtimes, lead changes and ties at period breaks, comebacks (bigger later), clutch plays (game-winners, buzzer-beaters, shots to force overtime, defensive game-winners if the sim records them), individual lines scored by **rarity**: each line gets one combined rarity (its rarest stat in full, the next two at a half and a quarter past the top 10%, scoring efficiency against what big scorers usually shoot, assists against turnovers, small bumps for triple- and quadruple-doubles, 5x5s, 20/20s and perfect shooting; game score as the floor), measured against the distribution of real single-game lines and scaled to the season's scoring environment; it then counts by how often a game has a line that good (a top-10% line shows up in most games and adds nothing, a 40-point night a little, a 50-point game about what a close finish does, a once-in-many-seasons line tops the scale), only the game's best line in full plus 40% of a second big one (a star duel when it came from the other side), and a small bump for the season's best single game in a stat; shootouts, team season highs (points, threes), upsets (records, seeds, team ratings), streaks ended, milestones reached and records broken, playoff context (round, series won, winner takes all, title clinched; damped in routs), and what was at stake going in. Lowering it: blowouts, ugly shooting, sloppy (turnover-heavy) and low-scoring games, two losing teams. Works from box scores alone (no play-by-play); missing shooting or turnover numbers just skip those signals.
- **Scale:** raw points map to 0–100 through percentile knots measured on simulated games, so both scores spread over the range: about half of all games under 50, one in twenty 85 or more, one in twenty 20 or less (playoff games sit higher). The tables are `src/common/hype/calibration.generated.ts`, made by `tools/hypeCalibration/calibrate.ts` from seasons simmed in node by `src/worker/util/hypeCalibration.test.ts` (off unless `HYPE_CAL_OUT` is set; see the file headers); `bbgm.hypeCalibrationDump(season)` in the worker console dumps a real league's season in the same format.
- **Verdict:** _Better than billed_ when the rating beat the hype by 20+, _Didn't live up to it_ when it fell 20+ short, else _Lived up_ (rating 50+) or _A quiet one_; the text gives both numbers and the biggest reasons (plus what happened to the pre-game story lines).
- **Known limits:** record chases need a few seasons of history. Schedule badges are only computed for the next 8 games. Career highs are not detected (box scores of whole careers are not kept); late-game flow is read from period scores and clutch plays, not play-by-play. The calibration leagues are random-players leagues simmed with the default settings.

### Best games & top plays

- **Where:** _Best Games_ tab of Daily Schedule, `/l/:lid/best_games/:season` (`all` for all time).
- **What:** the season's (or all time's) best games ranked by the Games to Watch post-game rating (`src/common/hype`, the box score's "How it played out" score) with its reasons, and the top plays: game-winners, buzzer-beaters, late go-ahead and tying shots, defensive stops (blocks and steals in the last 24 seconds of a one-possession game), posters, alley-oops, dunks, chase-down blocks, pick-sixes, heaves, and the plays that capped big runs. Plays are read from the saved play-by-play (`storePlayByPlay`) and worded by the play-by-play flavor layer, so they read like they did live; games without saved play-by-play contribute their clutch plays from the box score instead (with play-by-play saving off, all plays come from there). Ported from League Explorer's bestgames.js / topplays.js / pbp-analysis.js; code in `src/common/bestPlays` and `src/worker/util/bestGamesData.ts`.
- **Known limits:** for speed, play-by-play is read for at most 120 games a season (the best-rated ones and every game with a clutch play). All time = the best of each season with stored box scores.
- **Load time:** a past season's result is kept across sessions (`util/pagePersist.ts`) and worked out in the background (`util/pageWarm.ts`: after the league loads, after phase changes and after games, once nothing is being simmed); the current season's for the session, with each game's plays kept so later visits only word the new games. See Performance.

### Award vote

- **Where:** _Award Vote_ tab of Award Races, `/l/:lid/award_vote/:season`.
- **What:** a simulated media vote for every league-wide regular-season award (MVP, DPOY, ROY, 6MOY, MIP, All-League, All-Defensive, All-Rookie and custom ones): 100 voters, NBA ballot points (MVP 10-7-5-3-1, other player awards 5-3-1, team awards 5/3/1 for a 1st/2nd/3rd team vote), each voter weighing the award formula, team record and scoring differently, plus availability, fatigue with the defending winner, a shared media narrative per candidate (moves the whole electorate together) and personal noise, all in units of the spread of the candidates' scores (`VOTE_PARAMS`). Calibrated on 20 seasons of a real simulated league (`awardVoteDump.test.ts` dumps, `awardVote.calibration.test.ts` reports): the MVP winner's first-place votes are typically 55–97 (median 72), unanimous votes are rare, the runner-up often gets 15–40 first-place votes, and 8–14 players get votes. Shows votes per place, points, ballot share and the narratives that helped or hurt. During the season it is the projected vote if the season ended today. Ported from League Explorer's awardvote.js; code in `src/common/awardVote` and `src/worker/core/awards/simVote.ts`.
- **Saved votes:** with `featureAwardVoteStore` (on) the vote is saved with the season's awards (`awards.simVote`) when they are given, so past seasons show the same breakdown; seasons without one show a vote reconstructed from the winners.
- **Deciding awards:** off by default, the vote is nudged to agree with the formula's winners and never changes who wins. With `featureAwardVoteDecides` on, individual league-wide awards go to the vote's winner (team awards still use the formula).

### Award key stats and Award History

Always on, in every sport (display only, so not a League Extra).

- **Where:** _Award Races_ (`/l/:lid/award_races/:season`, also for past seasons, where it shows the saved top finishers), the new _Award History_ tab (`/l/:lid/award_history/:award`: one award's winners in every season, a _Top finishers_ / _All teams_ toggle for the saved 2nd–5th places or 2nd/3rd teams, and a link to that season's race), and the preview tables of Award Settings.
- **What:** each award's stat columns come from its formula. The formula is parsed with the award formula evaluator (`FormulaEvaluator`, the same one scoring uses, including per-position formulas and the tracking/perimeter variants the award is really scored with) and every stat it reads is ranked by how much it moves the score among the players shown: for each player, the change in score when that one stat is set to the group's average (for Most Improved Player: to his value last season), averaged over the players. For a plain sum that is the size of its weight × the stat's spread; it works the same for products, `min`/`max` and any custom formula. The top 5 (each at least 3% of the movement) are shown, padded to 4 columns with the award's usual stats, next to the essentials (team, record or draft pick, Ovr, score, Win%). Scaling variables (`seasonFraction`, `teamGp`) and `numWon` are never columns; team win% is covered by the Record column. A formula that reads no valid stats keeps the old columns. A _Why these stats?_ link under each table shows the formula and each stat's share.
- **User-chosen columns (`statCols`):** each award setting has an optional `statCols: string[]` (in the award settings zod schema, so it travels with Award Settings export/import, league files and scheduled award changes; also in the league JSON schema). Missing or empty = _Automatic_ (the formula-based choice above). Otherwise those stats are shown, in order, on Award Races, Award History and the season's History page (the stats line under each winner). Keys are validated when shown (`validStatCols` in `keyStats.ts`): only stats the award can show are kept (the picker list below, so a key from another sport, a typo, or a season stat on a playoff series award is skipped), no duplicates, at most 8; if none is left, the automatic choice is used. Pages read the league's current setting for the award by abbrev (`awardStatColsSetting`), so a choice made now applies to past seasons too; an award no longer in the settings falls back to the `statCols` saved with it that season. Every sport (player box/advanced stats of that sport).
  - **Award Settings:** under each award, _Stats shown: Automatic / Custom (n)_ opens the editor: Automatic lists the stats the formula would pick; Custom starts from those and has a searchable list of every stat the award can show (formula stats first, tagged, with their share of the score), up/down, remove, a max of 8, and _Reset to automatic_. Saved with _Save settings_. The preview table shows the choice being edited; the stats offered come from the saved formula and range and refresh on save.
  - **Columns menu:** Award Races and Award History have a small _Columns_ dropdown (marked `*` when custom) with the same editor; a change shows at once (also in the other tables of the same award, like conference awards) and is saved straight into the award's setting (`awardSettings.saveAwardStatCols`), like editing it in Award Settings. Award Settings isn't God Mode gated, so neither is this. An award that isn't in the league's award settings (an old award only in history) keeps the choice for the visit only. The old per-browser choice (`localStorage` `awardStatCols:*`) is no longer read.
  - **Coach & Executive of the Year:** their race tables (basketball) show every variable by default; the same editor (Award Settings, under each formula, with the preview; and the Columns menu on Award Races) picks which variables, stored as `coachExecAwardFormulas.statCols.{coy,eoy}` (unknown variables dropped; saving formulas keeps the columns; `awardSettings.saveCoachExecStatCols`).
  - Newspaper award stories don't show award stats, so they're unchanged. Display only, so scoring and determinism are unaffected.
- **Picker list:** every stat the game tracks for that sport (tracking stats only with Advanced Tracking Stats on; playoff series awards offer the series stats). Award Races and Award Settings load these stats for the candidates only.
- **History stats:** each player's stored stats for that season (and range: regular season, playoffs or combined). Stats not tracked in an old season show a dash. Playoff series awards (Finals MVP, ...) only keep the stats saved with the award: from now on that includes the stats the formula reads, older seasons have the award's usual stats only. When the formula changed over the years, each season is ranked with the formula it had then.
- **Code:** `worker/core/awards/keyStats.ts` (ranking), `getAwardCandidates.ts` (loads the picker stats for the candidates only), `worker/views/awardHistory.ts`, `worker/api/awardSettings.ts` (`saveAwardStatCols`, `saveCoachExecStatCols`), `ui/components/AwardKeyStats.tsx`, `ui/components/AwardStatCols.tsx` (Columns menu, saving), `ui/components/StatColsEditor.tsx` (the editor), `ui/views/AwardSettings/EditSettings.tsx`. Tests: `keyStats.test.ts`, `keyStats.football.test.ts`, `views/awardKeyStatsViews.test.ts`, `views/coachExecAwardsViews.test.ts`.

### Coach & Executive of the Year

- **Setting:** `featureCoachExecAwards`, always on (core), basketball. Coach of the Year needs the coaching staff (`coachingStaff`, also core); Executive of the Year is not given in college leagues (programs have no general managers). Other sports have no coaches or GMs, so neither award exists there and nothing shows.
- **What:** two built-in awards next to the player awards, given after the playoffs at the same point as MVP (`newPhaseBeforeDraft`: Executive of the Year right after `doAwards`, before the GM carousel so the GM who ran the season still runs the team; Coach of the Year in `staff.runOffseason` once the coaches' season records are written). The highest formula score wins; ties go to the better win%, then the lower coach id / team id. No random numbers (the old Coach of the Year's `gauss(0, 0.01)` tie noise is gone). A news item announces each winner (a notification when you win Executive of the Year).
- **Candidates:** Coach of the Year: every head coach who coached at least half of his team's regular-season games (win% from his own games, the rest from the team). Executive of the Year: every team's front office: its AI general manager (`t.gm`), or, for a team you control, "You" (a team with no GM at all would be "<region> front office").
- **Formulas** (Award Settings, _Coach & Executive of the Year_ section under the player awards, with the variables listed, a preview of the race for the formula being edited, _Reset to default_ and its own _Save formulas_ button; stored in the game attribute `coachExecAwardFormulas`, `undefined` or a missing key = the default, an invalid stored formula falls back to the default). Variables (`src/common/coachExecAwards.ts`):
  - `winp`: regular-season win% (ties half a win); `prevWinp`: the team's win% the season before (0.5 if none).
  - `expWinp`: the preseason expectation: the team's roster rating at its first game (`teamSeason.ovrStart`) against the league's, through the Season Odds win curve (the same projection the AI GMs fall back to); college mode: what the program's prestige expects (current season); last season's win% when no preseason rating was saved.
  - `rosterWinp`: the same for the roster at the end of the regular season (`ovrEnd`, or the current roster during the season): `winp - rosterWinp` is how much the team overperformed its roster.
  - `mov`: regular-season margin of victory; `playoffRoundsWon`: -1 missed, 0 lost in the first round, ...
  - `injuryRate` (Coach of the Year): man-games lost to injury per team game (each player's injury games that season, capped at a season, split between his teams by games played).
  - `movesWS` (Executive of the Year): value added by the season's moves (from the end of last season's playoffs to the end of this one's): win shares with the team of the players it drafted, signed as free agents or traded for, minus the win shares elsewhere of the players it traded away, per 82 games. Re-signings and extensions of its own players aren't counted (they add no player), and neither are draft picks traded for future seasons.
  - `payrollEff` (Executive of the Year): win% per dollar of salary paid this season against the league's (0 = league average, 0.5 = 50% more wins per dollar), bounded to -1..2.
  - Default Coach of the Year: `winp + 0.5 * (winp - expWinp) + 0.3 * (winp - prevWinp) + 0.3 * (winp - rosterWinp) + 0.005 * mov + 0.02 * playoffRoundsWon + 0.01 * injuryRate`; college: `winp - expWinp + 0.3 * (winp - prevWinp) + 0.05 * max(0, playoffRoundsWon + 1)` (the old prestige-expectation scoring).
  - Default Executive of the Year: `0.25 * winp + 0.5 * (winp - prevWinp) + 0.5 * (winp - expWinp) + 0.01 * movesWS + 0.1 * payrollEff + 0.03 * playoffRoundsWon`.
- **Stored once:** Coach of the Year on the coach (`Coach.awards`, as before, so past winners keep showing). Executive of the Year on the winning AI GM (`Gm.awards`, `{ season, type, tid }`, kept when he moves to `formerGms` or another team), or, when the winner has no AI GM (your team), on that team season (`TeamSeason.execOfTheYear = { name, user }`). Readers: `getCoachAwards` (`worker/core/staff/data.ts`) and `getExecAwards` / `getExecAwardsForSeason` (`worker/core/awards/coachExecAwards.ts`). Older leagues need no upgrade: the new fields are optional.
- **Pages:** Award Races (both races for any season, top 10 with every variable and the score, the winner marked once given, and win odds during the regular season when Season Odds is on, the same projection as the player awards); Award History (both in the award dropdown: every winner with team, record and playoff result, and a link to that season's race); the season's History page (Coach of the Year and Executive of the Year under the individual awards, in every league type now); Coaches page (unchanged, reads the coaches' awards); Front Offices (a GM's Executive of the Year seasons in his details); GM History (your Executive of the Year seasons).
- **Code:** `src/common/coachExecAwards.ts` (variables, docs, defaults, projection, ordering), `src/worker/core/awards/coachExecAwards.ts` (variables per team, candidates, awarding, readers, races), `src/worker/api/awardSettings.ts` (`getCoachExecAwardSettings`, `saveCoachExecAwardFormulas`), `src/ui/components/CoachExecAwards.tsx`, `src/ui/views/AwardSettings/CoachExecAwardSettings.tsx`. Tests: `src/common/coachExecAwards.test.ts`, `src/worker/views/coachExecAwardsViews.test.ts` (a played season: scoring, storage, determinism, your team, every page, Award Settings, old-league data), `src/worker/core/staff/staff.test.ts`.

### League achievements

- **Where:** League menu → _League achievements_, `/l/:lid/league_achievements`; a _Recent achievements_ card on the dashboard; unlocks are announced as notifications.
- **Rarity:** measured on simulated random-players BBGM leagues, not on real NBA history (BBGM sims are far more generous): common more than 0.6 times a season, rare 0.15–0.6, legendary 0.025–0.15, impossible rarer. No tier that any calibration league unlocked in its first 5 seasons is impossible. The data is `src/common/leagueAchievements/calibration.generated.ts`; regenerate it with `tools/leagueAchievementsCalibration/`: `src/worker/util/leagueAchievementsCalibration.test.ts` sims a league headlessly in Node (fake-indexeddb, skipped unless `LA_CALIBRATE_OUT` is set; see its header) and records the tally every season (`sim.cjs` does the same in a headless browser against the dev server), and `calibrate.ts` turns the outputs into bands.
- **Saved state:** which tiers were already announced, and the log, are kept in the league (the `leagueAchievements` game attribute), so exporting and importing a league carries them. Older versions kept them in a separate `league-achievements` database: it is read once per league and moved in.

### Trade grader

- **Where:** League menu → _Trade Grader_, `/l/:lid/trade_grader[/:tab/:team/:season/:ws]`; grades also show on Trade Summary and the transactions list.
- **What:** each side of every trade graded by the win shares what it received produced for it. A toggle counts **regular season + playoffs** (default, `ws` = `all`) or **regular season only** (`rs`); Trade Summary and the grades on the Transactions list follow the last choice made on the grader (per browser). Each side notes the titles its team won with a player it received playing for it in those playoffs.

### Contract value

League menu → _Contract Value_: a value index, bargains and albatrosses, and the value index shown on player salaries.

### League timeline

- **Where:** League menu → _Timeline_, `/l/:lid/timeline[/:from[/:to]]`; linked from the dashboard ("From the records") and from each season row of League → History.
- **What:** season by season (newest first): champions (with the finals result), MVPs and Finals MVPs, the newspaper's front page lead, the top 3 storylines, league achievements unlocked, records (best/worst regular season record in league history so far, single-game scoring records), the 3 biggest trades (by the trade event's importance) and comebacks. Every entry links to its source page (playoffs, player, newspaper edition, storylines season, achievement, box score, trade summary). Filters: season range (navigates), kinds (remembered per browser), team and a text search.
- **Known limits:** headlines are computed for at most the 25 latest seasons of the range (finished seasons are cached in memory). Entries from Newspaper, Storylines and League Achievements are left out while those features are off (the page says which). Storylines need the Wrapped snapshot: on a first visit they appear when it's ready. Achievements come from the saved unlock log, so ones unlocked before achievements were tracked in the league don't show.

### Records book

- **Where:** League History → _Records Book_ tab, `/l/:lid/records_book[/:team]` (league, or one franchise from the team dropdown).
- **What:** top 10 lists of career totals (points, rebounds, assists, steals, blocks, threes, games, seasons), single-season totals and per-game averages (70% of the team's games), single-game highs, playoffs (career, one playoffs, single game) and team seasons (most wins and losses, best and worst record, longest winning and losing streaks). Holders link to player, team and box score pages; active players get a badge, marks set this season are highlighted, records broken this season are flagged with the previous holder (and listed at the top), and season records show their progression. Franchise totals, award counts and feats are left to the stock Team Records, Awards Records and Statistical Feats pages (linked).
- **News:** with _Record News_ (`featureRecordsNews`) on, a news item and notification when a league record is broken: career total (once the old record is meaningful, e.g. 10,000 points), single-season total, single-game high or wins in a season beating a mark from an earlier season, winning streak longer than any before (10+).
- **How:** players are read once per league, season and phase and the cache's players are laid over them on each request. Single-game highs (league top 10, franchise top 3) and streaks need box scores, so they are kept as games are played in the `recordsBook` game attribute (export/import carries it); a check runs after each day of games (debounced, waits out bulk sims) and works from the box scores it hasn't seen. A league without the attribute starts from the box scores it still has, quietly (a league in its first season starts empty, so its games count from the first day).
- **Player page rankings** (`featurePlayerRankings`, on by default; off with the Records Book): a _Rankings_ section on player pages (below the stats tables) listing where his career totals rank all-time and in each franchise he played for, regular season and playoffs separately, in points, rebounds, assists, steals, blocks, threes, free throws made, games, minutes, double-doubles, triple-doubles, win shares and VORP, plus his top single seasons (regular season totals summed over his teams that season: points, rebounds, assists, steals, blocks, threes). Lines read "5th all-time in points (31,204)", "Franchise leader in assists for the Boston Celtics", "2nd in Boston Celtics history in playoff blocks"; ties use competition ranking (1, 1, 3) and show as "T-2nd". Active players get the gap to the next player up ("152 behind #4 Name"). The first 8, most significant first (stat weight × scope × how high the rank is, league above franchise above single seasons, playoffs a bit lower), with _Show all_. Each line links to the matching Player Stats page (career or season totals, league or that team, regular season or playoffs) and the franchise's Team History.
  - **Meaningful only:** shown down to a depth that grows with the league: all-time top N with N = players with stats / 25, at most 5 per season of history, between 5 and 50 (none in a league's first season); franchise top N = the franchise's players / 10, at most 2 per season of the franchise, between 3 and 10 (none in a franchise's first season, so expansion teams start empty); single seasons top 10 at most. A franchise ranking needs half a season of games with it (5% of a season in the playoffs), so a short stint isn't a franchise career.
  - **Franchise = tid**, so a relocated or renamed team stays one franchise (named by its current name), and a traded player is ranked with each team for his totals there. Leagues imported with real history are read like any other: their retired players' stats count.
  - **How:** retired players' totals never change, so their top lists (league top 50, franchise top 10 per stat, regular season and playoffs, and the top 10 single seasons) are kept in the `playerRankings` game attribute (export/import carries it). The first player page in a league reads every retired player once; after that only the players who retired in each finished season are added (read by the `retiredYear` index, a watermark like the Records Book's), a comeback player's old entries are replaced. The players in the cache and this season's retirees are laid over the kept lists to build the board, which is memoized until games are played or players move, so a player page only reads the cache. A kept player who was deleted (Delete Old Data) makes the lists rebuild on the next view. Code: `src/common/playerRankings.ts` (pure), `src/worker/util/playerRankingsData.ts`, `src/ui/views/Player/Rankings.tsx`.
- **Known limits:** single-game highs and streaks only cover games simmed while box scores existed; real-history imports have no older box scores. The check records the season it last ran in, so one delayed past the season rollover still processes last season's playoffs from the database; only box scores deleted before a check runs (several seasons auto-played with box-score deletion set to keep fewer seasons) are missed.

### Milestones

- **Where:** Player Stats → _Milestones_ tab, `/l/:lid/milestones[/:team[/:horizon]]`; dashboard card _Milestone Watch_ for your team (`featureMilestonesDashboard`).
- **What:** active players within reach of a career mark (points, rebounds, assists, steals, blocks, threes, games; ladders like 10,000/12,500/... points) or of the next player above them in the all-time top 100, with the need, their pace and the projected game (game number, day and opponent from the schedule; "next season, about game N" beyond it). Horizon: this season or a season and a half. Recently reached milestones with the game they came in.
- **News:** milestones of your players are news (with a notification, `featureMilestoneNotifications`); big ones (20,000 points, 10,000 rebounds, ...) are news for anyone. Pre-game "needs N for X career" lines stay with Games to Watch.
- **Reached this season** includes players who played this season and retired right after it, labelled _(retired)_ (page and dashboard card).
- **How:** pace is this season's per-game rate (last season's before 10 games, else career) times the share of the team's games he plays. The same after-games check as the Records Book finds the exact game a mark was reached in.

### Rivalries

- **Where:** _Rivalries_ tab of Head-to-Head, `/l/:lid/rivalries[/:tidA-:tidB]`; the _Rivals_ card on Team History; League Stats' More links.
- **What:** one rivalry score per pair of teams (`src/common/rivalries`, gathered by `src/worker/util/rivalries.ts`): playoff meetings weighted by round (Finals most), series length and upsets; even regular season head-to-head; close games (3 points or fewer, or overtime) from stored box scores; same division (or conference); star duels (a 25+ point scorer on both sides in a playoff game); a bonus for a meeting last season and back-to-back meetings. Everything decays with a 6 season half-life. Tiers: Mild, Simmering (10+), Heated (22+), Bitter (40+). The page shows the top rivalries, Finals rivals, every Finals matchup and, for one pair, generated lines and a season-by-season timeline (record, running edge, playoff series, score).
- **Shared score:** the hype "rivalry" reason (`hypeRivalryPoints`) and the "Playoff rivalry" storyline use the same score.
- **Known limits:** close games and star duels only count seasons whose box scores are kept. Teams are by tid, so a relocated franchise stays one team (see _Franchise reboots_).

### Rivalry stories

- **Setting:** `featureRivalryStories` (League Extras, "Rivalry Stories"). Off for existing leagues, on for new leagues; it also needs _Rivalries_ on. Basketball only. Off: nothing is logged or read, the Rivalries page, Newspaper, Storylines and Games to Watch are as before, and games are unchanged (it only reads the finished box score, after the sim; `outcomeInvariance.test.ts` passes unchanged).
- **Game log:** after every day of games one compact record per game goes to the `rivalryGames` store (league database version 77; indexes `pair` "a-b" and `season`), in one transaction for the day (`src/worker/util/rivalryStories.ts` `recordRivalryGames`, called from `game/play.ts`; the record is built by `src/common/rivalryStories/record.ts` from the box score): teams, score, overtimes, playoff round and series state, records after the game, marquee tags, clutch plays (plain text), technicals and ejections when the game's play-by-play was recorded, head coaches (with Coaching Staff), and every player who played (points, rebounds, assists, FG; with Advanced Tracking Stats also his defended shots and his on-floor net points for that game). The same write keeps a `rivalrySeasons` row per season (by pair: close games, 3 points or fewer or overtime, and whether a playoff game had a 25+ point scorer on both sides), which is what the rivalry score reads from box scores: with the setting on the Rivalries page, the Rivals card and the newspaper read those rows instead of every stored box score (the same counts while the box scores exist, `rivalryStories.test.ts` checks it; after a box score is deleted its season keeps counting, where without the setting it drops out). About 1 KB a game. Records stay after the box score is deleted, so the stories cover every season since the setting was on. When the setting is turned on in a league with history, or after an import, the first read logs the stored box scores that are not in the log yet (no tracking numbers or coaches for those); it compares keys only (the stored box scores' gids against the log's gids in that range) and reads just the missing box scores. A day played with the setting off makes the next read with it on check again, in the same session. Each season row also keeps every pair's highest single-game points by a player (`highs`, on rows started since it was added), the newspaper's bar for "a new high for the pair".
- **Rivalry page** (_Rivalries_ tab, one pair, below the existing timeline; `src/ui/views/RivalryStory.tsx`, built by `src/common/rivalryStories/aggregate.ts`):
  - **All-time record:** regular season (from the head-to-head table, the whole history), playoff games and series (from the playoff series), the current streak and each team's longest (from the log), and the number of logged games, average margin and overtime games.
  - **Head-to-head by era:** 5-season blocks from the first season they met: wins each and playoff series.
  - **Playoff history:** every series with its round, result, upsets and a link to its Series Recap.
  - **Memorable games:** the 5 best (a drama score from the log: close and overtime finishes, playoff stakes with a bonus for clinchers and deciding games, the night's top scorer over 30, clutch plays, technicals and ejections) and the 5 closest, each linking to its box score while it is still stored.
  - **Players:** player duels (points against the other side: games, total, per game, best night), defense ("X held BOS shooters to 38.2% on 52 defended shots", players with 15+ defended shots; the closest defender as Advanced Tracking Stats defines it, per defender, not per opposing player), on-floor net points in rivalry games, players who **crossed the rivalry** (played for both in the log, or now on the other roster; "trade" or "free agency" when a transaction event says so), **coaching matchups** (head coach vs head coach records; GMs are not tracked, BBGM's AI teams have none) and marquee appearances (national TV, holiday, scheduled rivalry games).
  - **Heat timeline:** the rivalry score at the end of each season (the same score as the rest of the page) with its change and why: playoff meetings (round, result, upset), close games, the standings (both contenders within 5 games, or lopsided), players crossing over, a season-series sweep, or "the history fades". One row per season (season, heat bar, the reason), the reason wrapping under the season on a phone instead of scrolling sideways; the Rivalry timeline table above it wraps its text so its six columns fit at 390 px.
- **Newspaper:** a _Rivalry watch_ page (Features in the league paper, the front page of a team's edition when it is involved): recaps of the season's big rivalry games (pairs Simmering or hotter, or scheduled rivalry games): series clinchers, upsets (the winner's record 20+ points of win% worse, 10+ games in), heated games (an ejection or 3+ technicals), big nights (40+ points, or a new high for the pair's log) and classics (overtime or a 2-point finish); and, in the current season, previews of the next marquee or scheduled rivalry games of Heated pairs (any game of a Bitter pair, any rivals in the playoffs): all-time series, streak, last meeting, this season's meetings and the player who has scored most against the visitors lately. Text in `src/common/rivalryStories/text.ts`, page in `src/common/newspaper/rivalry.ts`, facts from `getRivalryNewsFacts`.
- **Storyline:** _Rivalry renewed_, two teams meeting in the playoffs again (their Nth series, the last one and the playoff series record; more points for recent and repeated meetings). It is filtered out with the setting off (`storylinesData.ts`).
- **Games to Watch:** upcoming games of a pair that already met this season get a context line under the matchup ("Third meeting this season; the last two were decided by 3 points.", "the Celtics have won all two, by 12 a game", "season series 2-1").
- **Ratings:** the text reads results and box score stats only, never a rating, so it is the same under the _No Visible Player Ratings_ challenge (tested with the challenge's own patterns in `rivalryStories.test.ts`).
- **Speed:** the pair page reads only its own pair's records through the `pair` index (memoized until the next game is logged), and the rivalry scores read one small row per season instead of the stored box scores. The Newspaper reads the season through the `season` index, and its "new high for the pair" bar from the season rows' `highs` (before, it read every logged game of every rival pair, and compared each game against the season's list: the part that grew with the league's length); with older rows it still reads the rival pairs' games. The backfill reads keys, not box scores. Measured with `src/worker/util/rivalryStories.bench.test.ts` (skipped unless `RIVALRY_BENCH` is set; node and fake-indexeddb, so slower than a browser): a league built with N past seasons of real size (1,230 regular season games and a 16-team playoff a season, the log and season rows as the after-game hook writes them, head-to-heads, playoff series, team seasons, box scores for the default 2 past seasons) and the current season half played, each read from a fresh session, median of 3, before → after, at 2 / 10 / 20 seasons:

  | Read                                    | 2 seasons   | 10 seasons    | 20 seasons     |
  | --------------------------------------- | ----------- | ------------- | -------------- |
  | Backfill (once a session)               | 677 → 13 ms | 583 → 47 ms   | 513 → 59 ms    |
  | Backfill, all box scores kept           | 558 → 9 ms  | 2,052 → 47 ms | 4,951 → 100 ms |
  | Rivalry score (`loadRivalries`)         | 11 → 7 ms   | 17 → 28 ms    | 31 → 44 ms     |
  | Rivalries pair page (most-met pair)     | 18 → 17 ms  | 24 → 28 ms    | 60 → 58 ms     |
  | Newspaper Rivalry watch, current season | 113 → 44 ms | 283 → 51 ms   | 682 → 74 ms    |
  | Newspaper Rivalry watch, last season    | 238 → 87 ms | 755 → 105 ms  | 1,589 → 102 ms |
  | Games to Watch (2 days)                 | 58 → 67 ms  | 40 → 65 ms    | 36 → 52 ms     |

  The rivalry score and the pair page still grow a little with the league (one row per season; the pair's own games), the rest are flat. The bench stops the background page warm-up (`pageWarm.ts`) first: it reads every stored box score after a league loads, and its work would otherwise land on whichever read runs then. In the browser (production build, a new league after 2 seasons) the Rivalries page for one pair loads in 34–36 ms.

- **Tests:** `src/common/rivalryStories/rivalryStories.test.ts` (aggregates against raw games, record building, text without ratings, the newspaper page, defaults) and `src/worker/util/rivalryStories.test.ts` (a headless league: nothing logged while off, every game logged with the setting on and backfilled for the days before, records match the box scores, the page's story matches the raw games, newspaper loads).
- **Known limits:** streaks, games, duels and coaching only cover logged seasons (the all-time record and eras use the full head-to-head history). Matchup-defense numbers are per defender over all shots he defended in the game, not man-to-man splits. Teams are by tid.

### Series recaps

- **Where:** _Series Recaps_ tab of Playoffs, `/l/:lid/series_recaps[/:season[/:tidA-:tidB]]`; a _Recap_ link on every started series of the bracket; Rivalries timeline and Finals matchups link to them.
- **What:** per series (`src/common/seriesRecap`): headline and generated text, result with seeds, game by game (score, margin, overtime, running series score, box score links), series leaders and series MVP (best game score per game on the winning side), turning points (biggest win, biggest fourth-quarter comeback, overtime games, the swing game, the closeout, stored clutch plays), upsets by seed and comebacks in the series (down 3-1, ...). When a series ends the news item ("X defeated Y in the 1st round, 4-3") gets a line about a comeback, upset or sweep and a recap link, and is kept in the news for every round.
- **Conference Finals MVP:** a playoff series award before the finals (the default _Semifinals MVP_, `sfmvp`, given for each series of the second-to-last round, which is the conference finals in a league split by conference) is named in that series' end-of-series news ("X was named Semifinals MVP.", tagged with the player) and shown at the top of its recap, computed with the award code itself from the series' stats (`core/awards/seriesAwards.ts`) until the season's awards are given, then read from them, so all three agree. Season History already lists it with the playoff awards. Basketball, not in college leagues.
- **Known limits:** needs the series' box scores; older seasons whose box scores were deleted show only the result.

### League eras & rule-change votes

- **Where:** _Eras_ tab of League History, `/l/:lid/league_eras`; news items. Setting `featureLeagueEras` (on for new leagues, off for existing ones), options `featureLeagueErasVotes` (on) and `featureLeagueErasUserVote` (`auto` / `yes` / `no`).
- **Era engine:** when a season is over (`newPhaseBeforeDraft`, after inflation), `src/worker/core/leagueEras` records the season's style and sets next season's. Each of `threePointTendencyFactor`, `pace`, `foulRateFactor` and `orbFactor` takes one step of a bounded random walk on a log scale around a baseline: `x' = (1 − revert) x + step z + pull`, clamped to ±bound (three-point tendency 0.12 / 0.035 / ±0.4, pace 0.15 / 0.012 / ±0.08, fouls and offensive rebounds 0.2 / 0.03 / ±0.2). The pull is what won: half of how much more the top quarter of the league (by win%) differed from the league average than the top quarter usually does (a running average of that edge, 0.3 weight on the newest season, kept on the era record as `edgeNorm`), in three-point share of shots (capped at ±0.03) and possessions per game (±0.01). The good teams take more threes every season (+7% to +20% of the league's share, because good shooters win), so the raw edge used to pull the three-point tendency up by the full cap every year, a one-way drift toward its bound (+49%) that lowered FG% and scoring. The baseline is the league's style when the drift starts. In a real-player league, keys still set by an upcoming scheduled event follow the real history (the baseline tracks them), and the drift takes over from the last real values when the real seasons run out.
- **Randomness:** its own seeded stream (`Rng` keyed by `leagueEras-<season>` and the league salt), never `Math.random`, so the main random stream is unchanged and a league replays the same eras.
- **Record:** the season's awards row, key `era` (`src/common/leagueEras.ts`): style values, baseline, era name ("Pace & Space era", "Grind-It-Out era", "Three-Point era", "Balanced era", ...; from the three-point and pace ratios to the baseline), the league three-point share and possessions per game, and any vote. News when the era name changes or one season moves the three-point tendency by 6%+ or pace by 2.5%+.
- **Rule-change votes:** after the drift, at most one proposal a season and none within 3 seasons of the last, with a 35% chance when a trigger fires: heavy tanking (at least max(3, teams/8) teams under .300) → a flatter lottery (`nba1990` → `nba1994` → `nba2019` → `nba2027`, as a scheduled event for next preseason); a team over the tax that won the title or .750+ → luxury tax rate +0.25 (max 3); three-point share over 47% / under 22% → the line moves (tendency and its baseline ×0.92 / ×1.08). Every team votes (AI teams by self-interest: tanking teams and taxpaying teams mostly vote no), two thirds pass it, and the proposal, the count and your vote are in the news. Your team votes by the setting (auto = your team's interest). Not in college leagues.
- **Known limits:** v1 only drifts four settings; the vote has no interactive prompt (the setting decides).

- **Parity (measured, not changed):** `src/test/parity/parity.script.test.ts` (`RUN_PARITY=1`, seeded headless multi-season sim with new-league defaults) records win% SD, margin per team-ovr point, margin SD, team-ovr SD and win% per point of margin. Baseline (seeds 1-2, 4 seasons): win% SD .145-.203 (mean .169), margin per ovr point .47-.66 (mean .54). Win% per point of margin is .025-.030 (NBA ~.03), so game-to-game noise is not too low; the spread comes from talent: team-ovr SD grows from ~10 in a new league to ~13-15 within three seasons. Restoring the comeback boost (`SIM_FIX_PERF_FACTOR` 0.1 → 0.3) cut the margin slope to .43 but left win% SD at .173, so it was not shipped; the lever is roster-building (talent concentration), not the sim.

### Unretirements

- **What:** the old console script `tools/console-scripts/unretire.js`, built in (off by default). Once a season, when free agency starts, with the chosen chance (default 10%), one random retired player who retired at the minimum age or older (default 35) comes back as a free agent with a fresh ratings row for the season (for someone who retired at the end of this same season, that season's row is the one boosted, since free agency is still part of it), boosted by a random 3-6 ovr (settings) by raising his non-height ratings one point at a time, favouring his strengths. Dead players, and anyone who already came back (including through the script), are skipped. A news event ("… has come out of retirement and is now a free agent") is logged and shown, and the comeback appears on the timeline.
- **Code:** `src/worker/core/phase/unretire.ts`, called from `phase/finalize.ts`.

### Extra frivolities

Redraft, Draft Pick Value, Jersey Numbers by Team, Family Trees, League Economics and Home Court (the ones available in the sport). One switch hides the lot.

### Face style gallery and extra face parts

- **Where:** Tools → _Face Style Gallery_ (`/face_style_gallery`, in and outside a league; all sports).
- **What:** every option of a face feature (hair, hairBg, eye, eyebrow, facial hair, accessories, glasses, head, mouth, nose) drawn on four sample faces with different skin and hair colours, to check how parts overlap. _New sample faces_ rerolls the faces.
- **Extra parts:** `src/common/faceParts.ts` wraps facesjs so the fork can register its own SVG part options. Registration is append-only (ids start with `fork-`, a facesjs id or an already registered id is never replaced), so existing faces render exactly as before, and facesjs' `generate()` never picks a fork part (its random picks filter by a gender table internal to facesjs). Three example hair styles (`fork-flat-top`, `fork-mohawk`, `fork-buzz`) are included.
- **Setting:** _Use the fork's extra face parts in this browser_ on the gallery page (browser-local, `localStorage.forkFaceParts`, default off). On, the example parts are registered at startup, so a face that names one draws it; off, such a face draws no hair there. The gallery itself registers them while open, to show them.
- **Licence:** facesjs is Apache-2.0 (`node_modules/facesjs/LICENSE`); the fork's parts are new SVG, not copies of facesjs art.
- **Known limits:** groundwork only: nothing assigns fork parts to players yet (no generation, no face editor support; the external face editor doesn't know them). With a hat accessory facesjs hides hair ids it doesn't know, fork ones included. Only the UI registers parts (the worker doesn't render faces).

## Faces

All face work sits on top of facesjs 5 (Apache-2.0) without patching it: extra parts are registered into facesjs' part tables
(`src/common/faceParts.ts`, append-only, ids start with `fork-`), and a draw-time layer (`src/common/faceRender.ts`) handles what
a stored facesjs face can't express by itself. Nothing in this section uses `Math.random` or the game's random stream, so no sim
or league result changes (the outcomeInvariance test is unchanged).

### Styles

Phase 0 audit (every facesjs 5.0.4 and fork style, drawn and compared): a proposed style that facesjs or the fork already draws
was dropped as a duplicate (buzz/induction = `short-fade`, `fork-buzz`; mid fade = `crop-fade`; caesar = `crop`; side part =
`parted`, `fork-side-part`; textured quiff = `juice`; crew cut = `short*`; high-top = `high`, `tall-fade`; flat-top =
`fork-flat-top`; afro medium/large = `afro`, `afro2`; blowout = `blowoutFade`; sponge curls = `curly*`; straight-back
cornrows = `cornrows`; shoulder locs = `fork-locs`; locs tied up = `dreads`; man bun = `fork-top-knot`; horseshoe =
`short-bald`; 5 o'clock shadow = `fork-stubble`; pencil / chevron / horseshoe mustache, soul patch, Van Dyke, chin puff,
circle beard, short/medium/long full beards, neck beard, mutton chops, chinstrap with mustache = facesjs' own; ...).

New parts (ids start with `fork-`; `src/common/faceParts.styles.ts`, `src/common/faceParts.hair.ts`):

- **Short and faded:** `low-fade`, `high-fade`, `skin-fade-part` (skin fade with a hard part), `high-tight`, `line-up`,
  `waves-fade` (360 waves with a fade), `edgar` (French crop).
- **Designs:** `shaved-part` (two shaved lines), `side-design` (zigzag above the temples), `frosted-tips`, `dyed-top`
  (bleached top over a dark fade), `grey-streak` (rare). Star / number designs were dropped: unreadable at roster size.
- **Textured / natural:** `twists-medium`, `twists-fade`, `afro-small`, `bantu-knots` (rare).
- **Locs and braids:** `starter-locs` (with a fade), `locs-dyed` (dyed tips), `locs-top` (freeform, with `locs-long` or
  `locs-long-dyed` behind the head), `locs-back` (tied back, with `locs-long` or facesjs' ponytail), `cornrows-zigzag`,
  `box-braids` (short, or shoulder length with `box-braids-long` behind), `braids-bun`.
- **Thinning (face aging, selectable by hand):** `recede-light`, `widows-peak`, `recede-medium` (with the existing
  `fork-receding` as the high hairline), `thinning-crown`, `comb-over`.
- **Medium and long:** `slick-back` (short, or long with `slick-back-long` behind), `pompadour`, `half-up-bun`,
  `middle-part-long` (with `long-wavy` behind), `wolf-cut`, `mullet` (behind the head, with a short top).
- **Other features:** brows `brow-unibrow` (rare), `brow-slit` (both brows: brows are mirrored), `brow-thick-straight`;
  eyes `eye-deep`; noses `nose-upturned`, `nose-crooked`; ears `ear-cauliflower` (rare); mouth `mouth-gap` (rare); skin
  `acne` (young players only, clears up after 21), `lip-scar`, `chin-scar`, `face-tattoo` (rare); accessories
  `earring-studs`, `bandana` (team colour); glasses `goggles-modern`.
- **Facial hair:** `heavy-stubble`, `patchy-stubble`, `chin-stubble`, `boxed-beard`, `lineup-beard` (sculpted),
  `beard-line` (shaved line), `huge-beard` (rare), `anchor`, `handlebar` (rare), `walrus` (rare). Greying versions of every
  beard come from the beard colour (below), not extra parts.

How they're drawn: fades are bands of the hair colour at falling opacity clipped to the short-hair outline (not gradients:
gradient ids are shared by every face on a page, so a hair-coloured gradient would paint every face in one face's colour);
twists and locs are caps with a lobed outline and strand lines; braids are clipped to the head. Long locs and braids hang
behind the head (hairBg). Each part was checked on light to dark skin, blond to black hair, fatness 0 to 1, under headbands,
at roster thumbnail size and in the dark theme.

### Generation rules

- **Setting:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ League Extras _New Face Styles_ (`featureForkFaces`, on for new leagues, off for existing ones). It was
  "Fork Face Parts", which mixed the fork's parts in at random.
- **What:** after facesjs generates a male face (players, draft prospects, coaches and GMs; a face made to look like a
  relative's is left as facesjs makes it), `src/common/faceStyles.ts` picks:
  - _hair_ from a catalogue of facesjs and fork styles, each with the hair textures it fits (coily for facesjs' black skin
    palette; straight or, sometimes, curly for the others), a rarity tier (common 6, uncommon 2, rare 0.45 weight) and an
    age fit (young styles such as twists, locs, designs and dyed tops fade out after 27; side parts and a shaved head are
    likelier after 30). Long locs and braids bring their hairBg.
  - _facial hair_: whether he has any is still facesjs' 50/50; which one is by rarity (huge beards, handlebars, walrus and
    neck beards rare).
  - _colour_: facesjs' natural colours, plus a small dyed or bleached share (3.5% up to 27, 1% after) drawn on the hair only
    (`face.fork.hair`, see the beard colour note), so the brows keep his natural colour.
  - _skin and glasses_: 12% of players 21 and under get a little acne; 30% of men 40 and over (coaches, GMs) get glasses.
  - the fork's other parts (heads, eyes, brows, noses, mouths, ears, lines, glasses, headbands) mixed in, each with its
    rarity (unibrow, cauliflower ear, gap teeth, face tattoo and bandana rare).
- **Batch C** (`common/faceParts.batchc.ts`, all original designs: facesjs 5.0.4 is the latest release and its repository
  has no parts beyond it): hair French crop (straight/curly, common), curtains (straight, young), Caesar (every texture,
  mature), short coils (coily, young), curly mop with optional long curls behind (curly, young); facial hair goatee with
  stubble (common) and fu manchu (rare); tapered brow; monolid and downturned eyes; pointed and bulb noses; pointed ear
  (rare); mouthguard in team colour (rare) and pursed mouth; cheekbone and narrow-jaw heads; nose scar and teardrop tattoo
  (rare); nasal strip (rare), striped headband, single earring; shoulder tattoo and arm script on the shoulders beside the
  jersey. Not added: wristbands and chains (outside or under the jersey crop), hair ties (no visible spot), a raised brow
  (looked off at thumbnail size).
- **Batch D:** see below.
- **Batch E:** see below.
- No era curves: style frequency doesn't depend on the league's season. Seeded from the face facesjs made, so no random
  numbers are drawn and no league result changes.
- **Face editor:** Customize Player → _More styles_ lists every fork part of every feature (hair, hair behind the head,
  facial hair, ...). User choices are never overwritten: generation only runs for new faces, and aging skips edited faces.

### Batch D

Original SVG parts in `common/faceParts.batchd.ts` (append-only `fork-` ids after batch C; nothing copied from facesjs). Each
candidate was first drawn on four sample faces next to the closest facesjs and fork styles; the ones that were already there
were dropped. Every kept part was checked on light to dark skin, blond to black (and grey) hair, fatness 0 to 1, under the
facesjs and fork headbands, at roster thumbnail size (40 px) and in the dark theme.

- **Hair (generated):** `burst-fade` (fades out in rings round the ear; every texture, uncommon), `frohawk` (coily or curly,
  rare, young), `afro-puff` (coily uncommon / curly rare, young), `high-top-slanted` (coily, rare, young), `jheri-curl`
  (wet-look curls with shine, coily, rare, alone or with the fork's `curls-long` behind), `bowl-cut` (mushroom cut over
  shaved sides, straight, rare, young), `viking-braids` (braided strip over shaved sides with two long braids behind,
  `viking-braids-long`; straight, rare, young), `buzz-leopard` (bleached buzz with dark rosettes; every texture, rare,
  young; counts as a buzz for hair loss). Young styles use the existing age fit (they fade after 27).
- **Facial hair (generated):** `goatee-extended` (goatee and mustache spread along the jaw, uncommon), `braided-chin` (chin
  beard with a braid and a tie, rare), `pencil-beard` (pencil-thin line along the jaw with a curl at each cheek and a pencil
  mustache, uncommon). The two fuller ones count as short beards for beard events.
- **Face and body (mixed in at random, rare):** `septum-ring` and `brow-piercing` (accessories), `ear-gauges`
  (accessories), `mouth-grill` (a grin showing gold teeth, so the grill always shows), `body-neck-ink` (a small star on the
  side of the neck between the jaw and the collar: the older `fork-body-neck-tattoo` sits under the jaw, where the head
  covers it, and is left as it is so existing faces keep their look). Piercings, gauges and the neck ink are hard to see at
  thumbnail size, like the earring studs.
- **Nose bandage** (`nose-bandage`, gauze and tape over the bridge of the nose): never generated or stored. With _New Face
  Styles_ on, the player page and Compare Players (current season) draw it while he's out with `Fractured Nose`, `Facial
