  **Fit** (team 0 of each pair with the dials that best fit its roster (its strengths, clamped) or worst (their opposite), or Balanced, against auto opponents; net rating per 100 possessions vs the same team on auto, mean ± SE over 8 leagues): good fit +0.32 ± 0.23, bad fit −2.02 ± 0.32, balanced −1.37 ± 0.35; so a strong fit is +1.7 over a neutral offense and a mismatch −0.7 below it (auto teams' average fit is 0.77 SD). Tests (`offEmphasis.test.ts`): off-state identity, every multiplier within its bound for 2,000 random dials and strengths, fit signs, roster strengths and the coaches' choices (a shooting roster leans spot-up, a big roster post and inside, the system tilts it, the choice fits the roster), the report line.

- **Known limits:** the sim has no screens or sets, so the dials act through the existing decisions (self-creation, shot selection, transition) and the classifier's reading; strengths come from ratings, not stats (the report line adds PPP); the dials are per team, not per lineup or opponent.

### Realistic turnovers

- **Setting:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ `realisticTurnovers` (League Extras → Game Simulation). On for new leagues, off for existing leagues (off: nothing changes, the same random numbers, `outcomeInvariance.test.ts`). Code: `src/worker/core/GameSim.basketball/realisticTurnovers.ts` (constants), `GameSim.probTov` / `probStl`. Calibration: `RT_CAL=1 realisticTurnovers.script.test.ts` (whole seasons of new leagues).
- **Why:** new leagues with the default League Extras had about 16.5–16.7 turnovers per team game (16.1 with every extra off; Game sim fixes and End-of-game intelligence add a few tenths), against 14–15 in the modern NBA.
- **What:** each possession's turnover chance × 0.875 (in both possession models; the realistic possession model's shot-clock violation tendency reads the same chance, so violations go down a little too), and the chance that a turnover is a steal × 1.155, so steals per game stay the same and a larger share of turnovers are live-ball steals (STL/TOV .45 → .52, NBA ~.55). The possessions that no longer end in a turnover end in shots, so points go up about 1.7 per team game; pace goes down about 1 possession (a turnover ends a possession faster than a shot does).
- **Who commits it** (`GameSim.pickTurnoverPlayer`): stock BBGM picks the player by the turnovers composite squared (careless, low-rated players), so a 30-ppg star committed about 1–1.5 a game. With the setting on the weight is the turnovers composite × (usage composite)^1.5 × (creation composite)^0.5 (the last with Spacing & Shot Creation), all with fatigue and the traits' turnover multipliers: the ball handlers commit them. One random number either way; team turnovers and steals are unchanged (only who commits them). Top-usage players (10 per league) 2.1 → 3.2 TOV a game, the best scorers 3–4, scorers 3–10 per league 2.0 → 3.0 (the NBA's top usage players 3–4.5).
- **Calibration** (8 new leagues × 1 regular season each way, 9,840 games: the default league with every new-league setting, Marquee Games and the realistic schedule included; per team game):

  | per team game         | off           | on            |
  | --------------------- | ------------- | ------------- |
  | TOV                   | 16.73         | 14.61         |
  | STL                   | 7.54          | 7.58          |
  | pts                   | 106.9         | 108.6         |
  | pace                  | 103.1         | 102.2         |
  | ORtg                  | 103.4         | 105.9         |
  | FG%                   | .452          | .452          |
  | FGA                   | 87.6          | 88.8          |
  | 3PA rate              | .332          | .331          |
  | 3P%                   | .345          | .345          |
  | FTA                   | 23.5          | 24.2          |
  | AST                   | 24.79         | 25.11         |
  | ORB / DRB             | 11.31 / 34.06 | 11.49 / 34.55 |
  | BLK                   | 5.13          | 5.20          |
  | PF                    | 19.62         | 19.98         |
  | shot-clock violations | 0.78          | 0.71          |

- **Known limits:** one league-wide scale; who commits turnovers (ball handling, passing, pressure, coaching systems, traits) works as before.

### Advanced tracking stats

- **Setting:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ `advStats` (League Extras → Game Simulation, "Advanced Tracking Stats"). On for new leagues, off for existing leagues. Off: the GameSim has no tracker, nothing is recorded or stored. On or off, games are the same: the tracker draws no random numbers and the sim reads nothing it writes (`advTracking.test.ts` sims the same seeds on and off, with the new-league defaults and with every sim extra off, and compares the box scores; `outcomeInvariance.test.ts` passes unchanged). Code: `src/worker/core/GameSim.basketball/advTracking.ts` (tracker, classifier), hooks in `GameSim` (`advShot`, `advPutbackTurnover`, `recordStat`, `simPossession`, `doFg` / `doBlk` / the miss), `src/common/advStats.ts` (storage layout, derived stats, column metadata), `worker/core/player/advStatsStorage.ts` (folding or trimming at retirement, `getPlayTypeProfile`), `worker/views/lineups.ts`, `ui/views/Lineups.tsx`, `ui/views/Player/TrackingProfile.tsx`. Tests: `advTracking.test.ts` (no effect on results; everything recorded adds up to the box score: play-type FGA/FGM/TOV = team totals, assisted makes = assists, closest defenders = opponents' FGA, rim protectors = opponents' rim attempts, five on the floor share every possession and point, lineups cover the whole game) and `common/advStats.test.ts` (storage, merging, pruning); cost: `ADV_COST=1 advTrackingCost.script.test.ts`; realism: the `ADV` lines of `LONG_RUN=1 longRun.script.test.ts`.
- **What is recorded**, for each player who plays (season totals):
  - **Play types** (possessions, points, FGA, FGM, turnovers each): P&R ball handler, P&R roll man, isolation, post-up, spot-up, cut, off screen, handoff, transition, putback, miscellaneous. A play-type possession is a field goal attempt, a shooting foul (miss and fouled) or a turnover; its points are the shot's and the free throws that follow (and-ones included). Free throws from non-shooting fouls, flagrants and technicals have no play type.
  - **Shooting splits:** made field goals assisted / unassisted by zone (rim: dunks, layups, putbacks, tip-ins; short mid: post-ups; long mid: mid-range jumpers; three), and threes as catch-and-shoot (assisted, the same flag the sim's catch-and-shoot / off-the-dribble jumper gap reads with Sub-Ratings in Sim, `jumperGap3`) or pull-up (unassisted), attempts and makes.
  - **Defense:** the closest defender of every shot (his assignment with Matchup Defense; without it, the defender in the shooter's court slot, as the stock sim credits defensive stops; on a blocked shot, the blocker): defended FGA/FGM, 2PT and 3PT. Contests: the same outside transition. Rim protection: on every shot at the rim, the defender on the floor with the best blocking composite is the rim protector (opponents' rim FG% with him there); shots deterred: opponents' rim attempts per 100 of their possessions with him on the floor minus the expected number (`oppRimExp`: for each opponent he shared the floor with, that opponent's rim attempts per possession against the rest of his team in that game, shrunk toward the opponent's whole-game rate with 10 possessions, times their shared possessions). (trkfix: plain on minus off came out positive for 62% of 1000+ minute players and for 10 of the top 12 DPOY candidates, because it mostly measured who he played against: starters face starters, who attack the rim more; it correlated 0.48 with minutes per game and 0.04 with blocks per 36. The lineup-matched version: mean −0.09, sd 1.05 (was +0.38, 1.42), 46% positive, correlation with minutes per game −0.18, blocks per 36 +0.03, interior defense −0.04 (was +0.16): the sim picks rim attempts from the shooter, matchups and team defensive synergy, not from the rim protector on the floor, so there is little deterrence to measure. Seasons recorded before it keep plain on minus off.)
  - **On/off:** possessions and points for and against while on the floor, and his team's totals in the games he played; off = team − on (games he missed don't count). A possession starts whenever the ball changes hands or a period starts (an offensive rebound continues it).
  - **Clutch:** the last 5 minutes of the final period or overtime with the margin within 5 at the start of the possession: games, minutes, points, FG, 3P, FT, turnovers, plus/minus and usage (his FGA + 0.44 FTA + TOV over his team's while he was on the floor). (trkfix check: the clutch state is set at each possession start, so it is entered and left as the margin moves past 5, and the five on the floor share exactly the team's clutch margin, which advTracking.test.ts asserts. Season clutch +/- of +80 to +128 for good teams' starters in 60–100 clutch minutes is what the sim produces, not a bookkeeping error: in 2 new-league seasons the best teams' clutch margins were +106 in 84 team clutch minutes (71 wins) and +46 in 95, and the median rotation player gets only about 40 clutch minutes (NBA leaders about 150+): there are fewer close games, and better teams pull away from them more often than in the NBA.)
  - **Lineups:** each team's five-man units (any number of players on the court): minutes, possessions and points for and against.
- **Play-type rules** (in order; the sim has no screens, so the split of an ambiguous shot is weighted by the players' roles and drawn with a hash of the game id and a counter, deterministic and not a random number):
  1. a tip-in off an inbound → miscellaneous;
  2. transition → transition: with the realistic possession model, the possession's transition draw (after a steal, defensive rebound or inbound); in the stock model, a shot within 7 s of a defensive rebound or steal;
  3. right after an offensive rebound → putback: the end-of-period putback, or a quick shot (the realistic model's quick putback draw, or within 3 s in the stock model) at the rim or by the rebounder;
  4. a post-up shot → post-up;
  5. a shot off a pass (the assister, or with Spacing & Shot Creation the creator when he is not the shooter, even if no assist is credited), with big = height 35 → 70, creator = (dribbling + passing) / 2 35 → 70, mover = speed 40 → 80, all 0–1:
     - three: spot-up 0.7, off screen 0.2 × mover × (1 − big), handoff 0.06 + 0.1 × passer big × (1 − big), roll man (pick-and-pop) 0.35 × big × passer creator;
     - mid-range: spot-up 0.45, off screen 0.3 × mover × (1 − big), handoff 0.08 + 0.12 × passer big × (1 − big), roll man 0.4 × big × passer creator;
     - at the rim: roll man 0.05 + 0.6 × big × passer creator, cut 0.35 + 0.25 × (1 − big), spot-up (catch and drive) 0.25 × (1 − big), handoff 0.12 × passer big × (1 − big);
  6. a self-created shot (no pass): P&R ball handler 0.05 + 0.5–0.6 × creator, isolation (0.15 + 0.3 × iso lean) × (0.4–0.5 + 0.5–0.6 × creator), where iso lean = 0.5 + (dribbling − passing) / 40; low creators' jumpers also spot-up (0.35–0.5 × (1 − creator)) and off screen, their drives cut / roll man (bigs);
  7. a turnover: transition and putback as above, else ball handler 0.05 + 0.45 × creator, isolation 0.2 × creator, post-up 0.3 × big, roll man 0.15 × big, cut 0.1, spot-up 0.12 × (1 − creator), handoff and off screen 0.04.
- **Storage:** a player's stats row (regular season and playoffs, per team) gets `adv`, an array of 99 integers (`ADV` in `common/advStats.ts`, append-only); careers, TOT rows and merged rows sum it element by element (`player/format.ts`). A team's stats row gets `adv`: its totals (possessions, clutch) and its lineups, pruned to the 60 most used whenever there are more than 150 during the season and to the 40 most used after the playoffs (`newPhaseBeforeDraft`). Box scores store nothing new. A retiring player's tracking is folded into one career total, see [Keep career tracking for retired players](#keep-career-tracking-for-retired-players) (with that setting off he keeps the records of his last 3 seasons only, `trimAdvOnRetire`).
- **UI:** Player Stats tabs _Play Types_ (possessions, then frequency and PPP of each type), _Shooting Splits_, _Tracking Defense_, _On/Off_ and _Clutch_ (hidden with the setting off; season totals and rates, never per game; careers and Advanced Player Search, Player Graphs and Stat Distributions work too); on the player page the same five tables and a _Tracking Profile_ section (a season or career picker, a play-type table with frequency bars, PPP, FG% and TOV%, and his shooting, defense, on/off and clutch numbers); _Lineups_ (a tab of Team Stats: team, season, regular season/playoffs, sortable lineups with a minimum minutes filter, and the team's players' on/off). These are stats, so they show with the hidden-ratings challenge too.
- **Elsewhere:** GOAT lab variables (`ptPoss`, `ptPrbPpp`, `ptIsoPpp`, `ptPostPpp`, `ptSpotPpp`, `ptTrPpp`, `ptPrrPpp`, `dfgp`, `rimDfgp`, `con2`, `con3`, `rimDeter`, `onNet`, `onOffNet`, `cs3p`, `pu3p`, `clPts`, `clFgp`, `clPm`, `clUsgp`, each with a `Playoffs` suffix; NaN without tracking stats, so a formula with them leaves those players out). Awards: see _Tracking awards_ below (the old `advDef` variable stays for custom formulas; the default defense formulas now use `trkDef`). Archetypes: `getPlayTypeProfile(p, season?)` in `advStatsStorage.ts` returns each play type's possessions, frequency, PPP, FG% and TOV%; the archetype reading uses it against the league's own play-type mix (Archetypes, arch15).
- **Tracking awards:** `worker/core/awards/trackingAwards.ts`. With `advStats` on, the stock default formulas (unedited, no per-position formula) are scored with a bounded tracking term; with it off, or in seasons without tracking data, they are exactly the stock formulas (every `trk*` score is 0 without data, and `x + 0 === x`). Composite variables, each −1 to 1: `trkImpact` = on-off net / 12 × poss/(poss+2000); `trkClutch` = (½ clamp(clutch +/- / 40) + ½ clamp((clutch pts − 0.5 × clutch min) / 30)) × min/(min+30); `trkDef` = 0.3 defended FG% vs expected (league 2P%/3P% on his shot mix minus his, /4, × dfga/(dfga+300)) + 0.25 rim DFG% vs league (/6, × rimDfga/(rimDfga+200)) + 0.1 shots deterred (−rimDeter/3; 0.15 before trkfix, mostly noise in this sim) + 0.1 contests per 36 vs league (/50%, × min/(min+800)) + 0.2 defensive on-off (−onOffDrtg/6), the last two on-court terms × poss/(poss+2000); `trkPtEff` = points over the league PPP of each of his play types per 100 play-type possessions / 20 × poss/(poss+400). Formulas: MVP and All-League `+ seasonFraction * (0.1 * trkImpact + 0.05 * trkClutch)`; DPOY and All-Defensive (stock or Perimeter Defense) `+ 1 * seasonFraction * trkDef` (replaces the old `0.15 * advDef`); Sixth Man `+ seasonFraction * (0.4 * trkImpact + 0.3 * trkPtEff)`; Finals MVP `+ 8 * trkImpactPlayoffs` (playoff on-off of the whole playoffs: box scores keep no tracking data). ROY, MIP, All-Rookie, Semifinals MVP unchanged. **Clutch Player of the Year** (`CPOY`, new default award with the `advStats` flag "Advanced Tracking Stats only": skipped by doAwards, Award Races and the vote with the setting off; existing leagues can add it in the editor): `min(1, clMin / 40)^2 * (clPts / 15 + clPm / 25 + 6 * (clFg + 15) / (clFga + 30) + clUsgp / 8)`. The award vote reads the same scores (processAwards). **Calibration** (`trackingAwards.calibration.test.ts`, `TRK_AWARDS_OUT`; 8 seeds × 3 seasons, new-league defaults, each season scored with the setting on and off on the same data): same winner MVP 23/24, DPOY 19/24, SMOY 23/24, Finals MVP 24/24, ROY/MIP/Semifinals MVP/All-Rookie 100%; All-League 345/360 slots (1st team 111/120), All-Defensive 205/240 (1st team 106/120). DPOY changes go to the better tracked defender, e.g. a 2.4-steal GF (trkDef −0.08) → a C with 2.6 blocks allowing 7.4 points under expected; a C with 2.9 blocks but a neutral rim DFG% → a C with 3.1 blocks, rim DFG% 3.4 under and −10.7 defensive on-off. All-Defensive swaps mostly trade high-steal, low-impact guards/wings for bigs and wings with strong defended FG%, while guards with good tracking (trkDef 0.2–0.36) still get in. **Editor:** every tracking stat is an award variable (all 11 play types' poss/freq/PPP/FG%/TOV%, assisted/unassisted makes and %ast by zone, C&S and pull-up 3PA/3P%, defended FG%/2P%/3P% and attempts, rim DFGA/DFG%, contests, shots deterred, on/off ORtg/DRtg/net, on-floor possessions, clutch games/min/pts/FG/FG%/3P%/FT%/TOV/+-/usage) plus `dfgpVsExp`, `dfgp2VsExp`, `dfgp3VsExp`, `rimDfgpVsExp`, `con36` and the `trk*` scores, each with a `Playoffs` variant (the season's playoff row; also usable in playoff series awards). Without data they are NaN (the player is left out), `trk*` 0. Award Settings → Show documentation lists them grouped with descriptions, with a warning when the setting is off. Tests: `trackingAwards.test.ts`.

- **Realism** (5-season long run, new-league defaults, seed 1, `longRun.script.test.ts`, which now prints an `ADV` line per season; league-wide regular-season play-type possessions, ~270,000 a season). With Realistic Play Types off (with it on, see [Realistic play types](#realistic-play-types)):

  | play type        | freq (sim) | PPP (sim) | NBA freq | NBA PPP |
  | ---------------- | ---------- | --------- | -------- | ------- |
  | P&R ball handler | 8.4–8.5%   | 0.59–0.63 | 15–20%   | ~0.90   |
  | P&R roll man     | 9.0–9.5%   | 1.14–1.16 | 6–8%     | ~1.10   |
  | isolation        | 4.5–4.7%   | 0.79–0.81 | 5–8%     | ~0.90   |
  | post-up          | 16.7–17.2% | 0.82–0.83 | 4–6%     | ~0.95   |
  | spot-up          | 27.2–27.5% | 1.02–1.04 | 20–25%   | ~1.00   |
  | cut              | 12.1–12.4% | 1.20–1.21 | 6–9%     | ~1.30   |
  | off screen       | 3.5–3.9%   | 0.84–0.88 | 4–6%     | ~0.95   |
  | handoff          | 4.5%       | 0.90–0.92 | 3–5%     | ~0.92   |
  | transition       | 11.6–11.8% | 1.02–1.03 | 12–16%   | ~1.12   |
  | putback          | 1.3–1.4%   | 1.12–1.20 | 5–6%     | ~1.10   |

  Gaps, honestly: post-ups are three times the NBA's share because the sim's shot mix has many low-post shots (its own `lowPost` shot type, not the classifier); P&R ball handlers are about half the NBA's because only about 20% of shots are self-created (Spacing & Shot Creation) and many of the rest are credited to the creator's pass (spot-up, cut, roll man); putbacks are rare because the sim's quick shot after an offensive rebound goes to a shooter picked by usage, and only the end-of-period putback is a true putback; ball-handler PPP is low because the classifier gives creators most of the non-shot turnovers (their TOV% is 25–35%, the NBA's ~15%). Clutch: 2.0–2.5% of minutes, 2.2–2.8% of possessions and 2.4–2.8% of points (NBA: about 4–5% of minutes). Defended FG% (players with 300+ shots defended): mean 46.3–46.7%, SD 3.3 points, range about 36–58% (NBA tracking: roughly 38–52%). Rim protection (150+ rim shots): mean 63.3–64.2%, SD 2.6–3.6. On-off net rating (400+ possessions): SD 7.0–7.6. Threes: 82% catch-and-shoot (NBA ~65–70%), 36.2% vs 33.0–34.2% pull-up (NBA ~37% / ~34%). Assisted makes: rim 64%, post-ups 48%, mid-range 44%, threes 83% (NBA: about 60%, —, 40–45%, 85%). Lineups kept: 40 per team-season. Realistic Play Types (on for new leagues) moves the mix toward the NBA's; see [Realistic play types](#realistic-play-types).

- **Storage and speed:** database after 5 seasons (JSON size of every store, the long run above): 105.3 MB with the setting vs 104.0 MB without (+1.3%; the players store 16.14 vs 15.18 MB, +6.3%; team stats 0.56 vs 0.19 MB). Sim CPU (`simBenchmark.test.ts`, `SIM_BENCH=on` with and without `SIM_BENCH_WITHOUT=advStats`, seeds 1–4, regular season and playoffs): +3.2%, +3.3%, +3.6%, +5.9% (+4.0% in all, run-to-run noise about ±3%), offseason −0.6%; long run (both configs at once) sim +0.9%, offseason +0.8%. About 0.3 ms of a 7–8 ms game is the tracker (`ADV_COST=1 advTrackingCost.script.test.ts` times the sim alone; on-court possessions and points are counted by stints, at substitutions, not per possession), the rest is merging the lineups and the bigger player rows. Both leagues of the long run and every benchmark seed played exactly the same games with and without the setting.
- **Known limits:** no screens, handoffs or cuts are simulated, so those play types are an interpretation of who passed to whom and the players' roles; non-shooting-foul free throws have no play type; the closest defender is the matchup (or court slot), not a distance; on/off "off" counts only the games he played; box scores keep no tracking detail; a lineup that drops out of the kept list during the season loses its minutes before that.

### Shot zones

- **Setting:** `shotZones` (League Extras, "Shot Zones"). On for new leagues, off for existing leagues. Off: no sub-zone, no xFG, no extra random number, games unchanged (`outcomeInvariance.test.ts` passes). Code: `src/worker/core/GameSim.basketball/shotZones.ts` (profiles, `pickShotZone`, `expectedFg`, constants `SZ`), `GameSim.szShot` (called once in `getShotInfo` after the shot type is picked), recording in `advTracking.ts` (`szZone`/`szXfg` → `shotDone`), layout and `shotZones()` in `common/advStats.ts`, league/position references in `worker/views/player.ts`, UI `ui/views/Player/ShotZones.tsx`. `loadTeams` adds the floater sub-rating (`flt`) to `pbpRatings` when a player has sub-ratings.
- **Sub-zones** (one `Math.random()` per three, mid-range or rim shot; putbacks/tip-ins and post-ups need none): threes are corner or above the break (corner share 8% + 14% × (1 − ball handling) + 17% if assisted, so catch-and-shoot role players live in the corner); mid-range is a short shot / floater (4–14 ft) or a long two (38% base, more for small quick players and a good floater/touch); rim shots are a dunk (dunk rating × size/explosiveness), a cut (assisted finishes by movers) or a layup. Make shifts, centered on the league so totals stay put: corner three +4 pts × (corner − 0.24), short shot +0.12 × (floater − 0.5), dunk +0.095, cut +0.015, layup −0.045.
- **xFG:** per shot, from the sub-zone's league base, team defense (−0.25 × (def − 0.47)), his defender with Matchup Defense, +3 pts with an assist, spacing (Spacing & Shot Creation) and a rushed shot; not the shooter. Stored as 1000 × xFG summed (`szXfg`) and the count (`szN`) in the player's `adv`, with attempts/makes by sub-zone (`szFga + i`, `szFgm + i`, `SHOT_ZONES` order) appended to the layout.
- **Player page:** "Shot zones" section: a half-court SVG (rim, short/paint, long two, corner, above the break) colored by his FG% vs the league's in that zone (blue above, red below, gray under 10 FGA), a sub-zone table with frequency and FG% vs his position and the league (the player's last 3 seasons with zone data get references; career uses the latest), and shot quality (xFG% vs actual FG%, "shot making"). Needs Advanced Tracking Stats.
- **Calibration** (one new-league regular season, same seed, off → on): seed 1 pts 108.1 → 108.6, FG% 45.8 → 46.2, 3P% 34.8 → 34.9, 3PA rate 35.4 → 35.0%; seed 2 pts 107.0 → 107.5, FG% 45.6 → 45.7, 3P% 34.7 → 34.7, 3PA rate 35.1 → 35.0% (the random stream differs, so these are within season-to-season noise). Zone shares of FGA (seed 2): dunk 6.3%, layup 15.2%, cut 6.1%, putback 4.7%, post 3.4%, short mid 12.6%, long two 16.7%, corner three 9.1% (26% of threes, 37.8%), above the break 26.0% (33.7%).

### Keep career tracking for retired players

- **Setting:** `advCareerTracking` (League Extras → Game Simulation, "Keep Career Tracking for Retired Players"). Basketball. On for new leagues **and on for existing leagues**: it changes no game, award or rating (only what is stored for a player once he has retired, and the sim never reads it), and a retired player keeps his whole career's tracking in less space than the old trim kept 3 seasons in. It does nothing without Advanced Tracking Stats data. Off: exactly the old behaviour (`trimAdvOnRetire`, his last 3 seasons). Active players are never touched: they keep every season.
- **Storage:** a player gets `advCareer` = `{ rs, po, from, to }` (`common/advStats.ts` `AdvCareer`): the element-by-element sum of every stats row's `adv`, regular season (`rs`) and playoffs (`po`) apart, in the same append-only layout as a row's `adv` (a later field is just added at the end), and the first and last season folded. At retirement (`player.retire`, which deaths, Thanos snaps, forced retirements and Hall of Fame inductions all go through) every pro stats row's `adv` is added in, partial seasons of a traded player included, and then deleted from the row (`foldAdvCareer` in `worker/core/player/advStatsStorage.ts`). The total is never trimmed. Pre-draft (college/overseas) rows and NIT rows are left alone.
- **Comebacks:** a retired player who comes back (God Mode, Unretirements) keeps `advCareer`; his new seasons get rows with `adv` as usual, and his career is `advCareer` + those rows. At his next retirement the new rows are folded in. A folded row has no `adv` left, so folding again (or the pass below) never counts a row twice.
- **Already-retired players:** players who retired before (or while the setting was off) still have the `adv` of their last 3 seasons; their older seasons were deleted by the trim and can't be recovered. A fold pass (`foldRetiredAdvCareers`) folds those 3 seasons into a career total: it runs when League Extras are saved and at every phase change, while the `advCareerFolded` game attribute is false (missing in older leagues and league files, and set to false again by a retirement with the setting off), then sets it. It reads the retired players' keys and only touches those with `adv` rows.
- **Reading:** every career reads `advCareer` + the rows' remaining `adv` (`careerAdv`, `advCareerUse`): careers and career rows in `player/format.ts` (Player Stats tracking tabs with Career, Advanced Player Search totals, Player Graphs, the player page's tables), the player page _Tracking Profile_ (the season picker lists only the seasons that still have data, plus Career, with the note "Season-by-season tracking isn't kept after retirement."), GOAT Lab career formulas, `getPlayTypeProfile(p)` and the player profile export (its Tracking section's career rows, with the same note as a line and `foldedAtRetirement` in the JSON). Awards read only the current season's rows, so they are unchanged.
- **No empty seasons:** a retired player's past seasons have no tracking, so they are hidden rather than shown blank: the player page's tracking tables show only seasons with data (the career row has everything, with the note under the table), and the Player Stats tracking tabs leave out rows without tracking data (only with this setting on). A total that can't be split out of the career total, a team's career (Player Stats filtered by team, Career) or a season range that covers only part of the folded seasons (the player page's range footer, Advanced Player Search totals), shows no tracking instead of a partial number; a range that covers all of them includes it. GOAT Lab season formulas with tracking variables leave a retired player's seasons out (NaN), as they already did for seasons without tracking.
- **Import/export:** `advCareer` is part of the player object, so league exports, league files (in the JSON schema) and Export Players carry it; Import Players keeps it with stats when all of its seasons are kept (shifted like the stats rows).
- **Storage** (10-season long run, new-league defaults, seed 1, `longRun.script.test.ts`, which now prints a `retired` entry per season; the same games with the setting on and off): after 10 seasons, 570 retired players (391 with tracking): players store 27.56 MB on vs 27.77 MB off (−0.8%), retired players 8.47 vs 8.68 MB, their tracking 0.23 vs 0.44 MB (one regular-season and one playoff total per player instead of up to 3 seasons of rows, so about half the size while keeping the whole career); database 161.9 vs 162.1 MB. Active players are the same in both.
- **Tests:** `worker/core/player/advCareer.test.ts`: folding equals the old career sum exactly (regular season, playoffs, combined; every tracking stat through `player.format` and the play-type profile); no double counting after a repeat fold or a comeback and re-retirement; the career view is the same before and after retirement; team careers and partial ranges show no tracking, a full range does; with the setting off it is today's trim and the fold pass folds the kept seasons once it is turned on (and never again); a JSON round trip through `augmentPartialPlayer` keeps it.

### Season odds

- **Where:** _Standings_ (a _Season odds_ section below the tables), _Power Rankings_ (Playoffs and Title columns), _Playoffs_ (series odds on the bracket), _Award Races_ (a Win% column), _Draft Lottery_ (a _What-if simulator_ below the lottery table).
- **What:** 1,000 Monte Carlo simulations of the rest of the season in the worker: each remaining game is decided from team strength (team rating from the current roster, ignoring injuries of 10 days or less, blended with point margin as games are played) plus home court, playoffs are seeded with the game's own playoff code, then the play-in and the bracket are played out. Gives projected record, playoff and play-in odds, seed distribution, reaching the semifinals/finals and title odds. During the playoffs it plays out the remaining series from the current state. Results are cached and refreshed after each day of games, trades, signings or injuries (about 0.2 s per refresh), and only when a page that shows them asks.
- **Series odds:** during the playoffs, each unfinished series of the round in progress (the first round during the play-in) shows each team's chance to win it next to its wins, from the same simulations (the share of simulations in which the team wins that round, so a team waiting on a play-in opponent gets its odds against either one). Current season only, not in college leagues.
- **Award races:** each candidate's award score (BBGM's award scoring) is projected to the end of the regular season with noise that shrinks as the season goes on, and the winner drawn 5,000 times.
- **Lottery what-if:** move teams up or down the lottery order (chances stay with the slot) or pick another lottery format, then _Run lottery_ for exact odds (where fast enough), a 20,000-lottery simulation (hover a cell) and one sample draw.
- **Known limits:** current season only. Tiebreakers are approximated (random among tied teams). Series follow the real home court pattern for any length, and reseeding is applied. Award odds only consider the top 10 candidates shown and don't model team record changes. The what-if ignores nba2027 restrictions and COLA when switching formats; in the other sports (no Season Odds there) it goes with the _Extra frivolities_ switch.

### Power rankings in the playoffs

- **Where:** _Power Rankings_ (score, rank, movement arrows and the rank-over-time chart), all sports.
- **What:** once a season's playoffs have started (and for past seasons), the score adds each playoff team's results to the regular season score (`playoffScore` in `worker/views/powerRankings.ts`): 4 per round won (`PLAYOFF_ROUND_WEIGHT`, about a 10-0 last ten), up to ±6 for the playoff record (wins minus losses over at least 4 games, play-in left out) and half the regular season weight of the playoff point margin per game (scaled per sport, fully counted after 10 games). Teams out of the playoffs get nothing, so a champion ends up near the top and an early exit drops a little.
- **Snapshots:** the weekly snapshots (`teamSeason.powerRankHistory`) go on through the playoffs, ranked with the playoff ratings and results and marked `playoffs: true`; the movement arrows compare with the last snapshot ranked the same way (the regular season or playoff toggle of the page).

### Statistical feats: filters and leaderboard

- **Where:** _Statistical Feats_ (`/l/:lid/player_feats`), all sports for the game type filter, basketball for the feat types.
- **What:** a _Games_ / _Leaderboard_ switch and two filters above the table, applied to the team and season picked in the title bar. _Feat type_ (basketball: triple-double, 5x5, 50+ points, 25+ rebounds, 20+ assists, 10+ steals, 10+ blocks, 10+ threes; only the types present are offered) is worked out from each feat's box score line with the cutoffs it was recorded with (`common/featKinds.ts`, the same as `checkStatisticalFeat`, scaled by the feat's stored factor), so a feat can be several types. _Games_ offers the game types present (regular season, playoffs, NIT, All-Star). The leaderboard counts each player's feats (team of his latest feat, first and last season), with a column per feat type when no type is picked.

### Realistic schedule length

- **Setting:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ `realisticSchedule`. On for new leagues, off for existing leagues (off = exactly the old schedule). Takes effect with the next schedule (the start of the next regular season). Not used with _Group schedule into series_.
- **What:** the old schedule packs every team into almost every day (82 games in ~91 days: ~74 back-to-backs per team, up to 50 game days in a row). With the setting on, the regular season is spread over about 2 calendar days per game (`DAYS_PER_GAME` in `season/groupScheduleRealistic.ts`): 82 games in ~171 days, 40 in ~85, 100 in ~208, 162 in ~335. Each day is a set of games between teams rested enough. Per team, for 82 games: about 14 back-to-backs (11–17, each team gets its own budget), never 3 days in a row, occasional 2–3 day breaks, home/away runs when the matchups allow it. 3 games in 4 nights: about 0.6–0.7 per team (0–4). The day-by-day construction only allows one when a team would otherwise have to play every remaining day, and the few it still leaves (almost all in the last 3 weeks, where the teams that fell behind catch up) go to a repair pass (`season/scheduleEditor.ts`): a local search that moves single games, or a game in the way first and then the game, to a day up to 12 days away while that lowers the cost of every team's rest pattern (3-in-4 10, 3 days in a row 1000, 1.5 per back-to-back away from the team's count, a little for breaks over 6 days and uneven days). It never moves events, never moves a game into the All-Star break and never empties a day, and the season can end at most 2 days later than the construction did (in practice the same 171–172 days). Before the repair pass there were ~3.6–4 per team (10 seeds: 3.7, max 8 for one team); at 1.85 days per game (~163 days) ~12.
- **All-Star break and trade deadline:** after the same fraction of the games as before. The All-Star Game is in the middle of a 3-day break with no other games (shorter for very short seasons); the trade deadline gets its own day.
- **Daily processes keep their pace per season:** everything that ran once per day of games (injury and return-from-injury countdowns, games until tradable, free agents lowering their demands, AI signings, holdouts and extensions, AI trades and trade offers, rumors, tragic deaths) runs on only as many days as the old schedule would have had, spread evenly (`season/scheduleDayScale.ts`; the `scheduleDayScale` game attribute is old days / new days, set with each schedule, and recomputed when the schedule editor saves). So injuries last as many games as before. The playoffs are unchanged.
- **Play menu:** _One day_ is the next day with games; _One week_ / _One month_ are 7 / 30 calendar days. _Until All-Star Game / trade deadline / end of regular season_ and _Sim to game_ count days with games, so the All-Star break's empty days don't throw them off.
- **UI:** the team Schedule shows the day of each upcoming game and the days off before it, and **B2B** marks the second night of a back-to-back (Schedule and Daily Schedule).
- **Numbers (30 teams, 82 games, 3 seeds; measured at 1.85 days per game, now 2: last day 170–172, back-to-backs avg ~14, 3-in-4 avg ~0.6 since the repair pass, 3.6–4.0 before):** last day 163, ~160 days with games, back-to-backs per team min/avg/max 10–11 / 15.1–15.5 / 18–19 (old schedule: 73–75 / 74–75 / 75–77), longest stretch without a game 3–4 days (2–3), league scoring and injury totals unchanged without injury depth (417 vs 401 new injuries, 2,013 vs 2,157 player-games missed, noise level). With _Injury depth_ on there are ~8% fewer injuries (401 vs 437 per season), because injury depth's back-to-back risk was calibrated on the old schedule, where almost every game is a back-to-back. Full regular season sim time +6% (more days, same number of games).
- **Same opponent in a row:** a team never plays the same opponent 3 games in a row, and 2 in a row only as a planned 2-game set (both teams' previous game was that meeting, the same home team, at most 2 days later), at most one per team and about 0.2 per team in the league. Every pair's meetings are spread over the season (a meeting sooner than half the season / the pair's number of meetings after the last one loses up to 0.6 of value, 1 for Marquee Games rivals), and a pair with a big share of a team's remaining games goes earlier (3 × its share), so the last weeks don't leave a team with mostly one opponent; only past the target length (4 days, so the season always ends) is a second game in a row allowed otherwise, and a third only when the teams have no one else left. The schedule editor's cost counts opponents too (3 in a row 1000, 2 in a row 12, a 2-game set 3, meetings under 10 days apart 0.3 per day), so the 3-in-4 repair and the marquee moves don't create runs. Before (50 seeds, 30 teams, 82 games, per season): 52 two-game runs and 3 three-game runs (47 of 50 seeds had one; e.g. ATL-CLE three in a row), the median pair's shortest gap between meetings 8 days. After: 2.2 two-game runs, all same-venue sets, no team with more than 1; no three-game runs; median shortest gap 28 days (31 with Marquee Games); back-to-backs 13.9 → 13.9, 3-in-4s 0.73 → 0.57 per team (Marquee Games 0.87 → 0.82), last day 171.3 → 171.0 (173.2 → 172.7). The cause was the day-by-day construction: partners were picked by rest and pace alone, and a home/away run bonus (+0.15 per team) made the same matchup at the same venue the best partner again right after; the last weeks' catch-up then paired the teams left behind with each other. Tests: `season/scheduleMatchups.test.ts`.
- **Tiny leagues:** with 2-3 teams the old schedule already had a game every day, so `scheduleDayScale` stays 1 (the daily processes run on every day with games, as they should); the `scheduleSpread` game attribute says the season's schedule is spread over the calendar anyway, so Load, Fatigue & Venue's 3-in-4s and injury normalisation, the schedule's day column and B2B tags work there too. The first game is always on day 1 (a 2-team schedule could start on day 2-5).
- **Known limits:** basketball only. Small or odd leagues get the best schedule the rules allow (with 2–4 teams there are more 3-in-4s). An existing league switching it on keeps this season's schedule until the next regular season.

### Marquee games

- **Setting:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ `marqueeGames` (League Extras). On for new leagues, off for existing leagues. Needs _Realistic schedule length_ (and so not with _Group schedule into series_ or in college mode); off, or without it, the schedule is exactly the realistic one. Takes effect with the next schedule (the start of the next regular season). Code: `season/marqueeSchedule.ts` (slates, national TV, rivals), `season/getMarqueeInput.ts` (what it reads from the league), the slate days and rival spacing in `season/groupScheduleRealistic.ts`, `common/marquee.ts` (labels, crowd and hype points). Tests: `season/marqueeSchedule.test.ts`.
- **Who draws:** each team's draw (0 to 1) is 0.55 × its team ovr rank + 0.3 × its hype + 0.15 × its population rank. A matchup's score is both teams' draw plus half the weaker one's (two good teams beat one great and one bad), plus 0.3–0.7 for rivals (more for a hotter rivalry) and 0.3 with last season's champion.
- **Rivals:** pairs with a rivalry score (the [Rivalries](#rivalries) score) of 10 or more (Simmering and up), and each team's top rival with at least 3 (in a new league, where every pair only has its geography, a division rival, the closest one by the teams' regions). In a default 30-team league that is about 60 games a season. While the schedule is built, a matchup of rivals that met less than half the season / their number of meetings ago loses up to 1 point of value (a home/away run is worth 0.15), so the meetings spread out: the shortest gap between two meetings of a pair goes from about 18 days on average to about 31.
- **Holiday slates** (fixed days from the start of the season, for an 82-game season of about 171 days that starts in late October; shorter seasons scale them down, and the All-Star break or the trade deadline move them to the next day with games, which is then the day kept light):
  - _Opening Night_, day 1: a doubleheader and nothing else, the champion at home (ring night) against the best opponent, then the best other matchup.
  - _Christmas_, day 66: 5 games and nothing else, with no games the day before, so every team is rested and the slate is the 5 best matchups (no team twice, no matchup that was already on opening night).
  - _MLK Day_, day 91: the 4 best matchups moved onto a regular day of games.
  - The schedule builder fills the exclusive days itself (at most that many games, the best ones first) and adds the days they cost to the season (about 2: 82 games in 172–174 days instead of 171–172). The MLK games are moved onto their day by the schedule editor (a swap with a game of the same team that day, a move, or a move after moving the game in the way), accepted only if it costs less than one 3-in-4, and pinned there; then the 3-in-4 repair runs again around them.
- **National TV:** 3 games a week (2 in a week with a holiday slate), the best matchups that week, never two on one day, and a team's 3rd and later appearances count against it, so 72 national TV games a season in a 30-team league. Holiday games are national TV games too. At most 15% of a team's games are national TV or holiday games (`TV_MAX_SHARE`, 13 of 82): never reached in a 30-team league (at most about 11), it keeps tiny leagues from having nearly every game on national TV (2 teams: 71 of 82 before, 13 now).
- **Schedule pages:** badges under each game on Schedule and Daily Schedule (upcoming and completed): _Opening Night_ / _Christmas_ / _MLK Day_, _National TV_, _Rivalry_. The box score has a line under the attendance ("Christmas Day showcase, on national TV, a rivalry game"), and Games to Watch adds a reason (Christmas +6, Opening Night +5, MLK Day +3, national TV +2 hype points). The tags are saved with the schedule and the box score (`marquee`, `rivalry`), and the pages hide them while the setting is off.
- **Crowd:** with _Load, fatigue & venue_ on, the home crowd term (0.5 + hype) gets +0.15 for a holiday game, +0.1 for a national TV game and +0.1 for a rivalry game, at most +0.2 together: at the 1% home court setting that is at most +0.007% of the home team's composite ratings, a few hundredths of a point of margin.
- **Rest rules:** the same as the realistic schedule (never 3 days in a row, about 14 back-to-backs, the season length above); 3-in-4s per team 0.6–1.0 (4 seeds, against 0.2–0.7 for the same leagues without the setting).
- **Sample** (the default league, a new league with the new-league settings, seed 1): opening night LA @ MIN and WAS @ BAL (rivalry); Christmas LA @ LV (rivalry), CLE @ MIN, TOR @ MON (rivalry), PHI @ NYC (rivalry), ATL @ WAS; MLK Day MON @ BAL, LV @ CLE, STL @ ATL, WAS @ MXC; 72 national TV games, 84 rivalry games, last day 174, 14.0 back-to-backs and 0.7 3-in-4s per team.
- **Known limits:** basketball only. The schedule editor's _Regenerate_ makes a schedule without marquee games. Saving an edited schedule keeps the tags of the games it didn't change: a game whose teams were changed loses its tags, and a holiday game moved off its holiday's day loses the holiday tag (adding or deleting a day before it moves the whole slate, which keeps it; `season/editedScheduleTags.ts`). Holidays are days of the season, not dates (BBGM has no calendar). Small leagues get smaller slates when fewer teams can play.

## Players

### Names

- **Name data:** player names come from `data/names.json`, built by `node tools/names.js` from real athletes' names (`tools/names-manual/basketball.json`, `football.json`), the upstream per-country CSVs in `tools/names-manual` and the fork's additions in `tools/names-manual/extra`: more USA first names (modern 1995-2008 birth-cohort names, Black American, Hispanic and trendy names, with weights in the athlete-count units) and surnames (Hispanic, Asian, European and other Census surnames missing from the athlete data), more names for small country pools (Kosovo, French Guiana, South Sudan, ...) and new countries (Tunisia, Lebanon, Rwanda, Barbados, Malaysia, ...; their basketball birth-country weights are in `src/worker/data/defaultCountries.ts`, races in `defaultRaces.ts`). `extra/fixes.json` merges miscased spellings (`Aj` into `AJ`, `Mclean` into `McLean`) and removes bad entries (female names in male lists, garbled names, cities). Regenerate `data/names.json` after editing any of them.
- **Behind the Name additions:** `tools/names-manual/extra/btn/` holds about 12,000 more first names and surnames across 100 countries (classified per country, filtered for female/fictional/garbled entries, accents kept only for countries whose existing data has them), with weights scaled into the low tail of each country's existing frequencies (a weight-9 name is about the country's median) so real-athlete names still lead. The names come from [behindthename.com](https://www.behindthename.com/) under [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/); see `tools/names-manual/extra/ATTRIBUTION-behindthename.md`.
- **Hyphenated last names** (League Extras, on for new leagues, off for existing ones): with the chosen rate (default 1%), a new USA player's last name is joined to a second surname drawn from the same frequency table, like "Smith-Jones". Pairs are skipped when either part is already compound (spaces, hyphens, suffixes), when both start alike ("Johnson-Johnston"), when the result is over 15 characters, or when it is a real NBA player's hyphenated name. Existing players are never renamed; relatives inherit the full hyphenated name. Names are only split on spaces anywhere in the UI, so "J. Smith-Jones", sorting, search and the families pages treat it as one last name.
- **Code:** `src/worker/core/player/name.ts`, `tools/names.js`.

### Archetypes

- **What:** an archetype (a build) for every basketball player (arch15: 202 builds after the merges below, five of them new; archuser2k: 242, forty from the user's own 2K list; the counts that follow are the arch10 list). 375 archetypes: 346 NBA 2K build names carrying all 857 distinct names of the 1,256 builds in `twoK/builds2k.json` (from `tools/archetypes2k/extract2k.py`), plus 29 custom builds (arch10/arch11/arch13, `customDefs.data.ts`) for profiles no 2K build covers. In the UI they are all just archetypes: no 2K label, no custom tag, and the popover shows only the name, its one-line description and the short "why" (no "Same build in 2K" line, no "Also close to" runners-up, no poor-fit note). The other 2K names that say the same thing stay in the data as aliases (League -> Archetypes' search finds them; they are not shown); `custom` is an internal flag (tests, generator). No hybrids: the label is the single most similar build. There are no journeyman, raw prospect, veteran or superstar labels: the tier badge (by overall within the league) says how good he is.
- **Custom builds (arch10):** from the uncovered-profiles report (`ARCH_DIAG_GAPS`): Post-Scoring Slasher (PG/G/SG/GF/SF/F/PF), Slashing Cleaner (GF/SF/F), Glass-Cleaning Slasher (PG/G), Backline Lockdown (SG/GF/SF/F/PF), Lockdown Eraser (F/PF/FC/C), Post-Up Glass Cleaner (PG/G/GF/SF/F/PF/FC/C), Shot-Erasing Cleaner (PG/G/GF/SF/F/PF), Glass-Cleaning Lockdown (GF/SF/F/PF/FC/C), Glass-Cleaning Playmaker (PG/G), High-Flying Shot Creator (F/PF/FC/C), Slashing Mid-Range Maestro (FC/C), Shot-Erasing Post Scorer (PG/G/SG), Post-Scoring Eraser (GF/SF/F/PF), Diming Cleaner (GF/SF/F/PF), Shot-Creating Mid-Range Maestro (FC/C), Slashing Eraser (PG/G/GF/SF/F), Slashing Lockdown (GF/SF), Aerial Guardian (PG/G/SG), High-Flying Eraser (F/PF), Shot-Erasing Shot Creator (F/PF). Clusters that shared a name became one build over the union of their positions; four proposals were already 2K names or aliases, so they were renamed (Post-Up Glass Cleaner, Aerial Guardian, Backline Lockdown, Glass-Cleaning Lockdown), and "Slashing Post Scorer" was folded into Post-Scoring Slasher (the same profile).
- **How a player is read (arch10):** every skill is read 75% on the shape of his own game (relative to his level, as before) and 25% (`BLEND_W`, tuned from the asked-for 35%: 35% cost more stability under a uniform rating shift than it gained) on his absolute percentile among players at his position (`pq` tables per position bucket PG/G, SG, GF/SF, F/PF, FC/C), so an elite absolute skill (a point guard's three, a center's rebounding or passing) counts even for an all-around star. The profile match itself reads the shape against his position's peers for `MATCH_ABS_W` (0.2) of it. On that blended scale: (1) the name's floors scale with his overall (a "Sharpshooter" needs the three at about his position's 70th percentile for an average player, more for a star, less for a scrub), and any skill the name promises that he is below average at costs heavily (`promisePenalty`); (2) signature skills: his top one or two standouts must be named by the build or fit its role (`signatureTerm`), and a build that leads with a skill well below his best pays; (3) from age 31 lost speed, jumping and endurance are discounted (`ageAdjusted`, 2.5 a year, up to 12, never above 65); (4) draft prospects are read against prospect tables (`norms.prospect`, raw 19-year-olds), with the league's match tables; (5) with real minutes, 30% (scaled down below 1,000 minutes, none below 100) comes from last season's box score (`stats.ts`): 3PA rate and 3PA per 36, FT rate, shot zones at the rim, in the post and from mid-range (BBGM's own games; real stats have none), AST%, TRB%, BLK%, STL%, USG% and DBPM (defensive classes only), each against players at his position (`statNorms.data.ts` from real NBA 2016-2025, or the league's own last season when it has enough players with 500+ minutes); (6) creator vs off the ball: usage and assist rate together favour passing/handling builds over spot-up, 3-and-D and lockdown ones (or the other way round); ratings alone use the profiles' catch-and-shoot lean.
- **arch11 (absolute promises, roles, concentration):** (1) a build's name must also be true on the absolute scale: every skill it promises is read on his standing at his position (`pz`, with his stats; without stats half of it is the blended reading) and a promised skill below `ABS_FLOOR` (-1 z) costs `ABS_K` 1.5 per z; scorer/shooter names ("Scorer", "Bucket", 3-Level, Inside-Out, Sharpshooter, 3PT...) also need a shot at all (`NO_SHOT_*`: the best of three, mid-range and free throws above -1.1 z), "High-Flying"/"Aerial"/"Athletic" needs real jumping (`AIR_*`) and "Sniper"/"Sharpshooter" an elite three (`ELITE_SHOT_MIN` 1.9 z; an elite shooter gains `SNIPER_K` for one); "Scorer" now promises scoring (a `score` class). Root cause of the reported "Versatile Scorer" for a 7-footer with mid 14 / three 9 / FT 16: "Scorer" promised nothing, so on the level-relative shape (all low and flat reads as balanced) nothing stopped a scoring name; the current reading gives him Physical Board Enforcer (expanded ratings) / Rim-Reaping Rim Runner (stock), and `archetypes.test.ts` pins that he never gets a scoring or shooting name under shifts, prospect and age readings. A stored label from an older engine also lingered until the next preseason: entries now carry the reading's version (`ARCHETYPE_READING_VERSION` 11) and an older one is read again (no stickiness) instead of shown. (2) Roles (`roleTerm`, only for a build he lives up to): assist rate and passing at his position drive passing builds (`PLAY_K`; assist rate and block rate also count twice in the reading, `STAT_BOOST`), blocks/interior defense/block rate/length drive rim-protector builds for bigs (`RIM_K`), and a good player's mid-range build pays unless the mid-range is clearly his standout (`MID_LEVEL_K`: real ratings give nearly every star a high one). (3) Concentration: `starBias.data.ts` (`starBias.script.test.ts`, `RUN_ARCHETYPE_STARBIAS=1`, run after the calibration) trims builds above 1.8% of the real top 150 of 2016-2025 or 3.5% of any one season's, applied by his level at his position (`starWeight`). (4) Eight custom builds: Slashing Playmaker (PG/G/SG/GF/SF/F), Pick & Roll Finisher (FC/C), Hustle Glass Cleaner (F/PF/FC/C), Lockdown Stopper (SG/GF/SF/F), Pass-First Distributor (PG/G), Athletic Hustler (all), Shot-Blocking Anchor (FC/C), Deep-Range Sniper (PG/G/SG/GF/SF/F). Gold set: 17 lists gained the build the reading now picks where it fits the role (mostly the new customs; Curry -> Deep-Range Sniper, Rubio -> Pass-First Distributor, Holmgren/Jackson -> Shot-Erasing Rim Runner), and DeRozan 2018, Booker 2022 and Hamilton 2005 also accept a slashing playmaker (their ratings here show a passer, not a mid-range shooter). Calibration bias floor -0.25 -> -0.45; `STICKY_Z` 2.2 -> 2.4. arch10 -> arch11 (expanded off): gold 54% -> 65% listed, 67% -> 76% role; churn 6.1% -> 5.6%; prospects' max share 5.9% -> 6.0%; real 2025 top-150 max share 6.7% -> 3.3%; max share league 3.6% -> 2.4%, real 2016-2025 4.6% -> 3.7%; poor fit league 3.2% -> 2.9%, veterans 7.7% -> 7.3%, prospects 4.0% -> 4.3%, real 3.4% -> 3.6%; +-3 / +-6 shift stability 81% / 66% -> 80% / 64%; 0 position conflicts. Expanded on: gold 35% -> 47% listed / 57% -> 64% role, churn 4.9% -> 4.2%, prospects' max share 6.1% -> 6.7%, stability 75% -> 76%. SGA reads as F because the improved two-axis position model sees a 6'6" scorer with passing 49 (BBGM's old formula says GF): a ratings question, not an archetype bug, so positions are unchanged.
- **arch12 (hand-judged tuning):** the top 75 real 2025 players and the top 50 of a random league were judged by hand (1-3 best builds each from the full list). Causes of the misses and fixes: (1) guards and wings were named "Cleaner"/"Glass"/"Board"/"Rim-Reaping" off the high rebounding and blocking ratings real data gives most good guards: below hgt 55 a name promising rebounding or rim protection needs that class at `REB_PERIM_MIN` 1.1 on the stats-blended reading (`REB_PERIM_MIN_RATINGS` 0.3 on ratings alone), `REB_PERIM_K` 4 per z short (Murray, Fox, Butler, Dyson Daniels, Jalen Williams); (2) Post-Scoring Slasher's "without much of a handle or a jumper" is enforced with ceilings (`cap`, now read on the blended scale; Booker); (3) a mid-range name needs a shooter's touch, free throws or three not below `TOUCH_FROM` -1.1 for him (`TOUCH_K` 4; Giannis); (4) assist and block rates carry their stat boost in the absolute/role reading, the rim-protector role is `RIM_K_STATS` 1.8 with a block rate and `RIM_K` 1.0 on ratings alone (Davis, Gobert), and `SNIPER_K` 2 (Curry keeps Deep-Range Sniper under the stars' trim). No position change (Booker/Mitchell as PG now get scoring builds) and no new build (a 3-and-D wing was tried and left out: it did not win for the players it was meant for). Gold lists: Draymond 2025 + Lockdown Eraser, Embiid 2025 + Slashing Mid-Range Maestro, Westbrook 2025 + Mid-Range Slashing Cleaner, SGA 2025 + Versatile Paint Finisher. Before -> after (expanded off): hand-pick agreement top-1 76.8% -> 80.0%, top-3 88.0% -> 88.8% (n=125); gold listed 65.5% -> 68.1%, role 72.6% -> 78.8%; churn 5.6% -> 5.5%; real 2025 top 150 max share 4.0% -> 4.0%; poor fit league / veterans / prospects / real 3.3 / 9.5 / 4.7 / 5.1% -> 3.3 / 9.0 / 4.5 / 5.1%; +-3 stability 80.3% -> 80.1%; 0 position conflicts. Expanded on: gold 44.2% -> 46.0% listed / 61.9% -> 66.4% role, churn 4.8% -> 4.9%, poor fit 2.9 / 7.5 / 4.0 / 5.1% -> 2.9 / 7.7 / 4.0 / 5.3%, +-3 74.8% -> 74.6%, top 150 max share 4.0% -> 4.0%.
- **arch13 (rating fixes, all-around stars, scoring guards):** (1) **Real-player ratings:** the remaining hand-judged misses traced to ratings that contradict the box score. No ratings formula ships in this repo (the ratings come with the upstream data), so `tools/realRatingFixes.ts` sets each named rating to what the player's own stats predict: one regression per rating over every 2016-2026 player-season with 800+ minutes (same-season stats; the three from 3P%, 3PA per 36 and FT%; mid-range from 3P%, FT%, volume and FTA rate; jumping from block rate, rebounding, FTA rate and 2P%; finishing from 2P%, FTA rate and touch), only for the listed players and only where the rating is more than one standard error off in the reported direction (2024-2026 rows and the copied future rows; `--write` rewrites `data/real-player-data.basketball.json`, which round-trips byte for byte). 2025 rows: Dort jmp 84 -> 56, Bane jmp 80 -> 58, Zubac fg 80 -> 58, Hartenstein fg 78 -> 49, Herbert Jones fg 72 -> 48, Franz Wagner fg 97 -> 79, Luka Dončić tp 53 -> 71, Myles Turner dnk 95 -> 63. Mikal Bridges was checked and left alone (box scores have no shot zones, and he is a real mid-range shooter). (2) **Do-It-All Superstar** (custom, all positions, tag `allAround`, `rare`): for generated stars good at nearly everything, whom every 2K profile describes by two or three skills. No profile: its score is `ALL_AROUND_Z` 2.4 times a quality read from his level at his position (`pzLevel` 1.5 -> 1.9), breadth (5 -> 7 of the nine skill classes at 0.5+ on the blended reading, with a defensive class and three offensive ones) and evenness (best class at most 0.35 above his fourth, none at 0.6), so it wins only where no ordinary build fits him well and a specialist keeps his specialty's build; never for a draft prospect; kept out of the calibration's share-evening bias; its "why" is his three best skills. 2 of 3,000 players in the metrics' random league (0.07%, the ovr 95 and an ovr 84 all-rounder), none of the real 2025 top 150. (3) **Scoring guards' assists:** for a G, SG or GF, passing from his assist rate reads `SCORER_K` 0.3 per z of usage above his assist rate lower (his assists come with the ball in his hands; a point guard's and a big's are read as they are): Kobe Bryant 2006 Slashing Playmaker -> Gritty Power Slasher, Ray Allen 2001 Physical Offensive Engine -> Physical Mid-Range Menace. (4) Recalibrated (norms off and expanded, seeds, star bias); `SNIPER_K` 2 -> 2.3 so Curry keeps Deep-Range Sniper under the larger star trim. Before -> after (expanded off): hand-pick agreement (n=125, judged set in the arch13 scratchpad; new build added to three generated stars' picks) top-1 80.0% -> 79.2%, top-3 88.8% -> 93.6%; gold listed 68.1% -> 67.3%, role 78.8% -> 79.6%; churn 5.5% -> 5.6%; real 2025 top 150 max share 4.0% -> 3.3%; max share league 3.2% -> 3.1%, real 2016-2025 4.9% -> 4.8%; poor fit league / veterans / prospects / real 3.3 / 9.0 / 4.5 / 5.1% -> 3.2 / 8.6 / 4.2 / 4.9%; +-3 stability 80.1% -> 80.4%; 0 position conflicts. Expanded on: gold 46.0% -> 46.0% listed / 66.4% -> 67.3% role, churn 4.9% -> 5.0%, poor fit 2.9 / 7.7 / 4.0 / 5.3% -> 2.9 / 7.1 / 3.9 / 5.3%, +-3 74.6% -> 75.0%, top 150 max share 4.0% -> 4.0%. Still missed: Dort, Bane and Hartenstein (other ratings), Bridges, Luka (now a three-point build, not yet an engine).
- **arch14 (judged set, fitted model, families, merges, traits, catch-alls):** (1) **Judged set** (`judged.data.ts`): 400 players judged by hand (1-3 builds each, best first): real NBA 1992-2025 (12 seasons drawn by overall band: stars, starters, bench, fringe; the 2025 top 75 and 35 more 2025 players; gold players left out so gold stays independent), 100 random-league players and 60 draft prospects; 30% of each population is a fixed held-out test set (by a hash of the key). (2) **Fitted model** (`model.ts`, `fit.script.test.ts`, `RUN_ARCHETYPE_FIT=1`, `ARCH_EXPANDED=1`): every term of the score (profile match, bias, standouts, roles, promise penalties, ...) is now a weighted sum with per-class "promised skill" weights, family biases and a family classifier (log probability `gamma`, gate `tau`/`gate`) and a stats-decided close call (`tieMargin`), fitted by a ranking loss regularised toward the hand settings with 5-fold CV. Held-out result: the fully fitted model did **not** beat the hand weights (test top-1 79.2% -> 73.3%, top-3 93.3% -> 94.2%, family 86.7% -> 92.5%; expanded 64.2% -> 60.0%), so `fitted.data.ts`/`fittedExpanded.data.ts` keep the hand weights for every reading term and take only the trait weights from the fit (the full fitted weights are in the file's header). (3) **Families** (`families.ts`): ten (playmaker, scorer/creator, shooter, slasher/athlete, perimeter defender, rim protector, rebounder, stretch big, post/interior big, all-around), from each name's head noun and the size of its holders; family agreement with the judge on the test set 86.7%. (4) **Merges** (`merges.data.ts`): 34 near-duplicate pairs (same promises; profile cosine 0.75+ or top-two ties for a quarter of their holders) merged, 375 -> 341 builds; the removed names are aliases of the kept build (search); the kept build keeps its own positions and height gate (taking the union cost gold role fit 4 points); stored labels with a removed id read as the kept one; `ARCHETYPE_READING_VERSION` 12. (5) **Name promises** (`score.ts promiseChecks`, `namePromise.script.test.ts`, a floor in `archetypes.test.ts`): every promised class at least ordinary on the blended scale, an elite three (0.8) for sniper names; 243 of 256 builds with 10+ holders keep every promise for 90%+ of them (98.5% of holders weighted); exceptions (79-89%): Backline Masher, Backline Middy Creator, Shot-Erasing Shot Creator (rimD), Catch & Shoot 3PT Sniper (elite), 2-Way Inside-Out Combo Guard, 2-Way Mid-Range Slasher, 2-Way 3PT Slasher, 2-Way Mid-Paint Scorer (def), Board-Hunting Bucket Getter (score), Physical Mid-Paint Shot Eraser, Shot-Erasing Skilled Popper, Rim-Reaping Diming High Flyer (rimD), 2-Way Inside-Out Scorer (shoot). (6) **Stats**: up to three seasons (`multiSeasonStats`, decay 1 / 0.5 / 0.25) when the last season has under 1,500 minutes (a full last season reads alone: reading veterans from three seasons always cost gold role fit, Curry and Gobert among the misses); stats weight `STATS_W_MORE` 0.05 more from 1,500 to 4,000 minutes; shot zones come from the league's own stat references as before. (7) **Traits** (user request): nine style groups (`TRAIT_GROUPS`: off-ball -> movement shooters, screen -> pick-and-roll/pop and rim runners, handle -> creators, defensive versatility -> switch/lockdown, hustle+motor -> glass/hustle, shot discipline -> shooters, clutch+late-game IQ+big game -> shot-makers, leadership+late-game IQ -> floor generals, durability+recovery+work ethic -> motor/iron-man flavors) each add a fitted weight (about 0.25-0.35 per unit of 25 above 50) to the builds whose names match; hidden traits are read too (the user accepted that a label may hint at them; `archetypeTraits` now passes every trait, the trait-flavored labels still read only visible ones); with Player Traits off the terms are 0 and the reading is unchanged. (8) **Catch-alls** (user request, `CATCH_ALL`): Versatile Scorer needs rim, mid-range and three each 0.4+ and creation (handle or usage z) 0.4+, 3 per z short; also floors for 3-Level Scorer, Crafty 3-Level Scorer, (Crafty) Walking Bucket, Mid-Range Slasher, Versatile Paint Finisher and Mid-Range High Flyer. Versatile Scorer league 1.03% -> 0.00%, real 2016-2025 1.51% -> 0.14% (before: Jae Crowder, Raymond Felton, Bilal Coulibaly, Luol Deng...); 3-Level Scorer real 4.77% -> 3.32%; Crafty Walking Bucket 2.26% -> 1.03%. Metrics before (master) -> after, expanded off: gold listed 67.3% -> 62.8%, role 79.6% -> 77.0% (gold lists are read through the merge map); churn 5.1% -> 5.1%; max share league 3.0% -> 3.3%, real 4.8% -> 3.7%, prospects 5.4% -> 6.0%; real 2025 top 150 3.3% -> 4.0%; poor fit league / veterans / prospects / real 3.4 / 7.9 / 4.0 / 4.9% -> 3.6 / 8.6 / 4.4 / 5.2%; +-3 stability 80.7% -> 81.2%; 0 position conflicts. Expanded on: gold 46.0% -> 46.0% listed, role 67.3% -> 66.4%, churn 4.5% -> 4.3%, +-3 74.8% -> 75.3%. Recalibrating the norms after the merges was tried and not kept (gold role 74.3% -> 71.7%). Pinned real players changed (archetypes.test.ts): LeBron 2025 Mid-Paint Bully, Luka 2025 Board-Hunting Middy Slasher, Capela and Adams 2025 rebounding/rim-protecting bigs.
- **arch15 (sub-ratings step 4: archetypes on sub-ratings and play types, sharper build list, new builds, names by position, re-tune):** (1) **Sub-ratings in the reading** (`subStyle.ts`, with Sub-Ratings on): each sub's lean is (sub − base − mean) / sd against the league's own spread (`norms.sub`, built in `getLeagueNorms` from the league's players; generated shapes have an sd of 3-10 points, real players' stats-derived shapes 1-2.5, so each is read against the league it is in; built-in tables from `subLeans.script.test.ts`). Seventeen features: blocks, interior D, perimeter D, steals, range, catch-and-shoot vs off the dribble (C&S 3 + spot-up − off-dribble 3 − pull-up), post moves, close shots, touch, contact vs finesse, handle, ball security, shot creation, vision vs accuracy, straight-line vs lateral, max vertical vs standing reach, offensive vs defensive rebounding. Each build wants some of them from its name's concepts (`buildWants`: "Eraser"/"Anchor" blocks, "Lockdown" perimeter D and lateral quickness, "Ball-Hawking" steals, "Sniper"/"Logo" range, "Catch & Shoot" the C&S lean, "Iso"/"Shot Creator" shot creation and the off-the-dribble lean, "Post" post moves, "Physical"/"Bully" contact, "Crafty" finesse and touch, "High-Flying" max vertical, "Rim Runner" straight-line speed, "Playmaker" vision, "Putback"/"Crash" offensive rebounding, ...); the new `sub` term is the normalised dot product (fitted weight 0.17). The leans also move the skill classes the name promises are checked on (`SUB_CV_K` 0.15 per z: blocks/interior D the rim-protection class, perimeter D/steals the perimeter class, post moves, range, handle/shot creation), and the family classifier reads eleven of them. Prospects' subs are read as scouted (`fuzzSubs`). (2) **Play types** (`ptFeatures`, with Advanced Tracking Stats on and real minutes): each play type's share of his possessions (iso, P&R handler, spot-up, post-up, cut, roll man, transition, putback, off screen + handoff), plus a little of his PPP where he runs it as often as most, as a z against **his position bucket in the same league** (`buildPtNorms` from the league's last season: the sim's mix is not the NBA's, post-ups about 3x and P&R ball handlers about half, so they are never compared with real frequencies), weighted by his sample (possessions / (possessions + 300)), over the same seasons as his box score (`combinePlayTypes`); the `playType` term (weight 0.6) is the same dot product with each build's play-type wants. Measured on a 3-season league with the new-league defaults (`longRun.script.test.ts` `LONG_RUN_DUMP`, `playTypes.script.test.ts`): play types change 10.9% of labels (8.7% to another family: post-up-heavy bigs to post builds, roll men to rim runners, spot-up wings to shooters), name promises kept 99.8% either way. The judged set has no play types (real stats have none), so the weight is the hand setting. (3) **Sharper build list** (`merges.data.ts MERGED_ARCH15`, `mergeCand.script.test.ts`; arch15 scratchpad `merges.md`): 145 more merges (same family and core promises with profile cosine 0.6-0.7+ and sub-want cosine 0.6+, or the reading could not tell them apart), 346 -> 202 builds. Unlike arch14's, a merged build keeps its own profile, positions and height gate as one of the kept build's profiles (`SCORING_ARCHETYPES`, `out`): a build is read as its best profile, so a merge never sends a player to whatever came next (the arch14 way cost 4 points of test top-1 here). The kept build is the one whose name promises least (every holder keeps its promises); it is open at the union of positions; a kept build is at most about 2.2% of the players. Stored labels with a removed id read as the kept one; `ARCHETYPE_READING_VERSION` 13. (4) **New builds** (gap analysis `gaps.script.test.ts` over a random league, veterans, prospects and real players with subs; scratchpad `new-archetypes.md`): 3 & D Specialist (3 & D Guard at guard; KCP, Dort, Jae Crowder, Wesley Matthews), Shot-Erasing Guard Dog (Shot-Erasing Lockdown Wing; stoppers who block shots, many veterans), High-Flying Stretch Big (Mo Bamba, Bender), Blow-By Rim Attacker (athletic guards with no jumper), High-Flying Faceup Big. (5) **Names by position** (`posNames.ts`, `posNames.data.ts`, scratchpad `position-names.md`): one id, a display name per position group (guard PG/G/SG, wing GF/SF/F, big PF/FC/C) that follows his current position: Slashing Playmaker / Downhill Point Forward, Lockdown Eraser / Help-Side Eraser, The Guard Dog / Eliminator, Rim-Reaping Rim Runner / Shot-Erasing Wing Runner, Edge Enforcer / Edge-Enforcing Switch Big, ... (16 builds); where a merged build's own name does not fit a position it is now open at, another of its names that does is shown. Search finds every name (`TWO_K_NAMES`); metrics count one build. (6) **Re-tune:** the arch14 norms are kept (a full recalibration cost 8 points of test top-1); the new builds' tables come from the calibration; a share trim (`shareTrim.script.test.ts`) lowers only builds above 2.7% of a league, 6% of veterans or 5.5% of prospects; the star trim re-run with targets for 202 builds (3% / 3.5% a season); seeds rebuilt. Seven judged players re-judged with their sub-ratings where a build is clearly right (two in the test split; scratchpad `rejudged.md`). The fitted model is now shipped whole (`fitted.data.ts`: CV-chosen, family classifier with sub-rating features, gate 0.25 at 0.65, close calls by stats), since with subs it matches the hand weights' test top-1 within noise and beats them on top-3, family and loss; expanded: fitted too (tune top-1 60.8 -> 64.0%, test 69.2 -> 66.7%). Off states: with Sub-Ratings off the features are absent, the sub term 0 and the classes unchanged; with tracking stats off no play types; with Player Traits off the trait terms are 0 (`ARCH_NO_SUBS=1` on the scripts measures the reading without subs). **Before (arch14) -> after (arch15), expanded off:** judged test top-1 79.2% -> 78.3% (77.9% without the two re-judged test players; without subs 76.7%), top-3 93.3% -> 95.0%, family 86.7% -> 91.7%; tune top-1 69.7% -> 70.0%; gold listed 62.8% -> 59.3%, role 77.0% -> 77.0%; churn 5.1% -> 5.7%; max share league 3.3% -> 3.1%, real 2016-2025 3.7% -> 3.6%, prospects 6.0% -> 5.8%, real 2025 top 150 4.0% -> 5.3% (Inside-Out Slasher); poor fit league / veterans / prospects / real 3.6 / 8.6 / 4.4 / 5.2% -> 3.8 / 7.8 / 3.7 / 5.5%; +-3 / +-6 stability 81.2 / 66.7% -> 83.8 / 70.8%; effective builds league 200 -> 126 (192 of 202 used), real 156 -> 100; name promises: 159 of 161 builds with 10+ holders keep every promise for 90%+ (Shot-Erasing Shot Creator 73% rim D, Catch & Shoot 3PT Sniper 87% elite), 99.1% of holders weighted. Expanded on: judged test top-1 64.2% -> 67.5%, gold listed 46.0% -> 44.2%, role 66.4% -> 63.7%, churn 4.3% -> 4.2%, +-3 75.3% -> 76.2%, real top 150 4.0% -> 4.0%. Targets not reached: test top-1 85% / top-3 96% / family 93%, gold role 82%, expanded role 75%, real top-150 4%. Pinned real players re-pinned (archetypes.test.ts): LeBron Mid-Paint Point Four, Giannis Crashing Mid-Paint Scorer, Durant Crafty Mid-Paint Scorer, Draymond Shot-Erasing Board Enforcer, Klay Catch & Shoot 3PT Sniper, Capela Physical Defensive Rebounder, Korver 2021 Deep-Range Sniper, Westbrook Board-Hunting Middy Slasher, Luka Diming 3-Level Scorer.
- **arch16 (no catch-all defaulting, a why from the real drivers):** user report: "archetypes seem like they are defaulting to Versatile Paint Finisher, Versatile Scorer, etc." **Measured where the user sees labels** (`userShares.script.test.ts`, `RUN_ARCHETYPE_USERSHARES=1`, over league dumps from `longRun.script.test.ts LONG_RUN_ARCH_DUMP` (new-league defaults, at creation and after 3 seasons) and the real 2025 ratings; the reading there matches the stored labels for 94.8% at creation). The generic builds (`generic.ts GENERIC_BUILDS`: Versatile Scorer / Paint Finisher / Paint Bully / -Scoring Cleaner, (Crafty / Gritty / Physical) 3-Level Scorer, (Crafty) Walking Bucket, (Crafty) Inside-Out Scorer, Inside-Out Slasher, Mid-Paint Bully, Crafty Mid-Paint Scorer, Mid-Range Menace, Mid-Range Slasher, Slashing Dual Finisher) held 13% of a new random league, 12% of its prospects, 14.5% after 3 seasons and 24% of the real 2025 players (21% of the top 150; Inside-Out Slasher 5.3%). **Causes:** (1) arch15 merges kept the name that promises least, so a generic name collected the profiles of up to eight specific builds (Versatile Paint Finisher: Drive & Rise, 2-Way Dual Finisher, Gritty Power Slasher, 2-Way Blow-By Ace, ...); (2) the arch14 catch-all floors (`CATCH_ALL`) looked up the profile id, so those merged profiles were read with no floor; (3) the generic penalty (`genericPenalty`) checked a `generic` tag no build had; (4) the share and star trims then pushed players off specific builds into the untrimmed generic ones; (5) real players' mid-range class is high for most stars (fg rating), so "Mid-Paint"/"Mid-Range" names fit nearly every star. **Fixes:** (1) every generic build has all-around floors on the blended scale (`CATCH_ALL`, 3 per z short), applied to every profile merged into it; Versatile Paint Finisher needs finishing 0.5 and athleticism 0.2, 3-Level names three 0.3 / mid 0.3 / finish 0.2, ...; (2) the generic penalty works (`isGenericBuild`), and every generic profile carries a bias of -0.3 (`GENERIC_BIAS`), the star trim targets generics at 1% of the real top 150 (1.5% a season; only generic and re-picked builds re-trimmed, the others keep arch15's), the share trim caps them at 1.5%; (3) when a generic build is still his best, `classify` shows the best specific build of the same family within 1.5 z that he lives up to (no promise or catch-all shortfall, not a poor fit), his previous one first, with `fitNote` (data only); (4) four merges' kept names re-picked to the most representative specific name (`MERGED_ARCH15`, the old name an alias for search, stored labels migrate through `mergedId`, `ARCHETYPE_READING_VERSION` 14): Versatile Paint Finisher -> **Drive & Rise**, Slashing Dual Finisher -> **Blow-By Dual Finisher**, Versatile-Scoring Cleaner -> **Board-Hunting Bucket Getter**, Rim-Reaping Poster Dunker -> **Backline Lob Threat**; a merged profile's holders now also have to keep the shown name's promises; (5) a mid-range name needs some jumper (`MID_SHOT_FLOOR`: three class -1 z or better). **Spot fixes:** Giannis Crafty Paint-Finishing Cleaner (not mid-range), Gobert Backline Lob Threat (rim protector / lob big), Shot-Erasing Mid-Paint Maestro needs rim protection 0.6, finishing 0.5 and mid-range 0.5 and a -0.4 hand bias (`SPOT_BIAS`): Wembanyama keeps it, Mobley Backline Mid-Range Scorer, Kawhi Ball-Hawking Mid-Paint Scorer; Inside-Out Slasher 5.3% -> under 1% of the real top 150. **Generic share before -> after** (largest generic build in brackets): new random league all 13.0% (1.9) -> 2.6% (0.6), top 150 5.3% (2.0) -> 0.7% (0.7), rotation 8.8% (1.7) -> 0.4% (0.4), prospects 12.0% (2.4) -> 3.3% (0.6); after 3 seasons all 14.5% (2.1) -> 3.6% (1.3), top 150 6.7% (1.3) -> 1.3% (1.3), prospects 10.8% (2.7) -> 4.1% (1.0); real 2025 all 24.3% (4.6) -> 9.3% (2.0), top 150 20.7% (5.3) -> 6.7% (1.3), top 240 21.7% (5.0) -> 5.8% (1.3). **Metrics arch15 -> arch16** (expanded off): judged test top-1 78.3% -> 77.5% (one test player judged to two generic builds: Ryan Anderson 2016), top-3 95.0% -> 95.8%, family 91.7% -> 90.8%; tune top-1 70.0% -> 69.0%; gold listed 59.3% -> 56.6%, role 77.0% -> 78.8%; churn 5.7% -> 6.1%; +-3 / +-6 stability 83.8 / 70.8% -> 84.7 / 71.3%; 0 hard position conflicts; name promises 156 of 160 builds with 10+ holders keep every promise for 90%+ (98.8% of holders weighted). Pinned: Giannis Crafty Paint-Finishing Cleaner, Westbrook Slashing Playmaker (`archetypes.test.ts`, plus an arch16 spot test). Spot check (top 50 random, top 75 real 2025, famous players): `spot16.script.test.ts`. **Why line** (user request, `whyText.ts`; reworded in arch2kfix, below): the popover's why cites what drove the pick: with Sub-Ratings on, the subs the build's name wants, else the base ratings behind the profile's strongest dimensions; with Advanced Tracking Stats and minutes, the wanted play type he runs most; a visible trait where its group counted. **Speed:** the worker's classify memo (page-hang hotfix) keys on the reading version too; scoring caches each profile's static parts and the model's weights as arrays and skips the match for builds his position rules out: about 1.7 -> 1.6 ms per player at identical results (best of 6 over 1,030 players of a new league).
- **arch17 (re-judged answer key with sub-ratings and play types in view; model kept):** the arch14 key was judged from base ratings only. **Re-judge** (`rejudge.script.test.ts`, `RUN_ARCHETYPE_REJUDGE=1`): every judged player's full profile (base ratings, skill classes, sub-rating leans against the league's own table, last season's box score and, for the simulated league, his play-type mix against his position's peers; the reading's pick never shown) for the 400 players of `judged.data.ts` plus 149 players of a simulated league 3 seasons in with Advanced Tracking Stats on (`judgedSim.data.ts`, sampled across 5 position buckets x 3 levels; their reading, box score and play types as the game makes them, `SIM_SUB_NORMS` that league's sub table; 45 in the test split by hash). Two judging passes in different hash orders, each written to disk before comparing (`tools/arch17/pickA.txt`, `pickB.txt`): they agree on at least one build for all 549 and on the first pick for 97.6%, but both passes are the same judge, so that is not an independent noise ceiling. The honest ceiling is the arch14 judge vs the arch17 judge on the 400 shared players: **55.5%** share at least one build, 30.5% have the new first pick among the old ones. New ids: the builds both passes accept first, then the rest (max 3). `fit.script.test.ts` reads the simulated players with their own sub table and reading (population `sim`). **Re-tune result (test split, n=160 scoreable):** the shipped arch16 model scores top-1 23.8%, top-3 40.6%, family 66.9% on the new key; a full refit (`tools/arch17/fit-report.txt`) reaches top-1 26.9%, top-3 49.4%, family 75.6% (fitted weights: `sub` 0.125, `playType` 0.704, `match` 0.35, `need` 0.02).
