Such a player still gets the nearest build (the popover says so); the diagnostics (`ARCH_DIAG_GAPS=file`) cluster poor fits by position, height band and standout skills and propose a new archetype (or an existing build to open to that position) per cluster, for the user to choose from.
- **Gold set and metrics (arch10):** `gold.data.ts` holds 113 real players across eras and roles (1947-2025 ratings in `data/`), each with a role spec (what an acceptable build must promise) and the 1-4 closest builds his position allows that fit it. `metrics.script.test.ts` (`RUN_ARCHETYPE_METRICS=1`, `ARCH_EXPANDED=1`, `ARCH_SPOT=file` for the top-50 spot check) reports the gold set's hit rate, churn, share spread, poor fit per population and stability under a uniform rating shift; `archetypes.test.ts` asserts floors. arch9 -> arch10 (random league / real NBA 2016-2025 with stats; expanded ratings off): gold set 43% -> 54% of players get one of their listed builds, 56% -> 67% a build that fits their role (the floor in the test is 60% / 40%: this fork's real ratings often contradict a player's real-life role, mostly perimeter defenders and players before 2000, whose ratings show no defensive or role signal, so the asked-for 85% was not reached); churn 12.2% -> 6.1% of seasons (diag: 11.5% -> 5.6%); max share 2.2% -> 3.6% / 4.8% -> 4.6%, effective builds (exp entropy) 244 -> 232 / 174 -> 167; draftable prospects' max share 20.2% -> 5.9% (effective builds 19 -> 74); poor fit league 6.5% -> 3.2%, veterans 21.7% -> 7.7%, draftable prospects 5.4% -> 4.0%, real 2.0% -> 3.4%; same build after a uniform +-3 / +-6 shift of every rating but height 82% / 68% -> 81% / 66%; 0 hard position conflicts; level-relative audit mismatches 1.5% / 2.0% -> 3.2% / 2.8% (the blended reading names a few skills that stand out at his position or in his stats rather than in his own shape; raw undeveloped prospects read 20% on the league-relative audit since they are now read against prospect tables, draftable ones 4.5%). With expanded ratings: gold 42% -> 35% listed / 51% -> 57% role, churn 12.7% -> 4.9%, prospects' max share 20.9% -> 6.1%, veterans' poor fit 20.0% -> 7.9%, shift stability 78% / 60% -> 75% / 57%.
- **Calibration and diagnostics:** `calibrate.script.test.ts` (`RUN_ARCHETYPE_CALIBRATION=1`, `ARCH_EXPANDED=1` for the expanded tables) evens shares within each group with a small additive bias (-0.4..+0.25); `seeds.script.test.ts` rebuilds the regenerator's targets; `diag.script.test.ts` (`RUN_ARCHETYPE_DIAG=1`) audits each label against the ratings on the level-relative scale. arch8 -> arch9, random league / real NBA 2016-2025 (expanded off): labels 301 -> 346 (all 2K), hybrids 13.9% / 10.0% -> 0, level-relative audit mismatches 1.5% / 2.0% (the absolute-scale audit the old level-gated labels passed at 0.15% / 0.18% now flags 21% / 31%, by design: a low-level player with a shooter's shape is a shooter), descriptor agreement 54.2% -> 56.0% / 46.4% -> 60.5%, max share 2.9% -> 1.9% / 3.5% -> 4.8%, 0 hard position conflicts, poor fit 6.2% / 2.0% (veterans 20%, prospects 1.9%).
- **Stored per preseason:** the label is saved on the player (`p.archetypes`, one entry per season) when the preseason phase begins, after development, so it holds all season and his history of changes is kept (it travels in league files; only the current entry is shown, the per-season list is kept internally). New leagues get a first entry for everyone; older leagues and players without an entry fall back to a live reading (stored the first time the player page is opened). Prospects are always read live, since their ratings can be regenerated. With the switch off nothing is stored or computed.
- **Where:** the player page (_Archetype_ line with a why popover, replacing _Build_; no history is shown), Draft Scouting (_Archetype_ column), Advanced Player Search (_Archetype_ filter), the roster page (_Roster composition_ panel with balance warnings) and League → _Archetypes_ (`/l/:lid/archetypes`: counts and best players).
- **Traits as scouted (sweep20):** a reading takes a player's traits as the user's scouts see them, which depends on the user's scouting level. That level was only loaded by `player.formatAll` (and only with Skill Badges on), so a reading made before any page had formatted players (the warm-up of the draft prospects after the league loads or the phase changes, the labels stored at a phase change, a page reached before any player table) read the traits with no level (the default level's uncertainty) and could give another label than the same reading a moment later; and since the trait estimates are part of the classify memo's key, the warm-up's results were never reused, so the first visit to Draft Scouting still took about 1.2 s headless. `getLeagueNorms`, which every reading calls first, now loads the level. Headless, a new league's first Draft Scouting load after the warm-up: 1208 ms -> 200 ms. Changing League Extras or God Mode settings now also restarts the warm-up (the norms are keyed on some of them). Test: `archetype.test.ts` ("sweep20: the traits a reading uses ...").
- **Regenerate ratings:** prospects can be aimed at an archetype (per prospect, or a class pool). It uses the same solver, so overall, potential and height stay exact; the preview shows the archetype the new ratings actually derive to (a check mark when it is the one asked for). The 447 builds are still available under _Advanced: build_.
- **Known limits:** a draft prospect's archetype uses scouting-fuzzed ratings (like skills and positions), so it can differ slightly from the true one (everyone else is read from true ratings, see Scouting fuzz); aiming at a very specific archetype succeeds about three to four times in five at a given overall (the class archetype pool only aims a prospect at archetypes his position allows). With the switch off, the Advanced Player Search still lists an (empty) Archetype column.

### Improved positions

- **What:** basketball positions (PG, G, SG, GF, SF, F, PF, FC, C) by **size band first, then role within it** (pos2; replaced the two-axis size/ball-skills model). Three readings of a player:
  - **Length** (inches): height (`66 + 0.27 * hgt`) plus 26% of the wingspan-minus-height gap (expanded ratings; none without), nudged by build: strength, rebounding (defensive 0.7, offensive 0.3; `reb` without expanded ratings), interior defense (`diq` without) and inside scoring push up, speed and perimeter defense down. Strength and rebounding weigh about a fifth of what they used to, so strong or rebounding guards stay guards.
  - **Ball skills**: passing, dribbling, three, speed, offensive IQ and perimeter defense, minus half his overall level (stars are good at everything), as a percentile of generated players.
  - **Playmaking** ("who runs the offense"): with minutes, assist% + usage%/10 of this and last regular season (by minutes; percentile of real NBA players with 500+ minutes), weighted up to 82% at 1,500 minutes and scaled down below; without minutes, passing, dribbling, offensive IQ and ball security (`drb` without expanded ratings), minus half his level.
  - **Band**: length minus 5.2 inches per unit of playmaking above the median: guard below 76.7, wing to 82.3, big above, with hard limits on height (under 6'4" never a big, 7'0" or taller never a guard). So a 6'6" primary ball handler is a guard, a 6'9"-6'11" point forward a wing or an F, not an FC.
  - **Role**: guards PG / G / SG, wings SG / GF / SF / F, bigs F / PF / FC / C (SG and F sit in two bands): the nearest centroid in (length, playmaking, ball skills), with a small per-position bias. Centroids, cutoffs and weights were fitted on the real players' listed positions (`bios.pos`, 2000-2015) and checked on 2016-2025 and on the 2025 stars. Everything is on fixed scales (inches and fixed percentile tables), so positions do not drift with the league.
  - Code: `positionRaw`/`positionAxes`/`sizeBand`/`improvedPosition`/`positionFor` in `src/common/archetypes/position.ts`; the worker passes his stats rows and last season's ratings (`pos.basketball.ts`, `develop.ts`, `augmentPartialPlayer.ts`). Diagnostics: `RUN_POSITION_FIT=1` on `positionFit.script.test.ts` (knots, shares, churn; `FIT=1` refits the biases); the real-NBA check is a test with a floor (`position.test.ts`).
- **Before -> after (pos2):** real NBA listed positions 2016-2025 (8,000 player-seasons, with the previous season's stats as a real-players league reads them): exact 37.0% -> 43.4%, exact or adjacent (one step on PG, G, SG, GF, SF, F, PF, FC, C) 74.3% -> 82.7% (expanded ratings 82.2%; BBGM's formula 36.8% / 79.3%; all seasons 1947-2025 72.2% -> 78.3%). 2025 stars: SGA F -> G, Luka F -> GF, Cade F -> GF, LeBron FC -> F, Durant FC -> F, Jokic FC, Curry PG, Giannis FC, Draymond FC -> F, Haliburton PG, Tatum FC -> F, Banchero FC -> F, Booker GF -> PG, Edwards GF -> G, Wembanyama FC -> C. Shares in a random league (sticky careers, ovr >= 38; old formula / pos2): PG 14.3 / 18.1, G 6.8 / 6.4, SG 7.0 / 9.2, GF 12.1 / 10.3, SF 17.3 / 14.6, F 16.4 / 15.1, PF 9.2 / 10.8, FC 11.8 / 9.9, C 5.2 / 5.7 (a generated player with no stats reads a little more often as a point guard). With expanded ratings a few stars on a band edge move one step (SGA GF, Durant FC, Curry G).
- **arch13 (fewer ratings-only point guards):** read from ratings alone (no minutes yet: a random league at creation, draft prospects, rookies), point guard's bias is `RATINGS_ONLY_PG` 1.2 lower, faded out as his stats take over (`fromStats` on the axes, 0..1 with the playmaking stats' weight), so a player with a full season reads exactly as before. Random league shares (sticky careers, ovr >= 38): PG 18.1% -> 14.2% (old formula 14.3%), G 6.4% -> 10.3%, the rest unchanged; real NBA 2016-2025 exact or adjacent 82.5% -> 82.6% (exact 43.8% -> 43.1%); the 2025 stars unchanged (SGA G, Luka and Cade GF, LeBron and Durant F, Jokić FC, Curry and Haliburton PG). Kobe 2006 and Ray Allen 2001 still read as G: their assist and usage rates are those of Curry and SGA (Kobe 2005 26.5% / 30.5%, Curry 2024 27.1% / 31.2%, SGA 27.8% / 32.8%), so no stats rule moves them without moving the 2025 star targets; their builds are now scorers' (Archetypes, arch13).
- **SG generation (sgfix):** sweep19 found a random league had too few shooting guards (5-7% of the rostered players at creation, 9% in sticky careers, against 12.9% of real NBA player-seasons 2016-2025 listed SG), and that the position model was not the cause (on the real players with their stats it gives SG 12.5% against 14.4% listed; raising SG's bias moves SGA off his G star target). The cause was generation: BBGM's three player types (`genRatings.basketball.ts`) are point (passing and handle 1.5x, 90% of the players 6'3" and shorter), wing and big (27% of the 6'3"-6'9" players, so a 6'4" was more often a big than a guard). Generated guards were small playmakers or wings; real SGs (6'3"-6'7", mean 6'5") shoot and score first with a moderate handle and a wing's passing (real listed SG, ratings: pss 43 / drb 47 / tp 53 / spd 55; PG 57 / 58 / 48 / 58), a profile no type produced. Fix, with Improved Positions on (off: the same random draws and ratings, exactly): a **scorer** type (jmp/spd 1.45, dnk 1.3, drb 1.1, pss 0.9, ft/fg/tp 1.5, oiq 1.15, endu 1.3) drawn by height band (`SCORER_MIX`: 5% up to 6'1", 30% at 6'1"-6'3", 75% at 6'3"-6'6" with bigs 27% -> 2%, 30% at 6'6"-6'7"); so that the league's talent stays put, wings of 6'6"-6'9" and the point type up to 6'1" have their type factors 25% and 10% closer to 1. Measured with `sgShares.script.test.ts` (`RUN_SG_SHARES=1`; new-league defaults; creation over 8 leagues, careers: 10 seasons of the population model over 2 leagues, rostered player-seasons, ratings-only playmaking; draft: top 60 by value of every class about to be drafted) — see the table below. Real NBA listed shares 2016-2025: PG 12.1, G 11.7, SG 12.9, GF 14.0, SF 7.9, F 9.2, PF 8.8, FC 12.3, C 11.1. GF stayed low and SF/F high (the position model's biases, fitted to the old formula's shares; re-fitted in fixes4, below). Mean ovr of the rostered players at creation 51.75 -> 51.99 (ratingInflation's first season, seeds 7 and 1: 51.75 -> 51.81). The position model, its biases and the real-NBA accuracy are unchanged. With the full engine (games and stats, so playmaking is read from assist and usage rates; one league, 10 seasons, rostered player-seasons): SG 5.1% -> 11.0%, PG 16.3% -> 14.1%, G 5.9% -> 8.2%. Archetypes (`metrics.script.test.ts` now samples the random league as a new league generates it, `ARCH_STOCK_GEN=1` for BBGM's types; norms unchanged), stock generation -> scorer type: gold 67.3% listed / 79.6% role -> the same, churn 5.6% -> 5.1%, max share league 3.1% -> 3.0%, prospects 6.6% -> 5.4%, real 4.8% (unchanged), poor fit league 3.2% -> 3.4% (within noise), veterans 8.6% -> 7.9%, prospects 4.2% -> 4.0%, real 4.9% (unchanged), effective builds league 227 -> 216. Recalibrating the norms on the new sample (`calibrate.script.test.ts`) was tried and not kept: gold 67.3% -> 56.6% and prospects' max share 5.4% -> 10.9% (left to the archetype rebalance).

  | Shares (%)                    | PG           | G          | SG          | GF          | SF           | F            | PF          | FC           | C          | Draft top 60 at 6'3" or shorter |
  | ----------------------------- | ------------ | ---------- | ----------- | ----------- | ------------ | ------------ | ----------- | ------------ | ---------- | ------------------------------- |
  | Real NBA listed 2016-2025     | 12.1         | 11.7       | 12.9        | 14.0        | 7.9          | 9.2          | 8.8         | 12.3         | 11.1       | about 16                        |
  | Creation, all players         | 15.0 -> 13.0 | 8.8 -> 9.2 | 6.5 -> 14.6 | 9.9 -> 7.5  | 15.3 -> 12.8 | 15.9 -> 15.9 | 8.1 -> 7.6  | 11.3 -> 10.4 | 9.3 -> 9.0 | 18.5 -> 17.3 (8 classes)        |
  | Creation, rostered            | 15.4 -> 13.7 | 8.7 -> 9.3 | 5.5 -> 12.7 | 10.2 -> 8.1 | 15.2 -> 13.1 | 16.3 -> 15.5 | 7.1 -> 7.0  | 11.9 -> 10.9 | 9.7 -> 9.8 |                                 |
  | Careers, 10 seasons, rostered | 14.1 -> 13.2 | 8.2 -> 9.1 | 5.6 -> 12.9 | 10.1 -> 7.0 | 13.9 -> 12.5 | 16.4 -> 16.0 | 10.8 -> 8.8 | 11.5 -> 10.6 | 9.4 -> 9.9 | 17.6 -> 17.3 (22 classes)       |

- **GF/SF/F re-fit (fixes4):** after the SG generation change GF was 7% of a random league and SF/F 13%/16% (the biases had been fitted to the old formula's shares). `POSITION_BIAS` and the guard band cutoff (`GUARD_MAX` 76.726 -> 77.33 inches; the wing/big cutoff unchanged) were re-fitted on the axes of random-league player-seasons against the real listed shares, with the real-NBA accuracy, the 2025 star targets and the unit-test fixtures as hard constraints; centroids and weights unchanged. Rostered player-seasons (population model, 2 leagues x 8 seasons, sticky), before -> after: PG 13.2 -> 12.2, G 9.1 -> 9.1, SG 12.9 -> 12.8, GF 7.0 -> 14.6, SF 12.6 -> 8.9, F 16.3 -> 11.3, PF 8.7 -> 10.0, FC 10.5 -> 11.2, C 9.8 -> 9.9 (real 12.1 / 11.7 / 12.9 / 14.0 / 7.9 / 9.2 / 8.8 / 12.3 / 11.1). Real NBA 2016-2025 exact or adjacent 82.8% -> 83.0% (exact 42.6% -> 42.6%); the 2025 stars keep their targets. G stays a little low: raising it moves Curry or Haliburton off PG. Archetypes read position as a prior, so wings that moved SF/F -> GF can change build.
- **Shares over a league's seasons (sgdrift):** sweep20 found the rostered shares drifting in a new league (SG 13.1% -> 8.1%, GF 13.6% -> 17.2%, PG 12.1% -> 15.3% in 3 seasons). Measured with the full engine (`sgShares.script.test.ts`, `SG_FULL=1`: 2 leagues x 10 seasons, new-league defaults, which now also prints each season's shares, the players who came in through the draft, rookies, playmaking read with and without stats by position and the league's assist% + usage%/10 quantiles): creation was right, but a guard's playmaking read from his stats sat about 0.1 above his ratings-only reading (SG 0.35 / 0.23, G 0.64 / 0.51) and the league's assist% + usage%/10 ran above the real NBA's (median 15.0 against 13.2), and once stats replace ratings the ratings-only PG bias fades out. The league also has more small guards than the NBA (8.9% of its players under 6'2" against 5.5%, 14% at 6'4"-6'6" against 21%), which the ratings-only reading hid. Players who came in through the draft settled at about PG 16, G 6, SG 12, GF 14, and with stickiness a league drifts to what its draftees read as. Fix (Improved Positions on; off is untouched):
  - **League-relative playmaking stats:** the stats part of playmaking is a percentile against the league's own quantiles of assist% + usage%/10 (active players with 500+ minutes over this and last season, worked out once a season the first time positions are assigned, `player/playStatKnots.ts`; the real NBA's table below 100 such players), so a league whose game makes more assists or usage than the real one doesn't move everyone toward point guard. A real-players league reads close to the real table.
  - **Re-fit** of `POSITION_BIAS` and the PG, G, SG and GF centroids (length and playmaking) on the axes of those full-engine leagues, aimed mostly at the players who came in through the draft, with the real-NBA accuracy, the 2025 star targets and the unit-test fixtures as constraints (`sgdriftFit.script.test.ts`, `SG_FIT_DUMP` from `SG_DUMP`), and `RATINGS_ONLY_PG` 1.2 -> 0.8 so a new league keeps its point guards (creation, 6 leagues: PG 12.1, G 12.2, SG 13.3, GF 14.3).
  - Rostered shares, 2 leagues x 10 seasons (average of the two), before -> after: season 1 PG 12.3 -> 11.4, G 10.4 -> 12.6, SG 12.9 -> 13.2, GF 13.6 -> 13.6; season 5 PG 15.6 -> 12.1, G 8.9 -> 12.6, SG 11.6 -> 11.7, GF 14.8 -> 14.3; season 10 PG 15.3 -> 12.7, G 7.2 -> 10.9, SG 9.9 -> 12.6, GF 17.2 -> 15.7, SF 7.6 -> 7.8, F 9.9 -> 8.2, PF 10.8 -> 10.3, FC 10.8 -> 13.1, C 11.3 -> 8.8; all 10 seasons PG 14.7 -> 12.6, G 8.8 -> 11.5, SG 11.1 -> 12.2, GF 15.2 -> 14.7, SF 8.1 -> 8.4, F 11.2 -> 8.4, PF 9.3 -> 8.8, FC 10.7 -> 13.7, C 10.9 -> 9.8 (real 12.1 / 11.7 / 12.9 / 14.0 / 7.9 / 9.2 / 8.8 / 12.3 / 11.1). A single league moves about 2 points either way from season to season. Real NBA 2016-2025 exact or adjacent 83.0% -> 82.6% (exact 42.6% -> 41.6%); the 2025 stars stay in their targets (SGA G -> PG). Archetypes (`metrics.script.test.ts`): gold 56.6% (role 78.8%) unchanged, churn 6.1% -> 5.9%, max share league 2.7% -> 2.6% / real 5.8% -> 6.1% / prospects 6.1% -> 5.9%, poor fit league 3.9% -> 4.1% / veterans 7.9% -> 7.5% / prospects 3.9% -> 4.4% / real 6.2% -> 5.6%. Two pinned real players read a new position and build: Korver 2021 SF -> GF (deep-range sniper -> crafty sharpshooter) and Westbrook 2025 G -> PG, his listed position (slashing playmaker -> mid-range slashing cleaner); Kobe 2006 and Jerry West 1966 read PG instead of G.
- **Stickiness:** when a player develops he keeps his current position unless the best one is closer by a margin (0.3) both on this season's reading and on the average of this and last season's (last season's ratings row and stats), so one season's change does not flip him. Position changes per player-season: generated careers 5.7% -> 2.0% (no stickiness 7.1%); real careers 2000-2025 with stats (real ratings are re-rated every season) 20.6% -> 10.7%.
- **When positions change:** for new players at generation, and when players develop (preseason, or a God Mode edit). Rows of earlier seasons are never rewritten. Custom string positions (`p.pos`) are untouched. As before, positions come from true ratings (not scouting fuzz).
- **Archetypes:** the stored position is the archetype prior (hard blocks unchanged: same nine labels); prospects' live labels and the ratings regenerator read the position the game would assign. A G now reads with the shooting guards (`POS_BUCKET`: a G is a combo guard who is not the primary ball handler). pos2 recalibrated the archetype tables (norms off and expanded, seeds, stat norms, star bias with `STAR_SEASON_MAX` 3.5% -> 3%) and added the reading's role-fitting pick to seven gold lists. Metrics, arch11 -> pos2, expanded off: gold listed 64.6% -> 65.5%, role 76.1% -> 72.6% (big scoring guards such as Kobe 2006, Ray Allen 2001 and Booker 2022 now read as G and get playmaking builds); churn 5.6% -> 5.6%; real 2025 top 150 max share 3.3% -> 4.0%; max share league 2.4% -> 3.2%, real 2016-2025 3.7% -> 4.7%; poor fit league 2.9% -> 3.3%, veterans 7.3% -> 9.5%, prospects 4.3% -> 4.7%, real 3.6% -> 5.1%; +-3 / +-6 stability 80% / 64% -> 80% / 65%; 0 position conflicts (diag). Expanded on: gold 46.9% -> 44.2% listed / 63.7% -> 61.9% role, churn 4.2% -> 4.8%, real 2025 top 150 max share 4.7% -> 4.0%.
- **Setting:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ on for new leagues, off for existing ones (off = the original formula, exactly). Turning it on in an existing league takes effect as players develop; mid-season, a player whose position is recomputed (e.g. a God Mode edit) may move to the new model before the preseason.

### Prospect variety

- **Where:** _A default part of how the game generates new basketball players (no setting)._ Every newly generated player (draft prospects, random players of a new league, random free agents); real players (and their random ratings in real-player draft classes) and players generated before this existed are never touched. Measured with the opt-in script `src/test/prospectVariety/variety.script.test.ts` (`RUN_PROSPECT_VARIETY=1`; `PV_CLASSES`, `PV_LEAGUE_SEEDS`, `PV_SEASONS`, `PV_CONFIG`, `PV_OUT`).
- **Why:** an audit of 20,000 generated prospects found few templates: one multiplier per rating group (athleticism, shooting, skill, inside) dominated a small per-rating noise, so free throws and threes correlated 0.90, speed and leaping 0.91; each multiplier was hard-capped at 1.2 (+1 sd, 16% of players on the cap); bigs who shoot, tall shooters and undersized interior players almost never happened; who declared early was nearly random; development had no one-skill developers and few busts.
- **Seeded:** every new random number comes from a stream of the prospect's own (`Rng`, seeded by his exact height and wingspan draws, which the stock generator already made), so the game's `Math.random` stream draws exactly as many numbers as before (a test counts them). Stored on the player: `p.genVar` (`{ v, s, c?, pre? }`: version, his variety seed, a ceiling multiplier, and until his class is saved the ages he developed at before the draft). Hidden from the UI.
- **1. Group factors:** the stock group draw is kept as the shared part of each rating's factor, mixed with a subgroup draw and an own draw (shooting: shared and own; athleticism: speed/leaping/dunking vs strength vs endurance; skill: handle/passing, offensive/defensive IQ, rebounding). The parts are mixed with loadings (`RATING_GROUP`) and scaled back to the stock spread (`FACTOR_NORM` 1; wider spreads made more extreme shooters and non-shooters, which the spacing model turned into scoring); offensive/defensive IQ keep more of the shared part, since ovr weighs them most and selection onto rosters magnified their independent part. Above 1 the factor is squashed softly toward 1.45 (`1 + 0.45 tanh((x - 1) / 0.45)`) instead of clipped at 1.2, and each rating's factor is scaled so its mean is the stock one (mean ratings and ovr at generation don't move). Ratings of drafted-age prospects (80 classes): free throws/threes r 0.90 → 0.69, speed/leaping 0.91 → 0.67, handle/passing 0.86 → 0.66 (raw at generation: about 0.6-0.65).
- **2. Special prospects:** the special-prospect bonus (and the scrub penalty) no longer move the hgt rating, which stands for his listed height and wingspan (the random number for it is still drawn).
- **3. Declaring:** the random part of who declares each year is ±15 on top of pot (stock ±50, which swamped the spread in pot), so the best prospects tend to come out young; in the last year about 6% of those still in school stay one more (fifth-year seniors), so a class has a few 23-year-olds (with draft ages at least two years apart).
- **4. Specialist spikes:** 8% of new players get one rating (a third of them two) 12-20 points higher (threes, handle, passing, defense, rebounding, inside scoring, athleticism...), his other ratings moved down so his ovr (before rounding) is unchanged.
- **5. Sub-builds:** within each player type: bigs (stretch big 16%, rim protector 20%, playmaking big 12%), wings (3-and-D 22%, slasher 20%), point guards (pass-first 25%, scoring point 25%), scoring guards (shot maker 20%, defender 15%): multipliers on single ratings' factors (stretch big: threes ×1.65, free throws ×1.3, mid-range ×1.25, inside ×0.85...), divided by their average within the type so the type's mean ratings don't move. Plus an outlier slot (2%): one group ×1.5, another ×0.7 (bigs mostly go up in shooting or skill, small players in the inside game: shooting 7-footers, small rim protectors). Both are shapes, not talent: his ovr is put back to what it would have been without them.
- **6. Class strength:** each class made from scratch draws a talent shift for its top 15 prospects (by pot), N(0, 1.5) ovr capped at ±3.5, from the league and class year. With Draft Class Shapes, 35% of classes whose shape is about strength (deep, top-heavy, weak, generational, no clear No. 1, two franchise prospects, boom-or-bust) also get a composition shape (guards, bigs, wings, international, shooters, defenders, point guards, stretch bigs, two-way wings, international bigs, home-country spike), and the other way around; their plans are merged (ovr changes add up, nudges follow each other). Stored as `p.draftClassShape2`; Draft Scouting and Mock Draft show it under the first. A forced shape (tests) never gets a second one.
- **7. Origin and path:** regional tilts, scaled per player by a draw in [0.5, 1.5], with his ovr put back: Europe (shooting, passing, IQ up; leaping, speed, dunking down), Africa (+1.5 inches of ape index, athleticism up; IQ, passing, shooting down), Australia/New Zealand (IQ and passing up). American college seniors (the last draft age) get IQ polish (oiq +4, diq +3, passing and free throws +1, scaled the same way; ovr kept) and a lower ceiling (their development rates ×0.88).
- **8. Development:** a new player's yearly development has a rate per rating group (athleticism, shooting, skill, inside) from his seed: a shared persistent part (sd 0.07) plus each group's own (sd 0.14), clamped to [0.35, 1.65], symmetric around 0.99 (offensive/defensive IQ take half of it: IQ grows mostly with age). The center is a little under 1 because more spread in development raises the ovr of the players who make rosters (teams keep the best): on full-engine leagues 1 put rostered ovr about 0.2 above before, 0.98 about 0.6 below. It multiplies each rating's yearly gain through age 27, after the rating's limits (so a fast developer isn't clipped back and every rating's mean change is unchanged; never pot estimates, so it stays hidden). A real bust tail: young players' (23 and under) yearly noise draw is still bounded at -4, and its part between -4 and -7 is added to every rating after its limits, minus its mean (0.418), so no rating develops more or less on average. (Applied inside the limits, as a plain -7 bound with a recentering, it moved IQ up and athleticism down, because their limits clip different sides.) The hidden trajectory (aging curve) also acts on the seasons a prospect developed before the draft, once his class is saved and he has a pid (`processNewDraftClasses`), centered over the league at every age.
- **Extras:** heredity also passes on shooting and skill tendencies (how much his shooting, and his handle/passing/offensive IQ, stand out from the rest of his game; h 0.25, at most 6 points, ovr kept). Near-identical prospects in a class (same position, within 2 ovr and 4 hgt, centered-profile cosine 0.985+) get 5 points moved between two ratings (ovr kept). A light archetype-mix steer: the archetype engine's lead skill (v2 nouns, read-only, from base ratings) of the top 60 prospects by pot; when one lead skill is held by more than 20% of them, up to 6 of its holders get 4 points more of their second skill and 4 fewer of it (ovr kept).
- **Measured** (`newDefaults` settings; 80 generated classes at the draft, and full-engine leagues, 8 seeds × 5 seasons, seasons 3-5 pooled; before → after):
  - Variety: free throws/threes r 0.90 → 0.70, speed/leaping 0.91 → 0.67, handle/passing 0.87 → 0.67; mean profile cosine within guards/wings/bigs 0.61/0.49/0.54 → 0.52/0.39/0.43; bigs in the class's top 10% from three 0.6% → 1.9%, 6'11"+ 0.5% → 1.0%, 6'6"- in the top 10% inside 0.8% → 1.6%; class top-5 ovr SD 2.95 → 3.36; distinct archetype labels in a class's top 60: 39 → 43, top label's share 9.7% → 7.8%; development over 4 seasons after the draft: SD 9.4 → 10.8, share who gained nothing 21.7% (about the same; the best prospects now come in younger and grow more); top-10 prospects' mean age 19.8 → 19.5, about 0.5% 23-year-olds.
  - Draft classes: ovr mean 32.6 → 32.5, SD 8.3 → 7.2 (declaring by talent: the best come out younger, so a class's top 5 is about 3 ovr lower at the draft, 50.5 → 47.6), pot mean 54.0 → 54.0, SD 9.1 → 9.0.
  - League: ovr 49.9 → 50.0 (SD 9.9 → 10.1), pot 56.1 → 56.3 (SD 7.8 → 7.9), rookies' ovr 39.1 → 38.6; rostered positions within 1.5 points each (C 13.0% → 11.8%, G 11.0% → 12.3%, F 6.0% → 7.5%, SF 8.3% → 7.4%); 3PA rate 0.397 → 0.403. **Scoring is up:** points 111.1 → 113.4 a game and TS% 57.6 → 58.7 (seed SE about 0.4 points and 0.2 TS points), already in a new league's first season: specialists and shooters are used well by the sim (spacing, shot selection). No single part causes it (each switched off alone left most of it).
  - Tests: the class-shape balance test (weighted ovr 0.08 / 0.00, pot -0.17 / -0.22) and the rating inflation test pass. The game-sim mechanics tests (`simLiveGameForTests.ts`, `outcomeInvariance.test.ts`) generate with the stock draw (`withStockGeneration`, test-only), since they were calibrated on that population; `expandedRatingsOff.fixture.json` was re-recorded (intended generation change).
- **Engine:** `common/prospectVariety.ts` (tables, draws, rates; pure), `worker/core/player/genRatings.basketball.ts` (rating draw), `worker/core/player/prospectVariety.ts` (ovr-neutral reshaping, senior polish, near-duplicates, archetype steer, pre-draft trajectory), `worker/core/draft/genPlayersWithoutSaving.ts` (declaring, fifth-years, class steps), `worker/core/player/developSeason.basketball.ts` (rates, bust tail), `common/draftClassShape.ts` / `worker/core/draft/classShape.ts` (second shape), `worker/core/player/heredity.ts`; tests in `worker/core/player/prospectVariety.test.ts`.

### Sub-ratings

- **Setting:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ `subRatings` (League Extras → Players). On for new leagues, off for existing ones. Basketball only. Step 1 of the sub-ratings plan: the data model, generation, development, display and editing. Ovr, value and every other formula keep reading the base ratings, and so does the game sim unless Sub-Ratings in Sim (step 3, below) is on. Step 2 (below) derives real players' sub-ratings from their stats and has scouting, the combine, skill badges and college stats read them; a later step lets the archetypes (step 4) read them.
- **Data model:** ten base ratings are each the weighted average of 2-4 sub-ratings (25 in all). The sub-ratings of a ratings row are one int array, `r.sub`, in the fixed order of `SUB_DEFS` (`common/subRatings.ts`); the layout version (`SUB_RATINGS_VERSION`, 1), the player's seed, hidden growth profile and generation info are stored once per player in `p.subP`. The base ratings stay stored and always equal the weighted average of their subs (`round(sum w s / 100)`, integer arithmetic; `fitGroup` places integer subs so the rounded average is exactly the base), so everything that reads base ratings costs nothing extra and is unchanged. Height, strength, endurance, free throws and offensive IQ have no sub-ratings.

  | Base                 | Sub-ratings (weight %)                                                             |
  | -------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | Defense (`diq`)      | Shot Blocking `blk` 20, Steals `stl` 20, Interior D `idf` 30, Perimeter D `pdf` 30 |
  | Three-point (`tp`)   | Range `rng` 40, Catch & Shoot 3 `tcs` 35, Off-Dribble 3 `tod` 25                   |
  | Inside (`ins`)       | Post Moves `pst` 40, Close Shots `cls` 35, Touch / Floater `flt` 25                |
  | Dunks/layups (`dnk`) | Contact Finishing `con` 50, Finesse `fns` 50                                       |
  | Mid-range (`fg`)     | Pull-Up `pul` 50, Spot-Up `spu` 50                                                 |
  | Rebounding (`reb`)   | Offensive Reb `orb` 50, Defensive Reb `drd` 50                                     |
  | Dribbling (`drb`)    | Handle `hnd` 50, Ball Security `sec` 25, Shot Creation `crt` 25                    |
  | Passing (`pss`)      | Vision `vis` 50, Pass Accuracy `acc` 50                                            |
  | Speed (`spd`)        | Straight-Line Speed `sls` 50, Lateral Quickness `lat` 50                           |
  | Jumping (`jmp`)      | Standing Reach `stj` 50, Max Vertical `mxv` 50                                     |

- **Expanded ratings folded in:** with Expanded Ratings on, its ratings stay what the sim reads, and the sub-ratings that correspond to them follow them (`anchorToExpanded`): the two rebounding subs are `oreb`/`dreb` (so `reb` = their average, as before), the perimeter side of defense (perimeter D 60%, steals 40%) is `pdef` and the interior side (interior D 60%, blocks 40%) `idef` (so `diq` = their average, as before), the catch-and-shoot minus off-the-dribble lean of the jump shooting subs (`(tcs + spu) / 2 - (tod + pul) / 2`) is `csh - dsh`, and the Ball Security sub is `bsc`. Wingspan stays its own rating. On the player page the subs take the place of the split rows (Perimeter D/Interior D, C&S/OTD, Ball Security, Off/Def Rebounding); editing a sub in Customize Player moves the expanded rating it corresponds to.
- **Generation (fictional players):** base ratings are generated exactly as before, with the same random numbers (the sub-ratings use their own seeded stream: the seed is a hash of what was already drawn about the player, so not one extra number is drawn from the game's stream; `subRatingsLeague.test.ts` checks a class and 12 seasons of development are identical on and off). Then each base is split by a shape (`generateSubShape`, `common/subRatingsGen.ts`):
  - **Build first:** a target build is drawn from the ratings builds (`common/ratingsBuilds`, 446 builds (447 less Balanced) with their share of real draft classes and height gates), tilted toward the builds his ratings already look like (cosine of his ratings profile with the build's, `BUILD_FIT_K` 4), and its tags and name lean the shape (a Rim Protector to blocks, a Sharpshooter to catch-and-shoot and range, a Floor General to vision, a Post Scorer to post moves, ...).
  - **Body:** length and leaping lean to blocks, quickness to steals, perimeter D and lateral quickness, strength to post moves and contact finishing, small players to floaters and finesse, ball handlers to off-the-dribble shooting and pull-ups, wings and bigs to spot-ups (`physicalLean`).
  - **Signature and weakness:** 78% get a signature (+6 to +11 on a sub his build leans to), 72% a clear weakness (-6 to -11 on one it leans away from).
  - **Correlated noise:** hands (steals, ball security, accuracy, handle), touch (floater, finesse, catch-and-shoot, range, spot-up), motor (offensive rebounding, blocks, contact), IQ (vision, ball security, interior D, defensive rebounding), burst (vertical, speed, shot creation, lateral), plus independent noise (sd 2.5).
  - **Specialists:** 3.5% (`SPECIALIST_CHANCE`) get an exaggerated shape (build lean 1.9x, signature +14 to +20, always a weakness).
  - **Background** (from his age at the draft, country, college and prospect story): one-and-done (19 or younger) raw and athletic, senior (22+) polished with high IQ, international passing and shooting, G League / Overtime Elite (American, no college) a pro-ready creator; a prospect story of "project" adds rawness.
  - **Sanity limits:** blocks capped by height, length and leaping (`40 + 0.5 length + 0.25 jmp`), lateral quickness capped for the tallest (`100 - 1.3 (hgt - 58)`), post moves by size and strength; at most 2 generational shapes per class (a sub 20+ above its base, `GENERATIONAL_OFFSET`; the rest are scaled down, `assignClassSubs`). Smaller groups' offsets are scaled up (2 subs 1.5x, 3 subs 1.15x) since the fit to a base halves a lean in a group of two.
  - **When:** a generated player is marked with his seed (`markGenerated` in `player.generate`), gets a first shape at his first development, and a draft class gets its final shapes with its background and the class cap at the end of generation, after its class shape (Draft Class Shapes) and after the ratings regenerator (`subsAfterRewrite`).
- **Hidden growth profiles** (`p.subP.g`, never sent to the UI): normal (44%), early peaker (11%), late bloomer (13%, more with the late bloomer prospect story), one-skill grower (10%), athlete-learner (10%), finished product (10%), shares shifted by background (one-and-done more athlete-learners and early peakers, seniors more finished products, internationals more late bloomers).
- **Development:** one development roll per base, as before (unchanged, same random numbers). Each base's change is then spread over its subs (`developSubs`): growth goes more to athletic ("fade") subs before 23 and to positioning/range/IQ ("hold") subs after; a decline hits fade subs (blocks, steals, perimeter D, contact finishing, offensive rebounding, speed, lateral quickness, max vertical) 1.2x (1.6x from 28, 2x from 31) and hold subs (interior D, range, catch-and-shoot, touch, finesse, spot-up, defensive rebounding, ball security, vision, accuracy, post moves) 0.5-0.7x; from 28 fade subs also drift about 0.6-1 a year below the hold ones. Early peakers' athletic subs grow faster and fade from 26, late bloomers' hold subs keep growing (2x from 25), a one-skill grower's chosen sub takes 3x the growth, athlete-learners grow skills (1.6x) not athleticism (0.6x) until 25, finished products barely change shape. Plus a little noise (sd 0.7) and mean reversion (2% a year), from the player's own seeded stream (season and age), so shapes drift believably. Then the subs are fitted to the new bases (and the expanded ratings).
- **Everything that writes ratings keeps them in sync:** development (`develop`), God Mode / Customize Player (each base has its subs under it: editing a sub recomputes the base, editing a base moves its subs keeping the shape), injuries, bonuses and the mentor bonus (a base changed outside development moves its subs with it, `syncRowSubs`), the ratings regenerator and Regenerate Build, draft class generation, class shapes and imported draft classes and players (`augmentPartialPlayer`), real players (a shape from their real stats, step 2), expansion and fantasy drafts (ratings unchanged).
- **Existing leagues and files:** turning the setting on, or opening or creating a league with it on, fills in every active player's sub-ratings once (`updateSubRatingsForLeague`): a neutral shape (subs near their base) with a lean from his size and physicals, seeded by his pid, and a seeded growth profile. Bases are never changed. Turning it off ignores the stored subs (and nothing updates them); turning it back on refits them to the bases, keeping their shape. League files: optional `sub` (array of 25 numbers) in ratings rows and `subP` in players; files with and without them import (round trip tested).
- **History:** only a player's last 5 ratings rows keep their sub-ratings (`SUB_HISTORY`, trimmed at each development), so long-retired players' old seasons have bases only, and their pages show bases only.
- **UI:** player page (each base rating with sub-ratings shows ▸: click it to expand its subs with their change since last season, or "Show sub-ratings" for all, remembered), the ratings history on the player page, Player Ratings and Compare Players (an optional "Show sub-ratings" view / section, remembered per table), Draft Scouting (each prospect's standout and weakest sub relative to its base, with scouting fuzz), Customize Player (God Mode editing), the player export (CSV/PDF: a "Sub-ratings by season" table; profile JSON: `sub` by name in the ratings history). Scouting fuzz is applied to the subs like the bases; the no visible ratings challenge hides them with the bases.
- **Code:** `common/subRatings.ts` (definitions, weights, fitting, edits; shared with the UI), `common/subRatingsGen.ts` (generation, neutral derivation, development; worker), `worker/core/player/subRatings.ts` (hooks: `ensureSubs`, `assignClassSubs`, `developRowSubs`, `pruneSubHistory`), `worker/core/player/updateSubRatingsForLeague.ts`, `ui/components/SubRatingsToggle.tsx`. Tests: `common/subRatings.test.ts`, `worker/core/player/subRatingsLeague.test.ts`; distributions: `SUB_DIST=1` on `worker/core/player/subRatingsDist.script.test.ts`.
- **Measured** (step 1):
  - **Bases unchanged:** with the setting on, generation and development draw exactly the same random numbers, so every base rating, ovr, pot, value and game is identical to the setting off: a draft class plus 12 seasons of development (with and without expanded ratings) in `subRatingsLeague.test.ts`, and a 10-season headless league (`longRun.script.test.ts`, new-league defaults, seed 1) with identical league averages (pts, fg%, 3p%, trb, tov, stl, blk, ast), mean and top-10 ovr every season; the sim benchmark's result hash is the same. The archetype metrics (`metrics.script.test.ts`, with `ARCH_SUBS=1`) are identical (archetypes don't read the subs yet).
  - **Performance** (CPU, on vs off): 10-season long run, sim 370.7 s vs 362.1 s (+2.4%, but the machine-load calibration was +1.7%: about +0.6% normalized; the sim never reads the subs), offseason 154.5 s vs 152.0 s (+1.6%); sim benchmark (one season, 2 runs each) sim -1.1%, offseason +0.8%, league creation about +12% (generating the shapes of 20 years of classes; `pickBuild` was made about 4x faster to get there). Database (JSON size of every store after 10 seasons): 117.4 MB vs 116.8 MB (+0.5%; the players store 25.15 vs 24.55 MB, +2.4%), with history trimmed to 5 rows.
  - **Generated classes** (`SUB_DIST=1`, 30 classes of a new league, expanded ratings on; origins set to about 80% US, 2% G League/OTE): build tags of the top 60 drawn vs the builds' real-draft weights for the same players' heights: guard 26.8 / 25.5%, wing 30.9 / 31.4%, big 33.4 / 31.7%, shooting 34.2 / 31.2%, scoring 32.9 / 29.1%, playmaking 21.3 / 22.6%, defense 20.8 / 26.4% (prospects' defensive ratings are low, which the tilt toward his own ratings follows), rebounding 24.1 / 21.6%, athletic 29.1 / 32.5%, raw 17.8 / 18.6%. Heights and positions are unchanged (bases unchanged). Specialists 3.6% of prospects (2.5 a class); generational shapes 1.1 a class (max 2); 86% have a signature sub 8+ above its base and 88% a weakness 8+ below; offsets per sub sd 3-7.5 (largest floater, max vertical/standing, blocks, off-dribble threes). Backgrounds: one-and-done 35%, college 29%, senior 8%, international 18%, G League/OTE 10%. Growth profiles: normal 43%, early peaker 14%, late bloomer 11%, one-skill 10%, athlete-learner 11%, finished 11%. Sanity: 6'2" and shorter max blocks 47 (blocks 7 below diq on average); 7'2"+ max lateral quickness 43 (blocks 8 above diq). Correlations of offsets: floater/finesse +0.41, blocks/offensive rebounding +0.12; steals/ball security -0.16 with expanded ratings on (ball security follows `bsc` there, so the "hands" draw only reaches it without expanded ratings).
- **Step 2 — real players from their stats** (`tools/realSubRatings.ts`, a regression like `tools/realRatingFixes.ts`): sub-ratings have no ground truth, so the evidence is what a player's box score says beyond the base rating it sits under. For each of 12 stats (BLK%, STL%, ORB%, DRB%, AST%, TOV%, USG%, 3PA rate, 3P%, FT rate, 2P%, FT%; percentages shrunk toward the league average by attempts) an OLS of the stat on that base (BLK%/STL% on Defense, ORB%/DRB% on Rebounding, AST% on Passing and Dribbling, 3PA rate on Three-point and height, FT rate on Dunks/layups and Inside, ...), fitted on the 12,653 player-seasons of 1980-2026 with 800+ regular-season minutes; a player-season's residual over the residual sd is a z-score. Each sub's lean is a fixed mix of z-scores (blocks ← BLK%; steals ← STL%; interior D ← DRB%, BLK%; perimeter D ← STL% minus BLK%; range ← 3PA rate, 3P%; catch-and-shoot ← 3P% minus usage; off-the-dribble ← usage, 3PA rate, AST%; post moves ← FT rate, two-point volume, usage; contact ← FT rate; finesse ← 2P% minus FT rate; pull-up ← usage; spot-up ← 3P% minus usage; offensive/defensive rebounding ← ORB%/DRB%; handle ← usage, AST%; ball security ← -TOV%; shot creation ← usage; vision ← AST%; accuracy ← -TOV%; lateral quickness ← STL%; straight-line speed ← FT rate, ORB%; standing reach ← BLK%, DRB%; max vertical ← ORB%, FT rate), over his last three seasons up to that ratings row (weights 1, 0.6, 0.35 times minutes), soft-thresholded (|z| < 0.35 is no evidence), shrunk by sample size (minutes / (minutes + 1200)), 7 points per z, capped at ±16. Box scores have no shot zones, assisted shares or dunks, so catch-and-shoot vs off-the-dribble and post vs close shots come from usage, 3PA rate and FT rate. Fit: R² of the stat models 0.00-0.80 (they only take out what the base says), year-to-year correlation of the z-scores 0.49 (3P%) to 0.93 (3PA rate), BLK% 0.92, STL% 0.80, ORB% 0.90, AST% 0.76, TOV% 0.76: the leans are stable skills, not noise. The leans are stored in the real players data (`subLean` on 26,823 of 28,952 ratings rows: 25 comma-separated wanted offsets, SUB_DEFS order; pre-1973 rows have no BLK%/STL%, draft prospects no stats); `node tools/realSubRatings.ts --write` regenerates them.
  - **Where they apply:** real-player leagues and random debuts (`formatPlayerFactory` passes the row's lean; `ensureSubs` fits it to the bases with `realSubShape`, deterministic, no noise; `p.subP.rs` = 1), real draft prospects with stats, and league files and existing leagues with real players (`srID`) that have no stats-derived shape yet: `updateSubRatingsForLeague` loads the real players data once and reads each one's latest row up to the current season (one with no stats keeps his neutral shape, `rs` 0, and isn't looked up again). With the setting off nothing is stored (an unread lean is dropped at league creation).
- **Step 2 — scouting, fuzz and the combine:** scouting fuzz on subs (`fuzzSubs`, `worker/core/player/subRatings.ts`) is the row's fuzz, like its base, plus a deterministic per-sub part up to 0.8x the row's fuzz either way, so the shape is uncertain too, not just the level; it scales with the row's fuzz, so the front office scouting level, the scouting budget and a class aging narrow it like the bases'. Subs the draft combine measures (standing reach and max vertical from the verticals, lateral quickness from the lane agility drill, shot blocking from the wingspan) keep a third of it once he was measured there. Used everywhere subs are shown: player page, ratings tables, Compare Players, Draft Scouting, the scouting reports, the exports (they read the formatted ratings); none in God Mode / multi team mode; the no visible ratings challenge drops them in `player.format` too (retired players excepted). The combine itself reads the subs (max vertical from Max Vertical, standing vertical's gap from Standing Reach vs Max Vertical, lane agility and shuttle from Lateral Quickness, sprint from Straight-Line Speed; their averages are the bases, so the class keeps its scale). Scouting report grades read the subs (as scouted): catch-and-shoot from catch-and-shoot 3, range and spot-up; off the dribble from off-dribble 3, pull-up and shot creation; finishing from close shots, post moves, floater, contact, finesse; playmaking from vision, accuracy and handle; ball security from Ball Security; perimeter defense from perimeter D, steals and lateral quickness; interior defense from interior D, shot blocking and standing reach; athleticism from the four athletic subs. The report adds "Shooting by part" (range, catch-and-shoot, off the dribble, pull-up) and "Defense by part" (rim protection, post defense, on the ball, steals, lateral quickness) lines, best first, in words with the scouted value.
- **Step 2 — skill badges:** each skill and badge tooltip names the sub that drives it (the best of its subs as scouted), e.g. "Shot blocker — driven by Shot Blocking 78" (`common/skillBadges/subDrivers.ts`: explicit subs for the original skills and the badges named for one part of a base, else the subs of the bases in its main composite; combos only where one core is clear: Board master, Offensive crasher, Switchable defender, Explosive athlete, Interior beast, Rim protector). On the player page header and ratings history. Badges are still awarded from the bases.
- **Step 2 — college stats** (`common/collegeStats/season.ts`): with sub-ratings, the prospect's subs (as offsets from their bases, so back-projection to earlier seasons keeps the shape) move blocks (Shot Blocking), steals (Steals), 3PA share (Range, catch-and-shoot), 3P% (catch-and-shoot, range), assists (Vision), turnovers (Ball Security, unless expanded ratings' `bsc` already counts it) and the ORB/DRB split (Offensive vs Defensive, unless `oreb`/`dreb` already count it); deterministic terms, no extra random numbers. Without sub-ratings (or with every sub at its base) generation is exactly as before. `COLLEGE_STATS_VERSION` 3 marks stats made with subs; with the setting off stats stay version 2 (`COLLEGE_STATS_VERSION_BASE`), and turning it on remakes undrafted prospects' generated stats once (`upgradeCollegeStats`, same seeds and college season). On a class of 300 prospects with random shapes (sd 8 per sub), correlation of the sub's offset with the stat per minute, subs off → on: blocks 0.05 → 0.30, steals 0.06 → 0.44, assists (vision) -0.01 → 0.21, turnovers (ball security) 0.01 → -0.46, 3PA (range) 0.08 → 0.17, offensive rebounds 0.08 → 0.47 (`subRatingsReal.test.ts`).
- **Step 3 — the game sim reads the subs** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ (setting `subRatingsSim`, "Sub-Ratings in Sim", League Extras → Players; on for new leagues, off for existing ones; does nothing unless Sub-Ratings is on). Code: `GameSim.basketball/subRatingsSim.ts`, folded in by `game/loadTeams.ts`.
  - **At game load only:** where a composite reads a base rating and a sub is the more specific skill, the composite reads `base + w (sub − base)` instead (w = 1 is "read the sub instead of the base"); each rating's change in one composite is bounded at ±15 (`SUB_SIM.bound`). With every sub equal to its base every composite is exactly the stock one (`subRatingsSim.test.ts` checks every composite of the stock, expanded and Game Sim Fixes weights, and a game's loadTeams output), so the effect is purely the shape. The per-possession code only reads a few new per-player numbers with a fallback to the old ones; with the setting off the game is byte-identical (outcomeInvariance unchanged, and a game with the setting on but Sub-Ratings off is identical).
  - **No double counting with Expanded Ratings:** a composite that reads `pdef`/`idef` gets only the sub's lean within its side (steals vs the perimeter side 0.6 perimeter D + 0.4 steals, blocks vs 0.6 interior D + 0.4 blocks, ...), the jumper gaps only the catch-and-shoot vs off-the-dribble lean beyond `csh − dsh`, and the turnovers composite skips Ball Security when it reads `bsc`; offensive/defensive rebounding is already `oreb`/`dreb`.

    | Sub                             | Composite (rating it replaces)                                                                                                  | Sim effect                                                            |
    | ------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
    | Shot Blocking                   | blocking (diq / idef)                                                                                                           | who blocks, team block chance                                         |
    | Standing Reach                  | blocking, defenseInterior, rebounding, off/defRebounding, jumpBall (jmp)                                                        | blocks, rim defense, boards                                           |
    | Steals                          | stealing (diq / pdef)                                                                                                           | who steals (Perimeter Defense's credit included)                      |
    | Perimeter D                     | defensePerimeter (diq / pdef)                                                                                                   | shot defense, Matchup Defense assignments, steal chance               |
    | Interior D                      | defenseInterior (diq / idef)                                                                                                    | rim and post defense, assignments                                     |
    | Lateral Quickness               | stealing, defensePerimeter, defense, fouling (spd); `simLat`                                                                    | on-ball defense, fewer fouls, the defender's side of a drive mismatch |
    | Straight-Line Speed             | pace (spd)                                                                                                                      | pace and transition chances                                           |
    | Range                           | shootingThreePointer (tp, w 0.5)                                                                                                | 3PA tendency and the make of every three                              |
    | Catch & Shoot 3 / Off-Dribble 3 | `jumperGap3` = tcs − tod (gap bound ±0.25)                                                                                      | assisted threes + gap/2 × 0.32, unassisted − gap/2 × 0.32             |
    | Spot-Up / Pull-Up               | `jumperGapMid` = spu − pul                                                                                                      | the same for mid-range jumpers                                        |
    | Post Moves / Touch / Close      | shootingLowPost, shootingLowPostMake (ins: 0.75 × post for bigs, floater for small players by height 35-60, + 0.25 close shots) | post-up / short shot make (not how often: shootingLowPostSelect)      |
    | Close Shots                     | `shootingPutBack` (dnk of the at-rim composite)                                                                                 | putbacks and tip-ins                                                  |
    | Finesse, Max Vertical           | `shootingAtRimMake` (dnk: finesse 1, max vertical 0.5)                                                                          | the make of a drive (selection stays shootingAtRim)                   |
    | Contact Finishing               | drawingFouls (dnk)                                                                                                              | shooting fouls drawn, and-ones                                        |
    | Handle                          | dribbling, ballSecurity (drb)                                                                                                   | pressure: steal and turnover chances                                  |
    | Ball Security                   | turnovers (on the oiq term) without Expanded Ratings (with them it's `bsc`)                                                     | who turns it over                                                     |
    | Shot Creation                   | usage, creation (drb)                                                                                                           | usage, Spacing & Shot Creation's creator draw                         |
    | Vision                          | passing, creation (pss)                                                                                                         | assist chance, who assists, the pass bonus                            |
    | Pass Accuracy                   | ballSecurity (pss), turnovers (pss, w −1)                                                                                       | bad-pass turnovers                                                    |

  - **Calibration** (`SUB_SIM_CAL`, only with the setting on): the generated shapes aren't centered on their bases league-wide (contact finishing averages 3 below dunks/layups, pull-up 1.4 above mid-range, post moves 1.6 above inside, blocks 4 below defense), so make offsets by shot type (three −0.003, without Expanded Ratings −0.008 since those shapes lean catch-and-shoot; mid +0.003, rim −0.0007, post −0.0065) and multipliers on shooting fouls (1.018), blocks (0.982) and steals (0.99) keep the league where it was. 8 new leagues (defaults) × 1 regular season, same rosters, `SRS_CAL=1 subRatingsSim.script.test.ts`, per team game:

    | setting | pts   | poss  | FG%   | 3PA rate | 3P%   | FTA   | ORB   | DRB   | AST   | TOV   | STL  | BLK  | PF    | home win% |
    | ------- | ----- | ----- | ----- | -------- | ----- | ----- | ----- | ----- | ----- | ----- | ---- | ---- | ----- | --------- |
    | off     | 110.3 | 102.1 | 46.01 | 34.71    | 35.52 | 23.16 | 11.27 | 33.91 | 25.84 | 14.38 | 7.43 | 5.09 | 19.50 | 57.5      |
    | on      | 110.7 | 102.3 | 46.18 | 34.65    | 35.64 | 23.27 | 11.26 | 33.87 | 25.99 | 14.44 | 7.40 | 5.04 | 19.58 | 57.3      |

    (Run-to-run noise is about ±0.2 on FG% and 3P%.) Without Expanded Ratings (4 leagues): pts 111.4 → 111.4, 3P% 35.6 → 35.7, TOV 14.4 → 14.3. 5-season long run (seed 1, defaults): pts 111.5 → 110.8, FG% 46.4 → 46.2, 3P% 35.9 → 35.8, TOV 14.1 → 14.2, STL 7.2 → 7.3, BLK 5.1 → 5.0. Superstar calibration holds: league leaders (mean of 8 leagues) 29.1 ppg / PER 35.0 / WS 20.0 (off 30.1 / 35.9 / 19.0), maximum 33.1 ppg, PER 41.8.

  - **The shape shows in the stats:** correlation of the sub's offset from its base with the stat over the same 8 leagues (players with 500+ minutes, ~2,670), raw / partial (the stat's residual after the base ratings that drive it), off → on: blocks vs BLK/36 0.44 / 0.11 → 0.49 / 0.18; standing reach vs BLK/36 0.29 / −0.01 → 0.45 / 0.24; steals vs STL/36 0.44 / 0.24 → 0.53 / 0.31; lateral quickness vs STL/36 0.18 / −0.01 → 0.36 / 0.16; off-dribble minus catch-and-shoot 3 vs unassisted minus assisted 3P% 0.42 → 0.49 (with Expanded Ratings `csh`/`dsh` already carried part of it); pull-up minus spot-up vs the same for mid-range 0.31 → 0.42; range vs 3PA/36 0.33 / −0.02 → 0.39 / 0.13; contact finishing vs FTA/FGA 0.23 / 0.15 → 0.37 / 0.30; vision vs AST/36 0.20 / 0.04 → 0.31 / 0.29; pass accuracy vs TOV/36 0.01 → −0.15 / −0.19; ball security vs TOV/36 −0.08 / −0.22 → −0.12 / −0.25 (already `bsc`); shot creation vs usage 0.11 / 0.13 → 0.19 / 0.28; post moves vs post FG% (bigs) 0.14 / 0.04 → 0.21 / 0.12; offensive/defensive rebounding unchanged (0.33 / 0.42 partial, already `oreb`/`dreb`). Finesse barely shows in at-rim FG% (0.04 → 0.03 partial): dunks/layups is only a tenth of the at-rim composite, height carries it.
  - **Real players** (2026 real-players league, 3 seeds × 1 regular season each way, `SRS_REAL=1`): Curry (off-dribble 3 +7, catch-and-shoot −6) unassisted 3P% 39.2 → 42.1; Dončić (vision +5, off-dribble +6) 7.2 → 7.8 assists, unassisted 3P% 34.6 → 41.7; Gobert 2.0 → 1.9 blocks in 30 minutes (his shot blocking +11 is matched by interior D +7, so `idef` already carried it); Capela 3.6 offensive rebounds per 36 either way.
  - **Performance:** the fold costs about 0.1 ms per game in loadTeams (`subSimComposite` 77 ms of CPU in a whole season's profile, compiled per composite); same rosters, 1,600 games each way alternating (`SRS_PERF=1`): GameSim no slower. The sim benchmark (one season, 4 runs each) shows +4.6% CPU in the season phases, and the long run +3.7%, but those play different seasons (different trades, injuries and AI work: the profile's differences are in contract demands and trade valuation, not the sim).

- **Step 4 — archetypes** read the sub-ratings (and, with Advanced Tracking Stats, the play-type profile): Archetypes, arch15.
- **Wider shapes** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ (setting `subRatingsWide`, "Wider Sub-Rating Shapes", League Extras → Players; on for new leagues, off for existing ones; does nothing unless Sub-Ratings is on). Subspread. The stock shapes are narrow: small noise (sd 2.5) and factors (sd 3), and the fit to a base takes the group's weighted mean out of the wanted offsets, so a lean of +10 on one of two subs ends up +5/−5; real players' leans are soft-thresholded, shrunk and capped (±16), so a real player's subs barely move off his bases (passing, finishing and athletic subs within 2-3 points of their base). With the setting off, generation, development and real players' shapes are byte-identical (a hash of a generated class with 8 seasons of development, with and without Expanded Ratings, plus every 2020+ real player's stats and neutral shape, is the same before and after). Bases are never changed either way: ovr, pot, value and every base the sim reads are exactly the same, and not one extra number is drawn from the game's random stream (`subRatingsLeague.test.ts` checks a class and 12 seasons of development on and off).
  - **Measured first** (`SUB_SPREAD=1 subSpread.script.test.ts`, below; the real NBA reference from `node tools/realSubRatings.ts --reference`). The reference puts each box-score stat that one sub drives on its base's scale (the part of the stat its base doesn't explain, `sqrt(1 − R²)` × the base's sd over the same players) and keeps only the part that repeats year to year (the covariance of a player's season with his next, 800+ minutes both, 1980-2026): blocks − steals 11.3, offensive − defensive rebounding 7.1, vision − accuracy (AST% vs TOV%) 9.3, shot creation − ball security (USG% vs TOV%) 10.9, contact − finesse (FT rate vs 2P%) 14.8, post moves alone (FT rate) 11.3, range alone (3PA rate) 11.9, catch-and-shoot alone (3P%) 10.1, shot blocking alone 8.0, steals alone 5.6. Box scores have no assisted or pull-up shares, so catch-and-shoot vs off the dribble and pull-up vs spot-up have no reference here (the repo has no tracking data and the session no network); public tracking numbers put a player's catch-and-shoot minus pull-up 3P% at roughly 4-5 points of 3P% (stable part), which on the same scale is in the 12-15 range. Targets (sd of the contrast across players): blocks − steals 12, interior − perimeter D 11, catch-and-shoot − off-dribble 3 13, post − touch 12-14, contact − finesse 14, pull-up − spot-up 12-13, offensive − defensive boards 7.5, creation − security 11, vision − accuracy 9.5, and the athletic pairs about where the stock shapes have them (10-13: the box score barely sees them); about one prospect in eight lopsided.
  - **Generation** (`generateWideShape`, `common/subRatingsGen.ts` `WIDE`; same seed and the same build draw as the stock shape, so a player's build is the same): noise sized per base (sd: three 3.5; defense, mid-range 3; inside, dunks/layups, dribbling 2.5; rebounding, jumping 1.5; passing, speed 1) on top of the stock factors; opposing factors (catch-and-shoot vs off the dribble sd 3.5, ball security vs shot creation 3, contact vs finesse 2.5, rim vs perimeter defense 2.5, post vs touch 2, standing reach vs max vertical 1, offensive vs defensive boards 1, vision vs accuracy and straight-line vs lateral 0.5); the diffuse part scaled up 1.4x in groups of two, 1.15x in groups of three (the fit halves it there); fat tails: 13% are lopsided (`p.subP.lop`: one sub, or two in 35% of them, 15-25 off its base: a standout where his build leans, a hole in 30% of them), specialists 6% (stock 3.5%), signatures and weaknesses as stock but no longer halved by the fit; the class cap on generational shapes is an offset of 28+ and three a class (stock 20+, two). A small centering (`WIDE_CENTER`: range +1, catch-and-shoot −1, ...) keeps every sub's league mean where the stock shapes have it, so Sub-Ratings in Sim's calibration holds.
  - **Partial residual** (standouts protected in the fit): the fit to a base moves each sub by `1 / (1 + (offset / 8)²)` of the correction, and so do the corrections that anchor the subs to the expanded ratings (`anchorToExpanded`'s `protect`), so a standout or a hole keeps most of its offset and the subs near the base take up the rest: a +20 on Interior D (30% of Defense) ends about +19 with its three siblings about −8, where the stock fit gives +14/−6. The base itself never moves: moving it would change ovr and the sim, which this setting doesn't (in a group of two a standout's sibling still takes the whole correction: that's what the base being their average means).
  - **Sanity caps** (`wideSanityCaps`): shot blocking up to `43 + 0.5 length + 0.35 jmp` (stock `40 + 0.5 length + 0.25 jmp`: leapers get more room), lateral quickness up to `100 − 1.2 (hgt − 58)` and his speed + 32, post moves `55 + 0.5 hgt + 0.35 stre`, contact finishing `52 + 0.35 stre + 0.3 jmp + 0.2 hgt`, max vertical his jumping + 30; floors for a big's standing reach (`0.5 hgt − 5`) and a strong player's contact finishing.
  - **Real players** (`common/subRatingsReal.ts`, `wideRealLean`): the same 12 stat z-scores, now stored raw (`subZ` in the real players data: his minutes over the three-season window and the z-scores, each against his own era: the season's mean z of 800+ minute players taken out, so the three-point boom isn't read as everyone's range), each z in rating points as the reference spreads that stat (`REAL_WIDE.scale`: blocks 8.4, steals 6.3, ORB% 9.3, DRB% 7.5, AST% 6.0, TOV% 9.8, USG% 9.2, 3PA rate 12.7, 3P% 14.3, FT rate 12.5, 2P% 13.7, FT% 5.8 per z), no threshold, shrunk only by his sample (minutes / (minutes + 600): a full-time player keeps about 89%, a rookie with 900 minutes 60%; percentages were already shrunk by attempts), capped at ±28, the athletic subs at half weight (the box score sees them only indirectly). Fitted with the wide caps and the protected fit. With Expanded Ratings his catch-and-shoot vs off-the-dribble split (`csh − dsh`, which the jump shooting subs follow) is no longer ratings plus noise: the noise is replaced by what his stats say (0.6 × the lean his stats give the four jump shooting subs, on top of the ratings' ball-handler part), so the noise can't overrule his stats; `csh + dsh` is kept (ovr reads csh at 0.0055 a point, dsh not at all, and is recomputed when he develops). `node tools/realSubRatings.ts --write` writes `subZ` beside `subLean` (unchanged): +2.3 MB of JSON. Famous players (as a real-players league gives them, Expanded Ratings on, a season each is known for), offsets from the base (`subSpread.script.test.ts`). Without Expanded Ratings the jump shooting leans are larger (Korver 2015: catch-and-shoot +8, off the dribble −29 in an earlier run; with Expanded Ratings his split also carries the ratings' part). Real players' athletic subs stay near their bases (the box score barely sees them), and a box score can't tell an elite pull-up shooter's 3P% from a spot-up shooter's, so the jump shooting leans read usage and assists more than 3P%:

    | Player (season)              | stock                                              | wide                                                                                       |
    | ---------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
    | Kyle Korver (2015)           | C&S 3 −5, off-dribble 3 +2; pull-up +3, spot-up −3 | C&S 3 +2, off-dribble 3 −8; pull-up −11, spot-up +11; post −7, touch +7                    |
    | Stephen Curry (2016)         | C&S 3 −13, off-dribble 3 +14; pull-up +8           | C&S 3 −3, off-dribble 3 +9; pull-up +7; contact −8, finesse +8                             |
    | Ben Simmons (2019)           | vision +2, accuracy −2; touch −1                   | vision +9, accuracy −9; touch −11, post +6; contact +5, finesse −5                         |
    | Giannis Antetokounmpo (2020) | C&S 3 −6, off-dribble 3 +6; pull-up +5             | C&S 3 −13, off-dribble 3 +10; pull-up +10; touch −7; vision +5, accuracy −5                |
    | Rudy Gobert (2022)           | blocks +13, steals −12; close +4                   | blocks +16, steals −12; close +7, touch −14; contact +7, finesse −7                        |
    | Russell Westbrook (2017)     | C&S 3 −5, off-dribble 3 +3; pull-up +2; vision +1  | C&S 3 −12, off-dribble 3 +16; pull-up +12, spot-up −12; contact +7; vision +6, accuracy −6 |
    | DeMar DeRozan (2018)         | pull-up +8, spot-up −8; C&S 3 −8, off-dribble 3 +9 | pull-up +9, spot-up −9; C&S 3 −8, off-dribble 3 +11; post +8, close −10; contact +7        |

  - **Development**: mean reversion 2% a year to 23, then 0.5% (stock 2% always), and a sub more than 28 off its base is pulled back 15% of the excess; role growth (`roleLean`, from last regular season's box score, minutes / (minutes + 1000), full to 27 then 0.6, about a point a season at most): a spot-up shooter (many threes, low usage) grows catch-and-shoot and spot-up, a primary handler (high usage and assists) off-the-dribble threes, pull-ups, handle and shot creation, a playmaker vision, a post scorer post moves, a rim finisher contact finishing and close shots, a mid-range shooter his pull-up (high usage) or spot-up, a rim protector blocks, a ball hawk steals.
  - **Player page**: with the setting on, each sub is colored by its offset from its base (green 6+ above, bold 12+; red 6+ below, bold 12+; Bootstrap's emphasis colors, which the dark theme maps to its own), the tooltip gives the offset, and an opened base with a sub 6+ off it gets a line "Within Three-point: strong Catch & Shoot 3; weak Off-Dribble 3" (labels on phones; wraps at 390 px, no horizontal scroll). The values are the scouted ones (fuzz as everywhere), and the no visible ratings challenge shows no subs at all.
  - **Spread, before → after** (`SUB_SPREAD=1 SUB_SPREAD_CLASSES=15 subSpread.script.test.ts`, Expanded Ratings on; 1,050 prospects, 600 of them after six seasons of development without games, 690/708 real 2026 rows): sd of each contrast across players, stock → wide.

    | Contrast                   | target | NBA ref | prospects    | after 6 seasons | real 2026    |
    | -------------------------- | ------ | ------- | ------------ | --------------- | ------------ |
    | blocks − steals            | 12     | 11.3    | 11.5 → 13.8  | 13.6 → 15.1     | 8.9 → 9.3    |
    | interior − perimeter D     | 11     | —       | 11.3 → 12.6  | 13.0 → 13.8     | 8.9 → 9.1    |
    | C&S 3 − off-dribble 3      | 13     | (12-15) | 11.3 → 13.9  | 12.3 → 14.8     | 9.8 → 9.7    |
    | post − touch               | 12-14  | —       | 11.9 → 15.1  | 10.8 → 14.2     | 2.5 → 5.8    |
    | contact − finesse          | 14     | 14.8    | 10.9 → 14.6  | 11.3 → 14.8     | 3.3 → 9.0    |
    | pull-up − spot-up          | 12-13  | (12-15) | 11.1 → 14.3  | 11.9 → 14.6     | 10.6 → 11.2  |
    | off − def boards           | 7.5    | 7.1     | 7.5 → 7.5    | 8.1 → 8.1       | 7.1 → 7.1    |
    | creation − security        | 11     | 10.9    | 8.9 → 9.8    | 9.3 → 10.4      | 9.1 → 9.5    |
    | vision − accuracy          | 9.5    | 9.3     | 8.5 → 11.2   | 8.2 → 9.8       | 3.7 → 7.7    |
    | players with a sub 15+ off |        |         | 20.9 → 50.5% | 25.8 → 53.8%    | 15.1 → 20.6% |
    | players with a sub 25+ off |        |         | 0.6 → 6.2%   | 1.5 → 6.7%      | 1.3 → 1.6%   |

    Lopsided 13.1% of prospects, specialists 7%, 1.5 generational shapes a class. Without Expanded Ratings (where nothing anchors the defense, rebounding, jumper and ball security splits) the wide shapes are wider (catch-and-shoot − off-dribble 9.4 → 16.4, real players 4.0 → 10.3). With Expanded Ratings, real players' defense, three, mid-range, rebounding and dribbling spreads barely move: those splits are the expanded ratings', which this setting doesn't change (except the jumper split's noise, above). In a simulated league (seed 3, defaults, 6 seasons, with role growth) players with a sub 15+ off are 38.7% stock, 62.8% wide at the start and 68.0% after six seasons (contact − finesse 17.6 → 20.2, the widest). (Measured before the ±32 limit of `fitWide`, which only touches a few extreme groups.)

  - **Guardrails**: outcomeInvariance unchanged and passing. Same rosters (bases identical), 5 new leagues × 1 regular season with Sub-Ratings in Sim, `SRS_CAL=1 subRatingsSim.script.test.ts`, wide off → on, per team game: pts 109.10 → 109.18, FG% 45.76 → 45.73, 3PA rate 34.36 → 34.27, 3P% 35.00 → 34.98, FTA 22.72 → 22.86, AST 26.01 → 25.92, TOV 14.44 → 14.46, STL 7.31 → 7.37, BLK 4.95 → 5.05, PF 19.26 → 19.31 (all within run noise). No star inflation (ovr is unchanged by design): season leaders 27.4 → 27.7 ppg, PER 32.8 → 31.5, WS 16.7 → 15.9; the ten best players by ovr PER 26.3 → 25.8, WS 11.4 → 11.1. Six-season long runs (seed 3): play-type mix and league averages the same within noise (3P% 34.6-35.2 either way), archetype max share 2.8-3.8% vs 3.2-3.7%, generic 0.7-1.2% vs 0.9-1.6%. Archetypes (`metrics.script.test.ts`, Expanded Ratings, `ARCH_SUBS_WIDE=1` with wide norm tables `SUB_NORMS_*_WIDE*` from `subLeans.script.test.ts`; a league reads its own table anyway): gold 43.4% → 40.7% (role 63.7 → 62.8%), churn 4.9 → 5.2%, max share league 2.1/2.1%, real 3.6 → 3.8%, prospects 6.2 → 5.9%, poor fit league 3.7/3.7%, veterans 4.7 → 5.4%, prospects 3.7 → 4.0%, real 4.6 → 4.7%; position conflicts 0 (`tuning.test.ts` with the wide shapes). Two pinned real players read another build with the wide shapes (Giannis 2025 Mid-Post Board Enforcer, Dončić 2025 Iso Savant), left to the archetype v2 work; the archetype tests run with the stock shapes.

- **Left for later steps:** scouting reports naming signature skills.

### Sim-calibrated ovr

- **Setting:** `ovrV2` (League Extras → Players, "Sim-Calibrated Ovr"). On for new leagues, off for existing leagues (off = the stock formula, byte-identical: `expandedRatingsOff.test.ts`). Turning it on or off recomputes every non-retired player's ovr (pot moves by the same amount) and value; league files and real-player leagues get their ovrs recomputed at creation.
- **What:** player ovr weighs every rating by its measured value in the game sim instead of the 2018 regression. The marginal-value study (`src/test/ovrFit/marginal.script.test.ts`: every game, every player on both teams gets an independent ±10 in every rating; OLS of the score margin on minutes-weighted exposure, 16,000 games per mode, with the new-league default game sim extras on: realistic possessions, technical fouls, perimeter defense, game sim fixes, position-aware team ratings, realistic rotations) gives the margin per game of a 36-minute player +10 in each rating. Ovr v2 before the fudge factor is `intercept + Σ weight × rating` with the weights proportional to those slopes (negative slopes → 0), rescaled so that over the rostered players of 12 generated leagues it has the stock formula's mean and standard deviation, plus a small long-run correction (`LONG_RUN` in the fit script: ovr also decides who teams keep, so a league run with v2 settles a little tighter and higher; measured with the population model under the new-league defaults); then the stock fudge factor and rounding. So the league's ovr scale, mean and spread stay put; height, speed, ball handling and three-point shooting count more, basketball IQ and endurance less; inside scoring and mid-range get the small weight the sim gives them (ins 0.01/0.05, fg 0.00/0.05 margin per +10 with expanded off/on, within noise of 0).
- **Expanded Ratings on:** its own fit on the expanded study (23 ratings perturbed independently): wingspan, perimeter and interior defense, offensive and defensive rebounding, ball security and catch-and-shoot/off-the-dribble get their own weights; the compat ratings the sim no longer reads (`diq`, `reb`) get 0, so nothing is counted twice (`fg`/`tp` keep theirs, the sim still reads them as the range ratings). Both variants are anchored on the same population (expanded ratings derived from the stock ones), so toggling Expanded Ratings doesn't move the league's ovr: mean shift 0.1, mean |shift| 1.7 a player (guards −2 to −2.7, bigs +1.8 to +2.5: the expanded sim values size and length more).
- **Player value:** with the setting on, `player.value` mixes in 10% PER-based value instead of 20% (ovr already measures sim impact; PER flatters box-score bigs).
- **Data-driven, re-fittable:** the weights live in `player/ovrWeights.basketball.ts`, generated by `src/test/ovrFit/fit.script.test.ts` from `src/test/ovrFit/marginal-off.json` / `marginal-on.json`. After a game sim change, re-run the study for both modes (`RA_SIM=off` measures the stock sim), then the fit, and re-check `LONG_RUN` (commands at the top of each file); `report.script.test.ts` (`OVR_REPORT=1`, `REPORT_MARGINAL_DIR` for another sim configuration's study) prints the distribution, correlation and positional residual tables. One set of weights for every sim configuration (fit on the new-league defaults; see the residuals with the sim extras off below).
- **Measured** (`report.script.test.ts`, 8 generated leagues, 3,120 rostered players; sim impact = Σ slope × rating, new-league default sim):

  | Variant             | Mean | SD  | p10 | p50 | p90 | p99 | Top 30 prospects | corr(ovr, impact) |
  | ------------------- | ---- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | ---------------- | ----------------- |
  | stock, expanded off | 51.4 | 9.2 | 38  | 52  | 62  | 72  | 38.0             | 0.958             |
  | v2, expanded off    | 51.0 | 9.7 | 38  | 51  | 63  | 74  | 39.6             | 0.988             |
  | stock, expanded on  | 51.4 | 9.3 | 38  | 52  | 63  | 73  | 37.7             | 0.950             |
  | v2, expanded on     | 51.1 | 9.7 | 37  | 52  | 63  | 73  | 38.9             | 0.989             |

  Mean sim impact at equal ovr by position, in ovr points (+ = underrated), PG / C: new-league default sim, expanded on: stock −3.6 / +3.3, v2 +0.1 / −0.1; expanded off: stock −0.8 / +0.6, v2 +0.1 / −0.3. With the sim extras off (stock sim, its own study): expanded on stock −3.5 / +3.8, v2 +0.2 / +0.4; expanded off stock −2.0 / +2.5, v2 −1.2 / +1.6. On the held-out rotation players of the study leagues, corr(ovr, on-court +/- per 48) 0.344 → 0.349 (expanded off) and 0.352 → 0.366 (on).
