- **NIT** (_NIT_ tab): the 32 best teams left out; regular season conference champions that lost their conference tournament and missed the NCAA field get automatic bids. Four brackets of eight (seeds 1-8), better seed at home until the semifinals, full bracket and champion. Prospects on NIT teams play the games: they are in the team's record, at the end of his draft-year stat line, in his postseason game log and a _NIT_ split, his page shows _NIT: Semifinals (seed 3)_, and honors are _NIT Champion_, _NIT Most Outstanding Player_ and _NIT All-Tournament Team_.
- **Season storylines:** each class's season draws a shape from its seed, and maybe a scoring environment, which bend what the season is simulated with. Version 3 (classes made now) draws from 20 shapes; version 2 classes keep the first seven with their old weights (a test pins whole version 2 seasons against hashes). The original seven: _a balanced year_, _a dominant favorite_ (the best team pulls 3+ points clear), _a wide-open year_ (the top of the country squeezed together, more swing in a game), _the year of the mid-major_ (non-power leagues better, six of their best teams much better), _a down year for the blue bloods_ (half of the 14 biggest programs 4-7 points worse), _one loaded conference_ (every team of one power league better), _chalk all the way_ (less swing in a game); _a scoring explosion_ or _a defensive slog_ (8 points a game more or fewer). A box at the top of the page has the headline and a written summary (whether the shape held, champion and Final Four, preseason No. 1 and weeks on top, the Cinderella, a 16 over a 1, unbeaten teams, bids by conference, first teams out, bid thieves, NIT champion, scoring). Version 3 adds: _two juggernauts_ (the two best teams 3+ points clear of the third: did they meet in the final?), _the year of the freshman_ / _a veterans' year_ / _a guards' game_ / _the year of the big man_ (prospects of that kind, by age in the draft year or position, add 35-45% more to their teams and the others 15-20% less; guards' years score 3 more points, big men's 3 fewer), _a Cinderella run_ (once the field is set, one 10-12 seed, from outside the power leagues when there is one, plays 10 points better in the tournament), _a wild first weekend_ (30% more swing in a game in the First Four and the first two rounds), _parity in the power conferences_ (power-league teams squeezed 40% toward their average), _a fallen powerhouse_ (one of the six biggest programs 8-11 points worse), _a conference down year_ (every team of one power league 3 worse), _the transfer carousel_ (every team ±2.6 points that the preseason poll does not know about), _a great team stumbles_ (the best team 4.5+ clear, 4.5 worse from the Elite Eight on), _pace and space_ (6 more points a game, more swing; never with a defensive slog). Each has its own summary sentence (two wordings for most, picked from the season; version 4 seasons pick from one or two more per shape and for the champion line, and a season that matched its draft class says so, see Draft class shapes). Engine: `common/collegeStats/ncaaStoryline.ts`. Long run over 2500 version 3 seasons: 1 seeds win the title 58% of the time (36% in wide-open years, 41% with parity in the power conferences, 52% with a Cinderella run, 58% in a balanced one, 66-68% with a dominant favorite, two juggernauts or chalk), a 16 beats a 1 in 0.9% of those games (2.7-2.8% in parity and conference-down years, 1.6% on a wild first weekend, none in chalk or freshman years), a team is unbeaten on Selection Sunday in 9% of seasons (17% with a fallen powerhouse, 15% when a great team stumbles, 3-4% in pace-and-space, big men's and guards' years), 1.35 double-digit seeds reach the Sweet 16 a year (2.1 with power-conference parity, 1.6-1.7 with a Cinderella run, a wild first weekend or pace and space; the Cinderella run's own team gets there about a third of the time), points a game 71.0 (74.7 pace and space, 72.1 guards, 69.5 big men). The test `ncaaExtras.test.ts` checks the long run over 640 seasons.
- **Versions:** `p.collegeSeason.v` is the version of the simulation the class was made with (4 now; missing = 1). Version 4 plays exactly like version 3 (a test checks whole seasons) except that a season can match its draft class's shape (`p.collegeSeason.cs`), and its summary has more wordings; version 3 seasons keep their text. Version 3 classes also store the prospect's position (`p.collegeSeason.pos`), so a guards' or big men's year is rebuilt the same way after his ratings change. A version 1 class (made before the weekly polls, the NIT and the storylines) is rebuilt playing every game exactly as it was played then, so its champion, records, final AP poll, bracket and prospects' lines do not change (a test pins this against hashes of the old output). It still gets the weekly polls, the NET, the bubble (in the order its field was really picked: SRS and record), a written summary and an NIT bracket, but its NIT games are not counted in records or prospects' stats (the tab says so), and its shape is always _a balanced year_. Prospects added later to such a class (only missing) keep it version 1.
- **Cache:** the rebuilt season is kept per class (last 8, keyed by league, draft year, seed, version and every prospect's school and boost), so the page and player pages do not replay it on every load (about 40 ms to build).
- **Storage:** no new store. Each prospect at an NCAA school gets `p.collegeSeason = { season, seed, school, boost, summary, v }`; the season is rebuilt exactly from the class's seed and every prospect's school and boost (`getCollegeSeason` in `worker/core/draft/collegeSeason.ts`), deterministic per seed. Generating stats for only the prospects missing them keeps the class's existing season (newcomers join with boost 0). Removing a class's college stats removes it. Engine: `common/collegeStats/ncaa.ts` (tests in `ncaa.test.ts`), stats integration in `common/collegeStats/generate.ts` (`ncaaSeason` option) and `season.ts` (`teamGames`/`postseasonGames`).

### College stats calibration (colstats round)

Audit of the draft-year college line of generated classes (6 classes of real BBGM prospects, expanded ratings off and on, NCAA schools with 40 minute games, 10+ games) against Division I numbers, and the fixes in `common/collegeStats/season.ts`:

- **Why it looked inflated:** every prospect was a 30 minute starter (base 32 mpg, p10 25 mpg, only talent moved it by 2.7 per class SD), so the bottom of a class scored 11 ppg; per-40 rates were already about right. Now a class-average prospect is a ~22 minute rotation player and minutes come from role (4.3 per class SD of talent, freshmen -2.6 / seniors +1.6, endurance), capped at 36.5.
- **Strengths and weaknesses:** the 3PA share is a smooth function of tp (none at tp 22 or less, the height's share at about 37, up to 1.7x for snipers, plus a little for stretch bigs) instead of a step at tp 30 that let non-shooters take a third of their shots from three; 3P% has a steeper tp slope (and leans on csh with expanded ratings, capped at 47%); FT rate reads dunk/inside aggression; rebounds read hgt, reb, jumping and wingspan (bigs 10-13 per 40); steals read perimeter D (diq) more than speed and come up to D-I levels (~1.3 per 40); blocks read jumping, interior D (diq) and wingspan more; ball security moves turnovers more; usage leans more on the scoring composite than on overall talent; the efficiency premium is a little smaller and the rim FG% cap is 78%.
- **No change elsewhere:** the stats use their own seeded streams, so nothing about draft class generation, the game sim or ratings changes; stored seasons are not rewritten. Two story hooks in `career.ts` were retuned for the new minutes (a bust's freshman minutes boost 2.5 → 4).
- **Tests:** `collegeStats/calibration.test.ts` (level and spread, shooting splits, size-based rebounding/blocks, rating correlations, expanded rating effects, freshmen vs seniors); `generate.test.ts` average mpg range 24-33 → 19-27.

### College stats round 2 (colstats2): what a new league actually shows

The colstats audit only looked at the current draft class. A new league has seven (`numDraftClasses`), and every class made after that is made seven years ahead, so nearly every class a user drafts from had its stats generated years before its draft year.

- **Root cause:** `toCollegeProspect` projected the ratings of a class years away forward (`forwardProject`: about +4 a year at 18, +3 at 19, +2.4 at 20, so +10 to +25 on every skill for classes 3-6 years out), assuming prospects develop before the draft. They don't: `newPhasePreseason` develops only players with tid ≥ FREE_AGENT, so an undrafted prospect's ratings now are his ratings in his draft year. The class z (minutes, usage) is relative and barely moved, but everything read from absolute ratings did: the 3PA share saturated for everyone (non-shooters with tp 25-29 projected to 45+ took 60% of their shots from three), 3P% and FT% rose, assist rates from pss rose. So classes 3-6 years out had median 13.5-15.9 ppg, 3.9-4.7 apg and 3PA rates of 0.55 (the current class: 9 ppg, 2 apg, 0.17-0.3), and every prospect looked like a high-assist, high-volume three-point shooter. The UI showed exactly what was stored; there was no display bug.
- **Fix:** no projection (`toCollegeProspect` reads the stored ratings). Partial generation (prospects missing stats, a remake of some) measures class rank against the whole class (`classProspects` option). Ratings lean a little more on the matching stat: rebounds 0.15 hgt + 0.13 reb per point, blocks read interior D (0.045 per point) more and jumping less, bigs get fewer assists than their passing says (−2.4 per 40 at full size), a non-shooter's 3PA share no longer gets random threes (the noise scales with his willingness), usage 0.044 per SD, minutes 4.8 per class SD capped at 35.5, rim FG% capped at 75% and post-ups at 66%. The G League is a pro roster (34 mpg cap, usage −0.035, minutes −5), and Overtime Elite and prep/postgrad seasons are 32-minute games at a per-game pace.
- **Existing leagues:** generated stats carry `p.collegeStatsV` (`COLLEGE_STATS_VERSION`, now 2). When a league is opened (`beforeLeague`), `draft.upgradeCollegeStats` remakes every undrafted prospect with generated stats (a stored career) and no or an older stamp, class by class, with the class's own seed (the one stored with its college season, else the automatic seed) and the class's college season kept exactly (schedule, results, bracket, each prospect's place and boost). Generation is deterministic, so this is a one-time, repeatable change. Drafted players keep the stats they were drafted with, and imported rows without a stored career are not touched.
- **Tests:** `worker/core/draft/collegeStatsUiPath.test.ts` builds seven classes the way a new league does (preseason, auto seed, the worker's `generateCollegeStats`) and reads the lines off the stored prospects: every class on the same scale, D-I levels and spread, top-5 vs the rest, the class leader in assists/blocks/rebounds/threes/steals ranks high in pss/hgt+idef/hgt+reb/tp/pdef, non-shooters and non-shooting bigs take almost no threes, pass-first guards vs rim protectors, the version stamp, and `upgradeCollegeStats` (remakes a stale class exactly from its seed, keeps its college season, leaves drafted players alone, then has nothing left to do).

### Prospect careers: transfers, redshirts, recruiting, unusual prospects

Part of _College & Overseas Stats_ (no toggle of its own): everything here is made when a class's college stats are generated, and is biography built around the ratings a prospect already has. Nothing reads back into ratings, age, value or how draft classes are generated for the pro game.

- **Where:** the player's _College/Overseas_ page (`/l/:lid/player_pre_draft/:pid`): a story line under the cards, _Scouting report_, _Teams_ (class year, notes), _Stats_, _Recruiting_, _Draft stock_.
- **Multi-school careers** (`common/collegeStats/career.ts`): a career plan is drawn per prospect from his own stream (seed, draft year, pid), separate from his stats stream, so a prospect with a plain career gets the same seasons as before. The draft-year school is always his own (the one tied to the simulated college season); earlier years can be elsewhere.
  - _Transfers_ happen between seasons, never during one (one school per season): up-transfers (a breakout at a smaller school, then a bigger program), down-transfers (buried on a big program's bench, then real minutes) and lateral ones. About 13% of sophomores, 25% of juniors and 34% of seniors at NCAA schools have transferred; a few seniors twice. With _More pre-draft paths_ (below) coaching changes drive some of them and each has a reason.
  - _Redshirt_ (about 6-11% of multi-year players, more for lesser prospects): his first year on campus with no games (no stats row; listed in _Teams_). It takes the place of a season, since his age is fixed: a redshirt junior (_RS Jr._) is 22. A 23-year-old senior usually has a redshirt year as his fifth year.
  - _Medical redshirt_ (about 4% of juniors and seniors): a season that ended after 3-9 games and did not count against his eligibility.
  - _Junior college_ (about 4-9% of juniors and seniors, more for lesser prospects): one or two NJCAA seasons (inflated numbers against weak competition, _NJCAA All-American_ / _All-Region_ honors) before a Division I school.
  - Class years (_Fr._, _RS So._ ...) are eligibility years. Honors, coaches and team records follow the school of each season.
- **Unusual prospects:** each prospect has a small seeded chance (about 8-10% of a class end up with one) of a story that fits what he already is: _five-star bust_ (a below-average prospect in his class: a top-25 recruit and lottery projection on arrival, fewer minutes every year), _unranked riser_ (a top prospect nobody ranked: bench minutes and often a small school or junior college early), _late-blooming senior_ (barely played until his last season), _7'3"+ project_ (from his listed height: raw, limited minutes), _undersized scorer from a small school_ (6'1" or under at a low-major: carries the offense), _older international_ (22+, a pro abroad since 17 or 18, up to six seasons), _reclassified freshman_ (enrolled a year early), _injury-shortened season_ (his draft-year season ends after 5-15 games; with the college season on he misses the postseason). Minutes, usage, the draft stock timeline and the recruiting rank reflect it, and the page says it in a line.
- **Recruiting:** for prospects with a US high school path (an NCAA or junior college first stop): stars, national and position rank, composite rating (a fixed function of the rank that agrees with the stars), the number of offers and the programs worth naming, finalists, early (November) or late (April) signing, and high school all-star selections (generic names: _High School All-American Game_, _National Senior All-Star Classic_, _Prep Hoops Summit_, _State Player of the Year_). Ranks are against the rest of his high school class, which is spread over several draft classes, so no two players of a class share a national rank or a rank at their position (`worker/core/draft/recruiting.ts`, `resolveClassRanks` in `profile.ts`). The rank is set once, when his stats are generated, and stored; players ranked later fit around it. Offers, finalists and the rest are derived from the rank and the player (not stored), so they also appear for older classes.
- **Scouting report:** strengths, weaknesses and a projection (`common/collegeStats/scoutingReport.ts`), written only from what the user can already see: draft-year ratings with scouting fuzz applied (never true ratings; none at all with the hidden ratings challenge, when the report is written from production alone), the archetype label Draft Scouting shows, height, pre-draft production and where the user's board has him (or where he went). Player traits are never used. Appears for old classes too.
- **Storage:** `p.preDraftCareer = { years: [{ season, school, level, cls, status?, move? }], story?, hsClass?, recruit?: { rank, posRank } }` on prospects whose stats were generated with the career model; removed with the class's college stats. Stats rows are unchanged (one row per season played, `teamName` = that season's school). **Classes generated before this keep their stored seasons**: the page falls back to what the rows say (one class year per row, recruiting rank derived as before and only bumped to the next free rank on a collision within the high school class). A stored career that no longer matches the rows (edited or re-imported stats) is ignored.
- **Engine hooks:** `season.ts` `mpgShift`, `usageShift`, `gamesCap` (all optional; nothing changes without them). Tests: `career.test.ts`, `profile.test.ts`, `scoutingReport.test.ts`, `worker/core/draft/recruiting.test.ts`.

### More pre-draft paths

- **Where:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features)_, as `featurePreDraftPaths` (`preDraftPaths` in the registry; off in a college league, like the college stats it is part of). It changes what new pre-draft careers look like when a class's college stats are generated: the player's _College/Overseas_ page (Teams notes, the tier next to each season abroad, the scouting report), _Draft Scouting → College/Overseas → Overseas_ (a _Tier_ column, the pathway team with a _Pathway_ badge), the player page's _Before the draft_ line (_Pro pathway: ..._), the Draft Board's pre-draft tag.
- **Transfer portal** (`common/collegeStats/career.ts`): a transfer is now tied to the coaching history the College/Overseas page already shows (`getHeadCoach`). When his draft-year school started a season with a new head coach during his years there, a transfer is 1.8x as likely, otherwise 0.62x, and the season the new coach arrived is 3x as likely to be the one he came in. That keeps the overall share where it was: 23% of players with two or more Division I seasons transferred (25% before), within the 15-25% target. Each transfer has a reason (`why`): _coach_ (either school changed coaches that season, about a third), _minutes_ (down and lateral moves, which already came with fewer minutes before: buried on a bench), _breakout_ (up-transfers after a big year at a smaller program). Every season line already carried the school he played for; the Teams table now says _Transferred from Wofford to Duke after his sophomore season, after a breakout season_ (or _..., to play for Duke's new coach_, _..., after a coaching change at Wofford_, _..., looking for more playing time_). Older careers without a reason get the plain sentence.
- **Pro pathway program:** 0-2 elite high school prospects a class (about 0.8 on average; fewer when a class has fewer of them) skip college for one season with a professional development team, _Ascent Pro Pathway_ (a made-up name). Candidates: North Americans young enough to be freshmen, headed to a Division I school, with class-relative draft stock (0.4 ovr z + 0.6 pot z) of 0.85 or more (about the top fifth); up to the 10 best, drawn from the class's own stream (seed, draft year) weighted toward the best. Read off the whole class, so remaking only some prospects picks the same ones; `getNcaaSchools` leaves them out of the college season. Their one season is 48-minute games against pro development teams (`PATHWAY_ENV`: pace 100, 30 games, 27-minute cap for a teenager, minutes −10 per 48 relative to a college role, usage −0.04, shooting efficiency −0.05), level of competition 84. For the same players that is about 7 fewer minutes a game than in college, 2-5 points lower FG% and 1.5-4 points lower TS%, and fewer points per game (per 40 minutes about the same: pros play faster). An elite one averages about 22 minutes, 14 points on 45% shooting, in line with real pro pathway seasons. No honors, no story (the pathway is his story), a top-25 recruiting rank (`adjustRecruitRank`), the scouting report says his numbers undersell him a little. His `college` field is kept (a blank one is filled with the team, as for any destination, and is treated as blank again when stats are remade).
- **Overseas tiers:** every overseas league has a tier from its level of competition (`getLeagueTier`): _top_ 76+ (EuroLeague, Liga ACB, NBL, Chinese CBA, LNB Pro A, EuroCup), _mid_ 60-75 (most first divisions), _development_ under 60 (NBL1, CEBL, PBA, LNBP, Puerto Rico BSN, New Zealand NBL, and a new generic _European Second Division_, strength 54, which is only a step on the way up: it is not in the destination list, so where blank colleges go is unchanged). His draft year is in his own league; going back a season at a time he was a tier lower with a chance by his age that season (55% at 17, 45% at 18, 36% at 19, 27% at 20, 20% at 21, 12% at 22+; x0.5 for the top of the class, x1.25 for the rawest), in a league of that tier weighted by where he was born, and never higher further back. In a test of 600 prospects abroad, an earlier season at 19 or younger is in a lower tier than his draft year 55% of the time, one at 21 or older 24%. Each tier is its leagues' environments: for the same 19-year-olds, top tier about 15 minutes and 7 points (EuroLeague), mid tier about 21 and 11, development tier about 25 and 15, and FG% rises about 2 points from top to development (`calibration` style test in `paths.test.ts`). The tier is stored with each season abroad (`tier`) and shown next to the league; careers stored before tiers show the tier read off their league. A move up gets a note (_Moved up from Polish PLK (mid tier)_), and the scouting report weighs the tier of his last league.
- **Randomness:** each part has its own seeded stream (`|tiers` per prospect, `|pathway` per class) or none (coaching changes are the programs' own streams), so a prospect with none of these gets exactly the seasons he had before (about 89% of a typical class; a test pins whole classes with the paths off against hashes taken before them). A pathway prospect leaves his would-be college team, so in a class with a college season that team (and the season around it) is not the one it would have been. College vs overseas shares are unchanged (the same number abroad; college + pathway = college before).
- **Old leagues:** no version bump: stats and careers already generated stay as they are (`upgradeCollegeStats` does not touch them), and only classes generated from now on use the new paths. Missing `why`/`tier` read as none / the league's tier. `p.preDraftCareer` is not in the league file schema (players allow extra properties), so the new fields go out and come back with exports and imports, and the existing import normalization (season shift) keeps them.
- **Storage:** `p.preDraftCareer.years[i]` gains `why?: "coach" | "minutes" | "breakout"`, `tier?: "top" | "mid" | "dev"`, and `level` can be `"pathway"`. Stats rows are unchanged (`teamName` is the league abroad, or _Ascent Pro Pathway_).
- **Engine:** `common/collegeStats/career.ts` (`planTiers`, `tierDropChance`, `pickLeagueOfTier`, `getMoveNote`), `generate.ts` (`getPathwayKeys`, the `paths` option, default on), `overseas.ts` (`PATHWAY_ENV`, `getLeagueTier`, `TIER_NAMES`, an optional `effShift` on a league environment, read in `season.ts`), `colleges.ts` (`OVERSEAS_EXTRA`, `PATHWAY_TEAM`), `scoutingReport.ts` (`getPathNote`). Tests: `common/collegeStats/paths.test.ts` (paths off = before, shares, transfer share and reasons, notes, pathway picks and environment, tiers only going up, younger seasons lower, tier environments).
- **Routes** (`career.ts` `pickCollegeRoute`, `applyCollegeRoute`, `getRouteText`; `generate.ts` `getClassRoutes`): how a prospect got to the draft when it was not the usual way, stored as `p.preDraftCareer.route` and told in a line under the story on the College/Overseas page (_Post-graduate year._ Spent a post-graduate year at Brewster Academy after graduating from high school in 2026, then enrolled at ...). Each is drawn from a stream of its own (`|routes`, `|route`, `|twoSport`, the class's `|nextStars` / `|abroad`), never the game's random numbers, and only rewrites biography (schools, minutes, games): ratings, age, ovr, value and position never change (measured below). A Division I prospect with a story, a redshirt or a junior college start gets no route; the rest:
  - _Post-graduate year_ (`postgrad`, a late reclassification; about 3% of Americans, 10% of Canadians and internationals at Division I schools with two or more seasons): his first season at a prep school (`PREP_SCHOOLS`, real public programs like IMG Academy, Brewster Academy, Putnam Science Academy; 32-minute games, level of competition 34) where he was one of the best players on the floor, then Division I a year behind his high school class. The early reclassification is the existing _reclassified freshman_ story.
  - _Academy_ (`academy`, about 16% of internationals at Division I schools): one or two seasons at a youth academy for his part of the world (`ACADEMIES`: _NBA Academy_ for Africa, Asia and Latin America, _NBA Global Academy_ for Oceania, and the made-up generic _European Youth Academy_ for a European club's youth team) before coming to the United States. Separately, 40% of internationals abroad whose first season (at 17 or 18) is in the development tier spent it at their academy instead (`planTiers`), then turned pro.
  - _Division II or NAIA to Division I_ (`smallCollege`, about 2% of North Americans with three or more seasons, more for lesser prospects): one or two seasons at a Division II or NAIA program (`SMALL_COLLEGES`, real programs; level of competition 42 / 38) with inflated numbers and _Division II_ / _NAIA All-American_ or _All-Region_ honors against the junior college bars, then a move up (_Transferred up from Ferris State (Division II)_). A further Division I transfer is half as likely, as after a junior college.
  - _A year away_ (`gap`, `military`, `mission`; together about 0.7% of prospects with three or more seasons, military and church missions for Americans only, a mission only for prospects 21 or older): his first year after high school has no team and no games (_Gap year_ / _Military service_ / _Church mission_ in the Teams table with dashes, no stats row), and he is rusty in his first season back. It does not use eligibility.
  - _Two-sport athlete_ (`twoSport`, about 0.7% of Americans at Division I schools): also on the football team every season but his last, joining the basketball team after the football season (7-12 games missed, fewer minutes; _Joined the team after the football season_ in the notes).
  - _NBL Next Stars_ (`nextStars`): 0-2 a class (about 0.5 on average) of the class's 20 best young prospects by draft stock (0.3+), not Australians or New Zealanders, North Americans only if headed to a Division I school, internationals 2.5x as likely as North Americans, spend their draft year in Australia's NBL instead of college or their own league.
  - _An American abroad_ (`abroad`): 0-1 a class (about 0.15) of the Americans headed to a mid-level role at a Division I school (draft stock -0.2 to 1.4) turned pro out of high school with a club in Israel, Italy, Spain, Turkey, Greece, China, France or the Adriatic League.
  - The classic routes are as before: _junior college to Division I_ (now from 85 NJCAA programs, `juco.ts`: the original 45, which a career with the paths off still draws from, and 40 more), _Division I transfers_, _redshirt_ and _medical redshirt_ (the injury redshirt), the _reclassified freshman_ story, the pro pathway program, the G League, Overtime Elite and the NBA Academy as destinations of blank colleges.
  - A prospect picked for the Next Stars or abroad is never one with a story (the class draw skips prospects whose own story draw gives one), so every story, and the sub-rating shape a story styles, is the same as before.
- **More leagues** (`colleges.ts` `OVERSEAS_EXTRA`, `LEAGUE_HOME`; `overseas.ts` `LEAGUE_ENV`, `PRO_SHORT`): the existing overseas leagues (EuroLeague, EuroCup, Champions League, Liga ACB, LNB Pro A, Italian LBA, Bundesliga, Turkish BSL, Greek Basket League, Lithuanian LKL, Adriatic League, VTB, Polish PLK, BNXT, Israeli Premier League, NBL, NBL1, Chinese CBA, Japan B.League, Korean KBL, Philippine PBA, Brazil NBB, Argentine Liga Nacional, Mexican LNBP, Puerto Rico BSN, New Zealand NBL, CEBL, Basketball Africa League; BSN and New Zealand NBL now with environments of their own) plus 33: second divisions and smaller first divisions in Europe (LEB Oro, LNB Pro B, Serie A2, German ProA, Turkish TBL, Greek Elite League, ABA League Second Division, Serbian KLS, Croatian, Slovenian, Czech, Latvian-Estonian, Israeli Liga Leumit, Hungarian, Bulgarian, Ukrainian, Georgian, Finnish, British, Portuguese leagues and the FIBA Europe Cup), Asia (Japan B2, Chinese NBL, Taiwan TPBL, Lebanese, Iranian leagues), Latin America (Venezuelan SPB, Dominican LNB, Uruguayan LUB, Chilean LNB) and Africa (Angolan, Egyptian, Nigerian leagues). Each has an environment (pace, game length, a teenager's minutes cap, minutes and usage relative to a college role, season length), a tier from its level of competition (all of these are development or mid tier), a short name for its honors (_All-LEB Oro First Team_, _Serie A2 Best Young Player_...; no two leagues share one) and the countries it is the domestic league of.
  - _Tied to birth country_ (`getCountry`, `homeLeagueFactor`): a prospect is 5x as likely to play in his own country's leagues (2x for a continental league, the BAL) and 0.55x as likely in another country's domestic league; leagues that draw from everywhere (EuroLeague, EuroCup, Champions League, Europe Cup, the G League) are unchanged. This applies to the draft year club of a blank college and to the lower tier leagues of his earlier seasons. With the paths on, a blank college picks from the full list (the old destinations plus the new leagues' `dest` weights) with better prospects leaning to stronger leagues and an international 0.5x as likely to be in the G League; who goes to college, and which college, is drawn exactly as before.
  - _Youth national teams_ (`honors.ts` `getYouthHonors`): a season at 17-20 that stood out earns _All-Star Five_ or _MVP_ at the FIBA youth event of his age and region (U17 World Cup, U18 EuroBasket / AfroBasket / Asia Cup / AmeriCup, U19 World Cup, U20 EuroBasket), with a higher bar for Americans. About 0.7 a class.
  - _Draft-and-stash_ uses the full list: a prospect from any of these leagues can be stashed, and a stashed player who has outgrown a development or mid tier league (ovr 44+ / 49+) moves up a tier for the next season (`getStashLeague`, seeded by player and season, most likely to his own country's league).
- **More colleges:** Division I already had all 364 programs (`COLLEGES`), with the prestige that keeps top prospects at realistic programs. New: the BBGM college names of programs listed under another name (_Brigham Young_ → BYU, _Louisiana-Lafayette_ → Louisiana, _Arkansas-Little Rock_ → Little Rock, _Houston Baptist_, _IUPUI_, _Nebraska-Omaha_, _Texas Rio Grande Valley_; `COLLEGE_ALIASES`) now play for that school (its conference and college season) instead of an unknown one, 43 Division II and NAIA programs, 20 prep schools, 3 academies and 40 more junior colleges, all as steps on the way (a draft year is still at a Division I school or one of the generic destinations).
- **Labels:** `getPreDraftLabel` names every pre-draft season _Overseas_, _G League_, _Pro pathway_, _JUCO_, _Prep_, _Academy_ (also Overtime Elite), _Small college_ or _College_ (`isSchoolLabel`: the first three are clubs). The College/Overseas page (season badges, Teams league column: _Prep / Postgrad_, _Youth academy_, _NCAA Division II_, _NAIA_), the player page's _Before the draft_ line, the Draft Board tag, Draft Scouting → College/Overseas (only clubs in the Overseas table), the profile export's _Path to the pros_ and the scouting report's competition level (prep, academy and small college seasons count as weak competition; the Next Stars, an American abroad and a two-sport athlete get a projection note) all use it. The bio college is still `getPreDraftSchool` everywhere (bio, newspaper, exports, Hall of Fame plaques, Colleges page), and a route's earlier stops never are: it is his draft year school or club.
- **Measured** (`worker/core/draft/preDraftRoutes.script.test.ts`, `RUN_ROUTES=1 ROUTES_CLASSES=40`: 40 generated classes, 2800 prospects, birthplaces and colleges drawn like the real game's, before vs after): every prospect's ratings, ovr, position, birthplace and value hash the same. Draft year destinations: Division I 2358 → 2359 (the unknown BBGM names now at their schools), overseas 330 → 369 (20 Next Stars, 5 Americans abroad), G League 31 → 19, pro pathway 26 → 26. Routes: post-graduate 22, Division II/NAIA start 11, academy 5 (+3 academy starts abroad), years away 5, two-sport 3, Next Stars 20, abroad 5: 74 (2.6% of prospects), next to junior college 32, Division I transfers 146, redshirts 62, medical redshirts 14. Top 10 of each class (400): Division I 310 → 305 (plus 3 before at BBGM names it did not know, now Division I), overseas 53 → 52, pathway 26 → 26, Next Stars 0 → 9, G League 2 → 5, Overtime Elite 3 → 0, generic prep/NAIA/NBA Academy destinations 3 → 2, an American abroad 0 → 1. Internationals at a club abroad: EuroLeague 98 → 80, Liga ACB 32 → 21, LNB Pro B 0 → 15, Adriatic League 18 → 20, ABA Second Division 0 → 10, NBL 8 → 15, BAL 1 → 6, G League 28 → 14; 76 of 346 in a league of their own country; 50 different draft year leagues (was 27). Earlier seasons abroad: top 289 → 257, mid 259 → 216, development 83 → 218.
- **Storage:** `p.preDraftCareer.route?` (`RouteKind`), `years[i].level` can also be `"prep" | "academy" | "dii" | "naia" | "gap"`, `status` `"gap"`, `move` `"small" | "prep" | "academy"`, `football?: true`. Old careers have none of these and read as before. Tests: `common/collegeStats/routes.test.ts` (shares, each route's career, no story lost, labels, youth honors, every league's environment and short name, home league lean, stash move-up), and the career/paths tests updated for the new levels.
- **Not included:** transfers between junior colleges or out of a pathway season, a pathway season for anyone but freshmen, a second pathway team, tiers for the G League (it is domestic), and coaching changes in an overseas club.

### Pre-draft context: schedule, teams, roles and age

- **Where:** _Always on_ wherever college/overseas stats are generated (default mechanics, basketball; no switch: `generateCollegeClass`'s `context: false` option exists only so tests can pin the old output). Shown on a prospect's _College/Overseas_ page (new _Context-adjusted stats_ and _Notable teammates_ sections, role notes in _Teams_), an _AgeProd_ column on _Draft Scouting_, and a bounded input to AI drafting and the Mock Draft. Code: `common/collegeStats/teams.ts`, `roles.ts`, `production.ts`, the season model hooks in `season.ts` / `generate.ts`, and `worker/core/draft/classTeams.ts`, `production.ts`.
- **Strength of schedule** (`season.ts`): every school or league already had a level of competition (conference scale, 0-100). Outside the pros it now moves more than shooting: efficiency is 0.16 points per point of strength from a mid-major schedule (75; was 0.11 from 70), and rebounds, assists, steals and blocks per 40 minutes are 0.4% lower (turnovers 0.4% higher) per point, so the same talent posts better raw numbers at Albany than at Duke. Pro leagues keep their own environments. The College/Overseas page shows each season's _SOS_ with raw and _schedule-adjusted_ numbers: _Adj Pts/40_ and _Adj GmSc/40_ (x(1 + 0.005 per point above 75), bounded 0.75-1.15) and _Adj BPM_ (+0.15 per point).
- **Team context** (`teams.ts`): a college, junior college, prep school, small college, academy or the pro pathway team is one roster a season. A team season (`getTeamSeason`, from a stream of the school and season, no draft year) has its games (drawn exactly as the page's estimated record draws them, so record and games played always agree; at most 34 below Division I), a pace (68 possessions per 40 +/- 4.5%) and how deep its supporting cast is. Each prospect's minutes and usage are shifted by his program (stronger programs: about 0.9 minutes and 0.12 usage sd less per 10 points of strength above a typical prospect's program, 84; weaker ones more), the depth draw, and the prospects on his roster: each teammate takes `max(0, z + 1.2)` load, 0.16 usage sd per load point, and 1.3 minutes per load point scaled by how close their positions are (half from a lesser teammate). A team's prospects together stay within a budget (72% of the team's possessions, 160 minutes a game); beyond it the class being made gives back, the lesser prospects most. His team's pace drives his possessions and the usage/assist/steal rates (`calculateAdvanced` `paceRatio`). The page shows _Pace_, _USG%_, per 40 and _Pts/100_ (per 100 possessions on the floor).
- **Class-aware teams:** rosters are shared by every draft class. Before a class's stats are made, `getClassTeamContext` gathers its prospects' possible teammates (five classes either side): classes with stats from their stored careers (`p.preDraftCareer`, which drafted players keep), classes without from their plan (`planClassMembers`, the class's auto seed, nothing simulated), and the games of team seasons another class's college season played (its draft year). A 2027 freshman and a 2026 junior at Kentucky are teammates in 2026: same games and pace, and each one's role makes room for the other's. Each season keeps what a teammate's generation needs to know about him (`tz` talent, `xm`/`xu` expected minutes and usage before teammates), and every member's role is worked out the same way whether his class is planned or made, so remaking a class with its seed gives the same stats (`upgradeCollegeStats` test). Only the class being made gives back to a team's budget: a new class joins existing rosters without changing anyone's stored stats. Linked college leagues keep their own rosters: a linked prospect is on no roster here (`linked`), and classes with imported stats but no generated career are not planned. Existing leagues: nothing is migrated; rosters and teammates are rebuilt lazily from stored careers (an old career without `tz` reads talent from his draft ovr and minutes/usage from his stats row), and only classes generated from now on play around their teammates. The College/Overseas page lists up to 5 _notable teammates_ a season (any class, drafted with pick, or upcoming) from the players in the cache.
- **Role changes** (`roles.ts`, stream `|role` per prospect): his hidden trajectory (lateness, -1 early to 1 late bloomer) puts a late bloomer 0.22 sd further behind and 1.5 minutes shorter per season back (early bloomers the other way), so his last seasons are the jump; breakout seasons (10% of seasons after one he played, x(1 + 0.6 lateness): +3-5 minutes, +0.45 usage sd, +0.15 talent) and down seasons (7%, x(1 - 0.6 lateness)) on top, never for a prospect with a story; 35% of freshmen (more for better ones, fewer for late bloomers) come off the bench and move into the starting lineup (minutes ramp up 4-8 over the season, starts only once he plays a starter's minutes, never in the first quarter of the season). Transfers keep the career model's bigger and smaller roles, and a teammate leaving opens minutes by itself. Stored as `p.preDraftCareer.years[i].role` (`breakout`, `slump`, `midStarter`) and noted in the Teams table.
- **Age-adjusted production** (`production.ts`): schedule-adjusted game score per 40 minutes of his last two pre-draft seasons (the last x1.5, minutes weighted) plus 1.8 per year younger than 20.5. The slope is the one that best predicted ovr three pro seasons later in the measurement below. On the College/Overseas page (with his rank in the class), as _AgeProd_ on Draft Scouting, and as a nudge in `runPicks` (AI teams) and the Mock Draft: 0.8 value points per class sd, scaled by minutes/(minutes + 400), bounded at +/-1.5 (_Productive for his age_ in the mock's reasons). Ratings still decide nearly every pick.
- **Unchanged:** ratings, ovr, pot, positions and values of every prospect (the measurement hashes them: identical); who goes where (schools, routes, stories).
- **Measured** (`worker/core/draft/classTeams.script.test.ts`, `RUN_CLASS_TEAMS=1 CLASS_TEAMS_LEAGUES=8`: 8 leagues x 4 upcoming classes, 2240 prospects with real-looking colleges and birthplaces, made class by class as a new league does; before -> after):
  - Draft-year minutes median 20.6 -> 19.9 (p10 13.7 -> 12.7, p90 28.6 -> 28.6); usage median 23.5% -> 22.9%. Freshmen 18.3 -> 17.5 minutes, 22.1% -> 21.4% usage.
  - 602 team seasons with 2+ prospects: their summed share of the team's possessions median 0.27 -> 0.25, p90 0.44 -> 0.38; minutes p90 70 -> 64 a game. Usage (residual on ovr) with a better prospect teammate +0.28 -> -1.37 points, without one -0.04 -> +0.20.
  - Prospects with a prospect teammate: 48.2% (any class), 22.5% with one from another draft class (who is at which school is BBGM's college assignment, unchanged).
  - Breakout seasons (+6 ppg on the year before, team seasons): 6.3% -> 9.7%; slumps (-3 ppg) 2.2% -> 6.5%. By trajectory, breakouts early/mid/late bloomers 6.0/6.0/7.6% -> 4.0/9.8/14.1%, slumps 3.0/1.9/2.7% -> 11.0/6.0/4.6%.
  - Stats rows with more games played than the team's record shows: 1477 of 3187 -> 0.
  - Correlation with ovr after three seasons of pro development (the game's `develop`, seeded): raw game score per 40 0.404 -> 0.383 (roles add context noise), schedule- and age-adjusted 0.449 -> 0.451 (slope 0 / 0.6 / 1.2 / 1.8 / 2.4 / 3: 0.412 / 0.435 / 0.448 / 0.451 / 0.446 / 0.436).
  - Generation time 87 -> 119 ms a class.
- **Storage:** `p.preDraftCareer.years[i]` gains `role?`, `pace?`, `tz?`, `xm?`, `xu?` (not in the league file schema; old careers read as the average pace and no role). Stats rows are unchanged. Nothing else is stored: team seasons, rosters, adjusted lines and production are computed when needed.
- **Tests:** `common/collegeStats/teams.test.ts` (team seasons and records, star teammate, locked members, budget, shared games and pace across two classes, determinism, role arcs by trajectory, freshman starts, adjusted lines, age adjustment, bounded nudges), `worker/core/draft/classTeams.test.ts` (two classes in the game path, teammates found, production nudges average zero), `collegeStatsUiPath.test.ts` (a remake gives the same stats).

### Draft board and AI drafting

- **Draft Board** (_Draft Board_): `/l/:lid/draft_board[/:season]`, linked from Draft Scouting and the Draft page. Rank your prospects, give them tiers and notes next to their pre-draft stats, builds and scout reports. Option _Auto Draft From My Board_: when auto drafting for you, the top-ranked available prospect on your board is picked. The Draft page also has a _Draft-day trades & team boards_ window (trades through the trade API, each AI team's needs and board).
- **AI drafts by need** (_AI Drafts by Need_, basketball): the need, roster and timeline factors of the AI draft decision below. Off: no fit factors (the information factors of the other settings still apply).
- **AI draft decision and Why** (basketball; `draft/draftDecision.ts` pure, `draft/draftDecisionData.ts` league data, used by `runPicks.ts`, the Mock Draft and draft-night trade boards). An AI team scores every prospect left as his true value (the AI's board, as always) plus bounded adjustments in value points, then picks with the same weighted choice as before (score ** 69, the main random stream, one draw per pick, so the draft stays seeded). Two kinds of factors:
  - _Fit_ (with _AI Drafts by Need_; times a pick scale of 0.3 at No. 1 rising to 1 by about pick 19, 1.15 in later rounds, so the best player available dominates the top of the draft): positional need (guard/wing/big share of the top 10, `draftNeeds.ts`, ±2 times the GM's need-vs-BPA scale); skill/archetype need (+0.3 per skill label none of the top 8 has: shooting, ball handling, playmaking, rim protection, perimeter defense, rebounding, post scoring, athleticism; at most 2); crowding (-0.5 with 3+ young players at his position in the top 10, -0.2 with 2: minutes blocked); timeline (contender: half projection-cone floor, half ovr now above the average of the 30 best left; rebuilder: cone ceiling; 0.08 per point, ±1.2, times 1 ± 0.4 by the GM's patience; cones from `scoutingReports/cone.ts` with true ratings, widened by the outlets' disagreement); positional scarcity (+0.4 when he is the only one of his position among the 9 best left, +0.2 with one other, for a team that needs the position); draft-and-stash (+0.5 for a prospect whose last season was overseas, after No. 10, when the roster is full or the team contends; +0.25 more for a frugal GM); coach system fit (0.25 per SD of fit to the head coach's offense 60% / defense 40%, ±0.5, Coach Systems); development-first front office (GM finance style: 0.04 per point of cone ceiling, ±0.4).
  - _Information_ (not scaled): age-adjusted pre-draft production (`draft/production.ts`, ±1.5, times 1 + 0.4 × the GM's analytics lean, +0.2 for an analytics finance style, clamped 0.5-1.6: one production term, no double counting); the outlets' consensus (0.02 per rank the consensus has him above his value rank, ±20 ranks, times 1 - 0.5 × analytics); combine performance (the old ±0.75 nudge) and athleticism (0.2 per 25 percentile points of the athletic drills, times 1 - 0.3 × analytics); medicals (-0.3 minor, -1.2 red flag on his actual risk, times 1 - 0.4 × the GM's risk tolerance); scout disagreement (0.3 × the GM's risk trait × the z-score of how far apart the outlets' boards are: gamblers like a split prospect, cautious GMs avoid him); the GM's upside lean (±1.5), analytics/old-school lens and player traits (as before).
  - The total is capped at ±1.2 value points at No. 1, rising to ±3.5 from the second round; a capped total scales every part alike. User teams drafting on auto still take the best player by value (or their board).
  - _Why_: for every AI pick, `explainPick` lists the top 1-4 factors that moved it, from the same parts: "Best player available on their board" when he was the best by value, then each factor where he beat the alternative (the best player by value when he wasn't it, else the runner-up by score) by at least 0.15, largest first, deduped; a medical risk he carried says "Medical flag lowered him, they took the risk"; draft-night trades add "Traded up to No. N to get him" or "Moved down and still got their guy". Examples: "Fills a need at big (only one big under contract)", "Adds shooting they lack", "Contender: ready to play now", "Upside swing (wide cone, high ceiling)", "Productive for his age", "Their GM loves analytics: production model #3", "Elite combine athlete", "Draft-and-stash: no roster spot", "Fits their Pace & Space system", "Cautious GM: steered clear of a prospect the scouts are split on". No ratings or value points (board ranks are public), so it is safe under No Visible Ratings. Stored on the player (`p.draft.why`), shown in the Draft page's _Why_ column (first reason, hover or click for all), in the draft news ("... Why: a; b.") and the Newspaper's draft-night story (_Why they picked him_, the top 8 picks), and on the Mock Draft.
  - Measured (`draftDecision.league.test.ts`, `RUN_DRAFT_DECISION=1`, 8 seeded new-league drafts, vs the previous logic): the top 3 by value went top 5 in every draft (100%, as before); mean |pick - value rank| 2.89 in the first round and 2.99 overall (before: 3.20 and 3.21, the total cap keeps picks closer to value); the best player by value went in 40.0% of picks (40.6%); picks with 3+ better players by value still on the board 25.4% (27.1%), and 9.6% of all picks are such reaches with a need, skill, scarcity or crowding reason. Most common reasons: traits, best available, combine athleticism, production for his age, the GM lens, upside swing, needs.
- **Mock draft** (_Mock Draft_, basketball): `/l/:lid/mock_draft`, a tab of Draft Scouting. A two-round mock draft of the current class (until its draft is over; during the draft, the remaining picks). Each team picks the way the AI does in `runPicks.ts`: the same AI draft decision (team context, class facts and factors, `draftDecision.ts`), with the same weighted random choice. The order is the league's draft order with traded picks once the lottery is done, otherwise one simulated lottery (like the projected Draft Lottery page). Pick protections and swaps are resolved on that order as a dry run with the same code as the real draft (`resolvePickConditionsPure` on copies: no saves, no news), and each affected pick says what happened (swapped, protected and stays, conveyed). Each pick shows the same Why as a real AI pick (`explainPick`), plus reach or steal against your scouting list; _Rerun_ draws again; your picks are highlighted. Pro projections: 120 careers per prospect run with the game's own development (`developSeasonSync`) from his ratings as your scouts see them, with a simple out-of-the-league rule, giving the median and 10th-90th percentile peak ovr and star (65+) / starter (58+) / bust (under 50 or gone within 4 seasons) odds, ported from the Draft Workshop. Cached per league, season and ratings in the worker; shown on the Mock Draft tab and as a _Pro projection_ section on a prospect's player page (`mockDraft.ts`, `proProjection.ts`, `mockDraftData.ts`).

### Draft-night trades

- **Where:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features)._ League Extras entry _Draft-Night Trades_ (`featureDraftNightTrades`, basketball; off in college leagues). Only in a real draft (draft phase), not the fantasy or expansion draft. Code: `worker/core/draft/draftNightTrades.ts`, the hook in `runPicks.ts`, `ui/views/Draft/DraftNightTrades.tsx`.
- **Pick value chart** (Draft page → _Pick value chart_): every pick still to be made in this draft with its value in points, where a typical No. 1 pick is 1000, next to the same pick in a typical class. A pick's value is the number the trade AI adds up for a pick whose place is known (`getPickSlotTradeValue` in `ValueChangeCalculator.ts`): the value of the prospect expected at that pick, from the prospects still on the board (`getPickValues`), on the trade value scale. So a deep class is worth more further down, a weak one less, and the chart moves as players are picked. "Typical class" is the average the AI uses for future picks.
- **AI-to-AI trades:** before each AI pick, a seeded chance of a trade attempt (20% in the first round, 7% later, times the _AI-to-AI Trades Factor_ setting; 0 turns them off). A team up to 10 picks below whose top prospect (its own board: the AI draft decision at that pick) will probably be gone before its pick (his consensus rank is ahead of its pick) is the buyer, picked by how urgent it is and its GM's trade appetite. The team on the clock says yes more often when its own top prospect should still be there at the buyer's pick, or it's rebuilding. Price: the buyer's pick plus the cheapest of its later picks of this draft or future picks (at most 2) that give the team moving down 3-12% more than its pick is worth by the trade AI's own valuation; the buyer may lose up to 40% of what it gives by its valuation (the price of getting the player it wants). Protected picks and picks with swap rights are never used. The trade must pass the trade summary checks (roster and cap rules) and is processed like any AI trade (`processTrade`: transactions, pick ownership, trade event) with the note "Draft night: the X traded up to No. N with the Y." in the news. At most 6 per draft; about 3 per draft on average (8 test drafts: 2 to 4 each).
- **Offers to you:** when your next pick is 3 picks away or closer, a card on the Draft page lists up to 3 offers: AI teams up to 12 picks behind you whose top prospect will probably be gone before their pick (seeded, 60% each) offer to move up into your pick, and AI teams ahead of you (when you're not on the clock yet) may offer to move down to your pick (more likely when their own top prospect should still be there at your pick, or they're rebuilding). Each offer shows what you give and get with chart points (future picks: a typical pick of their round, 10% less per year away, marked "about"). _Shop this pick_ asks every AI team up to 12 picks behind you and shows the best 5 offers. _Decline_ hides an offer for the rest of the session.
- **No free value:** an AI team moving up into your pick adds as much as it can while it still gains 3% by the trade AI's valuation with you as the partner (the same valuation as the Trade page, including its caution about your future picks), and it doesn't offer less than 80% of your pick's chart points. An AI team moving down asks for your pick plus the cheapest extras that give it 3%. _Accept_ goes through the normal trade proposal (`trade.create` + `trade.propose`), so the AI says yes only if the deal still works for it now, and the trade can be undone like any other.
- **Randomness:** every chance uses a seeded stream (`Rng`, keyed by the season, the pick and the team, with the league salt), never the game's main random stream: the AI draft selections draw the same random numbers as before, and the same league gives the same trade decisions. Offers are recomputed from the league's state, so reloading shows the same ones.
- **Old leagues and files:** nothing new is stored. The switch is forced on when an older league or file is loaded, like the other core features.
- **Tests:** `src/worker/core/draft/draftNightTrades.test.ts` (pricing helpers, urgency rule, attempt chance, a monotone chart for all 60 picks, no `Math.random` draws in AI trade attempts, every shopped offer gains for the AI by its own valuation, accepting moves the picks and a stale offer is refused, a whole draft with AI trades, at most 6).

### Frozen pre-draft ranks

- **Where:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ League Extras → _Frozen Pre-draft Ranks_ (`featureFrozenDraftRanks`, every sport, on for new leagues, off for existing ones; off in college leagues). Off: every rank is counted as before.
- **The bug it fixes:** the Draft page's _#_, Draft Scouting's _#_, the Draft Board's _Scouts_ column, the Mock Draft's _Board_ column (and its steal/reach notes), a prospect's projected pick and draft stock (College/Overseas page, stock timeline's _Now_ point, the scouting report's draft range) and the Newspaper's draft preview all ranked the class among the players **still undrafted**. Once the draft started, every pick moved everyone below up one, so the best player left became "#1": in a new league played to its draft, after 35 picks every one of the 35 players left had a different _#_ than before the draft, and the Draft page's #1 was a prospect ranked 31st before it (a second-round prospect in a 30-team league).
- **The snapshot:** when the draft phase starts (`newPhase` hook, after the lottery and the scouts' last board update), every prospect of the class gets `p.preDraftRank = { s, r, v, n }`: his rank on your scouts' list (`valueFuzz`, the old _#_ order), on the consensus board (true value, the order the AI drafts in and the Mock Draft and stock timeline used) and the class size. From then on (the class's draft has started: its season, phase ≥ draft, or any later season) every place above reads it; before the draft, ranks stay live.
- **Shown:** the Draft page's undrafted table has _Pre_ (pre-draft rank, frozen) and a separate _BA_ column (best available: the live order of the players left, never called his rank); the Draft Results table has a _Pre-draft_ column with the steal (+, green) or reach (−, red) in picks (5+ apart), titled "Drafted #12 · Pre-draft #34". Draft Scouting's _#_ becomes _Pre_ for the class being drafted, the Draft Board's _Scouts_ becomes _Pre-draft_, the Mock Draft of the remaining picks numbers its picks from the next real pick so its steal/reach notes compare with the frozen rank, the College/Overseas page says "pre-draft rank #N, projected #M before the draft" during the draft and "Drafted #12 · Pre-draft #34 (reach, −22)" after, and the player page's draft line adds "· Pre-draft #34".
- **Old leagues:** a class with no snapshot (the setting turned on mid-draft) is counted over the whole class, drafted players included, which gives the same ranks (values don't change until the next season), and the snapshot is stored when the draft ends. An older class with no snapshot falls back to the scouts' last stored grades of the class (`p.scoutDraft`, else the last `p.scoutSnaps` of the draft year, Scouting Reports setting); without either, no pre-draft rank is shown.
- **AI drafting is unchanged:** AI teams draft by value (`runPicks`), and your auto pick by your own Draft Board ranks; neither reads these ranks. A test runs the same draft (same league, same random numbers) with the setting off and on and gets the same 35 picks.
- **Reproduction** (Playwright, a new league played to its draft): without the setting, after 35 picks all 35 players left had a new _#_ and #1 was pre-draft #31; with it, after 33 picks all 37 players left kept their _Pre_ rank (#1 left: Pre 31, BA 1), the drafted players showed e.g. pick 1-8 "#18 −10" (a reach), Draft Scouting and the College/Overseas page ("pre-draft rank #31, projected #31 before the draft", "Drafted #1 · Pre-draft #1") agreed, and the Draft page had no horizontal page scroll at 1280 and 390 px, light and dark. **Tests:** `src/worker/core/draft/preDraftRanks.test.ts` (ranks unchanged across 35 picks on the Draft page, Draft Scouting, the Draft Board and the Mock Draft; nobody left shows #1 unless he was #1, no second-round prospect does; drafted players' pick next to their pre-draft rank; the whole-class and scout-grade fallbacks; the backfill at the end of the draft; with it off, the old renumbering; the AI's picks identical off and on). Code: `common/preDraftRanks.ts`, `worker/core/draft/preDraftRanks.ts`.

### Draft lottery night and combine

- **Where:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ League Extras → _Draft Lottery Night & Combine_ (`featureLotteryNight`, basketball, on for new leagues, off for existing ones; hidden in college leagues). Off: the Draft Lottery page, the College/Overseas combine, scouting reports and the AI draft are exactly as before, and nothing below runs.
- **Lottery night** (Draft Lottery page, `/l/:lid/draft_lottery`): _Start lottery night_ runs the real lottery once, in the worker, exactly as _Start lottery_ does without the setting (same code, same random numbers, same result: `draftCombine.test.ts` checks the picks and the number of draws with the setting on and off). The reveal is purely presentational: picks come out from the last lottery pick up to No. 1, every 2.5 seconds or with _Reveal next_, with _Pause_ and _Reveal all_. Each pick gets a stage card in the team's colors with its logo, "Moved up from 7th!" / "Fell from 2nd" / "Stayed at 5th", its odds of that pick going in and a one-line reaction (the AI GM by name and leanings with AI GM personalities on, otherwise the fans by the team's hype; your own team gets its own lines; the wording is chosen by a hash of season and team, no random numbers). Teams still in the running are listed with their No. 1 odds, and "Down to the final two" before the last reveal. Then a _Lottery winners and losers_ recap (biggest jumps and falls, the longest shot) that stays on the page for past lotteries, and a news item with the same summary (no notification, so it doesn't spoil the reveal). The pick table and its highlighting below work as before. Code: `common/lotteryNight.ts`, `ui/views/DraftLotteryNight.tsx`, the reveal state in `ui/views/DraftLottery.tsx`, the news in `genOrder` → `logLotteryNightNews`.
- **Draft combine:** held at the start of the draft lottery phase (after the playoffs) for the current class, with a news item (number measured, how many skipped the drills, the best vertical, lane agility and wingspan). About 1.25 prospects per draft pick are invited (75 for 60 picks), by projected stock from their draft-year ratings. Everyone invited is measured: height with and without shoes, wingspan, standing reach, weight, body fat, hand length and width. The drills: lane agility, shuttle, 3/4 court sprint, standing and max vertical, bench press reps (185 lbs), and shooting drills (spot-up threes out of 25, off-dribble jumpers out of 25, free throws out of 20). Results come from his draft-year ratings (hgt and wng with expanded ratings, stre, spd, jmp, endu, tp/ft/fg or catch-and-shoot/off-the-dribble with expanded ratings) and listed height and weight, plus realistic noise from a small RNG seeded by pid and draft year (`common/draftCombine.ts`): the same every time, for old classes too, and the game's random numbers are never used. **Stored when held:** each invited prospect's results are saved on him (`p.combine`, with his season) when the combine is held, so regenerating his ratings (Ratings Regenerator, a new build) or other later changes don't rewrite the combine; a class held before this (no stored results) is still computed live. Some skip the drills: top-3 prospects half the time, top 8 30%, injured prospects 85%, everyone else rarely; they are still measured.
- **Scaled to real combines** (2000s-2020s). Generated classes (8 classes, 560 prospects, `draftCombine.test.ts`), mean ± sd vs real: height without shoes 77.6 ± 3.7" (real 77.6 ± 3.3), wingspan 81.3 ± 4.3" (81.8 ± 3.9), standing reach 104.1 ± 5.1" (103.5 ± 5), body fat 7.3 ± 2.3% (7.2 ± 2.6), hand length 8.74 ± 0.51" (8.75 ± 0.45), hand width 9.49 ± 0.73" (9.5 ± 0.75), lane agility 11.38 ± 0.43 s (11.3 ± 0.45), shuttle 3.09 ± 0.13 s (3.08 ± 0.15), sprint 3.30 ± 0.11 s (3.28 ± 0.12), standing vertical 29.4 ± 4.2" (29.3 ± 3), max vertical 34.5 ± 4.0" (34.5 ± 3.7), bench 9.0 ± 4.5 reps (9 ± 5), spot-up 52% (53%), off-dribble 43% (44%), free throws 74% (75%). The test checks every mean within half a real sd, the sd within 0.55-1.6× and the 2nd/98th percentiles inside the real extremes (± half an sd).
- **Effort:** about one prospect in nine (`COAST_CHANCE`) coasts through the drills (`drillEffort`, a seeded stream of its own, `draftCombineEffort|pid|season`, so no other draw moves): an effort of 0.55-0.85 takes up to 4.5 × (1 − effort)" off the max vertical, 3.5 × off the standing vertical, 0.7 × s onto the lane agility, 0.22 × onto the shuttle, 0.14 × onto the sprint and 5 × reps off the bench. Stored as `p.combine.effort` (missing = full effort). Teams see through it: the _performance_ (the AI nudge) is read from what he'd have done going all out. The scouting report's combine line says "Didn't look like he went all out in the drills", and the Combine tab lists the coasters (_Questions about effort_, a * on the lane agility).
- **Listed vs measured:** before the combine the public numbers are his school's or club's listing (`listedMeasurements`, derived from pid, draft year and his true height and weight with its own seeded stream, nothing stored): heights run tall (10% listed an inch short, 20% exact, 44% an inch over, 19% two, 7% three), weights are a few pounds light and out of date (± 7 lbs, rounded to 5). The combine measures his true height and weight plus a little noise. 1.5" shorter in shoes than listed (or 1" taller), or 10 lbs off, makes a story (`measurementStories`: "Shorter than billed: listed at 6'9", measured 6'7.25" in shoes (6'6" without)", "Weighed in at 231 lbs, 16 more than his listed 215"): on the Combine tab (_Listed vs measured_, the listing on hover of the height and weight cells), the prospect's College/Overseas page, the scouting report's combine lines and a news item with the biggest surprises among the top 40 (_Tape measure surprises_).
- **Percentiles against history:** the Combine tab switches between the results, the class percentile and the all-time percentile (100 = best, weight: heaviest; green/red at 90+/10-), and the prospect's College/Overseas page has an _All-time_ column. All-time is against every stored combine of earlier classes of this league (`getCombineHistory`, memoized per season and phase); with fewer than 40 earlier results for a test (`MIN_HISTORY`, a new league) it is against real NBA combines instead (a normal fit of `COMBINE_REAL_RANGES`, the column says _vs NBA_).
- **Feeds scouting fuzz (modestly):** with Scouting Director & Regions, the combine's looks (4.5 × coverage factor, which narrow his ratings fuzz) are worth 0.55× for a prospect who was only measured (skipped the drills) and 0.8× for one who coasted (`combineLooksMult`).
- **Combine tab** of Draft Scouting (`/l/:lid/draft_combine[/:season]`): a sortable table of every invited prospect (DNP for skipped drills, green/red for the top/bottom 5 of each test, an athletic score = average percentile of the agility, speed and leaping drills, and the draft pick for past classes), _Standouts_ and _Disappointments_ by athletic score with each one's best or worst test, and the top 3 per test. Measurements and drill results only: no scouting info.
- **Player page:** the prospect's College/Overseas page (`/l/:lid/player_pre_draft/:pid`) has a _Draft Combine_ section with every result, his rank and percentile among the prospects measured (or "not invited"). It replaces the College/Overseas combine (College stats setting) while the setting is on.
- **Feeds scouting** (Scouting Reports setting): for a prospect who was measured, the size/length grade's range shrinks to 35% and his scout's noise on it to 40%; for one who did the drills, the same for athleticism (range 50%). The physical profile gets a _Combine_ line ("Measured 6'8" without shoes, 7'1.5" wingspan (+5.5" ape index). Top-2 max vertical (40.5"), best lane agility (10.4s)", "Skipped the drills while injured"), and the athleticism and size grade lines read the combine's vertical and wingspan. On player pages (and in the list summaries with _Scouting Summaries on Lists_ on).
- **AI draft:** each tested prospect has a _performance_: his drill results against what his ratings predict, about N(0, 1). Only big over/under-performers (beyond ±1) get a nudge of up to 0.75 value points (0.6 per sd beyond 1), then the nudge is centered so it averages exactly zero over the class (uninvited prospects and skippers included). AI teams add it in `runPicks` like the other draft adjustments; the mock draft uses it too ("Combine riser"). Your own picks never get it.
- **Tests:** `src/common/draftCombine.test.ts` (same results per pid and season, no `Math.random`, skip rates, ranks, the nudge summing to zero and bounded, reveal order, jumps/falls/recap, deterministic reactions, narrower grade ranges), `src/worker/core/draftCombine/draftCombine.test.ts` (generated classes against real ranges, the nudge over a class, the lottery with the setting on and off).

### Pre-draft injuries and combine medicals

- **Where:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features)._ The switch is `featurePreDraftMedicals` (basketball; on for new leagues, off for existing ones before the core features force it on; off in college leagues). Off: pre-draft careers, the combine, the AI draft and pro injuries are exactly as before.
- **Injury history** (`common/collegeStats/injuries.ts`, called from the career plan in `career.ts`): generated pre-draft careers (college, junior college, overseas and other pro clubs) can have injuries, drawn from a stream of their own (seed, draft year, pid), so a prospect with a clean history gets exactly the seasons he had before. Per season played, for a prospect of average durability: 9% a minor injury (2-8 games: sprained ankle, sore knee, strained hamstring, concussion, ...), 2% a longer one (8-20 games, capped at half the season: fractured foot or hand, torn meniscus, sprained knee, ...) and 0.6% a season-ending one in an earlier season (a torn ACL most often, also the meniscus, foot, ankle, Achilles or a herniated disc; after 12-21 games, past the medical redshirt window, so eligibility is unchanged). The medical redshirts (about 4% of juniors and seniors) and the injury-shortened season story that already existed get an injury type from the season-ending list. Injury names come from the game's basketball injury list. Over generated classes about 75% of prospects are clean, 16% have only minor injuries and 8% a major one. With Player Traits, the rates are multiplied by `exp(-0.03 (durability - 50))` (0.4-2.5): durability 30 → 63% clean and 12% major, 70 → 85% clean and 6% major; the injury-riddled draft class shape (which already lowers durability) gets more.
- **Games played match:** a mid-season injury is taken out of his games played for that season (`season.ts` `injuryGames`: at least that many games missed, the season's other draws unchanged), and a season-ending one plays only the games before it (`gamesCap`, like the medical redshirt). The stored injury records the games he actually missed (a season-ending one records 0: the rest of the season, his stats row has the games he played).
- **Storage:** `p.preDraftCareer.years[i].injury = { type, games, major?, ended? }` (with `status: "injury"` for a new season-ending one). It is part of the stored career, so league exports and imports carry it (players are exported whole; the schema leaves player fields open). Classes generated before this keep their stored careers: no injury types, and a medical redshirt or injury-shortened season counts as an "Undisclosed injury" for the medicals. The game's own random numbers are never used.
- **Shown:** the College/Overseas page's _Teams_ table notes each injury ("Missed 12 games: Fractured Hand", "Season-ending injury: Torn ACL", "Medical redshirt: Torn Meniscus").
- **Combine medicals** (`common/draftCombine.ts`, `worker/core/draftCombine`): every invited prospect has a physical at the combine. His medical risk is the sum over his pre-draft injuries (minor 0.45, major 1.4, season-ending 2.4, an ACL, Achilles or meniscus reconstruction 3; times 1 for the draft year, 0.8 the year before, 0.6 earlier) plus 0.8 if he is hurt now plus, with Player Traits, `(50 - durability) / 15` (between -0.7 and 1.8, so low durability alone is never a red flag). Grades: under 1 _Clean_, under 2.4 _Minor concerns_, else _Red flag_. The grade you see is your medical staff's read: the risk plus normal noise (seeded by pid and draft year, its own RNG) with sd `0.6 × (1 - 0.25 × scouting effect - 0.35 × medical effect)` (clamped 0.35-1.7×), the effects being your scouting and health budget items' 3-season levels (`levelToEffect`, 0 at the league average, average without the budget setting). Stored on the prospect with his combine results (`p.combine.medical = { grade, risk }`) when the combine is held, so it never changes afterwards; a combine held before this has no medicals. The Front Office early read shows a live grade with the same formula.
- **Shown:** a _Med_ column (green Clean, yellow Minor concerns, red Red flag; hover for the reason and the explanation) on the Combine tab, Draft Scouting and the Draft Board once the class's combine was held, and a _Medical_ line on the prospect's College/Overseas page Draft Combine section ("Torn ACL (2025, season-ending), Sprained Ankle (2024, missed 3 games)"). The actual risk stays in the worker. When the combine is held, a news item lists medical red flags among the top 20 prospects of the class (by projected stock, as your staff reads them).
- **AI draft:** AI teams take a little off for the actual risk (not your read): 0.3 value points for minor concerns, 1.2 for a red flag, added to the combine nudge in `getDraftCombineNudges` (so `runPicks` and the Mock Draft both use it; not centered, unlike the performance nudge). Your own picks never get it.
- **Pro effect** (one, modest): a player with a major pre-draft injury (a long absence, a season-ending injury or a medical redshirt) has 1.15× the injury risk in his first two pro seasons (draft year + 1 and + 2), through Injury Depth's risk factor (`preDraftInjuryRisk` in `player/injuryDepth.ts`). Only about 8% of rookies have one, so league-wide injury totals barely move.
- **Tests:** `src/common/collegeStats/injuries.test.ts` (rates, games played against games missed, season-ending injuries, clean prospects identical with injuries on and off, determinism, durability, older careers), `src/worker/core/draftCombine/medicals.test.ts` (risk and grades, noise narrowed by budget, deterministic, injury names in the game's list, every invited prospect graded and stored without `Math.random`, the stored grade read back, the AI discount, the red flag news, off, the rookie injury risk).

### Draft-and-stash

- **Where:** _Always on in every pro basketball league (core; see Core features)._ League Extras registry entry _Draft-and-Stash_ (`featureDraftStash`, basketball, `college: false`: never in a college league). Not shown in the settings forms. A league loaded from before it gets the switch on (`forceCoreFeatures`); nothing needs converting, since no player is stashed until someone stashes one.
- **What:** a team can draft a prospect whose most recent season before the draft was with a club overseas and keep his rights while he keeps playing there. Who counts: his last pre-draft stats row (or his last pre-draft career year, or his college field for a prospect with no rows) is a pro league abroad (`collegeStats/overseas.ts` `isOverseasLeague`: a pro club, not the G League, not a college or youth program). A league without generated pre-draft stats only knows this from the college field, so fewer prospects qualify there.
- **Stashing:** from the moment he is drafted through free agency of his draft year (phases draft, after draft, re-signing, free agency), while he has not played for his team, his team can choose _Stash overseas_ instead of keeping him. The rookie contract he got at the draft (or the hard-cap contract demand) is undone, not ended: it was never paid, so there is no dead money and no release. He leaves the roster and the payroll, any re-signing negotiation is dropped, and a news item says so. A player who was traded after the draft can be stashed by his new team.
- **Representation (why a tid):** a stashed player has `tid = PLAYER.STASHED` (-9) and `p.stash = { tid, season, league }` (the team holding his rights, the offseason he was stashed, his club league). A special tid was chosen over "on the team with a flag" because every roster, depth chart, game sim, payroll, roster-size, jersey-number, mood and AI loop already selects players by `tid >= 0` / `playersByTid`; with his own tid a stashed player is out of all of them without touching any, the way draft prospects (-2) and retired players (-3) are. The rights live in `p.stash.tid`; the few places that deal with rights (trades, the roster page) ask for `getStashedPlayers(tid)` (`worker/core/draftStash`). The cache loads tid -9 players with the active ones (`db/Cache.ts`), a league file keeps them as they are (the schema documents `stash` and the stats row's `stash`), and a player without `stash` is simply not stashed, so old leagues and files load unchanged.
- **While stashed:** he develops each preseason like any player (a new ratings row, `player.develop`, traits, values, archetype and face aging), in a separate pass after everyone else, so a league with no stashed players develops exactly as before (development itself uses the game's random numbers, like every player's). After each playoffs he gets the season that just ended as an overseas season: a stats row with `tid` DOES_NOT_EXIST, his league as the team name and `stash: true`, generated by the overseas stats generator (`collegeStats/season.ts` `simulateSeason` with the league's environment: pace, minutes caps, season length) from his current ratings, his age and his talent measured against a typical draft class, seeded by pid and season (`common/draftStash.ts` `simulateStashSeason`). `common/preDraft.ts` counts `stash` rows as pre-draft (overseas) rows whatever the season, so they show with his college/overseas stats (player page, College/Overseas page) and never as pro seasons, career totals, experience or leaders. No overseas honors are given for these seasons. Not for the season he was drafted in (he was stashed after it).
- **Bringing him over:** in any later offseason (draft through free agency phases), his team can _Bring over_ (player page or the roster page). He signs with the team holding his rights: within a season of the draft (`STASH_ROOKIE_WINDOW` = 1) the rookie scale salary of his pick for the rookie contract length of his round (when the league has automatic rookie contracts), otherwise his market value (`genContract` without randomness, never below the minimum) for 3 seasons. A buyout of his club contract is paid as a team expense (added to the season's salary expenses and taken from cash): none in the offseason he was stashed, otherwise a fraction of the minimum contract from 25% to 100% by the league's strength (about 95% for the strongest leagues). Blocked when the roster is full, outside the offseason, or (hard cap) when the contract would go over the cap. News item with the contract and buyout.
- **Rights lapse:** at the start of free agency of the season 4 seasons after his draft year (`STASH_MAX_SEASONS`) or the season he turns 27 (`STASH_MAX_AGE`), whichever is first, a player still abroad becomes a free agent (contract demand normalized with the other free agents), with a news item (a notification for the user's team). So the last chance to bring him over is the re-signing phase of that season.
- **Trades:** his rights are an asset like a player. The trade page lists a team's stashed players with its roster (a _Stashed_ badge), `updatePlayers`/`summary` accept them for the team holding the rights (no salary, no effect on team ovr), and `processTrade` moves `p.stash.tid` instead of `p.tid` (undo moves it back; the transaction is recorded for the new rights holder). AI valuation (`ValueChangeCalculator`): his value times 0.8 (`STASH_TRADE_VALUE_FACTOR`, a prospect who isn't here yet and might never come), no contract value, and he doesn't change the receiving team's ovr this season. AI-initiated trades (counter-offers, AI-to-AI, three-team trades) don't add rights on their own.
- **AI teams:** right after the draft (start of the after draft phase), for each AI team, its eligible picks worst first: stash a pick outside the top 10 when the roster is over the maximum or he is 4+ ovr below the team's 10th best player. At the start of free agency, before rights lapse: bring over a stashed player who is within 1 ovr of the team's 10th best player (within 4 in his last season before the rights lapse) when the roster has room, best players first. Fixed rules, no random numbers. A user's team on auto play or in spectator mode is handled like an AI team.
- **UI:** player page: for a user's eligible pick, a _Stash overseas_ box with what it does; for a stashed player, a _Stashed overseas_ box (league, since when, seasons abroad, rights holder and the season the rights lapse), with _Bring over_ and its cost for the user's team. The team line reads "Stashed overseas (team rights)" and links to the rights holder's roster. Roster page (current season): a _Stashed overseas_ table (position, age, ovr/pot, draft slot, club league, seasons abroad, rights until, _Bring over_ with the contract and buyout) and, for the user's team, the picks that can be stashed.
