News items are type `draftStash` ("Stashed Overseas", transactions).
- **Determinism:** the overseas lines come from a small RNG (`collegeStats/rng.ts` `Rng`, salted per league) seeded `draftStash|pid|season`, so the same league gives the same lines; the AI rules use no randomness. A league where nobody is stashed makes no extra draws: the preseason pass and the end-of-season pass do nothing.
- **Code:** rules `src/common/draftStash.ts`; worker `src/worker/core/draftStash/index.ts` (stash, bring over, seasons abroad, development, AI, lapse, page data); phases (`newPhaseAfterDraft`, `newPhaseBeforeDraft`, `newPhasePreseason`, `newPhaseFreeAgency`); trades (`trade/updatePlayers.ts`, `summary.ts`, `processTrade.ts`, `team/ValueChangeCalculator.ts`, `views/trade.ts`); API `stashDraftPick`, `bringOverStashed`; UI `ui/components/DraftStash.tsx`.
- **League moves:** with _More pre-draft paths_ on, a stashed player who has outgrown a development or mid tier league (ovr 44+ / 49+) spends the next season a tier up (`getStashLeague`), most likely in his own country's league; `p.stash.league` follows him.
- **Not done:** no overseas honors for stashed seasons, no moves down or sideways abroad while stashed, no renouncing rights by hand, AI teams don't put rights in trade offers on their own, the Draft page has no stash button (use the player page or the roster page).
- **Tests:** `src/common/draftStash.test.ts` (who counts as overseas, stash rows pre-draft never pro, lapse rule, stash and bring-over windows, rookie scale vs market contract, buyout, AI rules, the overseas line deterministic and sized like a pro club role), `src/worker/core/draftStash/draftStash.test.ts` (a mock league: stash off the roster and payroll, no season for the draft year, development, one season row per season and the same line again, bring over with rookie contract and buyout expense, a college pick refused, lapse to free agency, AI stash and bring-over rules, rights in a trade and its undo), `src/worker/core/draftStash/draftStashLeague.test.ts` (a real headless league with generated pre-draft stats: AI teams stash late overseas picks after the draft, the player, roster and trade pages load with them, the user trades for one, they develop in the preseason and get exactly one season abroad after the next playoffs).

### Scouting reports

- **Where:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ League Extras → _Scouting Reports_ (`featureScoutingReports`, basketball, on for new leagues, off for existing ones; hidden in college leagues). With it off, the short report on the College/Overseas page stays exactly as it was.
- **Player page:** a _Scouting_ entry in the player dropdown (next to Overview, Game Log, College/Overseas, Traits; `/l/:lid/player_scouting/:pid[/:scoutId]`) for every player: prospects, pros, free agents and retired players, and a one-line summary on the Overview.
  - _Header:_ archetype name only, comparisons ("Plays like X (2031 draft, No. 4; became a starter)", the closest past players of this league at the draft for prospects, at the same age for pros), floor and ceiling roles ("Floor: Rotation wing · Ceiling: All-Star"), and a confidence from the remaining scouting uncertainty (looks, budget) and the lead scout.
  - _Grades:_ letter grades A+ ... F for shooting (catch-and-shoot, off the dribble, free throws), finishing, playmaking, ball security, rebounding, perimeter defense, interior defense, athleticism, size/length, basketball IQ, motor/intangibles. Each is the percentile of a 0-100 score among his peers (the draft class's guards/wings/bigs for prospects, the league's for pros, today's league at his peak for retired players; the reference is stated), with a one-line explanation (several phrasings per level, plus the stat that backs it up). When unsure the grade is a range ("B to A-").
  - _Breakdowns:_ offense (scorer type, shot mix from his shot locations, an estimated self-created share, usage), defense (who he can guard, switchability, rim protection, team defense), physical profile (measurements, frame, room to grow, combine percentiles for prospects), intangibles from trait readings (visible traits as scout observations, hidden traits "Unknown: needs more looks" until they are known), red/green flags (injury history with body parts, FT vs 3P mismatch, turnovers, age vs production, competition level, tools vs production, decline), development plan (typical league aging only, never his own curve; work ethic only once known; the head coach's development philosophy and the development assistant with Coaching Staff/Systems), team fit (needs filled, redundancy, coaching system fit), a pro report (role, trend, ideal role, contract value, how to use him) and a bottom line with a draft range or role/contract recommendation.
  - _Printable card_ (Print scouting card: grade bars, radar of key grades, shot-mix chart, comparison cards, bottom line; prints on one letter page with only the card, in light colors in either theme, two columns, colors kept; the newspaper's print rule is scoped to the newspaper page, it used to blank every other printout), _two scouts' takes_ side by side, _stock up / stock down_ changelog ("Raised: Catch-and-shoot B- → B after shooting 41.0% from three"), and the _track record_ ("Our scouts said: lottery talent → became an All-Star").
  - _Strengths & weaknesses_ (draft prospects' full reports; `common/scoutingReports/skills.ts`, facts gathered by `worker/core/scoutingReports/skillSignals.ts`): the top 3-4 strengths and 2-3 weaknesses, each with a severity tier (_Elite_ / _Plus_ / _Average_ for strengths, _Average_ / _Minus_ / _Major concern_ for weaknesses; three wordings per item that scale with the tier), evidence lines, a trend arrow and, for weaknesses, _Fixable_ (with the development plan focus that targets it, e.g. "a "Add a three" development plan targets it (range + catch-and-shoot)"; "harder at his age" from 23) or _Structural_. Then the _swing skill_ (the grade with the most leverage: its weight for his position × the width of his grade range × how close it is to the middle, more when it is one of his weaknesses or a contradiction: "If it comes around, he's a starting wing; if it doesn't, an end-of-bench wing"), the _NBA role_ with a risk level (Low / Moderate / High / Very high from the report's uncertainty, the floor-to-ceiling gap, age, competition level, the swing skill's grade, major concerns and age-adjusted production, with the main reason), a comparison _anchor_ (his first Real Player Comp: "a close match at the same age, who became a starter"; else the closest past player of the league) and up to two rating-vs-stats _contradictions_ ("Great numbers, questionable mechanics: 43.0% from three, but the film grades it C-"; "The tools say yes, the numbers say not yet"). On the Scouting page under the card, in the PDF, and the swing skill with the top strength and weakness in the hover card (Draft Board, Mock Draft).
    - _Items_: situational (transition weapon / half-court scoring / half-court operator; zone buster / zones pack the paint; physical man defense / unbothered by it; big-game performer / fades against strong opponents from his game score per 40 in NCAA tournament games and against better-seeded teams vs the season, and conference vs non-conference play, plus a known Big Game reading); consistency and range (streaky shooter: the gap between his non-conference (Nov-Dec) and conference (Jan-Mar) three-point shooting, and between his last two seasons, against what sampling noise explains, or a known Consistency reading; steady shooter; NBA range / limited range from three-point volume per 40, accuracy, the combine's spot-up drill and range vs catch-and-shoot sub-ratings; "the jumper is real" (76%+ on 60+ free throws, under 34.5% from three) / "three-point mirage" (37%+ from three, under 69% at the line)); mechanics (slow / quick release from catch-and-shoot vs range; low release point from shots blocked per 40, height for his position and lift; left-hand dominant, can be forced right (his shooting hand is public; never more than a minus) / lefty who goes both ways / one-handed driver; finishes through contact / avoids contact from free throw rate and the contact vs finesse sub-ratings); feel (sees plays a beat late / sees it early; forces passes into traffic; plays within himself / hunts his own shot, with Shot Discipline); defense (gambles for steals / active hands; foul-prone at the rim / protects the rim without fouling, with Foul Discipline; lost off the ball; can switch 1-4 / targetable in pick-and-roll, with the lane agility drill and Defensive Versatility); physical projection (frame to add weight / maxed-out body from his weight for his height and age; late growth spurt when the combine measured him taller than his listing; length plays bigger than his height / short arms from the ape index); motor and character from scouted trait ranges only (plays hard every possession / coasts when ahead, also when he coasted through the combine drills; body-language concerns / even-keeled from Temper; leader from a known Leadership), read against his class's average reading; role translation (college post scorer without NBA size: about a quarter of his shots post-ups (fewer the shorter he is) under 6'9" for a big, 6'8" otherwise; day-one skill: an A- or better catch-and-shoot, perimeter defense, rim protection, rebounding, passing or finishing grade with a B or better floor; needs the ball to be effective). The letter grades fill in plainly ("Rebounding: Doesn't rebound his position") when nothing more specific applies, and never repeat a grade a specific item covers. Two items sharing a topic (range, release, effort, switching, ...) are never both shown.
    - _Evidence_: stats (pre-draft totals per 40, his last two seasons, schedule strength), splits, combine (measurements, drills, listed vs measured, effort), film (words from his scouted ratings and sub-ratings, never a number) and trait readings (the ranges the Traits page shows, with his class's average). 1 line per item when he is barely scouted, 2 when reasonably, 3 when well scouted (a good department adds one).
    - _Scout voice_: the lead scout's lean from his bias (tools, youth, athletes, generous: _upside_; production, harsh: _production_; others straight) weighs items (upside scouts like tools and projection and soften fixable weaknesses; production scouts like what the numbers show and doubt tools without them; shooter and size scouts lean their way) and adds his phrasing ("Projectable", "Proven, not projected", "Tools, for now"), plus a seeded error of his own (more for a weaker scout and a less-known prospect). The second opinion weighs the same item with his own lean and error and dissents when he sees it two tiers apart, doesn't see it, or reads it a tier apart with the opposite lean ("Shaw sees it as average (he wants to see it in the numbers)").
    - _Uncertainty_: _hedged_ ("Early read: ...", "could be elite; we need more looks") when the report's uncertainty is over 5.5 or ratings are hidden, _confident_ ("We're sure of it") at 2.75 or less.
    - _Trend_: ▲ / ▼ / ■ against our last snapshot of him the season before (the grade the item is about, "Catch-and-shoot C → B- since 2030"); without one, the item's own stat change (three-point percentage season to season).
    - _Rules_: reads only what the report reads (ratings with the user's fuzz, visible trait readings and known hidden ones as ranges, public stats, measurements, the combine, the shooting hand). Under the no visible ratings challenge no rating-based item fires, no film evidence or contradiction is shown and the report is hedged (stats, combine and trait evidence only; no real comps, as before). Seeded (league, player, scout, season), so a report reads the same on every load. Only the full report gathers the extra facts (the player's own records, plus his class's trait averages once per class); snapshots, rumors, blurbs, the scouts' room and Draft Scouting don't, so class-wide pages are unchanged (about 20 ms per full report in a test league). Samples: `RUN_SKILL_SAMPLES=1 npx vitest --run --project basketball src/test/scoutingSkills/samples.script.test.ts`.
- **Scouting department** (`/l/:lid/scouting_department[/:team]`, a _Scouts_ tab of Draft Scouting): every team has 2-5 scouts from its scouting budget level (three-season average), with names, faces (in suits), a specialty (college, international, pro, athletes, shooters), a quality and a small bias (overrates athletes, falls for shooters, loves size, trusts the box score, tools over production, tough/generous grader, young upside). The best-matching scout writes a report, the next one gives the second opinion; any scout can be picked. Better departments mean narrower ranges, earlier red flags (injury and shooting flags need less evidence) and better comparisons. Scouts are hired and leave on their own (each slot is a chain of scouts staying 2-10 years, drawn from the league seed), with hit rates per scout once their draft calls can be judged (3 seasons after the draft, or retirement; it was 4 before scout track records, see below).
- **PDF export:** the _Download player_ PDF has the report as its own page (grade bars, radar, shot mix, comparisons, flags, bottom line, pro report, offense, defense, physical profile, intangibles, development, team fit), continuing on more pages when it doesn't fit; _Export profile_ adds the two scouts' takes, stock up/down and the track record.
- **Scouting Summaries on Lists** (option of _Scouting Reports_, `scoutingListSummaries`, **off** by default): by default scouting info (grades, blurbs, reports) is shown only on the player's own pages (the Scouting page, the Overview summary, the pre-draft view) and in the player PDF export. Turned on, the short forms come back outside player pages: a blurb under each of the first 40 free agents shown (Free Agents), _Our scouts on this deal_ in the trade window, a hover card (magnifier icon) next to each prospect on the Draft Board and Mock Draft, _Scouting the {opponent}_ above the Playoffs bracket and the _Draft preview_ page in the Newspaper. Set it in League Extras (under Scouting Reports) or with the _Show scouting summaries_ switch at the top of Free Agents (current season), which saves the same setting. They come from the same reports, so they follow the no visible ratings challenge like the player pages do (no ratings read, production and measurements only). UI: `useScoutingListsOn` / `ScoutingListsToggle` in `ui/components/ScoutingReport/index.tsx`; Newspaper: `getNewsFacts`. The older _Scouting System_ notes (ovr/pot ranges from scouting points) are not part of this option: their text notes stay on the prospect's own pages, and Draft Scouting / Draft Board keep only the _Scout report_ range column you spend points in, whatever the option. AI GMs still read grades through their analytics/old-school lens on draft day (weights sum to zero over grades that are z-scores within the class, at most 0.8 value points, so no inflation).
- **For other features:** `worker/core/scoutingReports` exports `getScoutingSplit(pid)` / `getScoutingRumors(limit)` ("Scouts are split on X: one sees an All-Star, another a rotation wing") used by the rumor mill's _Scouting buzz_ (see Rumors); the scouting reports don't depend on the rumors feature.
- **Hidden information:** reports read only ratings with the user's scouting fuzz (none under the no visible ratings challenge, then production only with wide ranges), trait readings as the Traits page shows them (an unknown hidden trait carries no number), stats, measurements and public records. Never true prospect ratings, hidden trait scores or the aging curve (tests check the inputs against the true values).
- **Storage:** reports are derived on view. Stored on players: `p.scoutSnaps` (up to 8 snapshots: season, phase, one character per grade, overall, looks, five rounded stats; taken in the preseason, at the trade deadline, at the end of the regular season and before the draft, for players and this/next year's prospects) and `p.scoutDraft` (the department's take at the draft: tier, scout ids, each scout's tier, overall grade), under 200 bytes per player. Old leagues work when it's turned on; their history starts then.
- **Code:** `common/scoutingReports/` (pure: grades, categories, scouts, report, history, blurbs, tests), `worker/core/scoutingReports/` (inputs, pages, snapshots via a hook in `newPhase`, rumors, newspaper facts, GM lens in `runPicks`), `worker/api/scouting.ts`, `ui/components/ScoutingReport/`, `ui/views/PlayerScouting.tsx`, `ui/views/ScoutingDepartment.tsx`, `common/newspaper/pages-scouting.ts`.

### Scouting Director & Regions

- **Where:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ League Extras → _Scouting Director & Regions_ (`featureScoutingDepth`, basketball, on for new leagues, off for existing ones; needs Scouting Reports; off in college leagues). Pages: the _Scouts_ tab (Scouting Department) gets the Director, the priority, the region plan and scout careers; a new _Pro Scouting_ tab (`/l/:lid/pro_scouting`); the _Draft Board_ shows the Director's weekly updates with a lock per player; the _Staff Report_ (below, shared with Coaching Staff Depth). Off: nothing below runs (no stored data, no extra random numbers; `offIdentity.script.test.ts`).
- **Director of Scouting:** every team has one (a scout from the seeded chains of the scouting department, slot 9, a bit better than its scouts; `directorFor`). He plans the regions each time the scouting context is read: **East, West, Midwest/South, International, G League/Prep** (`prospectRegion`: an NCAA school's conference, pro clubs abroad or born abroad without a school → International, the G League, prep, academies, JUCO/NAIA/D-III → G League/Prep). Each scout gets one region, greedily where his quality × specialty affinity (`REGION_AFFINITY`: college scouts 1 in the US regions, international scouts 1.3 abroad, pro scouts 1.15 in the G League/Prep, ...) adds the most to what is still uncovered, weighted by the share of the class's top talent there (a weak Director misreads it a little, seeded). Coverage of a region = 0.08 + 0.1 × Director quality + Σ 0.7 × quality × affinity; _Blind spot_ under 0.18, _Thin_, _Covered_ 0.3+, _Strong_ 0.55+. The scouting budget sets the total capacity (2-5 scouts and their quality, as before).
- **Priority:** _Auto_ (default: the Director picks need when a position group is clearly short, else upside for rebuilding teams, ready-now for contenders, else best available) or best available, positional need, guards, bigs, international (doubles the International region's weight), upside, ready now. Prospects who match get 1.3× the looks and up to ~3 value points on the board.
- **Looks and confidence over the season:** after each week of the regular season (3.5 of the user's games), every prospect gets looks = 0.6 × his region's coverage × visibility (by his rank in his class as the user's scouts see it: top 14 1.5, 15-30 1.2, 31-60 0.8, else 0.4) × the priority bonus, a quarter for later classes. Big events: the national tournament for prospects whose school made it (`p.collegeSeason`, at the start of the playoffs), a late-season _showcase_ for the rest of the top 60, and the combine for those measured (`p.combine`), worth 3.5 / 2.5 / 4.5 looks × (0.6 + 0.8 × coverage). The share of a prospect's fuzz left is 0.35 + 0.65 e^(−looks/15) (`fuzzFactorForLooks`), applied to his latest ratings row's fuzz (`p.scoutLooks` keeps the fuzz before, the factor and the looks; a new season's row starts again with half the looks; fuzz changed by scouting points is kept), so the subs narrow with the bases (`fuzzSubs` scales with the row's fuzz). A late riser has few looks and stays uncertain. The scouting report's uncertainty and the board's confidence column read the factor. User's view only: AI teams always see true ratings.
- **Auto big board:** after each week and after the events, the Director ranks the current class (`rankBoard`: value as scouted + needs (2.5×, 8× with the need priority) + the priority, minus up to ~0.5 for a prospect he barely knows) into the Draft Board's ranks (two rounds + 10), keeping locked prospects where they are; a player the user moves with ▲/▼ is locked automatically, 🔒 toggles. The changes are summarized (new No. 1, risers and fallers of 4+ places, additions) on the Draft Board and the Staff Report. The user's draft-day auto pick takes the top of the board (the _Auto Draft From My Board_ option is implied). Delegation _Suggest_: the update waits for approval in the Staff Report; _Manual_: no updates.
- **Pro scouting** (`getProScouting`): every player on another team and every free agent ranked for the user's team: value as scouted + 6 × positional need + his level in the team's weakest area (shooting, playmaking, perimeter defense, rim protection, rebounding, the top 8 against the league's) and the shape of his sub-ratings there ("Perimeter defense: PDF 82, LAT 67 (+3 vs his ratings)") + 1.5 × system fit (Coaching Systems) + contract surplus (market value minus his contract, ±3), −2 at 32+. Top 30, three trade targets (≤ 30% of the cap) and three free agents, filed by the best pro scout. Scouting blurbs only with _Scouting Summaries on Lists_; with the no visible ratings challenge, production only (PER), no ovr, shapes or fit.
- **Scout careers:** experience (years in the job + 0.4 × years before, `scoutExperience`) adds up to +0.1 quality; after four years a scout picks up a second specialty. Reputation = hit rate of his judged draft calls ((hit rate − 0.45) × 2, scaled in over 5 calls) + "our guy" swings + 0.015 × experience (_Respected_, _Rising_, _Unproven_, _Questioned_). After each draft (`runScoutCareers`, every team): scouts retire (30% a year from 66, all at 70); respected sharp scouts (reputation 0.3+, quality 0.55+) are hired away as another team's Director (15% × reputation a year); a Director retires at 68 or leaves (7% a year) and is replaced by promoting the most respected scout with 5+ years (else a new hire). Departures are cuts in the seeded slot chains (`t.scoutCareer.cuts`; the slot's next scout starts that season), so bylines and old departments still resolve. News for the user's team.
- **"Our guy":** at the start of each season every scout of every team picks 1-3 favourites of the class (never the consensus top 3) through his bias (athletes, shooters, size, production, tools, youth) and specialty, with noise by his quality. Shown on his card. Judged later (star: an All-Star/All-League award or a 64+ ovr peak; bust: under 47 after four seasons): a news item and +0.3 / −0.2 reputation.
- **Calibration** (`RUN_STAFF_DEPTH=1`, `src/test/staffDepth/calibration.script.test.ts`; headless leagues with the new-league defaults; fuzz: seeds 3-4 × 3 seasons, the user's view of each class at the draft lottery, by his region's coverage):

  | Region coverage         | Prospects | Looks by the draft | Fuzz left (\|fuzz\| after / before) |
  | ----------------------- | --------- | ------------------ | ----------------------------------- |
  | Strong (0.55+)          | 336       | 34.3               | 0.44                                |
  | Covered (0.30-0.55)     | 22        | 24.1               | 0.51                                |
  | Thin (0.18-0.30)        | 6         | 8.6                | 0.70                                |
  | Blind spot (under 0.18) | 56        | 11.0               | 0.66                                |

  Blind-spot prospects still get the tournament/showcase/combine looks (the top of the class almost always plays in the tournament or is invited to the combine); a blind spot with no event keeps 0.8-1.0 of its fuzz. AI teams' views are unchanged (they see true ratings). Off identity: `src/test/staffDepth/offIdentity.script.test.ts` (both settings off, two seeded headless leagues, a season and the next preseason) gives the same `simFingerprint` hashes and next random number as master (0988d3a0… and 2169b493…).

- **Storage:** `t.staffDepth` (the user's priority, delegation, kept assignments, suggestions, board log, development report), `t.scoutCareer` (every team: cuts, Directors, reputation swings, our guys, log), `p.scoutLooks` (prospects), `p.draftBoard.locked`.
- **Code:** `common/staffDepth/` (`regions.ts`, `board.ts`, types), `worker/core/staffDepth/scouting.ts`, `report.ts` (Pro Scouting, Staff Report), `actions.ts`, hooks in `phase/newPhase.ts` and `game/play.ts` (weekly), `scoutingReports/input.ts` (cuts, experience, the looks factor), `draft/runPicks.ts`; UI `ScoutingDepartment.tsx`, `ProScouting.tsx`, `DraftBoard.tsx`, `StaffReport.tsx`. Tests: `common/staffDepth/staffDepth.test.ts`; calibration `src/test/staffDepth/calibration.script.test.ts` (`RUN_STAFF_DEPTH=1`).

### Scout consensus and flags

- **Where:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features; never a setting):_ _Scout Consensus & Flags_ (`featureScoutRoom`, basketball, needs Scouting Reports; off in college leagues and during a fantasy draft). Extends Scouting Reports and Scouting Director & Regions. Pages: _Draft Scouting_ (a _Scouts_ column: the consensus slot with an Agree / Mixed / Split badge, the scouts' slot range when they don't agree and a * for an outlier, the details on hover; a _Flags_ column: ▲ green and ▼ red counts, the flags on hover), the _Draft Board_ (_Room_ and _Flags_ columns, the same cells) and the prospect's _Scouting_ page (_The scouts' room_: the consensus, the summary, the outliers and every scout who has seen him with his slot and a one-line reason; the flags as chips at the top of _Flags_).
- **Who sees whom:** the user's scouts (Scouting Reports' department, quality with experience) and the Director of Scouting (with Scouting Director & Regions). A scout always sees the prospects of the region the Director assigned him (without a region plan: the regions his specialty covers fully); everybody sees the top 10 of the class; the Director sees the top 30; the rest of the room catches a prospect with a seeded chance (0.25 per big event he was seen at this season: tournament, showcase, combine; + 0.35 in the top 30, 0.12 in the top 60, 0.04 below), later classes only their top 5 (half the scouts). At least one scout (the best fit for his region) has always seen him.
- **Each scout's grade:** the prospect's value as the department sees it (`valueFuzz`: his true value through the department's shared scouting error, never the true value itself), plus the scout's lean, plus his own error. Lean (value points, ±2.5): his bias (athletes: 0.06 × (spd + jmp − 100); shooters: 0.08 × (tp − 50); size: 0.08 × (hgt − 50); production: 0.25 × (pre-draft points − 12); tools: 0.12 × (pot − ovr − 12); youth: +2 at 19, +1 at 20, −1.5 at 22+; tough grader −1.5, generous +1.5), and +2.5 when he is the scout's "our guy" this season. Error: a seeded unit (per league, scout, prospect and season, ±2.2) × (0.8 + 0.85 × the prospect's remaining uncertainty, the scouting report's: points spent, looks, budget, years to his draft) × (1.35 − 0.7 × quality) × 0.8 when he covers the prospect's region (1.1 otherwise) × 0.9 when his specialty fits the region. The grade is shown as the slot it would give him in his class ("No. 6, a top-10 pick").
- **Consensus and spread:** the consensus is the average grade, the spread its standard deviation: _Agree_ under 1.6 value points, _Mixed_ under 3.2, _Split_ above (_One look_ with one scout). The error scales with the remaining uncertainty, so a split room honestly means less is known: early in the season most of the top of a class is split, and it comes together as looks pile up (tests: in simulated classes the department's actual error is more than 1.5× larger on Split prospects than on Agree ones, and the average spread rises with the uncertainty). Outliers (3+ scouts): a grade at least max(3, 1.75 × the others' spread) from the others' average ("Baker: much higher than the room (sees a top-10 pick)"); with two scouts, 4.5 apart. The reason line names what drives his grade: "He's my guy", his bias ("Sold on the jumper", "Thinks he's undersized"), or how much he has seen ("Has seen him the most and has doubts", "Only caught him a few times, but came away impressed").
- **Red and green flags:** up to four short notes, strongest first, at most three of one color, never two on the same thing. From his traits (Player Traits on; the class's average reading per trait is the middle, so motor and hustle, which read low on prospects, mean he stands out in his class): _Relentless motor_ / _Runs out of gas_ (motor), _Plays hard every possession_ / _Questions about effort_ (hustle), _Keeps his cool_ / _Volatile temper_ (temper), and from hidden traits _Gym rat_ / _Questions about his work habits_ (work ethic), _High-character teammate_ / _Diva tendencies_ (ego), _Fierce competitor_ / _Questions about his fight_ (competitiveness), _May not accept a small role_ (role acceptance), _Natural leader_ (leadership), _Durability questions_ (durability). From his pre-draft record (10+ games): _Produced young_ (20 or younger, 31+ points + rebounds + assists per 36), _Old for his production_ (22+, under 25), _Shot mechanics need work_ (under 62% on 40+ free throws) or _Shooting translates_ (38%+ on 60+ threes and 78%+ on 30+ free throws), _Loose with the ball_ (4.2+ turnovers per 36), _Untested against top competition_ (JUCO and small schools), _Held his own against grown men_ (18+ minutes overseas). From his ratings as the user sees them, against his class (z-scores of speed + jump, three + free throw, offensive + defensive IQ): _Explosive athlete_ / _Limited athlete_ (±1.7), _Shot mechanics need work_ (perimeter players, −1.4), _Feel for the game_ (+1.7); none under the no visible ratings challenge.
- **Hidden traits:** read only through the scouts' own reading (the true score plus their fixed per-player error, sd 16 × the budget's multiplier, as on the Traits page), never the true score. A visible trait flags at 20 + 0.5 × its uncertainty from the class middle; a hidden one needs 24 + 0.9 × its uncertainty, and the department must be able to read him: readability (26 − uncertainty) / 16, halved when no scout covering his region saw him, no points were spent and his looks left more than 75% of his fuzz; under 0.35, no hint. A hint is always marked "(unconfirmed)", its tooltip says it is second-hand, and it never shows a number.
- **Storage:** none. Derived on view (`getProspectRooms`), the same on every load within a season (seeded `Rng` streams only, nothing from the game's random numbers, players untouched), so old leagues and files load and export as before.
- **Code:** `common/scoutingReports/room.ts` (who sees whom, leans, errors, the room), `common/scoutingReports/quickFlags.ts` (flags), `worker/core/scoutingReports/room.ts` (gathering, `prospectUncertainty` in `input.ts`), views `draftScouting.ts`, `draftBoard.ts`, `playerScouting.ts`; UI `ui/components/ScoutRoom.tsx`. Tests: `common/scoutingReports/room.test.ts` (deterministic, no `Math.random`, who sees whom, leans, outliers, spread vs actual error, flags and hidden hints), `worker/core/scoutingReports/room.test.ts` (a mock league: every prospect, stable across loads, players untouched, off).

### Big boards from outlets

- **Where:** _Always on in every league with Scouting Reports (basketball, not in college leagues)._ A _Big Boards_ tab of Draft Scouting (`/l/:lid/big_boards[/:season]`, also in the command palette), an _Outlook_ section on the prospect's College/Overseas page, and two news items after each draft.
- **The outlets** (`common/draftOutlets.ts`, made-up names): _The Hoop Ledger_ (consensus, careful), _Ceiling Report_ (upside: room between ovr and pot plus youth), _Box Score Bureau_ (production: last pre-draft season per 40 minutes, points + 1.2 rebounds + 1.5 assists + 2 steals + 2 blocks − 1.5 turnovers, plus true shooting, times a level factor (college 1, overseas 1.1, G League/pathway 1.05, JUCO 0.75, prep/small college 0.7), −1.2 per year of age over 20; no stats reads as a mystery, −0.6 sd), _The Vertical Lab_ (athleticism: the combine's athletic score once he did the drills, a read of his speed and leaping on tape before) and _Draft Wire Daily_ (consensus, noisy). Each prospect's score, in class standard deviations: his value (the read the league drafts by, as the Mock Draft) + the outlet's lean weight (0.45-0.5) × its signal + noise. The noise has a shared part (public perception, sd 0.32, the same for every outlet, so averaging the outlets doesn't recover the truth) and the outlet's own (sd 0.3 × its noise factor), both drifting between editions (75% fixed for the class, a fresh draw per edition) and shrinking as the season goes: way-too-early 1.3, preseason 1, early season 0.9, midseason 0.8, late season 0.7, after the tournament 0.6, post-combine 0.5, final 0.45. Seeded streams only (league seed, draft year, outlet, prospect, edition): the same boards on every load, nothing from the game's random numbers.
- **Editions:** preseason (and the offseason for next year's class), early/mid/late season (by the share of the user's team's games played: under 30%, under 65%, the rest), after the tournament (playoffs), post-combine (draft lottery), final (the draft). Later classes get a way-too-early board. The page shows each outlet's move since the previous edition (▲3 / ▼2).
- **The page:** outlet cards (lean, description, its No. 1), then the class's top two rounds + 10 by the consensus (average rank over the outlets): each outlet's rank (green/red when 10+ spots above/below the consensus), the spread (best to worst rank over the outlets), your board (your Draft Board's ranks first, then your scouts' list) and, once drafted, the actual pick. Ranks only, no ratings, so it shows the same under the no visible ratings challenge (like the Mock Draft, it reads the value the AI drafts by, blurred by the outlets' noise).
- **Stored:** when the draft starts (`newPhase` hook), every prospect of the class gets `p.outletRanks = { s, r, c, y }`: each outlet's final rank (OUTLETS order, 0 = off its board), the consensus rank and your board's rank. Past classes show these; a class drafted before they existed has no boards.
- **Track record:** after each draft a scorecard per class (`getOutletHistory`): _calling the draft_, the average spots off between each board's rank and the actual pick over the first-round picks (with how many first-rounders it had in its top 30), for every outlet, the consensus and your board; and _calling the outcome_, 3 seasons later (`OUTCOME_SEASONS`), the average spots off over each board's top 30 from where each player finished in his class (best visible ovr in his first 3 seasons, ties by minutes; capped at 60), with the actual draft order as one more board, and hits in its top 10. Best of each class in green, averages over every class. News: "Mock draft scorecard: The Hoop Ledger had 24 of the 30 first-round picks in its top 30, best of the draft outlets" and, 3 drafts later, "Three years on, Box Score Bureau's 2031 big board aged best of the outlets (…; your board: 9.8)".
- **Code:** `common/draftOutlets.ts` (outlets, editions, boards, consensus, signals, accuracy), `worker/core/draftOutlets/index.ts` (inputs, edition, stored boards, scorecards, news), view `worker/views/bigBoards.ts`, UI `ui/views/BigBoards.tsx`. Tests: `common/draftScoutingExtras.test.ts` (deterministic, no `Math.random`, closer to the value with each edition, each lean moves its kind of prospect, consensus, accuracy), `worker/core/draftOutlets/draftOutlets.test.ts` (a league: stable boards, stored at the draft, the scorecard after it, the page).

### Projection cones

- **Where:** _Always on with Scouting Reports (basketball)._ The prospect's _Scouting_ page header and the _Outlook_ section of his College/Overseas page.
- **What:** instead of a single potential, each prospect's report has a cone: projected peak floor / median / ceiling (`common/scoutingReports/cone.ts`). Median = the scout's read of his potential − 1 + 0.6 × the scout's optimism + 1.5 × trajectory; ceiling = median + 4.5 + 1.2 × u + youth (21.5 − age, 0 to 3) + 1.2 × an upward trajectory; floor = median − 6 − 1.1 × u − 0.6 × youth + a downward trajectory, never under his current ovr − 1. u is the report's uncertainty (scouting points, looks, budget, years to his draft and the scout's own error), so a prospect the scouts haven't seen much has a wide cone and one seen all season a narrow one. Width: _Narrow_ (10 or less), _Moderate_ (up to 17), _Wide_. The floor and ceiling roles of the report (and the blurbs, the second opinion's range, the stored tiers) come from the same numbers.
- **Trajectory** (−1 to 1): 0.6 × his production per 36 (points, rebounds, assists) from his second-to-last to his last pre-draft season with 150+ minutes (6 more per 36 = 1), plus 0.4 × the change of our scouts' overall grade over their stored snapshots of him since the season before his draft (3 grade steps = 1). Notes under the cone say why ("Production per 36 … up from 18.2 to 24.9 (2030 to 2031)", "His overall grade has risen in our reports", "Young for the class: more room to grow", "Wide because our scouts haven't seen enough of him yet"), ▲ trending up / ▼ trending down at ±0.25.
- **Hidden information:** the cone reads only what the report reads (ratings with the user's fuzz, public stats, our own snapshots), never true ratings or his development curve. Under the no visible ratings challenge it has no numbers: the three roles only, from the production-only projection the report already uses.

### Scout track records

- **Where:** _Always on with Scout Consensus & Flags (basketball)._ The Scouting Department page (`/l/:lid/scouting_department`): a _Scout track records_ table, and slot calls and flags on each scout's card.
- **Filed at the draft:** with the department's take (`p.scoutDraft`), every scout of the room who saw a prospect files his slot call (`rk`: [scout id, slot in the class]), the room's consensus slot (`cs`) and the flags raised on him (`fl`, "+"/"−" and the text, credited to the lead scout; hidden-trait hints are left out). A few dozen bytes per prospect.
- **Scored 3 seasons later** (`TRACK_SEASONS`, also the tier calls' judging, which used to wait 4): each class is ranked by where its players finished (best visible ovr in their first 3 seasons, ties by minutes, `classFinishRanks`). A slot call is a hit within max(5, 30% of the better of the two) spots; its error is capped at 40. A green flag is right when the player finished at or above the room's slot (+3), a red flag when he finished below it. Per scout: tier hit rate (as before), slot hit rate and average error with his worst miss, flags right with the last three, and calls pending.
- **Decisions:** information only: no AI draft or scouting weight reads it (AI teams' scout reputations still come from the tier calls and "our guy", as before). The user acts on it: with Scouting Director & Regions, _Promote_ makes a scout Director of Scouting at once (the Director is replaced, a new scout fills the slot; not for a scout hired this season) and _Let go_ ends his tenure now (his slot's next scout starts; one hired this season leaves after it), both recorded in the seeded scout chains (`t.scoutCareer.cuts`, `cutWhy: "fired"`, `dirs`) and logged. Refused in spectator mode and for other teams.
- **Code:** `common/scoutingReports/history.ts` (`slotAccuracy`, `flagAccuracy`, `classFinishRanks`), `worker/core/scoutingReports/index.ts` (`scoutTrackCalls`, filing in `recordSnapshots`), `worker/core/staffDepth/actions.ts` (`manageScout`), UI `ScoutingDepartment.tsx`.

### Customize Player: Regenerate build

- **Where:** a player page → _Edit_ (`/l/:lid/customize_player/:pid`), _Regenerate build_ button (opens a dialog).
- **What:** the same build engine for one existing player: preview, pin ratings, reroll; keeps ovr, pot and height.

## College mode

- **Where:** New League → College (`/new_league/college`, dashboard button, Ctrl+K "New League > College"). In a college league: League → _Top 25 & Selection_ (`college_polls`), _Conf. Tournaments_ (`college_conf_tournaments`); Players → _Recruiting_ (`college_recruiting`), _Departures & Arrivals_ (`college_departures`).
- **What:** play as a college program. Schools from the prospect model's school list (three sizes: power conferences 79 schools, major conferences 145, all of Division I 364), college rules (two 20-minute halves, 30-second shot clock, 5 fouls, one-and-one from the 7th team foul, two shots from the 10th), 13 scholarships, no contracts/trades/free agency/finances (pages hidden). Season: non-conference then conference schedule, weekly AP Top 25 from the real results, conference tournaments (champions get automatic bids), Selection (NET, quadrants, committee score, S-curve), national tournament (32 or 64) with the real game sim. Offseason: seniors graduate, the best players leave in an off-screen pro draft, an automatic transfer portal, recruiting (a class of high school prospects with stars and ranks; the user picks targets or leaves _Auto recruit_ on), signing day with news, walk-ons. Prestige drifts with results; the user's job security is judged against what the program's prestige predicts.
- **v2:** _College Quick Sim_ (`collegeQuickSim`, League Extras: Game Simulation; on by default for the major and full sizes): games without the user's team are played by a statistical model calibrated to the full sim (`college/quickSim.ts`), the user's games, conference tournament finals and the national tournament use the full sim; the full size sims a regular season in about 21-26 s headless instead of about 125 s. College awards (Player of the Year, All-American 1st/2nd/3rd teams, Freshman of the Year, Defensive POY, Most Outstanding Player, conference POY and all-conference teams, All-Freshman, Coach of the Year against prestige expectations) as award settings, so the awards, history and player pages show them. College-scale team rating (best programs in the high 80s, weakest in the 40s). Bracket: "National Champions", region names. Redshirts (Roster page _Redshirt_ button on freshmen who have not played; AI redshirts deep-bench freshmen). League files with players keep their rosters (class years from age) with a notice.
- **v3:** First Four (68-team field at the full size, `collegeFirstFour`), _College NIT_ (`collegeNit`, League Extras: Game Simulation; 32 or 16 teams, automatic bids for regular season champions, `/college_nit`), recruiting v2 (weekly points, scout / contact / home visit / official visit / offer / NIL with pitches, interest bars against the top 3 schools, in-season commitments and decommitments, signing day results with class rankings), a user-run transfer portal (`/college_portal`, 3 portal weeks, retention pitches, one transfer per player). Details and the interest model in [College mode](college-mode.md).
- **Rule settings for any league:** _Custom Shot Clock_ (`customShotClock`, `shotClockLength`) and _One-and-One Bonus_ (`oneAndOne`, `oneAndOneFouls`) in League Extras. Off = the sim is byte-identical (`outcomeInvariance.test.ts`).
- **Design, data model, calendar, speed and roadmap:** [docs/college-mode.md](college-mode.md). Code: `src/worker/core/college/`, `src/common/college/`, gated by `isCollege()` (`leagueType: "college"`). League Extras that need contracts, trades or a draft have `college: false` in the registry and are always off in a college league.
- **Tests:** `src/worker/core/college/collegeSeasons.test.ts` (creates a college league and plays three seasons through every phase; `COLLEGE_LEVEL`, `COLLEGE_SEASONS`, `COLLEGE_BENCH=<file>` for timings and stats), `schedule.test.ts`, `quickSim.test.ts` (calibration bounds and box score consistency); calibration scripts `collegeCalibration.script.test.ts`, `quickSimData.script.test.ts` (dumps a season of full-sim or quick-sim box scores for fitting). `COLLEGE_QUICK=1/0` forces the quick sim in `collegeSeasons.test.ts`.

## Coaching staff

- **Where:** Team menu → _Coaching Staff_ (`/l/:lid/staff`, `/l/:lid/staff/:team`), League menu → _Coaches_ (`/l/:lid/coaches[/:season]`: head coaches, the season's coaching carousel, Coach of the Year history), and a page per coach (`/l/:lid/coach/:cid`). Team Finances links to the staff instead of offering the coaching slider.
- **What:** every team has a head coach and three assistants (player development, offense, defense) with ratings (development, offense, defense, motivation), a style (pace, threes, defense, rotation, each -1 to 1), age and a contract. Salaries are paid as coaching expenses (not against the salary cap). The staff's development score is turned into an equivalent coaching budget level, so it replaces the coaching budget's effect on progs, normalized by the league's average staff each time levels are synced, so the league-wide development effect stays centered like default AI coaching budgets while the coach pool's ratings drift over the decades (see `staff.test.ts` and [rating-inflation.md](rating-inflation.md)). A team's score carries 65% of last season's (`t.staffDevCarry`), so a new coach's effect phases in and a team's development edge is stable from year to year (year-to-year change SD of the effect 2.4% → 1.1% of progs, same spread across teams; coach ratings move SD 1 a year instead of 1.5; see rating-inflation.md, "year-to-year swings"). After the playoffs each season: records are written to each coach (a coach hired or fired during the season gets only the games he was in charge of), Coach of the Year is awarded, coaches age/improve/retire, contracts expire, AI teams fire (losing head coaches especially) and hire from the pool (bigger markets pay more). Your expiring coaches leave unless you _Extend_ them (the new salary, his asking price, starts at once, but never below what he makes now); firing pays half the remaining contract as a buyout. Any vacancy still open in the preseason is filled automatically.
- **Pages** (`src/ui/views/Staff/`, `Coaches.tsx`, `Coach.tsx`; shared pieces in `src/ui/components/CoachUi.tsx`, wording and "what does this mean?" texts in `src/common/staffText.ts`): _Coaching Staff_ leads with the head coach (portrait, record against the preseason expectation, contract, overall; with Coaching Systems: both systems in plain words, scheme fit with the players helping and hurting it and the systems the roster would fit best, installation, job security, reputation, philosophy, personality, mentor), a _What the staff does for the team_ card (development level and rank, each job's share of player development, game-day strengths, payroll) and one card per assistant (what the job does, ratings as words and bars). For your team: _Hire a coach_ (one job at a time, recommendation tags for the leader in overall, fit with your roster, development, tactics and reputation, filters by system, "better than my coach", a sortable table with each candidate's difference from your coach and the development level you would have with him), a side-by-side _Compare_ with a better/worse verdict, and confirm dialogs that spell out the contract, buyout, the vacancy's effect on development and the installation period before anything happens (_Replace_ fires the current coach, then hires). _Coaches_ is a sortable table with views for head coaches, all staff and available coaches (fit with your roster), filters by team, system and job security, then the carousel as tagged moves and Coach of the Year. Each concept has a help popover. With Coaching Systems off the pages say so and hide the systems columns.
- **Staff faces:** coaches and GMs are drawn in suits (`src/common/faceParts.suits.ts`, `src/ui/components/StaffFace.tsx`): six original facesjs "jersey" parts (charcoal, navy, grey, open collar, blazer, team quarter-zip) registered with the fork's face parts but never given to players, picked per person from his id, with the team's colours on the tie or pin (a white or black first colour is skipped) and hair that greys from the mid 40s. Applied when drawn, so stored faces are unchanged and existing leagues get suits too.
- **Setting:** `coachingStaff` (basketball only). On by default for new leagues; existing leagues (DB version 76 migration) and league files from before version 76 keep the slider until it's turned on, which generates staffs.
- **Storage:** `coaches` object store (key `cid`, index `tid`), included in league export under _All team data_.
- **For other features:** `src/worker/core/staff/data.ts` has `getTeamCoachStyle(tid)` (for team strategy / game sim later), `getCoachingCarousel(season)`, `getHeadCoachForSeason(tid, season)` and `getCoachAwards(type)`.
- **Known limits:** in-game effects come with [Coaching systems](#coaching-systems) (`coachSystems`); without it the style is exposed only. Trainers are not modeled; scouts are, as a separate department (see [Scouting reports](#scouting-reports)). With the budget disabled, every team gets the average development effect, as with the slider.

### Coaching Staff Depth

- **Where:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ League Extras → _Coaching Staff Depth_ (`featureCoachingDepth`, basketball, on for new leagues, off for existing ones; needs Coaching Staff). Coaching Staff (three more cards and hiring tabs), coach pages (career and coaching tree), the Staff Report. In college leagues only the careers part runs (the switch is hidden there; the extra jobs and plans are pro only). Off: nothing below runs, and the staff code takes exactly the old paths (the coach `progress` draws the same four numbers).
- **Staff roles** (effects in `common/staffDepth/coaching.ts`, each against the league average of that job: z = (rating − mean) / 15, at most ±2, centered over the league, so the league is neutral at average staff):
  - _Head coach_: systems and game plan (unchanged). _Offensive / defensive coordinators_: unchanged inputs of Coaching Systems (40% of the offense/defense strength behind the make-probability bonus of the system; no sim change).
  - _Player development coach_: unchanged share of general development (staff dev score), and the size of the development plans (×0.75 at 30 ... ×1.25 at 80+).
  - _Shooting coach_ (new, rating `sht`): three-pointers, mid-range and free throws +0.4 rating points a season per z (×0.7 from 26, ×0.4 from 31), applied as a fixed shift of those ratings' yearly change; the shooting subs follow their bases.
  - _Strength & conditioning_ (new, `cnd`): players 27+ lose 0.35 points a season less speed, jumping and endurance per z (the aging hook: the same yearly change); in-game injury rate ×(1 − 0.04 z) (the `medicalInjuryFactor` input the Front Office's Medical already uses, multiplied, so both count once each: Medical is the budget, S&C the staff).
  - _Video/analytics coordinator_ (new, `vid`): with Coaching Systems, the head coach's game management +4 per z (re-centered by `normalizeCoachSims`) and his playoff series adjustments (adaptability) +8 per z. Without Coaching Systems he has no effect.
  - New jobs have ratings (derived deterministically for coaches made before), contracts (base $0.9M each at a $90M cap, about $2.7M a team for all three) and a hiring market like the others. Rating changes: the extra ratings follow each coach's age trend without random numbers.
- **Development plans that run themselves:** each preseason, before development, every rostered player gets a focus (`chooseFocus`): the biggest gap between what his position group needs (guards: three, handle, playmaking, perimeter D, pull-up, finishing, free throws; wings: three, pull-up, perimeter D, finishing, handle, glass; bigs: rim protection, glass, post, finishing, a three only if he has some touch) and what he has, +3 for shooting/free throws at 30+, +6 for the team priority. Ten foci (`FOCUSES`), each with bases and target subs ("Add a three: range + catch-and-shoot"). The plan spends 1.2 rating points (×0.85 at 23-25, ×0.6 at 26-29, ×0.4 at 30+, × the development coach) on the focus bases, as a shift within the yearly per-base roll (`TraitDevelopment.ratingDelta`, no extra roll): every other rating gives up enough that the player's ovr is unchanged by the league's own formula (gradient of `ovrRaw` at his ratings, expanded ratings followed, the tilt's own ovr change included), and each delta is made a whole number with the right expectation (seeded, since development floors ratings). Inside the focus bases 1.5 sub points (same scaling) move onto the target subs from their siblings (`tiltSubs`; the expanded ratings that correspond to them follow, sums kept). **League centering:** the plans are centered over the league each preseason (`prepareDevelopment` plans every rostered player first): the league's average plan rating deltas are taken off every planned player's, and its average sub tilt off every tilted player's subs (expanded ratings following), so a plan builds a player's focus relative to the league and the league as a whole develops like without plans. Uncentered, the staff's uneven choices (in a new league 110 finishing, 65 three, 44 rim protection, 13 perimeter D, 1 pull-up) moved catch-and-shoot ~0.33 a season ahead of off-the-dribble shooting and interior defense ~0.2 ahead of perimeter defense, and moved rating points from jumpers, ball skills and IQ to finishing, rim protection and rebounding at the same ovr. Centered, the rotation's diq gains ~0.4 instead of ~0.9 over 8 seasons and catch-and-shoot's lead grows ~0.6 less (the rest of both splits' drift comes with the rotation getting ~1 point taller); league scoring held at its level either way (4 seeds, 8 seasons, League Eras off). The user can override per player or set a team-wide priority (Staff Report); AI teams always run the staff's plans. After the preseason, the **Development Report** (Staff Report) shows each player's focus, his target subs before and after and his ovr.
- **Coaching careers and trees:** head coaches are made as head coaches only at league creation (`tree.initial`). New coaches enter the pool as assistants (the pool replenishment skips the head job), and a few retired players (6+ seasons, 30-40, about one in twelve, more with high IQ, at most three a season) start as offensive or defensive assistants (`exPlayerCoaches`, seeded). Head coaching candidates are former head coaches and experienced assistants, free (3+ seasons) or employed on any team (1+ season; internal promotions too), scored by head coach ovr + their team's success last season (up to +6, so good assistants are hired away after good seasons) + experience (up to +3) + 1 for an internal candidate. A promoted coach records his mentor (the head coach he last worked under) and the promotion, carries his mentor's systems (Coaching Systems, as before), and his old team fills the job. Coach pages get _Career and coaching tree_: where he started (or "former player"), promotions, his mentors upward, every head coach from his tree (three levels) and the assistants who worked under him. Staff hiring delegation: _Auto_ fills the user's open jobs in the offseason and re-signs nobody automatically (unchanged), _Suggest_ proposes a candidate per open job, _Manual_ is the old behaviour (open jobs filled in the preseason).
- **Calibration** (`RUN_STAFF_DEPTH=1`, `src/test/staffDepth/calibration.script.test.ts`, seeds 1-2 × 10 seasons, the same leagues with both settings on and off):
  - _Development plans_ (the same 400 players and random numbers developed one season with and without the staff's plan, development coach 55): the focus subs (or bases) change +1.69 a season with the plan vs +0.23 without (gain +1.44 and +1.48 in the two leagues: +2.15 at 22 and under, +0.91 at 30+; by focus: three +1.9 to +2.1, rim protection +2.0 to +2.1, handle +2.0, post +1.8 to +2.0, perimeter D +1.5 to +1.7, finishing +1.4, rebounding +0.8 to +1.0, free throws +0.8 to +0.9, playmaking +0.6 to +0.7); ovr change +0.01 (±0.02 between seeds): ovr-neutral.
  - _Shooting coach_ one z (15 points) above average: three-point rating +0.33 a season, free throws +0.32 (all ages, after rounding). _Strength & conditioning_ one z above: speed/jumping/endurance +0.34 a season for players 27+; in-game injury rate ×0.96.
  - _League-wide_ (10 seasons, on vs off): mean ovr of rostered players 50.24 vs 50.22, top 50 ovr 65.90 vs 66.01, injuries per team-season 16.5 vs 16.3: neutral within noise.
  - _Coaching careers_: about 5.1 promotions to head coach a season (17% of head jobs; 1-8), nearly all hired away from (or promoted within) an NBA staff; no head coach was generated as one after league creation. Head coaches by career start after 1 / 5 / 10 seasons (two leagues): made as head coaches at creation 27 / 11 / 5, initial assistants promoted 3 / 18.5 / 23, new assistants promoted 0 / 0.5 / 2. Employed coaches who started as assistants: 75% at creation (the assistants of the first staffs), 81% after 5 seasons, 86% after 10.

- **Code:** `common/staffDepth/` (`coaching.ts`, `devPlans.ts`), `common/staff.ts` (roles, ratings), `worker/core/staffDepth/coaching.ts` (z, plans, report), `careers.ts`, hooks in `staff/index.ts`, `staff/systems.ts` (video), `game/loadTeams.ts` (S&C), `phase/newPhasePreseason.ts`, `player/develop.ts`, `player/developSeason.basketball.ts`, `player/subRatings.ts` (`tiltRowSubs`), `player/ovr.basketball.ts` (`ovrRaw`).

### Coach market and succession

- **Where:** League Extras → _Coach Market & Succession_ (`featureCoachMarket`, basketball, on for new leagues, off for existing ones; needs Coaching Staff Depth; off in college leagues). Coaching Staff page: an Interest column and Interview button in the hiring table, a salary offer in the hire dialog, a _Coach market_ panel (offers, interviews, interim, heir), "Name associate head coach" on assistant cards. Code: `worker/core/staff/market.ts`, `common/staffDepth/coachMarket.ts`. Off: nothing below runs.
- **Interviews:** up to 3 per hiring season (the season a contract signed now starts). An interview reveals, with seeded noise, what the coach cares about, his game management, adaptability, youth/personality leanings (with Coaching Systems), teaching, his roster fit for head coaches and his interest in the job. An interviewed candidate is a little more interested.
- **Preferences:** each coach weighs team success, market size and salary (stable weights from his identity). Interest 0-100; under 30 he turns the job down. The user can offer 10% or 20% over the asking price.
- **Competing offers:** once per candidate and hiring season, a top candidate (top 3 at the job, or a high overall) may draw an offer from an AI team that has the job open or a clearly worse coach in it (45% for head coaches, 30% for assistants; none for eager candidates or a 20% premium). The user matches it (pays the rival's salary) or walks away (the rival hires him, firing its incumbent). Offers still open in the preseason go to the rival. A replacement hire now fires the old coach only once the hire went through.
- **Associate head coach (heir apparent):** one assistant per team. Each offseason he gains 0-2 in offense, defense and motivation (below 78). When the head job is open he is promoted at once (AI teams automatically, before the market; the user with "Promote him", or automatically if the job is still open in the preseason) and keeps the predecessor's systems and their installation (no new installation period). AI teams name the assistant with the best head coach overall (45+, 60 or younger).
- **Interim coaches:** a head coach fired during the season is replaced at once by the heir, else by the assistant with the best head coach overall as interim (a 1-season deal). After the season the user keeps him (contract length of choice) or replaces him (he goes back to his old job if it is open, else leaves); undecided by the preseason he stays. AI interims are kept at .500 or better (or 25% of the time), else replaced.
- **Determinism:** seeded streams only (`interview|`, `rivalOffer|`, `heirGrowth|`, `interim|` with the league seed, team, coach and season); no draws from the main random stream.

### Scout specialties and assignments

- **Where:** League Extras → _Scout Specialties & Assignments_ (`featureScoutSpecialties`, basketball, on for new leagues, off for existing ones; needs Scouting Director & Regions). Scouting Department (specialty on each scout, an _Assignments_ table), the prospect Scouting page byline. Code: `common/staffDepth/scoutFocus.ts`. Off: nothing below runs.
- **Specialties:** every scout has one: a region (US college, international), a position group (guards, bigs) or a skill (shooting, athleticism). From his existing seeded specialty (college, international, athletes, shooters); pro personnel scouts get guards, bigs or shooting from a seeded pick. Nothing stored.
- **Effect:** weekly and event looks at a prospect get ×1.35 when a scout assigned to his region has him in his specialty (the fuzz narrows faster). In scouting reports a scout whose specialty covers the prospect writes the report (unless clearly worse), his own error is ×0.75 (tighter grades), and the byline says so.
- **Assignments:** for each scout his region, specialty, how many of the region's current class are in his specialty, and up to six prospects there (in his specialty first).

### Staff Report and delegation

- **Where:** `/l/:lid/staff_report`: Team menu → _Staff Report_, a tab of Draft Scouting, the dashboard (_Your staff_), the Scouting Department, Coaching Staff and Pro Scouting pages. Shown when either feature is on.
- **What:** delegation per area (scouting assignments, development plans, staff hiring, draft board): _Auto_ (default; nothing needs the user), _Suggest_ (the staff proposes and the user approves or dismisses here: region reassignments, next season's plans after the playoffs, a hire per open job, board updates) or _Manual_. Then one card per area, each with one recommended action accepted with one click: _Scouting_ (Director, priority, top targets with confidence and looks, coverage badges, blind spots, the latest board changes; send a scout to the biggest blind spot, or keep the priority), _Development_ (the last Development Report, next season's plans with per-player overrides and the team priority; recommend a team priority when 3 of the top 8 need the same thing), _System fit_ (fit of the head coach's systems, the systems the roster fits best, weakest area, needs; watch Pro Scouting's best target), _Injuries and conditioning_ (S&C coach and his injury factor, older players, injuries; hire a better S&C coach when below average).
- **Dismiss for the season:** each card's recommendation (scouting, development, system fit, conditioning) has _Dismiss_ next to _Accept_: it collapses to a line with _Show it again_ until the season ends (`staffDepth.dismissedRecs`, the season and the dismissed recommendation ids, at most 50; a new recommendation has a new id and shows). Refused in spectator mode and auto play like the other actions.
- **Development table:** the last development's results sort by the change in the worked-on skills or in ovr (click the column, again for the other direction; not under No Visible Ratings, which has no numbers), first 10 with _Show all_.
- **Offense** (with Offensive Emphasis on): a card with who sets the offensive emphasis (Auto / Suggest / Manual, the same switch as the Offense tab), its fit to the roster and a recommendation line from the play-type PPP and the roster's strengths; see [Offensive emphasis](#offensive-emphasis).

## AI GM personalities

- **Where:** _Front Offices_ tab of League History (`/l/:lid/front_offices`; also in the League "More" links and the command palette), a GM card on each AI team's roster page (face, style, tenure, record, job security, _Show leanings_), and news items when a GM is hired, fired, retires or leaves (none in college leagues, which have athletic directors rather than general managers and no Executive of the Year). _Why?_ on the Trade page names the AI team's GM and how his leanings moved an asset's value.
- **What:** every AI team has a general manager with a name, face, age and ten fixed leanings, each from -1 to 1 (`GmTraits`, `src/common/types.gm.ts`): timeline (win-now ↔ patient), evaluation (old-school ↔ analytics), risk, luxury tax tolerance, draft pick valuation, trade frequency, loyalty, free agency (depth ↔ stars), draft board (need ↔ best available) and prospects (ready-now ↔ upside). They're drawn around 0 (sd about 0.45, a few mildly correlated: win-now GMs spend more and trade picks), so the league as a whole values players, picks and contracts like before. Your teams have no GM unless the AI runs them in spectator mode; the card says _General manager: You_. **Taking a team over** (switching teams after being fired, adding it in multi team mode, turning spectator mode off) ends its GM's tenure at once ("left": his last season is this one once the playoffs have started, else the one before) and puts him on the market; **giving one up** (leaving it, removing it from multi team mode, turning spectator mode on) names a new GM at once. Both happen in `ensureGms`, called whenever the user's teams change (`league.setGameAttributes`). Auto play changes nothing: your teams keep no GM while it runs (the AI runs them neutrally), so no GM is hired or fired on a team you'll take back. A GM named outside the offseason carousel (a team you gave up, an expansion team) is drawn from a stream seeded by his id, never the main random stream. **Contraction:** the team's GM leaves ("left") and goes on the market from the contracted team's list of former GMs, so its history stays on Front Offices; a revived or new expansion team names a new GM as soon as it's created. GM ids are allocated over every team, contracted ones and former GMs included. Turning the setting on once the playoffs have started starts the first tenures next season.
- **Effects** (`gmWeightsFromTraits` in `src/common/gmPersonality.ts`, all neutral at 0; with the setting off none of this code runs and every decision is exactly as before):
  - _Trade value_ (`team/ValueChangeCalculator.ts`): future picks and players ≤ 23 ×(1 ± 0.07) by timeline, veterans ≥ 29 the other way (±0.06); picks ×(1 ± 0.12) by pick valuation; a player style factor ±6% (analytics GMs like youth, potential, PER beyond the ratings; old-school GMs like ovr, size and experience); raw prospects ±3% by risk; the injury discount ×(1 ∓ 0.35) by risk; the weight of good/bad contracts ×(1 ∓ 0.3) by tax tolerance; and his own drafted or 3+ season players ±6% by loyalty when he'd give them up (which also drives re-signing, since that uses the same math). Trade values are value⁷, so these are smaller than the team strategy factors.
  - _Trades_ (`trade/betweenAiTeams.ts`, `trade/aiNeeds.ts`, `trade/aiOffers.ts`): each team's chance of being picked for an AI-AI trade attempt (both sides, every need-driven kind) and for an offer to you is ×(1 + 0.55 × trade frequency), averaging 1; win-now GMs are deadline buyers at a lower win% (0.45 ± 0.06); tax-tolerant GMs dump salary only a bit further over the tax (±5%).
  - _Free agency_ (`freeAgents/autoSign.ts`): dealmakers are a bit more active each day; star hunters rank expensive free agents up to 2.5 value points higher, depth builders cheaper ones. Contract amounts are unchanged (demands come from the player).
  - _Re-signing, extensions, options_ (`phase/newPhaseResignPlayers.ts`, `contract/hooks.ts`, `contract/options.ts`): the roster rank past which a team won't re-sign into the luxury tax (`AVOID_TAX_MIN_RANK`, 3) moves ±1 by tax tolerance; loyal GMs extend up to 60% more often and keep team options at a slightly lower bar; big spenders extend a top-2 player into the tax.
  - _Draft_ (`draft/runPicks.ts`, `draft/draftNeeds.ts`, mock draft): the need adjustment of _AI Drafts by Need_ ×(1 ∓ 0.75) by best available vs need, and up to ±1.5 value points for upside vs ready-now (pot - ovr gap and age); in the AI draft decision (`draft/draftDecision.ts`) also patience (timeline weight), analytics (production, consensus, combine athleticism and the grade lens), risk (medical discount and scout disagreement) and the finance style (analytics, development, frugal stash).
  - _Strategy_ (`team/updateStrategies.ts`): switch thresholds shift ±8 points (win-now GMs go for it sooner and hold on longer).
  - _Coaches_ (`staff/index.ts`, with the coaching staff on): analytics/patient GMs weigh development over motivation when hiring; patient GMs fire coaches less (×(1 ∓ 0.35)).
- **Jobs:** when the regular season starts each GM gets an expectation, the Season Odds projected wins (or, with Season Odds off, the roster rating against the league average). He's judged only on full seasons he ran (`judgedSeasons`): seasons before he was hired, or one he took over after it started, don't count. After the playoffs each season scores wins above the expectation (per 82 games), where after his first season the expectation is 60% the projection and 40% an improvement path from what his inherited roster was projected to win (a bad team is expected to climb towards .500 over five seasons, seven when rebuilding; a good team to stay good; `gmPathExpWon`), plus playoff success (2 for making it, 3 per round won), minus a penalty for missing the playoffs only when he was projected at .550 or better, minus 1. Losing with a roster that was projected to lose costs nothing. **Rebuild credit** (`gmRebuildWeight`, `gmProgressScore`): for a rebuilding team (0.45) and/or a patient GM (up to 0.35 more), that share of the score comes from progress instead of results: change in the value of his three best players 25 and under, extra first round picks, cap room and the win trend. Job security is the last three judged seasons weighted 1/0.6/0.35 (_Secure_, _Stable_, _Warm_, _Hot seat_). **Honeymoon:** no firing in his first two full seasons unless his job security is below -24, a season about 20+ wins under what his roster was projected to win; the earliest ordinary firing is after his third. Then the chance of being fired grows with how far below -9 that score is (-14 for a rebuilding team in his first four seasons, up to 3 more for a patient GM), shorter for win-now GMs hired into projected contenders (up to +4), ±1.5 by market size (big-market owners are less patient), plus droughts without the playoffs past 4 seasons (5 when rebuilding), from 20% up to 85%. GMs retire from 66 (certainly by 75). The **GM carousel** fills each job with the best of the former GMs on the market (out of a job within the last 4 seasons, fired or left, the user having taken his team over or it having been contracted; 66 or younger at the carousel; never back to a team he ran) and three new candidates, by career record plus noise. Front Offices lists exactly the GMs the next carousel can hire (`onGmMarket`); any other former GM retires at the next carousel. The firing news gives his record with ties and overtime losses.
- **Hot seat news:** at the trade deadline (in a league without one, once every team has played half its games), the AI GMs who would most likely be fired if the season ended now (`projectedFireProb`, the season so far projected to a full one and judged like the owner judges it, so the honeymoon applies) are in the news: "Sources say general manager X's job with the Y (record) is reportedly (seriously) in danger." A GM needs at least 10 games and a 30% projected chance (`HOT_SEAT_NEWS_MIN_PROB`), is reported with that probability (a stream seeded by the season and his id, never the main stream), at most once a season (`gm.hotSeatNews`), and at most 3 a season, worst first (`HOT_SEAT_NEWS_MAX`). Pop-up category _Staff & coaching_ (Settings > notification filter). Not in college leagues; never about the user's teams.
- **GM career achievements** (Achievements page, category _Front Office_, basketball, checked after the playoffs like the others, so not in college or spectator mode): _Survivor_ (10 AI GMs fired since the league started, needs AI GM personalities), _Executive of the Year_ (you win it, needs Coach & Executive of the Year), _Everywhere You Go_ (a title with every team you ran, at least 2; seasons are credited by `userControl`, so spectator and auto play seasons don't count), _Blueprint_ (a title within 4 seasons of a season your team, the same one every season since, was projected in the bottom 3 by its roster rating at its first game, `teamSeason.ovrStart`). Code: `worker/util/achievements.ts`, tests `achievementsGm.test.ts`.
- **Storage:** on the team objects: `t.gm` (current GM, with every season's record and expectation, tenures and job security) and `t.formerGms` (GMs who left that team, including retired ones without their faces, and a contracted team's). A retired GM's season rows are folded into `gm.career` (seasons, record, playoffs, titles) so long leagues don't keep every row; his tenures and awards stay for Front Offices and Executive of the Year. **Who ran a season:** the user's team seasons record `userControl` when the regular season and the playoffs start (`team/markUserControl.ts`): "user" if he ran the team himself at either point, else "auto" (spectator mode or auto play). Executive of the Year credits each past season by it (older seasons: the season's `userTid`, never today's user teams), and GM History lists every team you ran each season (multi team mode included) and skips spectator and auto play seasons. No database store, so league files keep them with the teams (`team.generate` passes them through). Code: `src/worker/core/gm/index.ts` (`getGmWeights`, `getAllGmWeights`, `ensureGms`, `onRegularSeason`, `runOffseason`), views `frontOffices.ts`, `gmShared.ts`.
- **Measured** (AI GM audit below, `AI_GMS=0` vs on, every other extra on, seeds 3/4/5 × 10 seasons, 30 teams; per-season means): AI-AI trades 12.8 → 14.5 per season (per-team totals over 10 seasons: mean 8.5 → 9.6, sd 3.0 → 3.8, so volume is a little higher and spread more by team; a team's count correlates only weakly, about 0.12, with its GM's trade frequency, since luck and needs dominate), teams over the tax 4.0 → 3.8, over the cap 15.8 → 15.1, mean contract 7.34% → 7.30% of the cap, win% sd 0.15 → 0.15, team ovr sd 13.7 → 13.4, strategy switches 2.9 → 3.2 per season, FA signings 36.9 → 38.4, re-signings 37.1 → 38.9, extensions 30.2 → 28.5. GMs: 2.2 fired and 0.2 retired per season, mean tenure about 6 seasons (capped by the 10 season runs). Fair job security (seeds 12/13/14 × 16 seasons, `AI_GM_DUMP`; before = the old rules, seeds 12/14): 2.7 → 2.0 fired per season; share of hires fired by team quality at hire (previous season win%, bottom/middle/top third) 47%/59%/38% → 29%/27%/50% (win-now hires on contenders have the shorter leash; few top-third hires); mean tenure of ended tenures 5.8 → 7.0 seasons (all tenures 6.4 → 7.3); fired within 2 seasons 4% → 9% of firings (only extreme misses), within 3 38% → 41%.
- **Known limits:** GMs don't make in-season coaching changes, have no contracts, and leave when you take their team over. Leanings never change.

## AI GM finance styles

_Always on in every league (core; see Core features)._ Setting `featureGmFinance` (basketball, off in college leagues; needs AI GM personalities and the `budget` setting). An existing league gets it when it is loaded. With it off nothing below runs and every AI decision is exactly as before.

- **Where:** a _Finances_ column on the Front Offices tab (the GM table and the market table, with the description as a tooltip), a _Finances: …_ line in the expanded row and on the team page GM card, a _Finance styles_ list at the bottom of Front Offices, the GM carousel ("hired X, a frugal GM") and the hiring news items ("… hired X, a frugal GM, as general manager").
- **The style:** each AI GM has one of six fixed styles (`gm.financeStyle`, `src/common/gmFinance.ts`), drawn when he's created from a stream seeded by his gmid (and the league salt), never `Math.random`, weighted by his traits: tax-tolerant (and win-now) GMs lean spendthrift, tax-averse ones frugal, analytics GMs analytics-first, patient ones player development. GMs from before (existing leagues) get the same style derived from their id by `ensureGms` (on load via `forceCoreFeatures`, and every preseason). Turning it on draws no main-stream random numbers, so the league's GMs and everything else stay the same.
- **Profiles** (`GM_FINANCE_STYLES`; spend in budget levels added to the owner's level, tax/FA as shifts of the GM's trait, ticket as a multiplier of the AI's automatic price):

  | Style              | Spend | Tax  | FA   | Ticket | Departments                                                            | Projects             | Deals        |
  | ------------------ | ----- | ---- | ---- | ------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------- | ------------ |
  | Spendthrift        | +6    | +0.4 | +0.2 | ×0.96  | even                                                                   | ×1.4                 | performance  |
  | Frugal             | −6    | −0.4 | −0.2 | ×1.06  | even                                                                   | ×0.5, 1.5 caps spare | long         |
  | Analytics-first    | 0     | 0    | 0    | ×1.02  | analytics ×1.6, scouting/travel ×1.1, coaching/facilities ×0.8         | —                    | owner/random |
  | Player development | 0     | −0.1 | −0.1 | ×1     | coaching ×1.35, facilities ×1.3, scouting ×1.1, analytics/travel ×0.85 | ×1.2                 | long         |
  | Facilities builder | 0     | 0    | 0    | ×1.03  | facilities ×1.45, medical ×1.05, analytics/scouting ×0.9               | ×2.4, 0.5 caps spare | long         |
  | Balanced           | 0     | 0    | 0    | ×1     | even                                                                   | —                    | owner/random |

- **Effects** (`src/worker/core/gm/finance.ts`, all bounded):
  - _Budgets_ (`newPhasePreseason`): the spend nudge is added to the owner's level (market size, cash nudge, owner type) before `allocateAi` splits it; the department multipliers multiply `aiDeptWeights` and are renormalized, so they only move money between departments. With the coaching staff on, coaching follows the staff as before.
  - _Ticket price_ (`writeTeamStats`, preseason reset): the AI's automatic price × the factor (bounded 0.94-1.07), so frugal teams sell fewer seats at a higher price.
  - _Payroll and tax_: the tax shift moves the GM's tax tolerance trait (bounded ±0.4), which feeds the existing weights: the roster rank past which he won't re-sign into the tax (`avoidTaxRankShift`, `newPhaseResignPlayers`), using cap exceptions into the tax in free agency, the weight of bad contracts in trades and when he dumps salary. The FA shift moves his star hunter/depth builder trait (free agency targets).
  - _Projects_ (Owner's Office `maybeStartAi`): the 25% start chance × the style's factor (at most 60%), and the cash reserve he wants beyond the cost (1 cap by default). Same seeded stream as before.
  - _Commercial deals_ (`aiStyle`): a traditional owner leaves the choice to the GM (his style 60%, else random); another owner type's preference comes first (60%) and the GM's style next (60% of the rest). Same seeded stream.
