- **Owner first:** the owner sets the envelope, the GM decides inside it. A bottom line owner gets no extra spending, tax, star chasing, cheaper tickets or extra projects from his GM; a big spender or win-now owner no cuts in spending, tax willingness or star chasing (`effectiveGmFinanceProfile`).
- **Measured** (`economyAudit.script.test.ts`, `ECO_BASE=defaults`, seeds 1-2 × 3 seasons, 30 teams, `featureGmFinance` off vs on, 180 team-seasons each). League means off → on: department level (without coaching, which follows the staff) 34.2 → 33.9, analytics 33.2 → 32.9, facilities 34.7 → 35.7, ticket price $44.7 → $45.5, attendance 13,018 → 12,820, ticket revenue $160.5M → $160.8M, payroll 1.03 → 1.03 caps, teams over the tax 18% → 16%, tax paid $4.6M → $4.3M per team, profit −$11.1M → −$11.0M: the league economy is unchanged within noise. Paired by team-season (same GM style in both runs), on − off: spendthrift levels +3.2, attendance +276; frugal levels −2.6, ticket +$3.4, attendance −967, profit +$6.1M; analytics-first analytics +8.4, facilities −3.6; facilities builder facilities +13.3, ticket +$2.3; player development analytics −8.0 and travel −9.0 (its coaching weight is unused with the coaching staff on); balanced within ±0.7. Deal styles signed (on vs off): frugal 51 long of 66 (12 of 66), development 50 of 52 long (15), spendthrift 40 performance of 84 (1 of 76), balanced unchanged. The cash nudge pulls the spend nudges back part of the way (frugal teams save, so their owner spends more), and no AI project started in either run (no team had a project's cost plus the reserve in cash in these leagues), so the project effect is untested at league scale. Payroll and tax effects are too small to separate from noise in 3 seasons.
- **Tests:** `src/common/gmFinance.test.ts` (stable seeded styles, spread and trait weighting, owner wins on conflicts, department multipliers keep the total), `gm.test.ts` _AI GM finance styles_ (same GMs and next random number with it on and off, existing GMs get the same style back, tax weights by style, bottom line owner blocks a spendthrift's tax, off = neutral). Measurement: `economyAudit.script.test.ts` with `ECO_GMF_OUT` (one row per team-season: style, owner type, department levels, ticket price, attendance, payroll, tax, profit, cash, projects, deal styles).

## Front office budget

- **Where:** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ Team → _Finances_ (the _Front office budget_ form replaces _Expense levels_, and _What your spending bought_ sits above Player Salaries), an _Analytics projection_ line in trade summaries for players you'd receive, a _Proj_ column on _Free Agents_, and the game sim, AI budgets and owner mood. Setting `featureFrontOffice` (basketball, on for new leagues, off for existing ones, off in college leagues). It needs the `budget` setting; with the budget off nothing changes.
- **Departments:** Scouting, Analytics (new), Medical (the health budget item), Travel & Sports Science (new), Coaching (set by the coaching staff when that's on) and Facilities. Each has a level from 1 to 100 like the old budget items, a slider with its league rank and the league average, its annual cost, and a one-line effect at the 3-season average level (and at the slider's level after 3 seasons). Analytics and Travel cost 0.4x a budget item's level (`DEPT_COST_SCALE`); their levels live in `t.frontOffice`, are paid each regular season game into `teamSeason.frontOffice.expenses` and recorded in `teamSeason.frontOffice.expenseLevels` like the others, and start (and are imputed for missing seasons) at the team's initial level, the average of its initial scouting, health and facilities levels, so a new level phases in over 3 seasons like the old items.
- **Owner envelope:** the owner funds every department at a target level by market size (`ownerTargetLevel`: popRank like `defaultBudgetLevel` without its random part, about 60 for the biggest market, 34 in the middle, 10 for the smallest). The bar shows the planned spend against it; more in one department means less in another unless you go over. Over the envelope (beyond 2%), end-of-season owner money mood drops 0.75 per 100% over (`overspendMoodPenalty`), on top of the lower profit. With the coaching staff on, coaching is its staff's salaries, which the budget doesn't set, so it is outside the envelope and the spending measured against it (before, the envelope counted coaching at the target level while the staff's payroll is about half that, so every team had about 10% of hidden headroom). Checked end to end (sweep19, a default league, the user's departments set before the season, played through the playoffs to the owner mood update at the end of the season, grace period ended, no auto play): every department at the owner's target (39), spend exactly the envelope, no penalty, money +0.49; every department at 100, 44% over the envelope, penalty 0.32 and money -0.12 (the rest of the drop is the lower profit, $83M -> $34M). Analytics and Travel & Sports Science spending counts as an expense in profit, the revenue/expense charts and owner money mood like the other departments, with no discount on the owner's profit expectation (before, the expectation was lowered by their cost at level 34, a free bonus for anyone who spent less).
- **Effects** (`src/common/frontOffice.ts`; every one reads `levelToEffect` of the 3-season average, 0 at level 34, linear below and tanh above, so the top of the scale buys less than the bottom costs; all neutral at the league average):
  - _Scouting_: unchanged and reused — the scouting budget already sets the scouting department's size and quality (2-5 scouts, Scouting Reports) and prospect fuzz (stddev 3.1 at level 1, 2 at 34, 0.94 at 100). With [Draft Lottery Night & Combine](#draft-lottery-night-and-combine) on, a department at a 3-season level of 55 or more (`EARLY_COMBINE_LEVEL`) gets an early read of the current class's combine from the playoffs on (the prospect's Draft Combine section says _early read_; the combine is a pure function of each prospect, so it's exactly what the combine will show at the draft lottery; not while a linked college league's automatic import is still to replace the class, see [College mode](college-mode.md)).
  - _Analytics_: your projection of a player's ovr next season (an 80% band from an age curve, `analyticsProjection`) is 1.4x as wide at level 1 and 0.65x at 100, and a better department's center sees through that share of the scouting fuzz; with Coaching Systems the head coach's game management is ±12 points (`coachSimEffects` input, the league is centered again by `normalizeCoachSims`) and with Realistic Rotations his youth/veteran minutes bias ×0.6 at the top to ×1.45 at the bottom; with AI GM Personalities a GM whose team spends leans analytics (±0.3 on his trait, `getGmWeights`).
  - _Medical_: the health budget's injury durations are unchanged (-12% to +13%); Medical adds the in-game injury rate (×0.93 to ×1.08, `medicalInjuryFactor` on each player in the sim) and the recovery of players 30 and older (another -10% to +11% of the duration, fully from 34). Nothing is counted twice.
  - _Travel & Sports Science_: the endurance penalties of travel and 3 games in 4 nights (Load, Fatigue & Venue) and of back-to-backs (Injury Depth) ×0.53 at the top to ×1.5 at the bottom (`travelPenaltyScale`); with neither feature on, endurance ±1.5% instead.
- **AI teams** (preseason, `allocateAi`): the owner's spending (the usual `defaultBudgetLevel` by market size, with its randomness) split over the departments by the GM's leanings (`aiDeptWeights`, mean 1 so the total stays): analytics GMs spend on analytics and less on scouting, old-school GMs the other way, win-now GMs on medical and travel, patient ones on scouting and coaching. Without GMs the leanings come from the team strategy and a fixed per-team taste.
- **What your spending bought** (measured, `teamSeason.frontOffice.review`, compared with the same events at level 34): injury games saved = the duration difference of each injury from the same random draw at an average health budget, plus the injuries the Medical rate avoided (Σ 1/factor - 1) times their length; prospect rating error = your mean |fuzz| on the current prospects against the same draws scaled to an average scouting budget; travel fatigue saved = the endurance percentage points of travel, back-to-back and 3-in-4 penalties your sports science staff took off. Early in a season (under 10 games) last season's review is shown.
- **Off:** none of this runs: no extra random numbers, the old budget code paths (AI budget resets, injury durations, sim factors) are taken literally, and the outcome invariance test passes unchanged. A seeded headless league with the setting off gives the same fingerprint as master (see Calibration).
- **Calibration** (`RUN_FRONT_OFFICE_CALIBRATION=1`, `src/test/frontOffice/calibration.script.test.ts`: 8 headless leagues (seeds 1-8) × 2 seasons with the new-league defaults, every team AI-run; _avg_ sets every team's Scouting, Analytics, Medical, Travel and Facilities to 34, _ext_ is the same leagues with even teams at 100 and odd teams at 1, levels set every preseason with `initialBudget` so the 3-season averages are the set levels from the first game; 240 team-seasons per extreme, per 82 games):

  | Measure                                                | Level 1 (min) | Level 34 (avg) | Level 100 (max) |
  | ------------------------------------------------------ | ------------- | -------------- | --------------- |
  | In-game injuries per team                              | 16.95         | 15.83          | 14.57           |
  | Injury games missed per team                           | 137.7         | 117.3          | 92.7            |
  | Measured injury games saved (season review)            | -26.9         | 0              | +24.2           |
  | Prospect fuzz, mean \|fuzz\| of a fresh draw (stddev)  | 2.47 (3.10)   | 1.58 (2.00)    | 0.61 (0.94)     |
  | Scouts in the scouting department                      | 2             | 3              | 5               |
  | Endurance penalty per tired game (travel, b2b, 3-in-4) | 4.28%         | 2.88%          | 1.53%           |
  | Share of that penalty taken off                        | -50%          | 0%             | +48%            |
  | Wins (paired with the same team at 34)                 | -2.37 ± 0.81  | 41.0           | +2.37 ± 0.79    |
  | Point differential (paired)                            | -1.31 ± 0.46  | 0              | +1.31 ± 0.43    |

  The win effect is zero-sum within a league (half the teams gain what the other half lose); by point differential it's about ±3.5 wins. League-wide the _ext_ leagues match the _avg_ ones (15.76 vs 15.83 injuries, 115 vs 117 injury games, 2.90% vs 2.88% fatigue per tired game), and with the AI's own allocation (_natural_) every department averages 37 (sd 21, like the old budget items, whose AI default also averages 37), with 15.93 injuries, 119.8 injury games and 2.86% fatigue: neutral within noise. Prospect fuzz is the scouting budget's existing formula (sampled), younger classes multiply it by up to 2.

- **Off is unchanged:** `src/test/frontOffice/offIdentity.script.test.ts` (two seeded headless leagues, a season and the next preseason with the setting off, every other new-league default on): the `simFingerprint` hash and the next random number match master exactly (ea8ef5d5… and f662b216… for seeds 11 and 12 on both).
- **Code:** `src/common/frontOffice.ts` (formulas), `src/worker/core/frontOffice/index.ts` (levels, payments, review, AI allocation), hooks in `game/writeTeamStats.ts`, `game/writePlayerStats.ts`, `player/injury.ts`, `game/loadTeams.ts`, `game/injuryDepthTeam.ts`, `GameSim.basketball/index.ts`, `staff/systems.ts`, `gm/index.ts`, `phase/newPhasePreseason.ts`, `season/updateOwnerMood.ts`, views `teamFinances.ts`, `freeAgents.ts`, `trade/summary.ts`, UI `src/ui/views/TeamFinances/FrontOffice.tsx`. Tests: `src/common/frontOffice.test.ts`, `src/worker/core/frontOffice/frontOffice.test.ts`; calibration `src/test/frontOffice/calibration.script.test.ts`.
- **Known limits:** the scouting review approximates the level the fuzz was drawn at by the current 3-season level. The Expenses bar graph doesn't show the two new departments separately (they're in the cash and profit). The rotation effect needs Coaching Systems and Realistic Rotations.

## Owner's Office

Setting `featureOwnerOffice` (_Owner's Office_, basketball, on for new leagues, off for existing ones, off in college leagues). It needs the `budget` setting. Off: none of the code below runs and no random numbers are drawn. Formulas in `src/common/ownerOffice.ts`, worker code in `src/worker/core/ownerOffice`.

- **Budget meeting:** every preseason the owner sets each team's goals (`teamSeason.ownerGoals`), with no random draws, from market size (pop rank), cash and last season's record: a payroll ceiling (93-105% of the luxury tax line, higher in big markets and with cash; above the line = willing to pay some tax), a profit target (higher in big markets and when cash is short, lower when he'll pay tax) and a win target (last season's win% + 3%, bounded 30-70%). The user gets a _Budget meeting_ message from The Owner; Team Finances shows the goals with how you are tracking. AI teams get the same goals (used only internally).
- **Owner mood:** at season end (`updateOwnerMood`) each goal scores -1 to 1 (wins vs target per 10% of the season, profit vs target per $15M scaled to the cap, payroll over the ceiling per 10% of the tax line). Wins add `0.05 × score` to the wins delta, profit + payroll add at most ±0.05 to the money delta. The annual evaluation says which goals were met.
- **Arena & facilities projects:** on Team Finances the user can start one project at a time (needs 25% of the cost in cash): Arena renovation (0.2 caps over 2 seasons, +6 facilities), Practice facility (0.3 caps over 2 seasons, +9), New arena (1 cap over 4 seasons, +12 and +10% stadium capacity). The cost is paid every regular season game from cash and recorded in `teamSeason.projectExpenses`, which `teamSeasonFinances.ts` counts as an expense (profit, owner mood, Team Finances expenses chart). It completes at the preseason after its last payment season (any unpaid remainder is paid then). Completed projects add to `t.ownerOffice.facilitiesBonus` (max 20, wears off 1 level a season), added to the facilities level of the last three seasons (`getLevelLastThree`), so attendance and everything else that reads facilities. Started after the regular season, payments begin next season.
- **AI teams:** in the preseason an AI team with no project and cash above a project's cost plus one salary cap starts one with 25% probability, from a seeded stream (season, tid), not `Math.random`.
- **The owner (`src/common/ownerProfile.ts`, `src/worker/core/ownerOffice/owner.ts`):** every team's owner has a name, a first season and four traits (0-1, average of two seeded draws, seeded by team and first season), stored in `t.ownerOffice.owner` at the preseason. Missing (existing leagues) = derived deterministically from the team and `t.commercial.ownerSince` (or the league's starting season). A sale (Commercial Deals & Ownership) gives the new owner a new profile; the honeymoon (forgiven grudges, `honeymoonOwnerMood`) stays, and a new owner makes no requests in his first season. Traits, all bounded:
  - **Patience:** firing line = −1 − 0.5 × (patience − 0.5), so −0.75 to −1.25 (stock −1), used by the annual evaluation (`genMessage`, including the "another season like that" warnings); a very patient owner (0.7+) can't fire you until one season after the grace period. Also shifts the budget meeting win target by up to ±2% and scales ultimatum stakes ×1.25 (impatient) to ×0.75 (patient).
  - **Meddling:** at most 1 / 2 / 3 requests a season (meddling < 0.3 / < 0.7 / higher), each eligible one made with probability 0.2 + 0.6 × meddling.
  - **Loyalty to stars:** trading or releasing the franchise player (best player by value at the preseason) during the regular season costs 0.04 + 0.12 × loyalty of wins mood (checked every user game), unless a "don't trade him" request is out (then that request's stake instead, +0.1 × loyalty).
  - **Luxury tax appetite:** payroll ceiling ×(1 ± 4%). AI teams: budget level −1/0/+1 at the extremes.
  - AI teams also: the project start chance ×(0.75 + 0.5 × patience), multiplied with the AI GM finance style's own factor.
- **Requests, ultimatums and mandates (user's team):** only where the evaluation judges them (one user team, not auto play/spectator, not God Mode, not college, after the grace period). At 25% of the regular season the owner may ask to cut payroll to the ceiling (if over it; due at the trade deadline, or season end without one), not to trade the franchise player (loyalty 0.35+), to play the young guys (the two best players 23 or younger averaging under 18 minutes: 18 minutes a game each from then on), and mandates a ticket price freeze (turns automatic ticket pricing off) or keeping a long-tenured fan favorite (4+ seasons with the team, 27+). At 50%, a team between .350 and .600 may get "make the playoffs or you're out". Candidates are shuffled and drawn from a seeded stream (season, tid, checkpoint). Stakes (before patience scaling): playoffs +0.05/−0.25 (playoffs mood), payroll +0.03/−0.12 (money), keep star +0.03/−0.15 (wins), young guys +0.04/−0.06 (wins); mandates cost 0.04 (money) / 0.05 (wins) when overridden, by the Override button on the Owner page or by breaking them (raising the price, trading the player). Requests settle at the trade deadline or in `updateOwnerMood`; their adjustments (plus the star reaction) are added to the deltas before the +1 cap, bounded to −0.5/+0.2 in total a season, and stored in `teamSeason.ownerSeason` and `ownerEvaluation.adjustments` (with `firingLine`). Each request is an inbox message from the owner plus an `ownerRequest` event (pop-up category Owner & finances); the evaluation message lists how they were settled and the breakdown shows them.
- **Owner page (`/owner`, Team menu, pro leagues):** name, type (with Commercial Deals & Ownership), years owned, traits, mood trend chart and breakdown (shared with the evaluation message, `views/ownerMoodSeasons.ts`), this season's goals with progress and pace, project status, pending sale, requests with status and override buttons, stored evaluations, and a projected call: this season's evaluation on the current pace (wins and judged profit scaled to a full season, the luxury tax the payroll owes now, playoffs from the win% until known, goals, settled requests) → safe (more than 1 above the firing line), warm seat (within 1), hot seat (within 0.35), likely fired (at or below), or safe while the owner can't fire you yet.

## Commercial deals & ownership

Setting `featureCommercial` (_Commercial Deals & Ownership_, basketball, on for new leagues, off for existing ones, off in college leagues), options `featureRevenueSharePct` (default 10%, 0 = no sharing) and `featureRevenueShareMode` (`equal` / `smallMarkets`). It needs the `budget` setting. Off: none of the code below runs, no random numbers are drawn and the books are unchanged. Formulas in `src/common/commercial.ts`, worker code in `src/worker/core/commercial`, state in `t.commercial`.

- **Deals:** each team has a sponsorship and a local TV deal. Fair value per season = base (0.2 / 0.22 salary caps) × (0.5 + 0.5 market + 0.25 recent success + 0.15 star power + 0.1 facilities), each input 0-1 (market from pop rank; success from the last three seasons' win% and playoff rounds; star power from hype and market; facilities level of the last three seasons plus the Owner's Office bonus). At expiry there are three offers, with seeded ±6% noise: long-term (5 seasons, 95%, fixed while the cap grows), standard (3 seasons, 100%) and performance (1 season, 85% plus up to 35% bonus scaled by the current win%, full at .700). The user gets a message and picks on Team Finances; if not, the standard offer is signed at the first regular season game. AI teams pick by their owner type (60%) or at random, from a seeded stream. In regular season games the deal payments (per game, with the difficulty fudge factor) go to `revenues.sponsorDeal` / `revenues.localTvDeal` and replace the per-game sponsor and local TV formula; playoff games earn the usual amounts. Missing deals (new league, feature just turned on) are set up at the first game.
- **Revenue sharing:** at the end of the regular season (`assessPayrollMinLuxury`, before owner distributions) each team pays the set % of its local revenue (tickets, merchandise, sponsorship and local TV, deals included) into a pool (`expenses.revenueSharing`), paid out equally or with weight 0.25 + (1 − market) (`revenues.revenueSharing`).
- **Ownership sales:** each preseason every team has a 2% chance (seeded by season and tid) to be sold to a new owner type, different from the current one: big spender, bottom line, win now (or the default traditional). The type multiplies the user's owner mood deltas (e.g. bottom line: money ×1.5, wins and playoffs ×0.8), nudges AI budget levels (+8 / −8 / +4) and changes the Owner's Office goals (payroll ceiling ×1.06 / ×0.95 / ×1.04, profit target −5 / +5 / −3 $M at the default cap). The user's team is announced a season ahead by message; the sale happens at the next preseason. Sales are news items (`ownerSale`).
- **Season P&L:** Team Finances shows every revenue and expense line of a season (including Analytics/Travel, projects, deals and revenue sharing), the totals, and a cash reconciliation: cash at start + profit − owner distributions = cash at end, with any difference from the previous season's ending cash shown as adjustments outside the books. New lines are also in the revenue and expense charts.

## Realistic AI economy

_Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ Setting `featureRealisticEconomy` (_Realistic AI Economy_, basketball, on for new leagues, off for existing ones, off in college leagues). Off = stock BBGM exactly (none of the code below runs, and no extra random draws).

- **Fair random AI-to-AI trades:** the classic random AI trade (`betweenAiTeams` `attempt`) lets the initiating team lose up to 15 value units (stock `|dv2| > 15` check). On: the initiating team's value change must be at least `FAIR_TRADE_MIN_DV` (-2) (the other side already gains, from `makeItWork`); a rejected attempt is retried with new players up to `FAIR_TRADE_RETRIES` (2) times, so trade volume stays close to stock.
- **Soft salary matching without a cap:** with `salaryCapType` none, a team over the luxury tax after an AI-to-AI trade (random or need-based two-team) can't take back more than 125% + $100k of the salary it sends out (`trade/realisticEconomy.ts`). User trades are unchanged.
- **Owner distributions:** at the end of the regular season (with the luxury tax), each AI team's owner takes 75% of the cash above one salary cap (`finances/ownerDistribution.ts`; a news item, no notification). The amount is recorded in `teamSeason.ownerDistributions` (not an expense, so profit is unchanged) and shown in the Team Finances cash chart tooltip, so cash change = revenue − expenses − distributions. User teams are left alone (in spectator mode or auto play every team is AI).
- **Cash drives spending:** in the preseason, an AI team's budget level (market size, before it's split over the departments) is nudged by its cash: `cashSpendingNudge` adds 4 levels per salary cap of cash above the one-cap reserve, bounded to ±6 levels (`finances/ownerDistribution.ts`). No random draws.
- **Measured** (`economyAudit.script.test.ts`, `RUN_ECO_AUDIT=1`, seed 1, 15 seasons, `ECO_SET='{"featureRealisticEconomy":false|true}'`), cash in $M, payroll in salary caps:

  | Config  | Season | Cash median off -> on | Cash max off -> on | Payroll max off -> on |
  | ------- | ------ | --------------------- | ------------------ | --------------------- |
  | default | 1      | 24 -> 20              | 171 -> 164         | 1.55 -> 1.55          |
  | default | 5      | 149 -> 161            | 1,072 -> 281       | 1.44 -> 1.33          |
  | default | 10     | 712 -> 179            | 2,325 -> 293       | 1.55 -> 1.32          |
  | default | 15     | 1,164 -> 193          | 3,328 -> 274       | 1.35 -> 1.38          |
  | no cap  | 1      | 31 -> 23              | 132 -> 197         | 1.49 -> 1.49          |
  | no cap  | 5      | 172 -> 155            | 795 -> 244         | 1.93 -> 1.14          |
  | no cap  | 10     | 299 -> 181            | 1,373 -> 282       | 1.49 -> 1.36          |
  | no cap  | 15     | 612 -> 187            | 2,300 -> 278       | 1.48 -> 1.38          |

  15-season means, off -> on: default trades/season 15.0 -> 16.1, payroll max 1.39 -> 1.32 caps, teams over the tax 6.5 -> 6.5; no cap trades/season 53.9 -> 35.7 (the salary rule blocks the payroll-ballooning ones), payroll max 1.55 -> 1.27, teams over the tax 8.9 -> 4.1, trades the audit scores as losing for a side 2.9 -> 0.9. 0 invalid states either way.

## Downloads

Works in every sport.

- **Tables as CSV.** DataTables (most tables in the game, native or not) keep the stock _Download spreadsheet_ item in their ⋮ menu. The fork's other tables and lists get a **Download CSV** menu at the top right of the page that lists every such table on screen, named by its caption or nearest heading (Draft Board, Mock Draft, College/Overseas, Coaches, Records Book, Milestones, Rivalries, Series Recaps, Award Vote, Newspaper, Traits, Storylines, ...). Card lists opt in with `data-csv-table` (`data-csv-cols` for the header, `data-csv-row` on each item: Best Games, Top Plays, Timeline, Games to Watch, Trade Offers, and the Wrapped deck, one row per slide, where a quiz slide's answer is only included once you answered it). The file is read from the rendered page, so it has exactly what you see: scouting fuzz, trait ranges and the no visible ratings challenge apply, and the secret aging curve never shows before retirement. The page list is `NATIVE_CSV_PAGES` in `src/ui/components/TablesCSV.tsx`; `data-no-csv` keeps a table out. Code: `src/ui/util/tableCSV.ts`.
- **Download players.** Every player table (roster, free agents, draft scouting/draft, player ratings and stats, trade pages, upcoming free agents, watch list, ...) has _Download players (PDF/JSON/CSV)_ in its ⋮ menu: the players the table shows with its filters, search and sort (all pages of it), for the table's season. The single player page has a _Download player_ button.
  - **PDF**: each player starts on a new page and gets as many landscape pages as his profile needs: overview (face, bio, contract, archetype, ratings with bars, skills and badges), stats (every stats table of the player page, regular season and playoffs, with career rows), ratings history, traits by category with status lines (aging curve only for retired players), college/overseas stats and honors, awards. Long tables continue across pages with their header repeated; tables too wide for a page are split into column groups that repeat Year/Team/Age. Each page has the player's name and "page x of y". Optionally (off by default) a section with this season's game log, and optionally a zip with one PDF per player instead of one PDF (`src/ui/util/zip.ts`, stored). Written by a small built-in PDF writer (`src/ui/util/miniPdf.ts`, JPEG faces) with an embedded Unicode font: `public/fonts/DejaVuSans*-latin.ttf` (DejaVu Sans, free license in `DejaVuSans-LICENSE.txt`), subsets of about 50 KB per weight covering Latin, Latin Extended A/B, Latin Extended Additional (Vietnamese) and combining marks, so names like Dončić, Şengün, Łukasz, Kėdainis, Þórsson or Nguyễn come out right and the text can be searched and copied. The fonts are fetched the first time a PDF is made (and precached by the service worker); without them the PDF falls back to Helvetica with accents dropped. Remake the subsets with `pyftsubset` (fontTools) from DejaVuSans.ttf / DejaVuSans-Bold.ttf with `--unicodes="U+0020-007E,U+00A0-024F,U+0259,U+02BB-02BC,U+02C6-02DD,U+0300-036F,U+1E00-1EFF,U+2010-2027,U+2030-203A,U+20AC,U+2122,U+2212" --layout-features='' --no-hinting --drop-tables+=kern,GSUB,GPOS,GDEF,FFTM,MATH,gasp`. The layout code is lazy loaded (`src/ui/util/playersPdf.ts`).
  - **JSON**: one readable file (`format: "zengm-visible-players"`) with the same data (game log too when ticked). For moving players into another league with exact ratings, the dialog links to the stock _Export Players_.
  - **CSV**: one row per player: bio, contract, scouted ratings, skills/badges, archetype, trait readings, season and career summary stats.
  - The worker (`src/worker/util/playerExport.ts`) gathers what the user can see, 20 players per request with a progress bar, so a big selection doesn't freeze the page: fuzzed ratings, no ratings at all under the no visible ratings challenge (except retired players, like the player page), the user's trait readings (ranges for prospects), and the aging curve only once revealed. Data shaping and CSV escaping (`src/common/playerExport.ts`) are tested in `src/common/playerExport.test.ts`.
- **Archetype tuning only (archuser2k, basketball).** _Tools > Export League_ has an _Archetype tuning only_ choice next to Default / None / All / Teams Only / Settings Only, and the _Your archetype tuning_ panel (League > Archetypes) an _Export tuning only (JSON)_ button beside _Export my picks_. The file (`kind: "bbgm-archetype-tuning"`, `common/archetypes/tuning.ts TuningFile`) holds only the archetype tuning: the user's "Doesn't fit?" picks (this league's and the browser-wide ones, each with its snapshot rows), the per-player overrides, the tuning fitted from the picks (`tuning`: per-build offsets, term multipliers, family offsets), its hash (`tuningHash`), the reading version (`readingVersion`), the league it came from, and, with _Include player snapshots_ (default on), the reading inputs of every pick by pick id (`snapshots`: what an offline refit reads). Import it from the tuning panel's _Import picks_ (pick files still work): the picks join this league's and, with the cross-league switch on, the browser-wide ones, the tuning is refitted (the same hash comes back), and the per-player overrides apply only in the league it was exported from (they are per player id). The developer refit reads it too (`ARCH_PICKS=archetype-tuning.json`: `fromPickFile` accepts both kinds). Code: `tuning.ts toTuningFile / fromTuningFile`, `worker/api/archetypes.ts tuningExportOnly / tuningImport`, `ui/views/ExportLeague.tsx`, `ArchetypeTuningPanel.tsx`; round trip tested in `worker/core/player/archetypeFeedback.test.ts` (export, reset, import into the same league with overrides, into another league without them, the refit reading it).
- **Export profile.** Every player page (Overview, Game Log, College/Overseas, Traits, Scouting) has an _Export profile_ button next to _Edit_ (also _Export profile (every page)..._ in the Overview's _Download player_ menu). A small dialog picks the format (JSON and PDFs in one zip, JSON only, or the PDF set) and the sections; sections that don't apply to him are greyed out with the reason. Works for active players, free agents, prospects and retired players, in pro and college leagues, and at phone width.
  - **Sections:** Overview (bio, team, draft or class year, contract, injury, archetype, ovr/pot, skills, badges, season and career stat lines, note, relatives, feats, league leader seasons, contract value index), Ratings (current and history, scouted), Stats (every stats table of the Overview, regular season and playoffs, game highs), Game Log (his latest season with games, the seasons that have one, team record in his games), College/Overseas (stats tables, honors, draft or projected pick, combine with percentiles, and the page's whole data: recruiting, college season, splits, stock, comparisons, story, scouting report), Traits (every reading with its category and status, what known traits mean in games, the known traits by season, the aging curve only once retired), Scouting (the report as the user's scouts write it: grades with notes, header, breakdowns, flags, pro report, development, team fit, the two scouts' takes, stock up/down, track record, the scouting department), Awards and honors (awards, honors before the draft, jersey numbers and retirements), Contract and mood (contract or asking amount, willingness, chance to sign, mood traits and components, salaries, career earnings; not for retired players, prospects or college leagues), Transactions (draft, signings, trades, releases), Injuries (current, history, by body part, injury prone, minutes restriction).
  - **JSON** (`<pid>_<name>_profile.json`, no byte order mark): `format: "zengm-player-profile"`, `schemaVersion: 1` (bumped when a field changes meaning; added fields don't bump it), `exportedAt`, the league context (`lid`, name, sport, season, phase and phase name, college or not, starting season, the user's team), `visibility` (ratings shown or not, the challenge, Player Traits, Scouting Reports), the `player` (pid, name, status `active`/`freeAgent`/`prospect`/`retired`, team, position, age, born, height, weight, college, draft, class year in college leagues, jersey, experience), `skipped` (requested sections that don't apply, with the reason) and `sections`: each has `title`, `note`, `facts` (label/value), `lines`, `tables` (`cols`/`rows`, like the PDF) and `data` (the section's structured data).
  - **PDF set**: a zip with a folder of one PDF per section (`01_overview.pdf`, `02_ratings.pdf`, ... `11_injuries.pdf`, and with Complete Profile Export `12_tracking.pdf` ... `16_legacy.pdf`; a section with nothing in it but a note, like "No awards yet.", gets no PDF), landscape like _Download players_ and drawn by the same code: the overview card (face, bio, ratings bars) and the scouting card (grade bars, radar, shot mix, comparisons, report) followed by the section's facts and tables, each page with his name, the section and "page x of y". Tables wrap their last column (trait status, events) instead of cutting it. Team colors too light for paper (white, yellow) fall back to navy.
  - **Complete Profile Export** _Always on in every league (core; see Core features). Before:_ (League Extras, History & Stories, `featureProfileExportFull`; on for new leagues, off for existing ones; profexp): everything else his pages and the league pages show about him. Each section then has `version: 2` (1 without it) and the file `complete: true`. Off, the five new sections are skipped with the reason and the other sections are as before. Added:
    - **Overview:** archetype tier, confidence, description and why line (the words that drove it, without the numbers), the user's archetype pick (archfb override), pro projection for a prospect (Mock Draft; the peak only with ratings shown), league leader seasons, feats and relatives as tables.
    - **College/Overseas:** season by season (school or club, class, conference, coach, record, AP rank, conference rank, NCAA/NIT result, transfer/redshirt notes), draft stock, similar prospects (ovr columns only with ratings shown), the college scouting report text, his story, the Scouting System notes, the Draft Lottery Night combine (measurements and drills with his rank in the class; not its performance number, which compares with his true ratings).
    - **Awards:** award names with the finish (an individual award row of 2nd-5th place reads "Rookie of the Year (5th place)", it used to read like a win), awards grouped by type with counts and seasons (All-Star selections, All-League, ...), award finishes from the stored awards (top 5, team made), his Award Vote rows (points, first-place votes, share; Award Vote on), this season's award races while the regular season is on.
    - **Contract and mood:** contract by season (guaranteed, options), contract type, release cost, extension, trade request, holdout, mood history, the "what would change his mood" scenarios.
    - **Transactions:** _All news_, every event about him (awards, injuries, Hall of Fame, feats too) with its type.
    - **Tracking profile** (Advanced Tracking Stats): play types by season and career (possessions, frequency, PPP, FG%, TOV%) and the shooting, defense, on/off and clutch groups.
    - **Recruiting:** pro leagues: high school class, stars, national and position rank, composite, offers, finalists, commitment, signing period, high school honors, reclassification, his path (schools, junior colleges, overseas clubs and leagues by season). College leagues: stars, rank, home state, recruiting class, commitment or signing, walk-on, the user's priority, the schools recruiting him (interest, offer, visit), what he wants only as far as the user's staff scouted him, how he left school.
    - **Playoffs and tournaments:** his team each season (record, seed, his playoff line, result, Finals, titles; a college league's national tournament the same way, plus NIT games), his college tournaments (conference tournament, NCAA region, seed and result, NIT), the postseason games of his draft year and its splits. No in-season tournament exists in this fork.
    - **Coaches** (Coaching Staff): head coach (with that season's record), development coach and the rest of the staff for each of his seasons, his fit with his head coach's systems now (Coaching Systems; from scouted ratings, not under the challenge), his own coaching career when he became a coach (record, awards); his college and overseas coaches. GMs are never former players in this fork.
    - **Legacy:** Hall of Fame induction, plaque and speech (basketball), GOAT Lab score and rank among every player with the league's formula and his best GOAT season, rivalry lines (Rivalries and Rivalry Stories: record and averages in his teams' rivalry games, hottest first), face history (Face Aging; the PDF draws the faces).
    - Code: `src/worker/util/playerProfileExtras.ts`; tests: `src/worker/util/playerProfileExportFull.test.ts` (a player drafted from a college tournament team two seasons in: every section, no leaks under fuzz and the challenge, League Extras off, the PDF set).
  - **Hidden information:** built from the same worker views as the pages, so the pages' rules apply: scouted (fuzzed) ratings, none at all under the no visible ratings challenge except for retired players (rating keys are also stripped from anything taken from a page, like a league leader season in a rating), trait readings as ranges while unknown with no score, the aging curve only once retired, the scouting report from the user's department. The raw player never leaves the worker; the report's internal takes are left out.
  - **Code:** `src/common/playerProfileExport.ts` (format, sections, helpers), `src/worker/util/playerProfileExport.ts` (`getPlayerProfileExport`, `getPlayerProfileExportSections`), `src/ui/components/ExportProfile.tsx` (dialog, files), `src/ui/util/profilePdf.ts` (PDF set), tested in `src/worker/util/playerProfileExport.test.ts` (sections per kind of player, scouted ratings and no leaks, the challenge, college leagues, a PDF and zip smoke test).

## Dark mode contrast

The dark theme is its own stylesheet (`public/css/dark.scss`, the app never sets `data-bs-theme`), so Bootstrap 5.3's `--bs-*-text-emphasis`, `--bs-*-bg-subtle` and `--bs-*-border-subtle` kept their light theme values there (`text-danger-emphasis` was #58151c on #212529). Hotfix, dark theme only (the light stylesheet is unchanged, same hash): those variables use Bootstrap's dark mode values for the theme colors (scouting flags and grades, scout hit rates, Mock Draft and Records Book highlights); `.text-danger` (mood badges, negative +/-, rating and value changes, injuries, warnings) and the red watch flag are #ff9999, at least 4.5:1 on the page, the sorted column, light bordered buttons and highlighted (table-info) rows; `text-bg-secondary` badges (confidence, rumor status, "Worth a look") have white text; `bg-primary` badges (National TV) and `bg-darkblue` news badges are darker behind white text; scouting letter grades as text (`.scout-grade-text`, D/F red, A green) are mixed 60/40 with white. Screen only: printing is as before.

## League salt

Fork features derive many values from fixed keys (pid, name/birth/ratings, season, gid, award), so two leagues with the same keys
(real-player leagues, or any new league since pids restart at 0) used to repeat the same "random" values. Each league now has a
`leagueSalt` game attribute (uint32) mixed into every seeded derivation through one helper (`common/leagueSalt.ts`: `salted(key)` for
string keys, `saltedSeed(n)` for numeric seeds). Both are the identity at salt 0, so old leagues, files without a salt and every
existing test give byte-for-byte the same values.

- **New leagues** (random players, real players, custom files): a random salt (from `crypto`, so drawing it never moves `Math.random`
  and a seeded league creation makes the same players). A league file with `leagueSalt` keeps it; a league file exported by this fork
  before salts (it has `playerTraitsSeed` but no `leagueSalt`) keeps salt 0.
- **Within a league** the salt is fixed: no flicker on reload, the same values after re-sync. Worker reads it from `g`, the UI from its
  synced local state (`gameAttributesSyncedToUi`).
- **God Mode > Re-roll league salt** (God Mode page, with a confirm): draws a new salt, records `{season, phase, from, to}` in
  `leagueSaltHistory` and an event log entry. Stored values are kept (existing players' sub-ratings and sub seeds, trait seeds, faces,
  college stats, coach profiles). Values derived from now on change (new players' shapes/traits/faces, future award votes, future
  college seasons, development and aging noise, injury losses). Values recomputed when shown change right away (face styles and face
  aging drawn on the page, newspaper, play-by-play flavor and story wording).
- **Vary Real Players' Sub-Ratings** (League Settings > Real Players, off by default): a real player's stat-derived lean gets bounded
  salted noise (sd 3, at most 6 points per sub) when his shape is first made (league creation). Off = exact real data. Trait seeds
  already differ per league (`playerTraitsSeed`, now also salted).

What is salted (each primitive, so every caller of it is covered):

| Primitive | Callers (what they key on) |
| ------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `ratingsBuilds/rng.ts` `Rng` (hash of the seed string) | sub-ratings (`subr/subn/subd/subseed                                                                         | ...`: pid or name/birth/ratings, season, age; incl. Wider Sub-Rating Shapes' real `subZ` leans and role growth), player traits (`traits/traitDrift/traitScout/agingCurve | playerTraitsSeed | pid...`), career aging injury losses ("never the same", pid/season/injury), mentor development, ratings builds (regenerate/randomize), expanded ratings, draft class shape and story, draft combine, pre-draft splits, scouting reports and scouts, staff depth (scouting/coaching/careers/regions) |
| `collegeStats/rng.ts` `Rng`, `profile.ts` club pick | college stats, recruiting, NCAA tournament/polls/storylines, scouting reports (pid, team, season) |
| `newspaper/text.ts` `Rng`, newspaper `seedKey` | newspaper wording, page order, mastheads (lid, starting season, section, tid) |
| `pbpFlavor/rng.ts` `Rng` | play-by-play flavor, trash talk, clutch, career calls, HoF stories, franchise stories (gid, pid, tid+season) |
| `faceSeed.ts` `seededUniform`/`seededRandom` | face styles (incl. batch D parts and beard fit choices), face aging, face events (face JSON, pid, season) |
| `wrapped/util.ts` `seededRng`/`pick` | Wrapped and storylines wording, quiz (seed text) |
| `awardVote` vote seed | award vote noise (season, award) |
| `hype/util.ts`, `lotteryNight.ts`, `draftClassStory.ts` picks | hype, lottery night and draft class wording (gid, seed text) |
| `draftStash.ts` seasons abroad | stashed players' overseas seasons (pid, season) |
| `coachSystems.ts` profile, `staff/systems.ts` coaching tree | coach profiles (name, birth, style), mentor inheritance (cids) |

Not salted, on purpose: `playerTraitsSeed` and rumor seeds (already random per league, though the traits stream is also salted);
college team colors and nicknames (stable branding of a school); cache version hashes (`wrappedData`, archetype tuning); test and
calibration seeds (`getFirstRoundSlotProbs`, `simLiveGameForTests`, sim metrics). No seeded randomness in the archetype v2 engine
(kNN vote and hysteresis are deterministic in the ratings), beard fit geometry or pre-draft ranks (frozen snapshots), so nothing to salt
there. The game sim keeps using `Math.random`; `outcomeInvariance.test.ts` is unchanged.

## Known limits (all features)

- Basketball only unless a section says otherwise; the other sports hide the menu items and links.
- The Wrapped, Storylines and Timeline pages share a whole-history snapshot that takes a while the first time in a big league.
- The league-wide averages with player traits, the possession model or injury depth on stay about the same as with them off, but individual games and seasons are of course different; use the switches (off) to replay the old behaviour.
- Existing leagues keep playing as before until the game-affecting switches (possession model, traits, injury depth, contracts, trades, drafting, coaching staff, GM personalities) are turned on in League Extras. Games already played replay unchanged.
- Draft board and scouting need prospects: the Draft Board is empty in a league without upcoming classes.

## Performance

**Page loads (sgdrift).** Best Games read and rated every box score of a season the first time it was opened in a session (2.5-3.2 s on a fast machine), and Team History, League Achievements, Home Court and Rivalries took 1.0-1.3 s. Profiled in the browser (`--remote-debugging-port`, the shared worker's CPU profile mapped through the source map):

- Best Games: the hype engine's scoring environment of each finished game went through every game of the season before it (quadratic: 1,230 games, about 75% of the rating time); wording the play-by-play of up to 120 games for the top plays (`FlavorSession`, most of it the phrase memory's name and number replacements); and the season's box scores were read three times.
- Home Court, Rivalries and Team History's Rivals card: every stored box score of every season was read (Rivalries and Team History with a pass over the whole store) for a few numbers each.
- League Achievements: the engine (about 1.4 s) ran again on the first visit of a session, and after every check that remembered an unlock, since that write to the game attributes changed the snapshot's signature.

Fixes (what the pages show is the same, checked on the rendered pages of a 5- and a 10-season league, before against after, in the first session and in a later one that reads the kept results):

- Hype: the environment of every game of a season comes from one pass (`envPrefix` in `common/hype/gather.ts`: the regular season games before a game are a prefix of the season's by day, so the running totals at the start of each day are kept; a test checks every game against the full pass). The phrase memory keeps its keys (`VarietyMemory.key`). Best Games reads a season's box scores once.
- Kept results: a past season's Best Games, and its box scores reduced to what Home Court, Rivalries and the Rivals card read (`util/gameSummaries.ts`), are kept in a database of their own (`page-cache`, `util/pagePersist.ts`), each with the number of box scores its season had and a version (Best Games also the team names and gender); the change log forgets a season whenever its box scores are written to, and a league when it is deleted or reloaded behind the cache's back. A game's plays are kept for the session.
- Background warm-up (`util/pageWarm.ts`): after a league loads, after phase changes and after games, once nothing is being simmed, it works out what isn't kept yet one season at a time (newest first), the current season's Best Games and the League Achievements engine. A page opened while it runs waits for the same work instead of doing it twice.
- The League Achievements remembered unlocks (`leagueAchievements` game attribute) no longer count as a change for the snapshot (`changeLog.noteWrite`).

Measured on a shared 4-core machine that was also running other jobs (it was 3-4x slower than the 2.5-3.2 s above), Playwright, click to the page drawn; first visit of a session with the league just opened (3 s), with the league open for a minute (the warm-up done), and in a later session; base = master before:

| League     | Page                                     | Base first visit | After, league just opened | After, warm-up done | After, later session |
| ---------- | ---------------------------------------- | ---------------- | ------------------------- | ------------------- | -------------------- |
| 5 seasons  | Best Games (each season with box scores) | 11.6-13.2 s      | 4.2-5.5 s                 | 45-67 ms            | 18-89 ms             |
| 5 seasons  | Team History                             | 1.12 s           | 0.20 s                    | 0.18 s              | 0.19 s               |
| 5 seasons  | League Achievements                      | 2.06 s           | 0.62 s                    | 0.55 s              | 0.66 s               |
| 5 seasons  | Home Court                               | 1.13 s           | 0.19 s                    | 0.18 s              | 0.23 s               |
| 5 seasons  | Rivalries                                | 0.98 s           | 0.11 s                    | 0.11 s              | 0.12 s               |
| 10 seasons | Best Games (each season with box scores) | 11.7-13.0 s      | 4.6-5.5 s                 | 48-65 ms            | 45-278 ms            |
| 10 seasons | Team History                             | 0.99 s           | 0.23 s                    | 0.22 s              | 0.19 s               |
| 10 seasons | League Achievements                      | 2.68 s           | 0.49 s                    | 0.61 s              | 0.53 s               |
| 10 seasons | Home Court                               | 1.00 s           | 0.20 s                    | 0.29 s              | 0.19 s               |
| 10 seasons | Rivalries                                | 0.83 s           | 0.12 s                    | 0.13 s              | 0.11 s               |

League Achievements' worker part is now about 50 ms; the rest is the browser drawing a page of a few hundred achievements (layout and paint), the same before. Best Games opened in the first seconds after a league loads, before the warm-up got to that season, still takes as long as working it out (about a third of before).

**Sim speed (perf2).** Measured with the sim benchmark (`src/worker/core/game/simBenchmark.test.ts`, one season of a new
default league, CPU time; `SIM_BENCH_PROF` with `SIM_BENCH_PROF_FROM`/`SIM_BENCH_PROF_TO` profiles only some phases,
`SIM_BENCH_HEAP` writes sampled allocations for `tools/perf/sim/heapTop.cjs`). Every change keeps the same seed giving the
same league: the benchmark's `hash` (rosters, contracts, ratings, records) and `hash2` (archetypes, scouting snapshots and
draft calls, deaths, every box score with its stored play-by-play, events) match before and after, with all extras on and
all off, and the GameSim outcome-invariance fixture is unchanged.

- Scouting reports: snapshots, scout calls and the rumors' scouting split don't classify an archetype (only a full
  report's header shows it, and a prospect's is a live classification each time).
- Archetypes: the per-build parts of the scoring that don't depend on the player are worked out once per build (the
  classification memo keys NaN and Infinity apart).
- Contract demands (stock): the auction's cumulative weights go into one reused buffer.
- Killing off old retired players (start of the draft phase): one read of the retired players by the `tid` index,
  ordered like the old scan of every player drafted in the last 110 years, so the same players get the same draws.
- GameSim: substitution values kept on the players instead of a new Map per call, lineup values, positional shares and
  sort scratch in reused buffers, fatigue worked out once per player in `ratingArray`; coaching system fits list each
  needs table once.
- Measured (CPU, all extras on, 2-3 runs each on a shared machine): regular season + playoffs about -9% (archetypes
  and scouting) and then another -4.5% (GameSim), offseason about -14%; with all extras off the season is unchanged and
  the offseason about -13% (contract demands). Left: GameSim itself (about 40% of a season), garbage collection (about
  15%), the preseason archetype update (about 1.7 s, every player is classified again after development).

**Sim speed (perf3).** Same benchmark, all extras on; `SIM_BENCH_PERF3_OFF=weekly` turns the change off for a comparison, and the
benchmark now also prints `hash3` (every player and team stats row's Advanced Tracking Stats) and the first regular season's league
totals per team game.

- Coaching system fits and player trait effects (shipped): the sim's trait effects for a player are kept for a week of games
  (`player/traits.ts` `getSimTraitEffectsWeekly`, worked out again when he changes teams or his ratings row or trait seeds are
  replaced), and a team's scheme fit is worked out again only when its rotation changes and once a week (`staff/systems.ts`
  `getWeeklyFits`, see Coaching systems). Neither reads anything else that moves during a season, so the sim inputs are the same as
  before: 8 leagues × 1 season with and without it give the same `hash`, `hash2` and `hash3` (so identical league totals). A trait
  score that depends on something else that can move in a season (his pro seasons played, which counts once he plays his first game)
  would wait for the next week; none of the 8 leagues had one. Measured: trait effects 0.45 → 0.16 s and fits 0.23 → 0.11 s of CPU over
  a profiled regular season and playoffs (26-27 s), about 1.5%; the benchmark (8 seeds, one run each) −1.7% and −1.9% CPU in the
  season phases (noise about ±3% per run).
- Preseason archetype relabel only for players whose inputs moved (tried, not shipped): each stored label kept what it was read
  from (ratings and sub-ratings after the age discount, ovr, position, traits, box-score and play-type readings, the tuning hash),
  and a preseason kept the label when those moved less than a threshold. After a season's development almost nobody qualifies: over
  3 seasons (570-617 players a preseason) a mean rating change of at most 1.5 and at most 6 on any one, with traits within 3, kept 0
  players; much looser limits (mean 2.5, max 10, traits 10, stats z 0.5) kept 17-32 (3-6%), of which 6 of 69 got a different label than
  a full relabel would have. The saving would have been at most 3-6% of the 1.6-2.4 s update, so it was left out
  (`archetypeRelabel.script.test.ts` in the reverted commit measures it).
- Advanced Tracking Stats added every 3 days (tried, not shipped): the games' tracking stats waited by stats row and were added every
  third day of games in pro leagues (player rows summed, team rows replayed game by game in order, so the in-season lineup pruning kept
  the same lineups), flushed at phase changes, when the sim stopped, on league close and before pages and awards that read them.
  Season totals were identical (the same `hash3` as game by game), but the per-game merges it saves cost about 0.1 s of a 27 s season
  (`addTeamAdv` 0.08-0.09 s, player rows a few ms), within noise, so the extra state (games not yet added, lost if the tab closes
  mid-sim) was not worth it.

**Sim speed (perf4).** Same benchmark, new league defaults (every core feature on), CPU time, base and change run alternately
3 times each (medians): regular season 23.9 s → 21.4 s (−10%), regular season and playoffs 26.1 → 23.7 s (−9%), with the
offseason and preseason 33.1 → 31.2 s (−6%); the offseason itself is unchanged (7.0-7.1 s, most of it the preseason archetype
update and development, which draws random numbers). GameSim alone on 185 captured real games: 6.8 → 6.1 ms a game. Results are
the same: the benchmark's `hash`, `hash2` and `hash3`, the outcome-invariance fixture, and every database store after each stage
of 4 leagues (5 seasons in all) with `simDeterminism.script.test.ts` (see Testing).

- GameSim: the game settings read during possessions are read once per game (`readSimSettings`); each player's injury rate
  before the tiredness terms is kept for the game (the same product in the same order, checked against its inputs) and the tired
  weights are worked out once a possession; team chemistry and switch defense bonuses, the possession traits (`deriveTraits`) and
  the lineup means of the constant trait numbers (foul, steal gamble, temper) are kept until the lineup (or a player's trait
  effects) changes; a player's shot selection bias is kept for the game; `isLateGame` once per loop over the players
  (`fatigueLate`); index loops and a reused buffer instead of per-call arrays in `pickPlayer` and `updatePlayersOnCourt`.
- Advanced Tracking Stats: the possessions each offensive player had and shared with each defender are counted per stretch of
  unchanged lineups and added to the maps when the lineups change (whole numbers, same insertion order).
- Stored play-by-play: the encoder writes character codes into a reused byte buffer instead of appending one character at a time
  to a string (the same string).
- Cache: array index keys parsed once (range queries parsed every key each time), rows appended in place instead of `concat`.
- Play-by-play career numbers: the single-game high keys made once.

Wrapped and Storylines read the whole league into a snapshot. Converted pieces that can't change any more (retired players, finished seasons' box scores and team seasons) are cached in a separate IndexedDB database (`wrapped-cache`), versioned by the converter code and invalidated through the league's change log, so later sessions only read what changed. Idle snapshots are evicted from memory. `tools/perf/wrapped/` holds the Playwright benchmark scripts (see the header of `bench.cjs`).

## Testing

- Unit tests: `node_modules/.bin/vitest --run --project basketball` (plus `football` and `baseball`, which must stay green since the features are gated).
- Test isolation: files share a worker (`isolate: false`). A league made with `league.createStream` starts its cache's automatic flushes; a cache replaced without stopping them (`resetCache` in a later test) used to keep flushing its stale records into whatever league was open next (an unhandled "objectStore.put is not a function" against a mock league, a ConstraintError against a real one). `resetCache` now stops the old cache, and a cache that is no longer `idb.cache` never flushes again (`Cache._autoFlush`).
- Sim calibration tables (not part of the normal run): possession model `POSS_METRICS=1`, player traits `TRAITS_METRICS=1`, coaching systems `COACH_CAL=1`, spacing & shot creation and matchup defense `SM_CAL=1` (`spacingMatchup.script.test.ts`) (see the test files' headers).
- Sim determinism check (not part of the normal run, for speed work): `SIM_DET=1 SIM_DET_SEEDS=1 SIM_DET_SEASONS=1 SIM_DET_OUT=/tmp/after.jsonl [SIM_DET_BASE=/tmp/before.jsonl] node_modules/.bin/vitest run --project basketball src/worker/core/game/simDeterminism.script.test.ts` plays a new default league with a seeded `Math.random` and a frozen clock (the rumors' seed and two short-lived caches read it) through a regular season, the playoffs, the offseason and a week of the next season, and hashes every database store at each stage (game attributes one by one). With `SIM_DET_BASE` (lines from the untouched code) it fails on any store that differs and names it. One seed per process.
- Long-run league check (not part of the normal run): `LONG_RUN=1 LONG_RUN_CONFIG=defaults|all|off|random LONG_RUN_SEASONS=20 LONG_RUN_OUT=/tmp/run.jsonl node_modules/.bin/vitest run --project basketball src/worker/core/league/longRun.script.test.ts` plays a spectator league season by season and writes one JSON line per season (league averages, ratings levels and age curve, positions and archetypes, rookie quality, player counts, roster sizes, salaries, sim and offseason CPU time with a fixed-work calibration, database and cache size) plus `PROBLEM` lines from its checks of every box score (points, minutes with overtime, team totals, plus/minus, fouls, starters, quarters), season totals against the box scores, advanced stats ranges, awards (winners, rookies, duplicates), the playoff bracket against the box scores, the All-Star MVP and the Records Book's top marks. `LONG_RUN_RANDOM_GAME=1` (with `random`) also randomizes the game rules (periods, quarter and overtime length, Elam ending, foul-outs, three pointers, players on the court, overtimes and shootouts, playoff format); `LONG_RUN_SETTINGS` adds settings, `LONG_RUN_FORCEWIN=1` forces God Mode results, `LONG_RUN_PROF=prefix` writes a CPU profile of the last offseason.
- Native sweep (not part of the normal run, `src/worker/core/league/nativeSweep.test.ts`, see its header): every League Extra on, every league page loaded at every phase. `NATIVE_SWEEP_FLIP=1` (sweep20) also turns every League Extra off on day 5 of the second season through the League Extras tab's call and back on on day 8, loading every page each time; `NATIVE_SWEEP_SLOW_MS=300` logs each page whose worker load takes longer than that. The long-run check's season line also has `archShares` (sweep20): the rostered players' stored builds, the generic builds' share and the biggest build's share.
- AI GM audit (not part of the normal run; `AI_GMS=0` turns GM personalities off for a before/after, and with them on it also logs GM firings/hires, strategy switches and per-team trade counts): `RUN_AI_AUDIT=1 AI_SEEDS=1,2 AI_SEASONS=4 node_modules/.bin/vitest --run --project basketball src/test/ratingInflation/aiAudit.script.test.ts` simulates a league with every extra on and writes one JSON line per season (`AI_OUT`, default `/tmp/aiAudit.jsonl`): AI-AI trades (three-team, with pick conditions, for trade requests), free agent signings, re-signings, extensions, trade requests (and how many were traded), holdouts, teams over the tax/cap, dead money, roster sizes, win% and team ovr spread, mean contract (% of cap), mean ovr, and the offers AI teams would send the user right now, valued from the user's side. `AI_CAPEX=0` turns salary cap exceptions off, `AI_REPEATER=1` / `AI_STRETCH=1` turn on their options; it also logs free agent signings above the minimum, their mean and total salary, exception uses by type, stretched releases, luxury tax paid and repeater taxpayers.
- Economy audit (not part of the normal run): `RUN_ECO_AUDIT=1 ECO_CONFIG=default ECO_SEASONS=15 node_modules/.bin/vitest --run --project basketball src/test/ratingInflation/economyAudit.script.test.ts`, one long league per run (configs `default`, `hard`, `none`, `repeater` (repeater tax + stretch), `small` (16 teams), `large` (40), `expansion` (2 teams added in season 5), `noGms`; `ECO_BASE=defaults` uses only the new-league defaults, lighter than every extra on). Each season: payrolls against the cap, tax and aprons, roster sizes, contracts against a value-based reference, unsigned good free agents, AI-AI trades valued by both teams' own trade AI right after they're made (`ECO_TRADE_DUMP=file` lists the lopsided ones), free agency, cap exceptions, GM and head coach changes, profit, cash and the owner's money mood, front office levels and spending against the envelope, rumor calibration, and invalid states (players without contracts, stale or duplicate picks, conditions owed to folded teams, hard cap overages). `firedUser.script.test.ts` (`RUN_FIRED_USER=1`): the owner fires the user after a season, he takes another team and plays on.
- Lint: `node_modules/.bin/eslint .` and `node_modules/.bin/tsc --build`.
